# FSR Reloaded — AGENTS.md

## Build
```bash
./gradlew.bat build          # compile + jar
./gradlew.bat runClient      # launch with mod
```

Java 25, Fabric Loom 1.16-SNAPSHOT, Minecraft 26.1.2, Fabric API 0.152.1+26.1.2 (see `gradle.properties`).

## Architecture
- **Split source sets**: client code under `src/client/java/`, shared resources under `src/main/resources/`
- **Entrypoint**: `com.fsr2mod.FSRMod` implements `ClientModInitializer`
- **Mixins** (5, in `com.fsr2mod.mixin`):
  - `GameRendererMixin` — `render(HEAD)` → `onRenderBegin`, `render(TAIL)` → `onRenderEnd`, `@ModifyArg` on `ProjectionMatrixBuffer.getBuffer(Matrix4f)` in `renderLevel` → `captureAndJitter` (world projection jitter), `@Redirect` on `ProjectionMatrixBuffer.getBuffer(Projection)` in `renderLevel` → `jitterHandProjection` (first-person hand projection jitter), `renderLevel(TAIL)` → `onSceneRendered`
  - `FramebufferBlitMixin` — intercepts `RenderTarget.blitToScreen` for FSR presentation
  - `MinecraftClientMixin` — `@Shadow mainRenderTarget` only
  - `MinecraftClientAccessor` — `setMainRenderTarget` accessor
  - `WindowMixin` — `onFramebufferResize` → `processor.onResize`; additionally `@Inject(RETURN, cancellable)` on `getWidth()`/`getHeight()` for RenderScale-style window scale override when `FSRProcessor.windowScaleActive` is true
- **FSR shaders are inline GLSL strings** in `FSR2Pipeline.java` and `FSR3Pipeline.java` (no separate `.glsl` files)
- **Jitter**: Halton(2,3) sequence in [-0.5, 0.5] render-pixel range (full FSR2 standard amplitude). Applied via `proj.m02 += jitter.x * 2/w` and `proj.m12 += jitter.y * 2/h`. Full amplitude ensures proper temporal sample distribution.
- **Accumulation weight**: stored in lock status texture B channel, guard `uFrameIndex > 0` to prevent garbage on frame 0. **Must** store `newAccumWeight / 15.0` (not `adjAccum / 15.0`) — `adjAccum` is 0 on every frame because it derives from `accumWeight` which was read from the same B channel, creating a zero trap. FSR2 had this bug (< fix 2026-06-28).
- **FSR4 crash — "allocator Reset FAILED storm" then heap corruption 0xC0000374 (2026-08-30, the 16:56 session after the jaggedfix deploy)**: the crash was NOT an allocator-protocol bug — the `fsr4_diag.log` tail proved the full chain: (1) `g_ffxDispatch` threw `0xe06d7363` (the deterministic FSR4-SDK-with-FG-context crash on this GPU); (2) the FSR4 SEH crash handler then did `Close()` + `ExecuteCommandLists()` on the PARTIALLY-RECORDED list — submitting corrupted GPU commands → AMD driver GPU timeout → TDR → `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_REMOVED` (`hr=0x887a0005` on the next `CreateRootSignature`/`CreateCommittedResource`); (3) after device removal every D3D12 call fails (`allocator Reset FAILED hr=0x80004005` storm — `ensureInitialized` returns cached READY and never actually calls `GetDeviceRemovedReason`, so removal is invisible); (4) Java's bypass-active branch swallowed every failure (`isBypassActive()` → lenient return) so no suspension/hard-disable ever fired — the loop spun (pool recreates failing 0x887a0005 ×7, device rebuild, crash again) until the process died with `STATUS_HEAP_CORRUPTION`. The FG `prepare` crash path already had the correct policy ("Do NOT close/execute the partially-recorded command list… submitting them causes a GPU timeout (TDR) which removes the entire D3D12 device") — the FSR4 dispatch path predates that lesson and does the opposite. **Fix (3 native + 1 Java)**: (1) new `D3D12Device::recreateCommandResources()` — releases + recreates the round-robin allocator pair + command list, zeroes per-allocator fence values (plain COM calls only, safe in an SEH `__except` handler); (2) the FSR4 `__except` handler now calls it instead of Close+Execute — no GPU submit, no fence signal (nothing was submitted; advancing without executing would store a never-signaled fence value the next pre-dispatch wait would block on for 2s), bypass (armed) takes over next frame; (3) the FG `generate` crash path had the SAME latent submit-after-crash — now Detaches the corrupted allocator+list like `prepare` does (next `prepare` reinitializes + recreates them); (4) Java bypass branch gained `fsr4BypassFailStreak` — the first bypass failure (SDK crash frame, resources not yet created) stays lenient, but N consecutive bypass failures mean the device is removed (the bypass dispatch succeeds every frame on a healthy device), so after `FSR4_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_DISPATCH_FAILURES` (5) it suspends + hard-disables to FSR1 instead of spinning until heap corruption; reset on a successful dispatch. Expected: the crash frame discards the poisoned list, next frame's bilinear bypass runs (FG stays alive), session continues with no TDR and no crash. Deployed 1.21.11 instance 17:05 (backup `.bak-20260830-crashfix`). **SECOND FIX 2026-08-30 (17:17, "now it just freezes at the first frame while the game is running in the background")**: the first fix's `recreateCommandResources()` used `cmdList_.Reset()`/`cmdAllocs_[i].Reset()` — but the crashed list is in the RECORDING state (the SDK never closed it), and RELEASING a recording-state command list is a D3D12 contract violation that corrupts the runtime's per-device allocator bookkeeping: `createCommandAllocator` FAILED immediately (diag log `recreateCommandResources: createCommandAllocator FAILED`), leaving `cmdAllocs_` NULL → every subsequent dispatch null-deref'd (SEH-caught) → Java's `fsr4BypassFailStreak` hit 5 and hard-disabled to FSR1 (game log `5 consecutive bypass failures — hard-disabling`) → but FG stayed ACTIVE (`fgActive()` only checks `fg4PermanentlyDisabled`) and hammered the broken D3D12 device every frame — `nativeFinish`'s 5000ms fence wait stalled the render thread ~5s/frame = the freeze (game log: disable at 17:10:29, no render logs until the user quit at 17:10:37). Fix (3 changes): (1) `recreateCommandResources()` now **Detaches** the list + allocators instead of releasing them (leaked to process exit — the exact FG-proven pattern: "Use Detach() not Reset() to avoid AMD Vulkan-on-12 teardown crash"; never-executed lists hold no GPU memory, so the leak is a handful of CPU-side COM objects per crash) — the recreate now SUCCEEDS on the healthy device and the bilinear bypass takes over next frame with no cascade; (2) dispatch gained a null-allocator guard before `commandAllocator()->Reset()` — if a future recreate genuinely fails, it returns false cleanly instead of null-derefing, and Java's streak still hard-disables to FSR1; (3) `fgActive()` gained `&& !fsr4HardDisabled` — a hard-disabled session must NOT let FG run (it interpolates from the FSR4 output on the same broken device); the session degrades to the D3D12-free FSR1 spatial render with no stalls, and a deliberate settings change (markDirty) clears the latch and re-arms both. Expected now: crash frame discards the poisoned list via Detach + fresh allocator pair, next frame's bilinear bypass runs (FG stays ACTIVE and healthy), NO teardown, NO rebuild, NO freeze. **THIRD FIX 2026-08-30 (17:34, "it is still just frozen")**: the second fix was correct but insufficient — the new HRESULT logging proved the real story: `createCommandAllocator[0] FAILED hr=0x887a0005` = **DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_REMOVED**. The FSR4 SDK's `g_ffxDispatch` throw itself REMOVES the D3D12 device (not just a corrupted list) once an FG `ffxCreateContext` exists on this iGPU — so by the time the post-crash handler ran, the device was already gone and the bilinear bypass (which runs on D3D12) could never succeed → every FSR4+FG session collapsed to FSR1 (game log 17:19: crash → 5 consecutive bypass failures → hard-disable → FSR1), which at qualityScale 0.25 reads as "frozen" to the user. The arm-after-crash design is fundamentally unsound on this part. Fix: **pre-arm the bypass BEFORE the first dispatch** on AMD-iGPU + FG sessions, so `g_ffxDispatch` is never called at all and the device stays healthy: (1) `D3D12Device` now flags the selected adapter as an iGPU (`isIgpu_`, AMD vendor && dedicated VRAM < 4GB — Radeon 860M reports 1998MB); (2) new JNI `Fsr4Native.isAmdIntegratedGpu()` + `Fsr4Native.armBypass()` (sets `Fsr4Context::g_bypassFsr4 = true` + `PRE-ARMED bilinear bypass` diag line); (3) both FSR4 init-success sites (GL + VKMod) call `armBypass()` when `isFrameGenDx12() && isAmdIntegratedGpu()` — the bypass dispatches on the healthy device, FG interpolates normally, NO crash, NO device removal, NO FSR1 fallback. Real FSR4 SDK is preserved on discrete AMD GPUs (isIntegratedAmd false) and after a settings change (destroy clears the latch). Both per-dispatch bypass logs are now rate-limited (first 2 + every 256th) since the bypass is the steady-state FG path on this hardware. Session runs bilinear+FG (smooth, frame-doubled; base is bilinear not FSR4 — the hardware cannot run the real FSR4 SDK with an FG context). **FOURTH FIX 2026-08-30 (17:44, "it is still completely frozen while the game runs fine in the background" — FG is NOT viable on this GPU at all)**: the third fix's pre-arm alone was still insufficient. The 17:38 diag proved it: with the pre-arm, the FSR4 SDK was never dispatched (no CRASH line), yet `bypass: CreateRootSignature FAILED hr=0x887a0005` — CPU-only `CreateRootSignature` fails only if the device is ALREADY removed, so the device died between FSR4 init OK and the first dispatch, i.e. **the FG `ffxCreateContext` itself removes the D3D12 device when it coexists with the FSR4 context** (FG init logs absent before the failure in the 17:38 diag). Three builds now confirm every variant dies with device-removed: (a) FSR4 SDK dispatch + FG → crash + removal; (b) pre-armed bilinear bypass + FG → removal before the bypass runs; therefore the coexistence is impossible and NO D3D12 recovery can help. Fix: **do not create the FG context at all on an AMD iGPU** — `FSRProcessor.maybeDisableFgOnIgpu()` (called at both FSR4 init-success sites when `isFrameGenDx12() && Fsr4Native.isAmdIntegratedGpu()`) sets `fgIgpuUnsupported`, which makes `fgActive()` return false so `Fg4Native` is never initialized, the FG context is never created, and the session runs **plain real FSR4** (real temporal upscale, no crash, no device removal). Logs a clear one-time `[FG_DX12] Frame Generation DX12 is not supported on this AMD integrated GPU…` warn. Discrete AMD GPUs (isAmdIntegratedGpu false) keep real FSR4+FG. The latch clears on a deliberate settings change (markDirty). Expected: FSR3_FG_DX12 on the Radeon 860M runs smooth real FSR4 with no freeze — plain FSR4 (no FG) is the only viable D3D12 mode on this GPU; for actual frame-doubling the 860M cannot run the FG SDK at all.
- **FSR4 jagged diagonal edges — DEPTH_INVERTED regression + jitter contract (2026-08-30, "fsr 4 edges are jagged when diagonal and not smooth even at 25% resolution" fix)**: TWO independent contract breaks in the port-era native, both restoring validated-baseline behavior. (1) **DEPTH_INVERTED re-added**: the 1.21.11 port set `FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_DEPTH_INVERTED` in `ffxCreateContextDescUpscale.flags` — but the shared pool depth is NON-reversed (0=near, 1=far, per DEPTH_COPY_CS / VK MV-gen), which is EXACTLY the state f7a8682 removed the flag for: the SDK linearizes depth backwards → every pixel lands on the far plane → the whole image is classified sky/disoccluded → ALL history discarded every frame → no temporal accumulation → pure spatial upscale = jagged diagonals at ANY scale. The flag was a harmless no-op with the constant-0.5 fallback (0.5 inverts to 0.5), so it stayed invisible until the 2026-08-29 Iris depth-version fix made the pack write REAL depth into the proxy → `probeFsr4Depth` → `uUseDepth=1` → non-reversed depth hits an inverted context. Fixed: `upscaleDesc.flags = FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_AUTO_EXPOSURE` (the validated Aug 6 baseline). (2) **GL jitter pass-through was (0,0)**: the render jitters ±0.5 render px (Halton) but the 2026-08-30 GL dispatch "revert" passed (0,0) to the SDK — the SDK assumes every frame is on the same grid, its history reads are off by the per-frame jitter delta (up to ~1 render px = up to 4 display px at 25%), beyond the neighborhood clamp → history rejected → spatial. Fixed: the GL dispatch passes the real `(jitterX, jitterY)` again AND the missing native Y-sign fix is restored: `jitterOffset.y = -params.jitterY` in `fsr4_context.cpp` + `fg4_context.cpp` (DirectX NDC +Y down vs our OpenGL +Y up — the documented 2026-08-08 fix lost in the native-source recovery; without it the real pass-through inverts vertical de-jitter). FG prepare got the twin fixes (`prepareDesc.flags = 0`, `jitterOffset.y = -params.jitterY`). MVs stay clean (context has NO `FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_MOTION_VECTORS_JITTER_CANCELLATION`; MV-gen uses clean matrices). Deployed 1.21.11 instance 16:23 (backup `.bak-20260830-jaggedfix`).
- **FSR4 VKMod crash-loop FREEZE — "runs for a bit, then completely freezes while the game runs fine in the background" (2026-08-30 fix)**: the freeze was NOT a fence wait — the `fsr4_diag.log` (which keeps writing even while the render thread hangs) proved it. On the VulkanMod path, `g_ffxDispatch` throws an MSVC C++ exception (code `0xe06d7363` — an SDK `throw`, CAUGHT by the SEH `dispatchFsr4VkMod` handler and returned as `JNI_FALSE`), and EACH crash was followed by a FULL D3D12 device + 10-texture pool rebuild (`bypass: resources destroyed` → `ensureInitialized: initializing` → pool create → init OK → dispatch CRASH → repeat) — the log ends mid-recreate with no follow-up `ensureInitialized`: on this AMD iGPU (Radeon 860M) that rebuild itself hangs the render thread forever while the server thread keeps ticking (the exact "game runs fine in background"). Root defect was an ASYMMETRY between the two dispatch sites: the GL FSR4 dispatch-failure block (onSceneRendered ~5881) increments `fsr4DispatchFailCount` and hard-disables after `FSR4_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_DISPATCH_FAILURES` (5) — but the VKMod dispatch site (~3677) had NO escalation: a failed `dispatchFsr4VkMod` was silently ignored (`dispatched` stays false, nothing counted, no latch), the dispatch re-armed every frame, and each crash fed the needsRecreate device rebuild — a crash-loop that never self-limits. Fix (4 changes): (1) VKMod dispatch site now MIRRORS the GL escalation — `!dispatched && copyReady` → `++fsr4DispatchFailCount`, at 5 → `fsr4HardDisabled = true` (with `else if (!dispatched && !copyReady)` excluded so a transient saturated-queue timeout is NOT counted, same as GL); (2) VKMod dispatch GATE (~3624, onSceneRendered) gained `&& !fsr4HardDisabled` (the GL gate already had it) so a hard-disabled session never re-arms the crash-prone D3D12 dispatch; (3) BOTH `needsRecreate` re-init blocks (GL ~3444, VKMod ~4248) gated on `&& !fsr4HardDisabled` so a hard-disabled session STOPS rebuilding the D3D12 device + pool (the hang); (4) `spatialFallbackActive()` gained `|| fsr4HardDisabled` so a hard-disabled session routes through the D3D12-free FSR1 spatial render (fresh frames, no touch of the broken device). Latch clears (user retries): `markDirty()` + `applyAutoScale()` already reset it for FG; both now ALSO reset `fsr4HardDisabled`/`fsr4DispatchFailCount` and the successful-init sites re-clear. Self-limiting like the FG latch: 5 real consecutive crashes (SEH-caught C++ exceptions are counted, transient queue timeouts are not) hard-disable to FSR1; a restart or deliberate settings change is the only re-arm. **SECOND-FIX 2026-08-30 (why the FIRST FIX alone did not stop the freeze)**: an additional self-defeating trap — BOTH `needsRecreate` re-init-success blocks cleared `fsr4DispatchFailCount`/`fsr4HardDisabled` ("clean slate after successful reinit"). But the crash-loop IS "crash → device+pool rebuild success → crash → …"; every successful rebuild wiped the counter, so the 5-strike escalation never latched, `needsRecreate` stayed open, and the expensive rebuild (the actual render-thread hang) kept firing forever. Correct recovery is only via a genuinely good dispatch (~5972 GL / success site VKMod, `fsr4DispatchFailCount = 0`) or a deliberate `markDirty()/applyAutoScale()`; a re-init that merely rebuilds then crashes again must NOT reset the escalation. So the reinit-success blocks now preserve the counters (the FG-only `fg4PermanentlyDisabled` clear stays — that one is the FG catch-22 fix, separate latch). Confirmed in the 10:23 GL session log: a single `g_ffxDispatch` crash now lands in FSR1 fallback and stays (clean, no freeze) — the freeze came from the crash→reset→rebuild loop this second fix closes.
- **FG DX12 permanent-disable latch clears on a deliberate settings change (2026-08-29, "FSR3 FG DX12 says it is only running a spatial upscaler after a quality change" fix)**: `noteD3D12RuntimeFailure()` sets `fg4PermanentlyDisabled = true` after `FG_MAX_RUNTIME_FAILURES` (3) failures, and the latch has TWO traps: (1) it was never reset for the session; (2) it GATES the FG re-init (`fgActive()` → `!fg4PermanentlyDisabled`) — a catch-22. A quality/version change under FG_DX12 tears down + re-initializes the FSR4/FG D3D12 contexts; during that transition storm (context teardown + Iris recompile + deep GL queue) a few transient `doFrameGenDispatch` status-2 failures or waitIdle-stall watchdog hits rack up to 3 → FG permanently killed → FG_DX12 degrades to FSR1 spatial for the rest of the session and the settings screen shows the FG DX12 pill as unavailable ("Too many runtime failures"). Fix: `markDirty()` (version/Iris-quality change) and `applyAutoScale()` (GL quality slider) now clear `fg4PermanentlyDisabled` + `fgRuntimeFailCount`/`fgStallConsecutive` BEFORE tearing down, so the next re-init runs FG again; a successful `Fg4Native.nativeInit` (both GL + VKMod init sites) also clears it (belt-and-suspenders). Self-limiting: genuine failures re-latch as before — only the catch-22 is broken.
- **Broken-composite VOTE PREDICATE v2 — probe fired but never voted on the 14:26 session (2026-08-25, "the problem is still happening" fix)**: the shared-site probe fix above worked (the 14:26 FSR4+pack session logged 36 `[TERRAIN-PROBE]` lines), but `brokenVote=0/3` for the WHOLE session while terrain was visibly missing — the old predicate `sky>110 && terrain<45 && terrainVar<250` only matched bright-NOON sky over a black void. The session showed two shapes it missed: (a) **dusk/overcast sky (luma 79-86) over a black void (terrain 1-4)** at 14:27:55-57 — the `>110` ceiling excluded any sky dimmer than noon; (b) **flat sky gradient painted over the terrain region** at 14:27:17-27 (~11s) — terrain block read bright horizon-blue (luma 104-122, var~1) under a dark-navy sky, excluded by `terrain<45`. Discriminator: every healthy probe had TEXTURED terrain (var 34-340); every broken one was flat (var<=4.4). Predicate v2 has two signatures: `void` = visible sky (`sky>55`, still above night's 26-27) over a dark flat void (`terrain<45`, `sky>terrain+30`, `var<250`); `skygrad` = smooth sky (`skyVar<25`) above a flat (`var<15`) blue-dominant (B>R && B>G) terrain block not darker than the sky (`terrain >= sky-20` — a real sky gradient is brightest at the horizon, so this holds for a sky-only composite day or dusk; real ground is never a flat blue patch). New `blockMeanRgb` helper; probe log appends `sig=void|skygrad|-`. Verified against the session frames: healthy 1/7/20/22 never vote, broken 13/33/34/35 all vote. Deployed 15:19 (backup `.bak-20260825-predicate2`) — NOTE the deployed file is `fsr-reloaded-2.0.1.jar`, not 1.9.0 (the 1.9.0 name is a stale build artifact from the Aug 21 commit; the 2.0.x is the live port build).
- **Terrain-disappearing probe/latch covers ALL swap paths — not just the FSR1 fallback (2026-08-25, "the terrain is still dissapearing. it wasnt happening 2 days ago / when the project was in 26.1" fix)**: the broken-composite machinery (TERRAIN-PROBE `evaluateProxyContentAndVote` → 3 votes → `irisSwapBrokenLatch` → native pass-through) originally ran ONLY inside the FSR1 spatial fallback (`if (spatialFallbackActive())` in onSceneRendered). The user's config is FSR4 — when FSR4 (or FSR2/FSR3) dispatches under the Iris swap, NO probe ran and no latch could engage, so a sky-only composite (pack pipeline rebuild after version switch / world join / dimension change) was upscaled + presented with zero protection. Root of the regression: the 26.1 instance that worked ran **Voxy/DH**, which excludes the swap path entirely (`!DistantHorizonsCompat.isLoaded()` → legacy native Iris path → terrain never touched the proxy); the 1.21.11 instance has no DH so the swap + Photonics path is live, and the 26.1 jar had no WindowMixin dim overrides / Photonics mixin (port-era additions). Session log: at world join with FSR1+pack+Photonics+swap the first probe read sky luma 146 / terrain luma 37 (terrain-missing signature, `brokenVote=1/3`) — the FSR1 path at least voted; the FSR4 path had no vote at all. Fix: the probe + vote now run from a SHARED site in onSceneRendered right after `renderTex` is resolved (`irisSwapActive && !VulkanModCompat.isLoaded()`), before the FSR4/FSR2/FSR3/FSR1 branches — every swap-active path feeds the latch (same 2s throttle, 40-probe cap). Latch still gates `irisTemporal` in onRenderBegin (`!irisSwapBrokenLatch`) → native pass-through = the proven pre-port terrain-visible behavior; cleared on world join.
- **FSR4/D3D12 context PARKING on switch-away (2026-08-25, "instead of tearing down the dx12 context every time")**: switching away from FSR4/FG_DX12 to a non-D3D12 version used to destroy the SDK context (`ffxCreateContext`) + the 10-texture shared pool — the expensive, crash-prone cross-API recreate (external-memory handle imports + GL import + ffxCreateContext are exactly the ops that fault the AMD driver when raced against pipeline churn). The D3D12 device already survived switches (`disposeD3D12` only runs at full `destroy()`); parking extends that to the context + pool. Lifecycle: `markDirty()` (version switch away) and the per-frame "FSR4 not selected" branch call `parkFsr4Context()` — keeps the SDK context + pool alive, clears `fsr4Initialized` so nothing dispatches (all dispatch gates read `fsr4Initialized`), keeps the size sentinel (`fsr4ContextRenderW/H/DisplayW/H`) for the unpark check. On re-select, both init blocks (GL at ~4000, VulkanMod at ~3250) call `unparkFsr4Context(renderW, renderH, dispW, dispH, requireGlImports)` FIRST: if parked dims match AND the pool type matches this renderer (`SharedTextureManager.fsr4PoolHasGlImports()` — GL path imports per-texture, VKMod leaves `fsr4TexIds` null), it restores `fsr4Initialized` (+ `fg4Initialized` when FG_DX12 is re-selected and was parked), resets temporal history + dispatch state (first dispatch reset=true clears the SDK's internal history), re-arms `VulkanModCompat.fsr4VkModActive` on the VKMod path + clears `fsr4InputPopulated` (wait for a fresh Copy1), and returns true — the caller SKIPS the whole init path, zero cross-API work. Mismatch (size changed while away, renderer switched, or FG_DX12 re-selected after a plain-FSR4 park — the FG context was never created) tears down properly and falls through to the normal init. `destroy()` + `applyAutoScale()` use `teardownFsr4Context()` (handles live AND parked — destroying the pool under a parked SDK context would be a use-after-free on the next unpark; shutdown order is SDK context before pool). Result: a same-size FSR4 off→on toggle or FSR2→FSR4 switch is instant + crash-free; only a size change while away pays the recreate.
- **View-space sky detection**: `getViewSpaceDepth()` converts buffer depth to view-space Z using device-to-view depth coefficients. Sky threshold = 10% of far plane view-space distance (`uFarViewZ`). Convention-independent.
- **FSR2/3 reactive-mask scale-invariance (2026-08-06, "shaking is very noticeable" at 65% scale)**: the reactive mask's `velScale = clamp(nucleusVel / 3.0)` and ACCUMULATE's `velFactor = clamp(mvPx / 10.0)` thresholds were in RENDER pixels — but the idle camera bob is constant WORLD motion, so its render-px MV grows with render resolution (0.5-2.5 render px at 25% scale → 2.6x larger at 65%: 420→1092 render width). At 65% the bob crossed the 3.0 render-px threshold → the reactive mask fired while STANDING STILL → `accumulationMask` tightened the ellipsoid clamp → history rejected → the jittered current frame showed through as shake. The log showed it: ACCUM_WEIGHT collapsed from 2.0 (25% session) to 0.7 (65% session) — steady-state W·r = ws ⇒ r ≈ 0.083 at 25% vs 0.24 at 65%, exactly velScale 0.33 vs 0.87. Fix: express both thresholds in DISPLAY px, anchored to the 25% behavior that was known-good (3.0 render px at 420-wide render = 12 display px; 10 render px = 40 display px): `velScale = clamp(nucleusVel * uDisplaySize.x / (12.0 * uRenderSize.x), 0, 1)` and `velFactor = clamp(mvPx * uDisplaySize.x / (40.0 * uRenderSize.x), 0, 1)`. At 25% scale the formula is bit-identical to the old one (1680/12·420 = 1/3.0); at 65% the threshold rises to 7.8/26 render px, so the same display-space motion (bob ~1.5-2.5 display px) no longer fires. Embedded + on-disk DEPTH_CLIP_CS/ACCUMULATE_CS both pipelines.
- **RCAS sharpening was a BLUR — sign + weight inverted (2026-08-08, "edges smoothening but very blurry" fix)**: after the box-anchor fix stabilized the accumulation, the user's next report was soft/blurry edges. Root cause in the RCAS shader (all 5 copies — FSR1/FSR2/FSR3 embedded + on-disk fsr2/fsr3 RCAS_CS.glsl): (1) the combine was `sharpend = c + w * (sharpend - c)` where `sharpend` is the 8-TAP LOCAL AVERAGE `(c*4 + t + b + l + r)/8` — `c + w*(avg - c)` moves the pixel TOWARD the average = a low-pass BLUR, the exact inverse of unsharp masking (`c + w*(c - avg)` pulls AWAY from the average); (2) the weight was `w = clamp(1.0 - sharp*d/dMax)` — HIGH in flat areas, LOW at edges, the inverse of an edge-adaptive sharpener, and it made the sharpness slider work BACKWARDS (higher sharpness → smaller w at edges → weaker effect). Net effect: at the user's `sharpness: 0.7` (rcasSharpness=5, sharp=5/8) every edge got ~37% of its blurred average added back in, permanently softening the temporal result the whole stability campaign built. Fix (both lines, all 5 copies): `w = clamp(sharp * d / dMax, 0, 1)` (0 in flat, `sharp` at strong edges, monotonic in the slider) and `sharpend = c + w * (c - sharpend)`. Behavior: flat areas unchanged (d≈0 → w≈0), edges pulled away from the average proportionally to edge strength × slider. Validated: glslangValidator clean on both disk shaders, compileClientJava + build pass, jar redeployed 18:54 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260807-rcasfix`); shader-cache is source-hash keyed so the changed source recompiles (no stale-bin risk). Script `scripts/patch_rcas_sign.py` (handles FSR1's `sharpened`/`result` variable names too). This is likely why the temporal smoothing always read as "soft": the stabilization fixes were working, but the only spatial-sharpening pass in the mod was simultaneously de-sharpening.
- **RCAS REPLACED with the REAL FidelityFX FSR1 RCAS (2026-08-08, "the grass is just a blurry mess" fix)**: the custom sharpener (unsharp mask + min/max clamp) failed TWICE in one day — the sign fix overshot (dark border around the sun), then the halo clamp FROZE bright thin features: with the center included in the clamp range, a grass blade IS the local maximum, so `clamp(sharpened, mn3, mx3)` returned the blade unchanged = ZERO sharpening on grass (and leaves, stars, held items) — it just stayed at its soft temporally-accumulated value, which at 0.45 render scale is a blurry smear. The fix is a full port of AMD's actual FSR1 RCAS (`ffx_fsr1.h` `FsrRcasF`, fetched from the FidelityFX SDK on 2026-08-08), all 5 copies (FSR1/FSR2/FSR3 embedded + on-disk fsr2/fsr3 RCAS_CS.glsl): a **5-tap cross filter with a negative lobe whose weight is SOLVED (hitMin/hitMax) so the output mathematically cannot clip out of [0,1]** — anti-overshoot is built into the solve, not bolted on: `hitMin = mn4 * rcp(4*mx4) * lowerLimiter`, `hitMax = (1-mx4) * rcp(4*mn4-4)`, `lobe = max(-hitMin, hitMax)` per channel, scalar `lobe = max(-0.1875, min(max3(lobe), 0)) * sharp` (FSR_RCAS_LIMIT = 0.25-1/16), `lobe *= nz` (FSR_RCAS_DENOISE noise detector: luma highpass normalized against the local range, `nz = -0.5*saturate(abs(hp)/(max-min)) + 1`), resolve `(lobe*(b+d+f+h) + c) / (4*lobe + 1)`. The ring min/max deliberately EXCLUDE the center — that is what lets a local-max grass blade still sharpen (the old clamp including the center was the freeze bug). Sun edge (ring contains a saturated 1.0) → solve drives lobe toward 0 → no dark border, by construction. Grass blade (ring below 1.0) → nonzero lobe → real pull, bounded by the clip solve. Sharpness plumbing unchanged (`uRcasConfig.x/8.0` in FSR2/3, `uSharpness*7` in FSR1); the 0.7 default now means genuine FSR1-grade sharpening. **Two bugs found while porting**: (a) the halo patch's FSR1 regex had consumed the `t/b/l/r` cross-tap declarations without re-declaring them (embedded FSR1 RCAS would have failed to compile at runtime — the new body declares `up/dn/lt/rt` itself); (b) the new FSR1 template's `{{`/`}}` braces were written literally into the Java text block (GLSL syntax error) — fixed both; all three embedded RCAS blocks now validate via glslangValidator extracted from the Java source, plus both disk copies. **Reviewer catch (2026-08-08, would have silently disabled ALL sharpening)**: the `hitMax` epsilon guard `max(4.0*mn4 - 4.0, 1e-7)` was WRONG — `4*mn4 - 4` is ≤ 0 for every valid color (mn4∈[0,1], so 4·mn4−4∈[−4,0]), and `max(negative, 1e-7)` returned `1e-7`, flipping hitMax from its correct negative value to a huge positive `(1−mx4)/1e-7` → `lobeV = max(-hitMin, hitMax)` huge positive → `lobe = max(-0.1875, min(pos, 0)) = 0` → pure pass-through, exactly the blurry-grass bug unrepaired. Fix: sign-preserving guard `min(4.0*mn4 - 4.0, -1e-7)` (the reference has no guard; the only degenerate mn4=1.0 case yields −Inf → lobe=−0.1875 → resolve is idempotent on flat content anyway). Also noted by the reviewer: the `hitMin` guard (positive denominator) and resolve guard (4·lobe+1∈[0.25,1]) are safe; FSR1 has a redundant double early-out (harmless). Validated: `glslangValidator -S comp` clean (2 disk + 3 embedded-extracted), compileClientJava + build pass, jar redeployed 19:30 (backups `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-rcas-fsr1port` 19:28 and `.bak-rcas-halo` 19:13). Script `scripts/patch_rcas_fsr1.py` (supersedes `patch_rcas_halo.py`; the solve replaces the clamp). NOTE for the user's config: `version=FSR4, qualityScale=0.7` was set at 19:16 — FSR4 uses the SDK's own internal sharpening (also real RCAS, wired to `config.sharpness`), so the grass complaint was from the FSR2-at-0.45 session; this port fixes the FSR2/3/1 GL path, and 0.7 scale also helps grass fundamentally (1.5× more render pixels than 0.45).
- **PROJECT REVERTED to f7a8682 (2026-08-06) EXCEPT the FSR2/3 work (2026-08-08, "revert the project to that aug 6 commit except for fsr 2")**: the user's decision after the FSR4 sun saga — the whole FSR4/native side goes back to the committed Aug 6 baseline, the FSR2/3 temporal-upscaler family keeps all its post-Aug-6 fixes. **Reverted to f7a8682**: `Fsr4Native.java`, `VulkanInterop.java`, `VulkanModCompat.java`, `jni_bridge.cpp`, `fsr4_context.cpp/h`, `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp/.spv/_spv.h`. **Kept current**: `FSR2Pipeline.java`, `FSR3Pipeline.java`, `FSR1Pipeline.java` (RCAS), `FSRConfig.java`, `SettingsScreen.java`, `ScaledRenderTarget.java`, `FSRProcessor.java`. Surgery required: the current FSRProcessor passed a `sharpness` arg to the FSR4 dispatch (added post-Aug-6, threaded through all three Java bridges + jni_bridge) — the Aug 6 native no longer accepts it, so BOTH FSR4 dispatch call sites in FSRProcessor (`VulkanModCompat.dispatchFsr4VkMod` at the VkMod block, `Fsr4Native.dispatchFsr4` at the GL block) had the `config.sharpness,` line removed to match the Aug 6 signatures (verified: every other bridge call the current FSRProcessor makes exists unchanged in the Aug 6 bridges, and the unchanged mixins only call Aug 6 APIs). Consequences: (1) the **FSR4 VK MV-gen is the Aug 6 version** — real per-pixel depth + the force-reactive sky bucket (depth>=0.999 → mv=0 + reactive=1.0) → sun/sky/moon render SPATIALLY: stable/straight/ghost-free, aliased at low render scales (the committed baseline; the 11:39 "sun accumulates" build is superseded); (2) FSR4 loses the post-Aug-6 native extras (T&C auto-reactive bindings, SDK-internal sharpness wiring — `config.sharpness` now only drives FSR1/2/3 RCAS, the deferred resume-probe fence, MV-gen staging refinements); (3) FSR4 Java-side additions that live in FSRProcessor/FSRConfig were KEPT (real-depth probe `fsr4RealDepthAvailable` — also gates FSR2/3's `uUseDepth`; parity bypass; target-FPS auto-quality) since they don't touch the reverted native surface. Validated: `build_native.bat` clean (fsrvk.dll 126,464 bytes, Aug 6 SPIR-V 6,944 bytes embedded), `gradlew build` green (jar `build/libs/fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar` 11:50). The untracked `scripts/*.py` + root `comp.spv` were left in place (not part of the commit).
- **FSR4 sun accumulates again — force-reactive bucket REMOVED (2026-08-08, "the sun is still jagged")**: the Aug 6 bucket (depth >= 0.999 → mv=0 + reactive=1.0 → SDK renders sky/sun/moon SPATIALLY) was restored at 11:08 per "restore the sun's behavior", but the user's next report was "the sun is still jagged" — a spatial fresh render of the sun disc at render scale is inherently aliased (at the user's 0.7 scale the sun silhouette is the most visible fresh-render content; this is exactly why the 08-08 v1 fix removed the bucket originally). Fix: deleted the early-return bucket from `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` again — the far plane now flows through the v8 machinery below (intact since the restore): real-depth MVs are exact at ndcZ≈1.0 (world-anchored sun/moon track correctly), flat gradient → camera-locked mv=0 (straight horizon preserved), the sun/moon white-body exclusion keeps the real MV (no tilt/ghost), and the gentle 32-px sky MV ramp + zero motionFloor + T&C sky guard + cutout colorEdge exclusion let the sun/cloud edges accumulate sub-pixel jitter samples → smooth like terrain. Fast turns still fully discard via the MV ramp (no ghosting). The hand-zone identity fix (depth < 0.55) is unaffected. GL-path FSR4 MV-gen in FSRProcessor.java NOT changed (dead under VulkanMod — same scoping as the restore). Validated: glslangValidator `-V` clean; SPIR-V regenerated (13940 → 13612 bytes) + `fsr4_vk_mvgen_spv.h`; fsrvk.dll rebuilt 11:39; `build` green (jar `build/libs/fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar` 11:39, new DLL embedded). Expected: sun disc edge smooth standing still / walking / slow pans (v6-v8 validated behavior); only fast flicks (>32 render px/frame) alias (motion-masked); horizon stays straight. Revert to the spatial sun = re-add the bucket (see the 2026-08-08 restore entry).
- **FSR4 SKY ACCUMULATION RESTORED on the VK path — bucket removed + v8 machinery ported from the GL MV-gen (2026-08-08, "the sky is still jagged. i dont think history is working properly" fix)**: the 11:50 revert to f7a8682 left `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` at the PURE Aug 6 baseline — force-reactive sky bucket (depth>=0.999 → mv=0 + reactive=1.0 + early return) AND no v8 machinery, no T&C, no hand fix (all post-Aug-6 work was wiped). The user's next report was exactly the bucket's signature: the sky stays jagged and "history" never engages on sky pixels — the SDK renders sky/sun/moon SPATIALLY every frame (reactive=1.0 = zero temporal accumulation), and at 0.7 scale a fresh render of the sun disc/cloud edges is inherently aliased. Fix: rewrote `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` (only the shader + SPIR-V header + DLL — no Java/native-surface changes needed) removing the bucket and porting the v8 SKY MOTION POLICY **1:1 from the GL `MV_GEN_CS` in FSRProcessor.java**, which KEPT the v8 machinery through the revert and is the reference implementation: (1) `isFarPlane = depth >= 0.999` no longer returns early — the far plane reprojects at its true depth (ndcZ≈1.0) and accumulates; (2) `isSkyLike = isFarPlane || (skyLuma > 0.5 && skyChroma < 0.25)` computed once from the 3x3 window (reused by the ramp + cutout); (3) camera-locked flat-gradient rule `if (isFarPlane && colorSpread < 0.08 && !(skyLuma > 0.88 && skyChroma < 0.06)) mv = vec2(0.0)` — colored gradient/horizon keeps mv=0 (straight under any pan, v4 slant fix), pure-white sun/moon body EXCLUDED (world-anchored, keeps real MV — no tilt/ghost, v8 fix); (4) gentler sky MV ramp `smoothstep(0.5, isSkyLike ? 32.0 : 16.0, mvLen)` + `motionFloor = isSkyLike ? 0.0 : ...*0.15` (v6 — sky MVs are exact so it can accumulate heavily during bob/slow pans); (5) cutout `colorEdge` disabled for sky-like pixels (`colorEdge && !isSkyLike`), `alphaEdge` stays (v5 — clouds/sun are not thin alpha-cutout geometry and were being forced fully reactive during ANY motion); cutout still gated on `mvLen > 0.5` (VK has no CPU uCamMotion — push constants are exactly 128 bytes). NOT ported (absent from the reverted VK native surface): the T&C auto-reactive block (needs uPrevColor staging + barriers + JNI — the GL path's T&C sky guard is irrelevant here) and the hand-zone identity fix (isHand + pose gate — the AGENTS.md hand entry describes it; follow-up candidate if the user reports hand jaggies again). Validated: glslangValidator `-V` clean; SPIR-V regenerated (6944 → 8452 bytes) + `fsr4_vk_mvgen_spv.h` (BYTE count — the 2026-08-06 word-vs-byte gotcha; verified 8452 present in the rebuilt DLL); fsrvk.dll rebuilt (126,464 → 128,000 bytes); `gradlew build` green (jar `build/libs/fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar` 13:15, new DLL embedded, `unzip -l` confirms natives/fsrvk.dll = 128,000); deployed to PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260808-skyaccum`). Expected (same as the 11:39 "sun accumulates" build): sun disc + cloud edges smooth standing still / walking / slow pans (accumulate sub-pixel jitter samples like terrain), horizon gradient stays straight (camera-locked mv=0), only fast flicks (>32 render px/frame) alias (motion-masked), and sky "history" (the SDK's accumulation) now visibly engages — reactive 0 and weight growth on sky pixels. The GL-path FSR4 MV-gen was already in this state (kept through the revert); the FSR2/3 pipelines were never affected.
- **FSR4 VK REAL-DEPTH WAS EMPTY under render-scale — the actual "jagged edges" root cause (2026-08-08, "i tested it in the gradle client with vulkanmod but the jagged edges are there" fix)**: after the sky-accumulation shader fix, the user tested the VK path (gradle runClient + VulkanMod, qualityScale 0.7) and the jagged edges PERSISTED. The native diag (`fsr4_diag.log`, written by the `diagLog` in jni_bridge.cpp — remember it appends to `C:\Users\arshjot\Downloads\fsr2mod\fsr4_diag.log`, NOT the game log) settled it: the MV-gen's depth input was **0.000 everywhere**. Both readbacks agreed — the STAGING depth (the shader's own output, what the SDK actually receives) AND the DIRECT probe of the bound source image read all-zeros. Root cause is a WRONG DEPTH SOURCE introduced by the render-scale + FSR4 combination (2026-08-05): `VulkanModMainPassMixin` binds `Minecraft.getMainRenderTarget().getDepthTextureView().texture()` — the mc main target's depth at NATIVE res — but under render-scale the world's render pass writes into the **SCALED framebuffer** (this mixin's `afterMainFramebufferAssigned` swapped `mainFramebuffer`, and `createRenderPasses`/attachments derive from it). The native-res mc main-target depth is a SEPARATE image that is never written in this mode → the shader sampled depth=0 → `ndcZ≈-1` (near plane) → MVs ~100x too large during any motion → the SDK rejected ALL history → spatial output → jagged edges on EVERYTHING (sky included), regardless of the accumulated-sky shader (`depth >= 0.999` could never fire). This also retroactively explains the whole sun/sky saga's flip-flopping: with constant-depth-broken MVs everything renders spatially, so the bucket-vs-accumulated distinction was nearly invisible — and the 2026-08-06 "real depth = mc target VIEW" finding was validated WITHOUT render scale (the non-scaled path's mc main target IS the live render target). Fix (Java only — `VulkanModMainPassMixin`): when `VulkanModCompat.renderScaleSwapped()` (render scale configured, enabled-independent) AND `fb.getDepthAttachment()` is a real image, bind the **scaled framebuffer's own depth attachment** (`depthSrc = depthImg; depthFmt = fb.getDepthFormat()`); the mc main-target resolution is skipped (`else if (!preferFbDepth)` on its assignment) and only runs in non-scaled mode. The one-shot probe log now reports `USING DMP fb depth` in render-scale mode. Validated: `gradlew build` green, jar redeployed 13:35 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260808-skyaccum` still holds the 13:15 build). **Next run must show**: (1) `REAL-DEPTH DIRECT READBACK: skipped (no real depth bound...)` ABSENT (it latched one-shot per process — only logs the first time real depth is missing; it fired in the 13:27 session); (2) the DIRECT/staging readbacks read real variation (terrain≈0.001-0.1, mid≈0.5, sky≈1.0) instead of 0.000; (3) if the scaled-fb depth turns out REVERSED (terrain≈1.0/sky≈0), flip `ndcZ = 1.0 - depth*2.0` in `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` (the AGENTS.md VK convention check) AND `imageStore(uOutputDepth, ...)` must store the non-reversed value for the SDK (FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_DEPTH_INVERTED is NOT set). If the scaled-fb depth is ALSO empty (worse case), the world's depth is not reachable through VulkanMod's framebuffer and the next candidate is the render pass's actual depth attachment image (VulkanMod internals) — the direct probe will tell us which way to go.
- **"Now it just smears" — the real depth was NEVER written under render scale; the world binds a NATIVE-size depth with a SCALED color (2026-08-08 root cause, "now it just smears" fix)**: with the scaled-fb depth bound, the next session's readbacks showed **1.000 everywhere** (staging AND direct, including the bottom rows that should be terrain) — a clear-to-far value, not real depth. The symptom matched: depth=1.0 (far plane) → reprojection delta ≈ 0 → the SDK ACCEPTS history but fetches it from the same screen spot → moving content smears (the previous all-0.0 depth gave the opposite: near-plane → ~100x MVs → history rejected → jagged spatial). Decompiling VulkanMod 0.6.8 (`javap` on the installed jar) found the actual mechanism — a VulkanMod ASYMMETRY in the main RenderTarget getters: `MainTargetMixin` overrides `getColorTexture()/getColorTextureView()/getDepthTexture()` to return the DMP (DefaultMainPass) framebuffer attachments, but NOT `getDepthTextureView()` — that getter still returns the mc main-target's OWN `depthTextureView` field, created by `createBuffers()` at NATIVE res. `LevelRenderer.renderLevel` sets `RenderSystem.outputDepthTextureOverride = renderTarget.getDepthTextureView()` and `RenderType` uses it for every world draw's depth attachment, while the color comes from `outputColorTextureOverride = getColorTextureView()` (MainPass = the SCALED framebuffer under render scale). Result: every world render pass binds a **480x300 color but a 1920x1201 depth** (at qualityScale 0.25) — mismatched attachment extents → the driver drops/ignores the depth writes → the scaled-fb depth stays at its 1.0 clear and the mc field depth stays empty (0.0) — even though the scaled-fb COLOR holds the scene. The 2026-08-06 "mc target VIEW depth = real" finding was only true WITHOUT render scale (same-size attachments there). **Fix (Java only)**: new `vulkanmod.VulkanModRenderTargetMixin` (@Inject HEAD, cancellable on `RenderTarget.getDepthTextureView`) — when `renderScaleSwapped()` and `this` is the main render target, return a cached `RenderSystem.getDevice().createTextureView(getDepthTexture())` (the MainPass depth = the SCALED fb's depth under render scale), so color and depth share the same extent and the world's depth writes land in the DMP scaled-fb depth — the exact image `VulkanModMainPassMixin` already binds to the MV-gen (binding 3). Cache keyed by texture identity (view recreated on fb recreate/resize); falls through to vanilla for all other targets and non-scaled mode. Registered in `fsr2mod.mixins.json`. This also makes the non-scaled D3D12 path consistent (its `getDepthTextureView().texture()` binding now resolves to the MainPass depth = the same image the world writes). Validated: `gradlew build` green, jar redeployed 13:54 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260808-depthalign`). **Next run must show**: the `REAL-DEPTH DIRECT READBACK`/staging readbacks read real variation (terrain≈0.001-0.1, mid≈0.5, sky≈1.0) instead of the uniform 1.0 — if they instead show REVERSED (terrain≈1.0/sky≈0), flip `ndcZ = 1.0 - depth*2.0` in `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` (VK convention check; `FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_DEPTH_INVERTED` not set) — and the smearing should be gone, replaced by proper temporal accumulation on both terrain and sky.
- **"Terrain still smearing + UI ghosting" — depth STILL reads uniform 1.0 after the redirect; added a COLOR sanity probe to decide readback-vs-content (2026-08-08)**: the 13:58 test (WITH the getDepthTextureView redirect active — the game log shows `getDepthTextureView redirected to MainPass depth` at 13:58:00) still showed **1.000 everywhere** in BOTH the staging and direct depth readbacks (5 recordings, rows 73/148/223 = 480x300 scaled-fb dims). Two competing explanations: (a) the world genuinely writes color but NOT depth into the DMP scaled fb (every readback across ALL sessions — 0.5 dummy, 0.0, 1.0 — has been uniform, never real variation → the VK path may have NEVER had real depth reach the MV-gen); or (b) the readbacks themselves are broken (both assume oldLayout=DEPTH_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL/COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL — if the image is in a different layout, both probes read garbage). **Decisive probe added (14:05 build)**: the direct readback now ALSO copies the DMP fb COLOR at the same 3 rows (offsets 768/1024/1280 in the same host buffer, COLOR aspect, COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL→TRANSFER_SRC→back) and logs `COLOR DIRECT READBACK` — if the color shows the scene (variation, sky bright / terrain greenish), the readback machinery is sound and the uniform depth is REAL (cleared, never written) → the world's depth writes are lost in VulkanMod's pipeline (next step: probe the actual render-pass depth attachment / pipeline depth-write state); if the color is ALSO uniform garbage, the readbacks lie (layout mismatch) → fix the readback/MV-gen barrier layouts first and the depth may already be fine. Also added to the one-shot mixin probe: `curLayout=` for both DMP fb depth and color (`VulkanImage.getCurrentLayout()`) so the barrier assumptions (depth=3, color=2) are verifiable in-log, plus the DMP fb color id. UI ghosting is the same broken-MV/depth disease (UI pixels get garbage depth → wrong reprojection → history misaligned; the UI also rasterizes at the scaled res so text is temporal-accumulated rather than sharp). Rebuilt fsrvk.dll (129,536 bytes) + jar, redeployed 14:05 to both gradle-client resources and PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `.bak-20260808-depthalign` still holds the 13:54 build).
- **"Terrain still smearing" — the color probe was decisive + the CAM-MOTION PLUMB (2026-08-08, "check the log" follow-up, fix)**: the 14:07 session's color probe answered the fork: the DMP fb COLOR read as garbage float32 (RGBA8 pixel data misread as floats = real content — sky bright at the top row, scene variation below) while the DEPTH read a clean uniform `1.000000` (valid D32 floats, `curLayout=3` DEPTH_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL confirmed in the one-shot probe) — **the readbacks are sound, the world genuinely writes COLOR but never DEPTH into the DMP scaled fb on the VulkanMod path** (decompiled pipeline depth state is fine; the writes are lost inside VulkanMod's GL-emulation pass binding). So REAL depth is unreachable; the fix must be the FALLBACK path. Also confirmed: the session ran at 480x300 → 1920x1201 = **qualityScale 0.25** (4x upscale — the mod's own warning says 0.5-0.65; the config was changed at some point from 0.7). **Fix = the documented "cam-motion plumb" follow-up (AGENTS.md 2026-08-06 cutout entry)**: the no-real-depth shader mode now gets a CPU translation signal instead of the FOV/near-plane-fragile parallax heuristic. (1) **Java** (`FSRProcessor` VKMod block): per frame, eye SPEED in blocks/sec (dt-normalized from nanoTime so the thresholds are FPS-independent — raw per-frame deltas shrink at high FPS: walk = 0.072 blocks/frame at 60fps but 0.03 at 144fps) + rotation delta in radians, from the SAME `vkCam.position()/rotation()` state the clean view matrix is built from (idle bob ≈ 0.13 blocks/sec, walk ≈ 4.3, sprint ≈ 5.6); new JNI `setVkModCamMotion(eyeSpeed, rotDelta)` (VulkanInterop + VulkanModCompat wrapper). (2) **Native** (`jni_bridge.cpp`): single `float g_vkModMvGenPush[34]` (ONE array — the 2026-08-05 contiguous-memory lesson: vkCmdPushConstants reads contiguous bytes, separate globals aren't guaranteed adjacent), `setVkModMvGenMatrices` writes offsets 0/64, `setVkModCamMotion` writes 128/132; push range + pipeline-layout range 128 → **136 bytes** (the old "exactly 128 = guaranteed minimum" was a conservative spec limit; every FSR4-capable GPU exposes maxPushConstantsSize 256 — a one-shot `maxPushConstantsSize=` diag log was added to prove it; existing pipeline-creation error handling covers the impossible 128-only case). (3) **Shader** (`fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`): push constants 2 mat4 + uCamMotion + uCamRotDelta (136B); removed the parallax block (its near point at clip z=0 maps to d=2n ≈ 1.0 block with the captured near=0.5, so idle bob ~51px parallax would have saturated the ramp → jagged standing still; and it was FOV-fragile — the fix is exact); no-real-depth translation ramp `smoothstep(2.0, 4.5, pc.uCamMotion)` (walk/sprint → fully reactive → NO SMEAR while moving; standing still / rotation → full accumulation); the cutout detector's motion gate switched from `mvLen > 0.5` (OPEN while standing still — idle bob lands 0.5-2.5px MVs in the clean matrices → shimmer at every flower/leaf edge, the exact GL bug fixed 2026-08-06) to `pc.uCamMotion > 2.5 || pc.uCamRotDelta > 0.01` (GL-validated semantics). The sky camera-locked rule stays gated `!noRealDepth` (locking everything to mv=0 with empty depth = the smear). Validated: glslangValidator clean, SPIR-V 8452 → 10588 bytes + header regenerated (BYTE count), fsrvk.dll 129,536 → 131,584 bytes, `gradlew build` green, deployed 14:24 to both gradle-client resources and PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260808-cammotion`). **Expected in the next gradle-client run**: (a) `maxPushConstantsSize=256 (need 136)` in `fsr4_diag.log`; (b) walking → no smear (spatial during motion, by design — the tradeoff of no real depth), standing still → smooth accumulation, slow pans → smooth (rotation MVs exact); (c) the `REAL-DEPTH DIRECT READBACK` staging/direct values stay uniform (expected — real depth is confirmed unreachable on this path); (d) NOTE the config is at 0.25 render scale — spatial-while-walking at 4x upscale is aliased by definition; 0.5-0.65 is the supported range and the single biggest quality lever remaining.
- **"It is still smearing" — cam-motion arrival + reactive-mask probes added (2026-08-08, 15:24 build, DIAGNOSTIC — no fix yet)**: after the cam-motion plumb, the user's next report was "it is still smearing". The whole reactive chain was verified on paper: Java `setVkModCamMotion` call site (FSRProcessor VKMod block, runs every frame — `sceneUpscaled` resets in onRenderEnd, `vkCam != null` + sane lastProj gate passes — the `VKMod MV-gen projection` one-shot fired at 15:13:24), native write into `g_vkModMvGenPush[32]/[33]` (offsets 128/132 match the GLSL std140 block: 2 mat4 = 128B + 2 floats), 136-byte push range + layout range (`maxPushConstantsSize=256 (need 136)` logged once), shader ramp `smoothstep(2.0, 4.5, pc.uCamMotion)` inside `if (noRealDepth)`, reactive staging image → `vkCmdCopyImage` → imported `g_vkModReactiveImage` (pool idx 3) = exactly what `dispatchFsr4VkMod` passes as `params.reactive`. NO functional bug found by inspection — so the next run carries TWO probes that fork the remaining possibilities: (1) **cam-motion arrival probe**: `setVkModCamMotion` now diag-logs `eyeSpeed=%.3f rotDelta=%.5f (call #N)` on the first 3 calls + every 300th — if this logs real values (walk≈4.3) while the user walks, the Java→native plumb works; (2) **reactive staging readback** (`REACTIVE READBACK (staging)`): mirrors the depth staging probe — 16x16 center block of `g_vkModMvGenStageReactive` (R8, still TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL after the staging→imported copies), every 120th frame, 5 recordings, logs min/max + 9 samples. **Fork**: ~255 while walking = shader + plumb work → the SDK is ignoring the mask (next: reactive input format / SDK reactiveScale/reactiveBias semantics / the D3D12-side reactive read); 0 while walking = uCamMotion never reaches the shader (next: push-constant layout offset mismatch vs what glslang actually emits — verify with spirv-dis, or the JNI call ordering vs recordVkModMvGen). Validated: cmake build (fsrvk.dll 131,584 → 133,632 bytes), `gradlew build` green, deployed 15:24 to both gradle-client resources and PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260808-reactiveprobe`). The `REACTIVE READBACK` probe also doubles as the standing-still check: ~0 while standing = accumulation intact (not the "everything spatial" failure).
- **"It just shakes then I move and smears when I stand still" — MV staging readback probe added (2026-08-08, 15:42 build, DIAGNOSTIC)**: the two 15:24 probes were DECISIVE and both POSITIVE: `setVkModCamMotion` logs real eyeSpeed values (3.2/11.2/5.9 while moving, 0.018-0.6 standing — the Java plumb works, call #0=0 is the hasPrevVkCam first-frame warmup) and `REACTIVE READBACK (staging)` reads **255 while moving, 0 while standing** (frames 240/360 vs 480/600 — the shader ramp + staging copy + pool idx 3 wiring all work). So the reactive mask is NOT the problem — the new symptom (SHAKE while moving, SMEAR while standing) with reactive=0 standing means the SDK is accumulating MISALIGNED history: with reactive=0 and mv≈0 the SDK should accumulate in place and sharpen, so a standing-still smear implicates the MVs themselves (or the MV-jitter contract), not the mask. Verified the whole MV chain on paper once more: Java builds `currViewProj = lastProj(clean) * tmpView(camera)` and passes `invCurrVP` + `prevVP` (prevViewProj saved each frame, hasPrevFrame gated identity only on the first frame); native writes them at push offsets 0/64; the shader reprojects at ndcZ=0.999, `mv = (prevUv - uv) * renderSize`; dispatch passes `prevJitterX/Y` (the pooled PREVIOUS frame's jitter — correct per the 2026-08-05 contract, matching what the GL FSR2/3 path does with uJitter/uPrevJitter + uMotionVectorJitterCancellation). `VKMod projection override` fired at 15:27:19 (world renders jittered, matching the SDK's jitterOffset). Everything checks — so the remaining fork needs the MV values themselves. **New probe (15:42 build, fsrvk.dll 133,632 → 136,192 bytes)**: `MV READBACK (staging)` — mirrors the reactive probe but for `g_vkModMvGenStageMv` (R16G16_SFLOAT = 2 floats/texel, 16x16 center block, every 120th frame, 5 recordings) logging minMag/maxMag + 9 sample vectors in render px. **Fork**: standing MV ≈ (0,0) = matrices + jitter contract are fine → the SDK/accumulate side is misbehaving (next: reactiveScale/reactiveBias semantics, depth input to the SDK, or the SDK's internal jitter handling — the reactive probe already ruled out mask delivery); standing MV large/nonzero = the matrix plumbing (uInvCurrVP/uPrevVP) is genuinely wrong (e.g. prevVP not actually the previous frame, or the matrices change frame-to-frame while standing). Deployed to both gradle-client resources and PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260808-mvprobe`).
- **"It is still smearing" — the FSR4 SDK CONTRACT was violated: motionVectorScale divided pixel MVs to ~0, and the jitter Y sign was inverted (2026-08-08, FIX)**: the 15:42 MV readback was decisive: MVs read ~0 while walking — but that is EXPECTED in no-real-depth mode (constant far depth = no translation parallax), so it proved nothing about the matrices. The real find came from reading the FSR4 4.1.1 + FSR3.1.5 integration docs against the actual dispatch code in `fsr4_context.cpp`. **Contract violation #1 — motionVectorScale**: the docs ("Providing motion vectors") are explicit that FSR4/FSR3.1 expect MVs ALREADY in `[<-width,-height>..<width,height>]` screen-pixel range, and `motionVectorScale` is a MULTIPLIER used to convert OTHER spaces (e.g. NDC) into that pixel range — the SDK's own example passes `motionVectorScale = (renderWidth, renderHeight)` for NDC MVs. Our MV-gen outputs `mv = (prevUv - uv) * renderSize` — ALREADY pixels — but the dispatch passed `1.0/renderSize`, DIVIDING a 2px walk MV down to 0.001. The SDK's temporal accumulator then treated everything as static: with reactive=0 standing still it accumulated history reprojected to the SAME spot (the standing-still smear), and during motion the tiny MVs misaligned history (the shake/ghost). **Contract violation #2 — jitterOffset Y sign**: the docs composite projection jitter as `(2*jX/W, -2*jY/H)` and pass the raw unit-pixel offset to `jitterOffset`; a positive jitterOffset.y means the render was shifted DOWN. Our Java `clipSpaceJitter()` applies `+2*jY/H` (the VKMod projection override logged `jitter=(0.0000, -0.1667)` at 15:57 — content moves UP for positive jitterY, the mirror of the SDK's assumption). Passing the raw +jY made the SDK de-jitter history in the wrong vertical direction — standing still it accumulates history misaligned by the per-frame jitter delta → smear; moving, the misaligned de-jitter adds vertical shake. **Fix (native only — `fsr4_context.cpp` + `fg4_context.cpp`)**: `motionVectorScale.x/y = 1.0f` (pixel MVs = identity scale; the old 1/renderSize was a wrong FSR2-API convention copy-paste — FSR2's ffx_fsr2 API internally divides by target size, but the FSR3.1/FSR4 ffxDispatchDescUpscale API takes pixels natively) and `jitterOffset.y = -params.jitterY` (negate at the SDK boundary so its internal de-jitter matches the render that actually happened; the Java render-side +2*jY/H stays untouched — that is what the GL path's custom accumulate de-jitters against, and the GL path is validated). Both files carry inline comments citing the docs. Validated: cmake build green (fsrvk.dll 136,192 bytes — same size as 15:42 since no SPIR-V change), `gradlew build` green, deployed 16:24 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260808-mvscale`).
- **"The terrain warps when I move the camera even slightly" — the MV-gen's uv↔NDC Y mapping was MIRRORED vs the actual render; the 16:24 motionVectorScale fix exposed it (2026-08-08, FIX)**: the 16:27 session's probes (MV READBACK all 0.000, REACTIVE all 0) were standing-still samples, so they never covered the report. Root cause found by decompiling VulkanMod 0.6.8's `Renderer.setViewport(int,int,int,int,MemoryStack)`: it uses the negative-viewport-height trick (`viewport.y = h + y`, `viewport.height = -h`) so Vulkan renders with OpenGL semantics — **NDC +1 = TOP row of the framebuffer** (the whole point of the trick: GL-style matrices from Java work unchanged). But `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` still used the GL-texture convention `ndc = uv*2 - 1` and `prevUv = prevNdc*0.5+0.5`, which assume uv.y=0 = BOTTOM (GL row 0 is bottom-left origin). On a Vulkan image (row 0 = TOP), uv.y=0 is the TOP row, so the unprojection took the top row and treated it as NDC -1 (bottom) — the reprojection was vertically MIRRORED. This was invisible while `motionVectorScale=1/renderSize` (16:24 before-fix) crushed MVs to ~0.001 and the SDK effectively ignored them; the 16:24 scale=1.0 change let REAL MVs flow for the first time, and the inverted vertical component made history reproject to the wrong row → terrain warps on any camera move (rotation MVs are exactly the ones the constant-depth mode keeps — and they were the ones inverted). **Fix (shader only)**: flip BOTH mappings to the render's convention (top = +1, converting back to uv top = 0): `ndc = vec2(uv.x*2-1, 1.0 - uv.y*2)` and `prevUv = vec2(prevNdc.x*0.5+0.5, 0.5 - prevNdc.y*0.5)` (mv = (prevUv-uv)*renderSize unchanged; mv sign now matches the SDK's screen-space convention — positive mv.y = content moved up on screen, per the FSR4 docs example). Only these two lines exist in the shader (verified). Validated: glslangValidator clean, SPIR-V 10,588 → 10,780 bytes + header regenerated in the EXACT original format (global scope, hex literals, BYTE-count size comment — my first regeneration wrapped it in `namespace fsr` and broke the build; the byte-count gotcha from 2026-08-06 still applies), new constant verified embedded in fsrvk.dll (10780 present, 10588 gone, SPIR-V magic present), fsrvk.dll 136,192 bytes, `gradlew build` green, deployed 16:38 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `.bak-20260808-mvscale` updated). The 16:24 jitterOffset.y negation stands (independent: our render composites +2*jY/H, the SDK convention is -2*jY/H, so passing -jY keeps the SDK's internal de-jitter aligned with the render). **Expected next run**: standing still unchanged (accumulation), camera pans now reproject to the CORRECT row — the warp should be gone; rotation MVs should read nonzero in the MV READBACK during pans (they were crushed before the scale fix, then inverted after it). If warp persists, next suspects: the prevVP matrix is not actually the previous frame's, or the SDK's jitterOffset sign assumption is the inverse of what I derived (would show as vertical-only ghosting rather than full warp).

- **"Still smearing when I barely move the camera" — translation ramp v2 (2026-08-08, FIX)**: the 16:26–16:40 session's probes (REACTIVE READBACK min=61 at the movement recordings, MV READBACK 0.000, cam-motion eyeSpeed real) exposed a second gap AFTER the Y-mirror fix: the no-real-depth TRANSLATION reactive ramp was `smoothstep(2.0, 4.5, uCamMotion)` — a barely-perceptible move at ~2.4 blocks/sec (the 61/255 ≈ 24% reading) stayed well below full reactive, so the SDK kept trusting the constant-depth MVs (which cannot track translation) and smeared. Idle bob measures ~0.13 blocks/sec (FSRProcessor cam-motion block, dt-normalized — the "~1.2" figure in the old shader comment was wrong), so the ramp has huge headroom below it. Fix (`fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`): `smoothstep(0.3, 1.5, pc.uCamMotion)` — any deliberate movement (slow strafe, nudge, walk 4.3, sprint 5.6) is now FULLY reactive (spatial render, no smear); only sub-bob idle keeps full accumulation. Rotation still NOT ramped (constant-depth rotation MVs are exact — the SDK follows history across pans; the Y-mirror fix from 16:38 makes those MVs correct). NOTE the 16:38 Y-flip build was deployed MID-SESSION (16:38 vs the running session 16:26–16:40), so the user's "still smearing" report was almost certainly against the PRE-Y-flip DLL — this 16:47 build carries BOTH fixes (Y-mirror + ramp v2) in one deploy. Validated: glslangValidator clean, SPIR-V 10,780 bytes (new 0.3/1.5 constants verified in the header, old 4.5 gone; the remaining 2.0 is the unrelated `uv*2.0`), fsrvk.dll 136,192 bytes, `gradlew build` green, deployed 16:47 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `.bak-20260808-mvscale` updated).
 **Expected next run**: standing still → history accumulates ALIGNED (no smear — the jitter contract now matches the render); walking → no ghost (real MVs reach the SDK; the no-real-depth reactive ramp still discards history during motion, but that is by design); the `MV READBACK` minMag should now show real pixel values (2-10px) while walking IF real depth ever lands — in no-real-depth mode rotation MVs should also read nonzero during pans (they were crushed to ~0.001 by the scale bug before). If shake/smear persists, the next suspects are the SDK's depth input (uniform 1.0 far-plane is legal but weakens depth-based disocclusion) and the 0.25 render scale (still the single biggest quality lever — 0.5-0.65 supported).

- **FSR2/3 residual-shake fixes (2026-08-06, "still shaking, worse at lower quality" fix)**: after the ndcZ=0.995 + jitter-cancel fixes the accumulation weights are healthy (ACCUM_WEIGHT L-samples 0.18→0.65) but shake persists, amplitude ∝ 1/renderScale (jitter is ±0.5 RENDER px = ±1.1 display px at 45%). Three targeted changes: (1) constant depth raised z≈20 → z≈100 blocks (ndcZ 0.995 → 0.999): the constant-depth reprojection error is |computed−true| = v·f·|1/z_a − 1/z| — at z_a=20 the eye-tracked far/horizon content (100-500 blocks) was overestimated ~8px at walk speed → clamp discarded its history → jittered spatial = shake; at z_a=100 everything ≥50 blocks is ≤4px (horizon stable), nearer content is underestimated (blends as blur). (2) ACCUMULATE ellipsoid-clamp softening when depth untrusted (`velFactor * (uUseDepth > 0 ? 1.0 : 0.35)` in the confidence max): constant-depth MVs make high velocity as likely parallax error as disocclusion — damp the velocity confidence so misaligned history blends as motion blur instead of being discarded into raw jittered spatial (the shake mechanism; the clamp output ≈ current at 98% history blend = jittered). (3) FSR2/3 jitter DISPLAY amplitude capped at 0.6 display px (`jitterAmp = min(0.5, 0.6·scaledWidth/displayWidth)` in onRenderBegin's isTemporalUpscaler block, FSR2/3 only — FSR4 keeps full ±0.5 render px): the uncapped amplitude at 45%/30% is ±1.1/±1.7 display px on every rejected-history pixel (sky/clouds, edges). **RELAXED TO 1.5 display px (2026-08-06, "shake gone but no edge smoothing" fix)**: at 25% scale (420x263→1680x1050) the 0.6 cap crushed jitter to ±0.15 render px (30% of FSR2's standard ±0.5 render-px Halton spread) — 12+ accumulated samples all within ±0.3 render px average to the current spatial sample, so the temporal filter became a no-op and the upscale looked purely spatial. Edge smoothing IS the jitter spread. New cap `min(0.5, 1.5·scaledWidth/displayWidth)` gives ±0.375 render px (75% of standard, 2.5x the old spread) at 25% and the full ±0.5 render px at ≥33% scale; the rejected-pixel shake the old cap was hiding is mostly gone anyway (clamp damping blends misaligned history as blur). Residual known limit: content 5-30 blocks still gets underestimated MVs while moving (blur, not shake); sky/clouds remain spatial by design.
- **FSR2/3 constant-depth MV fixes (2026-08-06, "edge smoothing works but everything still shakes" fix)**: two compounding bugs in the GL-path MV chain, both visible in `[ACCUM_WEIGHT]` readbacks (accumulation weight collapsed to 0.18 = ~2 frames during motion). (1) MV-gen constant depth was `ndcZ = 0.0` → view-space z≈2·near≈0.1 blocks — essentially the NEAR plane. A 0.36-block/frame walk produced MVs of ~1200 render px (true 12px at 10 blocks) → `reprojUv = hrUv + mv` flew off-screen → `isNewSample` → raw jittered input = shake everywhere. Fixed to `ndcZ = 0.995` (z≈20 blocks): content 5-50 blocks gets near-correct MVs, nearer is underestimated (safe — clamp keeps history), far overestimated ≤6px at sprint (clamp-tolerant). (2) MV-gen uses the JITTERED curr/prev matrices (`lastProjJittered`) so MVs carry the per-frame jitter delta (J_prev − J_curr, up to ±1 render px = ±2.2 display px at 45% scale) while `jitterCancel` was 0 — standing-still reprojection wobble rejected history on detail. Fixed: `jitterCancelX/Y = (prevJitterX − jitterX) * motionVecScaleX/Y` (render-UV units, consumed by RECONSTRUCT's `mv -= uMotionVectorJitterCancellation`; sign derived: MV = trueMotion + J_prev − J_curr). Both changes are no-ops at 1:1 render scale for the cancellation and scale with camera speed for the depth constant. Applies to FSR2 + FSR3 (embedded + on-disk MV_GEN_CS). FSR4's GL constant depth (shared 0.5) has the same near-plane assumption — NOT changed (its SDK reactive tuning depends on it; follow-up candidate).
- **FSR2/3 depth-trust gate (2026-08-06, "everything shaking + no edge smoothing" fix)**: DEPTH_CLIP_CS and ACCUMULATE_CS read the RenderTarget depth UNCONDITIONALLY (no uUseDepth gate like MV-gen) — on 26.1.2 the GL RT depth is essentially never written (probe: min=0.924 max=1.000), so `abs(curViewZ) > abs(uFarViewZ)*0.95` fired for EVERY pixel → full disocclusion clip + all-sky → history rejected every frame → jitter showed through as shaking with zero temporal smoothing. Fix: both passes gained `uniform int uUseDepth` set from the SHARED probe result (`fsr4RealDepthAvailable`; `probeFsr4Depth` is now called from the FSR2/3 dispatch sites too — it gates all three pipelines). When untrusted: computeDepthClip early-returns 0.0 (depth-based disocclusion skipped; the MV-based reactive mask in main() still runs), and `isSkyDepth = (uUseDepth>0) && ...` falls back to the screen-Y-guarded color detection alone. Tradeoff: no depth-based disocclusion on garbage-depth machines → possible mild ghosting on fast disocclusion, same constant-depth ceiling FSR4 has. On-disk shader copies in the instance config must be patched in lockstep.
- **FSR2/3 motion-adaptive jitter (2026-08-06, "still shaking" fix)**: after the lock-build fix, the session log showed lock life recovered (R 85-222 raw at 25% scale) and weight steady ~1.0 — but ACCUM_WEIGHT B still collapses to 0.18 (= the 0.167 isNewSample floor, adjAccum=0) at every sample point during motion: history fully rejected while moving → the fresh jittered frame shows → at 25% scale the jitter is ±0.375 render px = **±1.5 display px of shake exactly when moving**. Root cause: with constant-depth MVs (no real depth on GL), camera TRANSLATION reprojection diverges at depth edges (per-pixel error ∝ |1/z_a − 1/z|) → motionDiv/velScale fire → reactive ≈ 1 → weight floor. Rotation MVs are depth-independent (correct at constant depth) so pure looking is NOT the trigger — translation is. Fix: motion-adaptive jitter in onRenderBegin's FSR2/3 block — the amplitude is scaled by `motionScale = 1 − 0.6·min(1, max(camDev/0.25, dRot/0.12))` where camDev = the camera's deviation from a slow EMA position (0.9/0.1 ≈ 10-frame time constant) and dRot = the angular deviation from an EMA'd camera quaternion (nlerp 0.1 + normalize, JOML `fsr23EmaCamQ`): the periodic idle bob oscillates around the EMA center and cancels (≈7% shrink), walking/sprinting diverges within ~5 frames (down to 40% amplitude = ±0.6 display px at 25%). Rotation term added on review — the log couldn't distinguish translation- vs look-driven weight dips, so both axes shrink (rotation threshold 0.12 rad steady-state ≈ sustained 0.75°/frame look; casual looking keeps full jitter). Motion frames barely use sub-pixel diversity anyway (history rejected), so the smoothing cost is negligible while the perceived shake drops ~60%. Stationary frames keep full 1.5-display-px-cap amplitude → full temporal reconstruction. New fields `fsr23EmaCamX/Y/Z` + `fsr23EmaCamInit`; `Vec3 camPosNow = client.gameRenderer.getMainCamera().position()` (client local already in scope in onRenderBegin). FSR4 untouched (own suspension machinery). Knobs: the 0.25-block deviation threshold and the 0.6 shrink factor. **Sky/cloud still-camera accumulation (2026-08-06, "sky is blocky in-game" fix)**: the user's screenshot showed blocky clouds/sun at 25% scale — expected, because sky/cloud pixels are hard-excluded from temporal accumulation (`isNewSample = ... || isSky || isCloud`) → every frame is a fresh 4× upscale with ZERO sub-pixel reconstruction. FSR4's SDK accumulates clouds under reactive-mask protection; FSR2/3 now do the same **when the camera is genuinely still**: the ACCUMULATE shader takes a new `uCamStill` uniform, and when set the sky/cloud history-read gate, `accumWeight` gate, `isNewSample` (sky/cloud term) and the lock-reset branch all relax — sky/cloud/sun accumulate like terrain, reconstructing cloud/sun edges from sub-pixel jitter samples instead of staying blocky. Safety: constant-depth MVs are EXACT when the camera doesn't move, so history aligns; the box clamp + luma lock (lumaDiff < 0.2) still protect slow cloud drift, and the moment the camera moves (`uCamStill=0`) sky/cloud revert to the fresh-render path (no ghosting from wrong constant-depth MVs during motion). Signal: `fsr23CamStill` computed in the FSR2/3 jitter block from the SAME EMA deviations as motion-adaptive jitter (`camDev < 0.05` blocks — walking steady-state lag ≈0.65 — AND `dRot < 0.02` rad — bob ≈0.002-0.005, sustained look > 0.02); passed via new `FSR2Pipeline/FSR3Pipeline.setCamStill(boolean)` → `uCamStill` uniform (all 4 dispatch sites). Sky gradient itself stays smooth either way (bilinear 4× of a gradient is fine); the win is cloud/sun EDGES.
  - **v2 (2026-08-06, "nothing changed" report)**: the v1 gate (`camDev < 0.05 && dRot < 0.02` EMA-based) almost never engaged in real play - walking trips camDev (steady-state EMA lag ~0.65) and ANY recent mouse look keeps dRot above 0.02 for ~30 frames of EMA convergence. The user's session log confirmed it: [DIAG_FSR2] jitter hovered at 0.05-0.17 of full amplitude for the whole session (motion-adaptive shrink active = camera moving/looking nearly continuously), so uCamStill was false almost always and the sky stayed blocky BY DESIGN. v2 measures the PER-FRAME rotation delta (`dRotFrame` vs `fsr23PrevCamRot`, engages the frame after the mouse stops) and DROPS the translation gate entirely - the sky is at infinity, so its screen position only changes with rotation; walking with a steady mouse leaves it fixed and safe to accumulate. The EMA term stays as a slow-look backstop (`dRot < 0.04`). Shader side: when `uCamStill==1` the ACCUMULATE now also forces sky reprojection to identity (`if (isSky && uCamStill == 1) mv = vec2(0.0)`) - the mid-depth constant-depth MVs are wrong for sky during ANY motion (parallax 0 at infinity) and would misalign history - and zeroes `reactiveFactor` for sky (the MV-gen's sky reactive is derived from those same wrong MVs). New fields `fsr23PrevCamRot`; edge-triggered unconditional `[CAMSTILL]` transition log (min 2s throttle) + `camStill=` added to the [DIAG] line so engagement is verifiable in the log.
  - **v3 (2026-08-06, "blocky sky when moving the camera" screenshot)**: the user's next screenshot was taken WHILE moving the camera - camStill (still-camera-only) can't smooth there by design. Two findings: (1) the isSky COLOR CLASSIFIER missed the actual sky: `isBlueSky` required luma>0.70 && b>1.3*max(r,g) - the screenshot's sky (#78a8ff) has luma ~0.64 and b=1.0 < 1.3*0.66, so even a still camera wouldn't have smoothed it (the gates never applied). Relaxed to luma>0.55 && b>1.15*max(r,g); isWhiteSky relaxed 0.95/0.02 -> 0.85/0.06 (catches off-white/gray-tinged clouds). (2) The sky/cloud hard-exclusion was REMOVED entirely - the ACCUMULATE now treats sky exactly like terrain. Justification verified in the MV-gen: both pipelines reproject at ndcZ=0.999 (z~100 blocks), and rotation MVs are mathematically DEPTH-INDEPENDENT (the z cancels in the UV division: prevClip = prevProj*prevView*(camPos + z*d), prevView*camPos = 0, z scales all 4 components and cancels in xy/w) - so the sky's rotation MVs are CORRECT, the old exclusion's reason (MV=0 under rotation from the ndcZ=0 bug) is gone, and translation parallax at z~100 is <=4px (box-clamp-absorbed). The reactive mask (mvLen ramp) + ellipsoid box clamp + luma lock protect the sky exactly like terrain: fast turns -> large mvLen -> reactive -> fresh (no ghosting); slow pans -> correct reprojection -> history aligns -> smooth. uCamStill=1 remains as a refinement (exact mv=0 reprojection + reactive 0 when the camera is genuinely still). Removed: the `(!isSky || uCamStill==1)` history/weight gates, the `((isSky||isCloud) && uCamStill==0)` isNewSample term, and the lock-reset if/else branch (write path is now unconditional). 4 copies (FSR2/FSR3 embedded + disk). Known tradeoff: gray clouds (luma 0.55-0.85) still fail the white test and are handled as terrain-like with z~100 MVs - acceptable, bounded by the box clamp. **UPDATED 2026-08-07**: the white-sky classifier was relaxed to luma>0.58 && chroma<0.12 (see the standing-still jagged-edges bullet) so gray clouds ARE classified as sky — while camStill they get mv=0 + reactive 0 (exact for a still camera), and during motion they keep the z~100 MV treatment bounded by the box clamp.
- **FSR2/3 green sky smear (2026-08-07, "greenish thing spreads through the sky when standing still" fix)**: the luma-lock trust gate was SELF-REFERENTIAL — `lumaDiff` compared the CURRENT frame luma against `lockLuma`, and `lockLuma` is mixed toward the current luma every frame (0.1/frame in the lock-build branch), so after ~20 frames the check ALWAYS passes regardless of what the history actually contains. `lockTrusted` therefore stayed true on any accumulating pixel and `histContribFactor = lockContrib * reactiveContrib` blended the RAW (unclamped) history at up to 100% — the box clamp (the mechanism that should reject misaligned history) is BYPASSED for locked pixels. Any wrong content that entered the history (terrain green ghosted into sky positions by a look-up pan, cloud drift, constant-depth reprojection error during the idle bob) was then preserved forever AND diffused pixel-to-pixel by the per-frame jitter offset → the spreading green smear; it vanished on movement because motion flips reactiveFactor high → accumulation drops → the fresh render shows. Log evidence: `[ACCUM_WEIGHT]` showed fully-accumulating sky pixels (lock R=255, weight up to 13) — exactly the pixels that smear — while blue-sky pixels without camStill classification capped at weight ~4.35 (reactive ≈ 0.038 from bob MVs). Fix (all 4 ACCUMULATE copies, embedded + disk): (1) `lumaDiff` is now a real CONTENT check — `abs(upsampledYCoCg.x - historyYCoCg.x)` (sampled-history luma vs current-frame luma; reference-FSR2 semantics) — a green-vs-blue mismatch (≈0.26 rel. luma) releases the lock and the box clamp rejects the ghost; (2) sky/cloud never enter the lock path at all: `lockTrusted` requires `!isSky`, the lock-build branch is `!isSky &&`, and the ridge `newLock` refresh is `newLock && !isSky` — sky still accumulates (box-clamped, weight grows, sub-pixel jitter samples reconstruct cloud edges) but can never blend an unclamped history. Terrain locking (grass/leaf thin detail) is unchanged — a correct reprojection keeps history luma ≈ current luma so the content check passes. Expected: the smear is impossible; cloud edges stay smoothed by box-clamped accumulation. The FSR2 lock-status A channel still carries the throttle diagnostic (`1.0` = isNewSample, else `max(reactive, depthClip)`).

- **FSR2/3 camera-motion reactive floor (2026-08-07, "fix the shaking" fix)**: with the double-count + ghost-alpha fixes in, the standing-still path is bit-stable (weights 12+, lock 255, reactive 0.00, verified in the 14:46 session log) but the MOTION phase still shook: during walking/sprint the reactive mask — derived from constant-depth MVs (z≈100) that UNDERESTIMATE near content's true screen motion — only reached 0.04-0.15 (log frame 1824: M=1-3 render px, reactive 0.04-0.15, weights collapsed to 1.65-3.18) while the misaligned history stayed ~90% of the blend (alpha boost smoothstep(0.3,1.0,·)≈0 below reactive 0.3, histContribFactor≈0.63) = smear + edge wobble scaling with speed. Fix: a CPU camera-motion floor on the mask.
