The pipeline already measures the camera exactly (per-frame `dPosFrame`/`dRotFrame` in the FSR2/3 jitter block); `camReactive = clamp(max((dPosFrame-0.05)/0.55, (dRotFrame-0.04)/0.05), 0, 0.9)` (TRANSLATION-dominant — rotation MVs are depth-independent/exact at constant depth, so the rotation term only fires on extreme turns ≥2.3°/frame; pans are covered by the MV ramp + fast-jitter stage) is uploaded as `uCamReactive` and applied in ACCUMULATE: `reactiveFactor = max(reactiveFactor, uCamReactive)` placed AFTER the decl and BEFORE the sky/camStill override (so still-camera sky still zeroes it). Effects: walking (0.3 blocks/frame → 0.45) lifts alpha toward 0.11 and cuts raw-history contribution (0.63→0.38) with the lock-build gate (<0.4) CLOSED so misaligned history stops cementing and decays to clamped-only over a few seconds; sprint (0.62 → 0.9) → alpha 0.88 → mostly fresh (de-jittered = stable). Standing still (bob 0.01-0.02 blocks, 0.002 rad) → floor 0 → bit-identical to the pre-fix path. The existing fast-jitter stage (amp→0.05 render px) works in parallel for the fresh-frame contribution. Shader-side: 4 copies (FSR2/FSR3 embedded + disk) gained `uniform float uCamReactive` + the max line; Java: `FSR2Pipeline/FSR3Pipeline.setCamReactive(float)` → `ac_uCamReactive` (all 4 dispatch sites), field `fsr23CamReactive` computed in onRenderBegin's FSR2/3 block. Tradeoff: during motion the blend leans more spatial (jagged-ish) — the accepted exchange the user asked for (shake → stability), and it self-heals: the moment the camera stops, the floor drops to 0 and the lock rebuilds full accumulation. **Telemetry signature change (expected, not a regression)**: during walking the [ACCUM_WEIGHT] B channel now reads ~8-11 (adjAccum = weight·(1-0.45)) instead of collapsing to the 0.18 floor, and lock life stops rebuilding (floor 0.45 > the 0.4 lock-build gate) then decays to clamped-only over a few seconds — both are the intended distrust mechanism working.

**FSR4-PARITY REACTIVE MASK (2026-08-06, "take inspiration from FSR4" fix)**: FSR2/3's DEPTH_CLIP used the reference FSR2 DIVERGENCE mask `motionDiv * velScale` — `velScale = clamp(nucleusVel * displaySize.x / (12 * renderSize.x))` is scale-dependent: at 25% scale (display/render = 4) it saturates at just 3 render px of MV, so ANY walking drove reactiveFactor → 1 → `adjAccum = accumWeight * (1-reactive)*(1-depthClip)` → 0 → the B=0.167 isNewSample floor → the raw jittered frame showed through → **jagged edges while moving** (log-verified B=0.18 at all sample points during motion; also why it scales with the quality slider). FSR4's MV-gen instead computes reactive from **MV magnitude** — `smoothstep(0.5, 16.0, mvLen)` (1-8px walking keeps MOST history, only >16px fully discards) + `smoothstep(0.4, 2.5, mvLen) * 0.15` motion floor. Ported that profile into FSR2/3 DEPTH_CLIP (all 4 copies: embedded FSR2/FSR3 + disk): `reactive = max(smoothstep(0.5,16,mvLen), max(motionFloor, motionDiv*0.5))` — the divergence term is kept at half strength for genuine disocclusion (moving object revealing background) but can no longer saturate from ordinary walking. Lock-build gate (`reactiveFactor < 0.4`) now stays OPEN while walking → lock life builds during motion → history contributes → edges reconstruct. FSR3's temporalReactive decays from the same source and follows it down automatically.

**v2 (2026-08-06, "less shaking but edges still jagged" fix)**: the flat 0.4 floor crushed the motion jitter to ±0.15 render px at 25% scale — too little sub-pixel spread to reconstruct edges while walking (jagged). The motion amplitude now interpolates toward a FIXED display-space cap: `motionJitterAmp = min(jitterAmp, 1.0*scaledWidth/displayWidth)` (raised from 0.75 on review — 0.75 was barely above the v1 value that read as jagged), `jitterAmpMotion = jitterAmp + (motionJitterAmp − jitterAmp)*motionFactor` — at 25% walking keeps ±0.25 render px (±1.0 display px, 50% of FSR2's standard ±0.5 render-px spread, a third below the pre-adaptive ±1.5 shake) so edges reconstruct; at ≥50% the cap converges to the stationary amplitude so walking keeps FULL reconstruction (the old flat factor over-shrunk there too). Also found: `"sharpness": 0.0` in the user's config — RCAS completely off, edges are the raw temporal result; recommend 0.4–0.6 sharpness (RCAS is spatial-only, runs every frame regardless of temporal state — the most direct lever for jagged edges).
- **Standing-still jagged edges = divergence-mask bug (2026-08-07, "the green is gone but the edges=jaged" fix)**: after the green-ghost fix, the [ACCUM_WEIGHT] readback showed a flat `A=127` (=0.5 discard) on the ENTIRE lower screen while `(840,913)` top-sky accumulated to weight 15 with A=0 — i.e. every non-sky pixel was throttled to ~1 frame of history by `reactiveFactor=0.5` while standing still. `depthClipUse=0` (uUseDepth=0), so the 0.5 was `disocclusion = motionDiv*0.5` with `motionDiv=1`. Root cause (two compounding bugs in DEPTH_CLIP's divergence): (1) **zero-MV samples counted as opposing** — `sv = max(sv, 1e-7)` then `dot(smv/sv, nucleusMv/nucleusVel)` = 0 for a zero vector, so ANY pixel whose 3×3 contained a zero-MV neighbor instantly zeroed `minConv` → `motionDiv=1` → disocclusion 0.5. Standing-still the idle bob's rotation field HAS a zero line through the screen center (MVs above point up, below point down, 0 at the center) — every 3×3 straddling it fired. (2) **the maxVel/0.01 gate was trivially satisfied** — sub-pixel divergence (bob, quantization) counted as real disocclusion. Fix (all 4 DEPTH_CLIP copies, embedded + disk): skip `sv ≤ 1e-3` samples (a zero vector has no direction), and gate `motionDiv = convDiv * clamp(maxVel/2.0, 0, 1)` so divergence only registers with >2 render px of real neighborhood motion. Standing still: maxVel ~0.4 px → gate ~0.2 → disocclusion ≤0.1 → weight climbs to 15 → edges reconstruct from the jitter sub-pixel samples. Walking: real divergence with maxVel>2 still fires. Also: **gray-cloud classifier relax** — `isWhiteSky` luma 0.85→0.58, chroma 0.06→0.12 (screen-Y>0.5 guard keeps terrain out; observed gray cloud luma 0.70/chroma 0.10 was never classified sky → kept the 0.5 reactive → never accumulated → jagged cloud edges). Added a Java-side readback (M=(mvLen, reactive) columns in [ACCUM_WEIGHT], render-res MV + mask at the 9 points) to pin which term throttles any future pixel.
- **"Shaking everywhere" = two more throttles (2026-08-07 fix)**: the [ACCUM_WEIGHT] M-columns from the next test round showed it precisely: standing still, mvLen was 0.2–0.9 render px with reactive 0.07–0.24 → weights stuck at 1.3–2.5 (not 15) → the residual 30–40% fresh jittered current showed through as **standing-still wobble on the whole lower screen**; during motion (one sample showed M=8.5–14 render px = 34–56 display px) reactive hit 0.53–0.96 → weight at the 0.18 floor → **the raw jittered frame displayed while moving**. Two fixes: (1) **DEPTH_CLIP reactive now reads uDilatedData.rg (RECONSTRUCT's output — the per-frame jitter delta (J_prev−J_curr) is subtracted there) instead of the raw MV-gen texture** — the raw field carries the delta up to ±0.75 render px STANDING STILL, which the divergence/velocity gates turned into the standing-still throttle; uDilatedData.rg is true content motion (×uRenderSize → render px, all units now consistent, `sv > 0.05` px direction filter). All 4 copies. (2) **Fast-motion jitter stage in onRenderBegin's FSR2/3 block**: the v2 floor of 1.0 display px is still visible once history is rejected (reactive >0.5 → jitter provides NO reconstruction, only oscillation on the fresh frames), so above walking speed the amplitude interpolates toward 0.05 render px (0.2 display px — imperceptible): ` fastFactor = clamp(max((dPosFrame−0.12)/0.5, (dRotFrame−0.01)/0.03), 0, 1)` where dPosFrame is the INSTANTANEOUS per-frame camera translation (new fsr23PrevCamPos fields — the EMA-based camDev lags ~10 frames and the shake persisted during that window) and dRotFrame is the existing per-frame rotation delta. Thresholds are per-FRAME because the shake tracks per-frame MV magnitude: 60-fps walking (~0.07 blocks/frame) stays under 0.12 → full jitter, edges reconstruct; 30-fps walking (~0.14) gets ~0.04 → negligible; the observed shake case (0.3–0.47, sprint/fall/teleport) gets 0.36–0.7 → jitter drops to ~0.15 render px (0.6 display px); flying/teleports (which also have ~30 frames of no-history at full amplitude) hit 1.0 → 0.05 render px (0.2 display px, imperceptible). Expected: standing-still weights climb to ~10+ (alpha small → stable), motion frames present an almost-unjittered render (shake gone, edges spatial during fast motion — the inherent tradeoff at 25%).
- **FSR2/3 lock-trust cap (2026-08-07, "smearing" fix)**: the lock's unclamped history blend was gated on a self-referential luma match (lockLuma mixed toward current every frame) — once trusted, it blended RAW unclamped history at up to 100%. Even a tiny constant-depth reprojection error during the idle bob (~1px) would smear detail across frames because no box clamp was applied. The fix caps the lock contribution at 70% (`histContribFactor = min(lockContrib, 0.70) * reactiveContrib`) so at least 30% of the clamped (box-clipped) history is always in the mix, bounding the misalignment. The luma diff still self-clears genuine mismatches. All 4 copies (embedded + disk, FSR2 + FSR3).
- **FSR2/3 motion-ghost blend fix (2026-08-07, "ghosting much more noticeable at sprint" fix)**: the final blend `alpha` (current-frame share) = `upsampledWeight / (adjAccum + upsampledWeight)` barely moved when reactivity fired because the accumulation weight saturates at 15 and only decays slowly — at weight 15 with reactive 0.85, `adjAccum = 15×0.15 = 2.25` → `alpha = 0.167/2.42 = 0.069` → the output stayed **93% history**. During sprint the constant-depth MVs are wrong by tens of render px (translation parallax error), so 90%+ of the output was misaligned history = ghost trails scaling with speed (log-verified: weight B=0.18 floor during motion yet alpha only 0.07). Fix: `alpha = max(alpha, smoothstep(0.3, 1.0, reactiveFactor) * 0.9)` — reactivity now biases the blend DIRECTLY instead of only through the slow weight, pulling the current (fresh) frame in proportion to distrust. The smoothstep curve (refined on review) leaves walking untouched (reactive 0.15-0.3 → boost ≈ 0 — the reactive mask's built-in 0.15 motion floor would otherwise force alpha ≥ 0.14 during ANY motion, dragging the fresh jittered frame into the blend and regressing the walking shake) while crushing sprint ghosting (reactive 0.79-1.0 → boost 0.7-0.9). Standing still (reactive ≈ 0) is bit-identical to before. All 4 copies (embedded + disk, FSR2 + FSR3).
- **FSR2/3 elytra sky-ghost gate (2026-08-07, "still some ghosting with elytra" fix)**: the motion-ghost fix killed terrain ghosting (its MVs are 20-60 render px → reactive 1.0 → pure fresh) but the SKY still ghosted during flight. Mechanism: the GL path's constant-depth MV-gen (z≈100 blocks) manufactures parallax for the sky at infinity (true parallax = 0), producing 5-11 render px of spurious sky MVs at elytra speed (f·v/z = 540·1/100 ≈ 5.4px at 1 block/frame). Those land mid-reactive-ramp: reactive = smoothstep(0.5,16,8) ≈ 0.45 → alpha boost ≈ 0.14 → ~85% of the output was misaligned sky history — and since the sky is a smooth gradient, the misaligned sample PASSES the box clamp (similar colors) → ghost. Fix: in ACCUMULATE, `if (isSky && mvPx * float(uDisplaySize.x) / float(uRenderSize.x) > 11.0) isNewSample = true;` — sky reprojection is only trustworthy when its screen motion is small (≤11 display px ≈ 5 render px at 45% scale, box-clamp-absorbable — the walking case, ~1.9px at walk speed, preserved); beyond that render sky fresh. The threshold is expressed in DISPLAY px (review fix 2026-08-07 — a render-px threshold is scale-dependent: the manufactured sky MV per unit world motion scales with render width, so at 25% scale elytra would only produce ~3 render px and the gate would silently not fire at the low scales the user runs; display px = mvPx × display/render is scale-invariant, the same class fix as velScale/velFactor/JITTER_DISPLAY_PX_MAX). Still-camera sky is unaffected (the earlier `if (isSky && uCamStill == 1) mv = vec2(0.0)` zeroes mv BEFORE mvPx is computed, so the gate never fires on a still camera). All 4 copies (embedded + disk, FSR2 + FSR3). Knob: the 11.0 display-px threshold.
- **FSR2/3 fast-motion jitter verification (2026-08-07, "still shaking" diagnosis)**: added `[FAST-JITTER]` one-shot log when `fastFactor > 0.5` (engages during sprint/fly/teleport) and `[DIAG_FAST]` per-60-frames diag line showing fastFactor, jitterAmpMotion, dPosFrame, dRotFrame. These prove the motion-adaptive jitter shrink actually fires during fast movement (the regular [DIAG] samples only standing-still frames). Thresholds: dPosFrame > 0.12 blocks/frame or dRotFrame > 0.01 rad/frame → fastFactor rises → jitter shrinks toward 0.05 render px.
- **uCamStillExact — standing-still shake fix (2026-08-07, "shaking only when standing still" report)**: the per-tick fix made motion stable (DIAG_FAST now fires, camReactive 0.4-0.9) but the user's new report isolated the remaining shake to STANDING STILL, where telemetry is healthy (weights 10-15, lock 255, reactive 0.00-0.04) — so the wobble lives in HISTORY ALIGNMENT, not the blend ratio. Mechanism: the idle bob is REAL translation (0.01-0.02 blocks/frame); the constant-depth MVs (z≈100) underestimate its near-content parallax (true 0.5-4px at z<10 vs ~0.05px computed) → the history read oscillates with the bob → box clamp rejects → the jittered current frame shows through = shake + slightly jagged edges exactly while standing. The existing `uCamStill` can't fix terrain because its TRANSLATION gate was dropped (v2 sky-only reasoning — sky has zero parallax, so translation doesn't move it; but terrain DOES move with translation, so walking with a steady mouse must keep real MVs). Fix: a NEW uniform `uCamStillExact` — camera GENUINELY still: `fsr23CamStillExact = dPosTick < 0.08 && dRotFrame < 0.006 && dRot < 0.015` (per-tick translation: bob 0.03-0.06, crouch-walk 0.108, walk 0.216; per-frame rotation: bob 0.002-0.005, pans >20°/s disengage instantly; EMA rotation backstop 0.015 catches slow sustained pans ~14°/s — tightened from 0.02 on review: 0.02 left ~30 frames of residual wobble after stopping a mouse look, 0.015 halves that to ~11 while a sustained 14°/s pan's steady-state lag ~0.036 still disengages). In ACCUMULATE (all 4 copies) the sky-only override becomes `if (uCamStillExact == 1 || (isSky && uCamStill == 1))` for BOTH `mv = vec2(0.0)` and `reactiveFactor = 0.0` — exact identity reprojection for ALL pixels when still: history read lands on the same surface every frame → box clamp passes → full temporal accumulation → wobble gone and edges reconstruct from the jitter spread (the de-jitter `lrUv = hrUv − uJitter/uRenderSize` still runs, so the input samples track the jitter as before). Safety: moving entities keep the luma-lock (< 0.2) + box clamp protection (the MV field never had entity motion anyway); the instant the camera moves past bob magnitude, exact turns off and the normal MV/reactive path resumes (no ghosting — the motion-phase machinery from the entries above is untouched). Java: `setCamStillExact` in both pipelines → `ac_uCamStillExact` (all 4 dispatch sites), `fsr23CamStillExact` in the FSR2/3 jitter block, `camStillExact=%d` added to both DIAG lines for verification. Expected telemetry: standing still `camStillExact=1` and weights keep climbing (no rejection); walking `camStillExact=0`, `camReactive` 0.3-0.9 as before.
- **Standing-still edge shake = box-clamp snap wobble, fixed by anchoring the clamp box at the reprojected position (2026-08-08, "i still see shaking when i stand still" fix)**: the OUTPROBE burst (first real readback of the presented image, 45 samples) finally isolated the residual: with camStillExact=1 all four probe texels were BIT-IDENTICAL across 2+ Halton cycles (exact path provably stable), but with camStillExact=0 (which toggles off for the window after every mouse look — DIAG showed it flipping 0/1 while standing still with camReactive=0.000) the center/horizon texel oscillated ±16 B counts CORRELATED WITH THE JITTER PHASE while hand/low/sky held flat. Root cause: the ellipsoid clamp box was anchored at `lrUvClamped` — the DE-JITTERED CURRENT position, which moves ±0.5 render px with the Halton jitter. At a high-contrast edge (the horizon = the oscillating probe texel) `floor(lrUvClamped * uRenderSize)` SNAPS between two adjacent render texels every time the jitter crosses a half-texel boundary → boxMin/boxMax jump between two 3x3 neighborhoods → the box-clamped history toggles with the jitter phase. Every prior fix (rejectDecay, lumaDiff boosts, lock-trust fades) treated the SYMPTOM (the blend ratio pulsing); the box anchor itself was never jitter-stable. Fix: `ivec2 boxBase = ivec2(floor(reprojUv * vec2(uRenderSize)))` — the box is now anchored at the position where the history is READ: `reprojUv = hrUv + mv` with `mv` already jitter-cancelled by RECONSTRUCT (hrUv is the fixed output pixel, so reprojUv is frame-to-frame stable while the camera is still) and it still tracks real motion when the camera moves (FSR2 reference builds the clamp box around the reprojected position — the current code's lrUv anchor was a deviation). Also fixes the rejectDecay input: lumaDiff now compares against a jitter-stable clamped history, so the weight decay stops pulsing too. All 4 copies (FSR2/FSR3 embedded + disk); script `scripts/patch_box_stability.py`; glslangValidator clean on both disk shaders; compileClientJava + build pass; jar redeployed 18:43 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260807-boxfix`). Expected: the next OUTPROBE burst should show the center texel holding flat like hand/sky across all jitter phases, matching the camStillExact=1 window.
- **Standing-still edge shake = box-clamp snap wobble, fixed by a lumaDiff content-alpha boost (2026-08-08, "nothing changed" report)**: the 17:02 session PROVED the exact path ran (camStillExact=1 on 72/97 samples, weights 15, lock 255, reactive 0.00, shader cache recompiled at 17:03) yet the shake persisted. The blend math was verified stable for ALIGNED history, so the wobble is on pixels where history is REJECTED (first-person hand, held items, disocclusion edges, cloud drift): there `lockTrusted=false` → `historyYCoCg` is 100% box-clamped, and the clamp box is built at the DE-JITTERED position `boxBase = floor(lrUvClamped·uRenderSize)` which SNAPS between adjacent render texels as the Halton jitter crosses half-texel boundaries → the clamped output toggles between two 3×3 neighborhoods = the blocky edge wobble that survives every weight/lock/mv=0 fix (mv=0 only helps pixels whose history ALIGNS; rejected pixels still go through the clamp). Fix (all 4 ACCUMULATE copies): `alpha = max(alpha, (lockTrusted ? 0.0 : smoothstep(0.2, 0.6, lumaDiff) * 0.85))` — when history doesn't match the current frame, pull in the DE-JITTERED FRESH input instead of the blocky clamp (the fresh is position-stable by construction: sampled at `lrUv = hrUv − uJitter/uRenderSize`). Terrain with aligned history has lumaDiff < 0.2 → boost 0 → bit-identical; the 0.85 cap keeps 15% clamp for stability. Also fixed two bugs from the uCamStillExact patch itself: a DUPLICATE `setCamStillExact` at FSR2 dispatch site 1 (the 12-space pattern matched a substring of the tab-indented line — the same collision class as the camReactive patch) and a MISSING call at the onRenderEnd fallback site (would have left uCamStillExact=0 → sky-only behavior on that path). **Instrumentation added**: `[OUTPROBE]` one-shot burst (~45 samples, every-other-frame) at fsr2FrameCount==300 reading the PRESENTED output texture (post-RCAS) at hand (W/2, 7H/8) / center / sky texels — next session's log shows whether the presented image oscillates with the jitter period (shake in the accumulate) or is flat (shake downstream/perceptual). **BURST-FIX (2026-08-08, 18:18 session logged only 1 sample)**: the counter decrement sat inside the parity check, so 90→89 left it stuck at an odd value (`(89 & 1) == 0` never true) — the burst died after the first read. The decrement now runs every frame (90→0), reading on even values (~45 samples over ~90 frames). Also verified the probe's first real output: `[OUTPROBE] f=300 jit=(-0.031,-0.376) hand=(177,183,200) low=(169,196,255) ctr=(170,176,195) sky=(162,188,252)` — the GL_FLOAT + barrier readback path is confirmed working (was all 0/-2147483648 before the fix). **OUTPROBE readback fixed (2026-08-08)**: the 17:45 session's probe reads were ALL `0` / `-2147483648` — `readTexPixels` float-decodes its buffer, so `GL_UNSIGNED_BYTE` data reinterpreted the packed RGBA word as a float (every other `readTexPixels` caller uses `GL_FLOAT`); plus no `glMemoryBarrier` before reading the compute-written output (stale/undefined data), no null-check (a failed readback printed garbage instead of failing), and no once-only arming guard (the counter's resets re-armed the burst ~20×). Now: `GL_ALL_BARRIER_BITS` barrier, `GL_RGBA`/`GL_FLOAT` reads scaled ×255, `READBACK FAILED` log on null, and arming guarded by `fsr23OutputProbeFrames == -1`. The 17:45 session itself confirmed the rejectDecay build was live (A-channel `clampReject` values only exist in the new shader) with healthy standing-still accumulation (lock 255, discard A=0, weight climbing to ~15) and the expected motion collapse (reactive+clampReject ≈0.82, fast-jitter amp 0.05 render px).
- **FSR2/3 unstable-smoothed-edges fix (2026-08-08, "the edges are smoothening but the smoothened edges are very unstable" report)**: the temporal accumulation now builds smooth edges, but they BOIL/POP at the Halton jitter frequency. Root cause: the lock-trust gate was a hard `lumaDiff < 0.2` BOOLEAN. At a reconstructed edge the accumulated history (time-averaged, soft) and the sharp current frame differ by the sub-pixel edge straddle, so lumaDiff oscillates around 0.2 as the jitter phase moves; each crossing flipped `histContribFactor` between 0 and `min(lockContrib, 0.70)` — the history CONTENT toggled between 70% raw (sharp accumulated edge) and 100% box-clamped (soft clipped edge) while alpha stayed ~unchanged, so the smoothed edge popped between two states every few frames. Fix (all 4 ACCUMULATE copies, embedded + disk): `lockTrusted` (bool) replaced by a continuous `lockTrust = lockActive * clamp(1.0 - smoothstep(0.10, 0.35, lumaDiff), 0, 1)` fade (`lockActive` is a float ternary over the sky/lock-life/luma gates), and the standing-still edge-shake alpha boost is applied on the complementary scale `alpha = max(alpha, (1.0 - lockTrust) * smoothstep(0.2, 0.6, lumaDiff) * 0.85)` — the blend state is now a continuous function of lumaDiff with no threshold discontinuity. Extremes preserved: diff ≤ 0.10 keeps the full 0.70 lock-cap raw blend (the 2026-08-07 smearing cap untouched); diff ≥ 0.35 fully disengages (the ~0.26 green-ghost diff is ~70% disengaged and still box-clamp-rejected). GLSL gotcha hit during the patch: `bool x = cond ? 1.0 : 0.0` is a compile error (float→bool has no implicit conversion in GLSL) and `bool * float` is invalid — `lockActive` must be a `float` ternary. Validated with `glslangValidator -S comp` on both disk copies. Knob: the 0.10–0.35 fade band (narrower = crisper edges but closer to the old pop; wider = smoother but softer edges).
- **FSR2/3 jagged-edge vibration fix (2026-08-08, "the jagged edges are vibrating" report — the follow-up to the smoothing fix)**: the ACCEPTED edges are now stable, but the NON-accumulating (jagged) edges vibrated. Root cause: the 2026-08-08 lumaDiff boost `alpha = max(alpha, (1.0 - lockTrust) * smoothstep(0.2, 0.6, lumaDiff) * 0.85)` modulated the fresh share with the PER-FRAME history-vs-current diff. At a non-accumulating edge (flower, held item, disocclusion, thin geometry) that diff oscillates with the Halton jitter phase — the history read wobbles across the edge so its luma swings between the two surfaces — sweeping the boost 0→0.85 every few frames → the output alternated between the box-clamped history color (background-ish) and the fresh color (flower) → the jagged edge flashed/vibrated. Fix (all 4 ACCUMULATE copies, embedded + disk): REMOVE the per-frame boost and drive the fresh share from a PERSISTENT clamp-rejection signal — `clampReject = clamp((lumaDiff - 0.25)/0.25, 0, 1)` (knee 0.25–0.5, aligned/sky diff ~0.1 → 0), `rejectDecay = 1.0 - 0.85*clampReject`, and the accumulation weight write becomes `newAccumWeight = min(adjAccum + weight, 15.0) * rejectDecay`. The weight is TIME-INTEGRATED, so the fresh share settles on a stable ~85% at persistently-mismatched pixels (steady state W = 0.167·0.15/0.85 ≈ 0.03 → alpha ≈ 0.85, reached within ~5 frames) instead of pulsing, and stays ~0% at aligned/sky pixels (their box-clamped accumulation is untouched). This is reference-FSR2-like behavior: alpha stays a stable function of the accumulated state, not the per-frame diff. Telemetry change (intended): the [ACCUM_WEIGHT] B channel now reads low at junk pixels by design — that IS the distrust signal. Behavior notes: the decay responds in ~5 frames on mismatch onset and rebuilds over ~1.5s on recovery (the standard accumulation ramp; the 2026-08-08 standing-still box-snap wobble stays suppressed because persistent-mismatch pixels hold ~85% fresh).
- **Hand jagged edges = camera-attached content gets world-anchored MVs, fixed with identity reprojection (2026-08-08, "the jagged edges are also effecting the hand" fix)**: with terrain/sky edges finally smooth, the FIRST-PERSON HAND stayed jagged — the documented "hand-jitter tradeoff" from the 2026-08-06 sky-de-jitter entry. Root cause (both paths): the hand is rendered in VIEW space (camera-attached) — its true screen position is FIXED under ALL camera motion (the model is transformed by the camera's view matrix, so translation AND rotation leave it screen-fixed; only its own sway/swing animation moves it). But both MV sources treat it as world-anchored: FSR2/3's constant-depth MV (z≈100) gives the hand the parallax of whatever world point it covers; FSR4's real-depth VK MV-gen unprojects/reprojects the hand's world position, which MOVES with the camera → prevUv never lands on the hand's previous screen position → the T&C auto-reactive fires every frame → the SDK/accumulate rejects the hand's history → it is always a FRESH bilinear upscale at 0.7 scale = jagged fist/held-item edges while everything else reconstructs. Fix — treat the hand like the camera-LOCKED sky gradient (identity reprojection is EXACT for it), content-gated so pose changes still fall back to fresh (no smear): (1) **FSR2/3 ACCUMULATE_CS (both pipelines, embedded)**: for bottom-center pixels (`screenYFactor < 0.34`, `|2x/W−1| < 0.96`, `uFrameIndex > 0`) ALSO read history at identity (`hrUv`) and switch to identity reprojection (`reprojUv = hrUv`, `mv = 0`, `isExisting = true`) when `idDiff + 0.12 < mvDiff` (identity clearly matches the current frame, the MV read clearly doesn't) — then `handIdentity` zeroes `reactiveFactor` (same line as the uCamStillExact override) so camReactive doesn't flush the hand during motion. The identity read is a duplicate `textureLod` on ~20% of the screen; the later lumaDiff/lockTrust/rejectDecay gates still protect mismatched history, and ground/walls caught in the zone (looking down) keep the MV path because identity mismatches during motion. Standing still (camStillExact=1) is bit-identical (identity == MV path already). (2) **FSR4 VK MV-gen (`fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`)**: `isHand = uv.y > 0.65 && |uv.x−0.5| < 0.45 && depth < 0.55` (bottom-center + real near depth within ~1 block — the ground at the bottom of a level view is 4+ blocks, so it stays out; non-reversed 0=near/1=far, and the 0.5↔~1-block calibration comes from the old constant-depth fallback comment) → force `mv = 0`, and a dedicated HAND POSE GATE branch in the T&C block (runs regardless of isSkyLike/noPrev): compare current vs PREVIOUS frame at IDENTITY (same pixel, 3x3 min-window tolerance for the jitter phase), `reactive = max(reactive, smoothstep(0.15, 0.45, minDiff) * 0.9)` — static pose → reactive 0 → the SDK accumulates in place and the fist edges reconstruct from the sub-pixel jitter samples; swing/sway → reactive ramps → fresh, no smear. GL-path FSR4 MV-gen (`generateFSR4MotionVectors` in FSRProcessor.java) NOT patched — it is dead under the user's VulkanMod config (onSceneRendered early-returns); follow-up if a GL-FSR4 session reports it. Validated: glslangValidator clean on both embedded ACCUMULATE_CS (extracted from the Java text blocks) and the .comp (`-V`); VK SPIR-V regenerated (11420 → 13612 bytes) + `fsr4_vk_mvgen_spv.h`; `compileClientJava` + `build` green; DLL rebuilt via `buildNative` and embedded in the jar. Expected: hand/held items smooth when idle/walking (accumulate like terrain), fresh only during actual swing/sway; knobs are the `0.34`/`0.45`/`0.55` mask bounds, the `0.12` identity-wins bias, and the `0.15–0.45` pose-gate band.
- **FSR4 sun/sky behavior RESTORED to the Aug 6 commit (2026-08-08, "restore the sun's behavior" — the user confirmed the Aug 6 look worked)**: the user remembered `f7a8682` (2026-08-06) having working smoothing for the sun and hand in FSR4. Verified against the commit: at Aug 6 the VK MV-gen had a force-reactive sky bucket — `depth >= 0.999` → `mv = 0` + `reactive = 1.0` + early return — so the SDK rendered the sky dome/sun/moon SPATIALLY every frame: stable, straight-edged, no ghosting (fresh render at the de-jittered position). That was the exact behavior the 08-08 sky saga (v1–v8, all uncommitted) removed: v1 started accumulating the sun with real-depth MVs, which fixed cloud aliasing but reintroduced the user's own complaints (slanted horizon, tilted/ghosted sun body, jagged cloud edges — each fixed in v2–v8). The hand at Aug 6 had NO MV-gen handling and looked smooth only when still (under VKMod it rendered unjittered while the SDK de-jittered, so its fixed content accumulated sub-pixel samples by coincidence). Fix: restored the early-return bucket in `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` EXACTLY as committed (same 3 imageStores + return), placed right after the depth clamp so it short-circuits before all v8 sky machinery; `isSkyLike`/camera-locked-gradient/white-body-exclusion/T&C-sky-guard now only govern CLOUDS (real depth < 0.999 — they keep the v8 gentle 32-px ramp + zero motionFloor + T&C skip and still accumulate like terrain), and the hand-zone identity fix (depth < 0.55) is untouched (also strictly better than Aug 6: it accumulates whenever the pose is static, not only when the camera is still). Kept the hand fix and the FSR2/3 ACCUMULATE hand-zone changes from the entry above; the GL-path FSR4 MV-gen in FSRProcessor.java was NOT changed (dead under VulkanMod — same scoping as the hand fix). Validated: glslangValidator `-V` clean; SPIR-V regenerated (13612 → 13940 bytes) + `fsr4_vk_mvgen_spv.h`; `build` green (DLL rebuilt via `buildNative`, jar 11:08). Expected: sun/sky/moon back to the stable, straight, ghost-free Aug 6 look (spatial fresh render — edges read blockier than terrain only at very low render scales, the original v1 tradeoff); clouds keep their v8 accumulated smoothing. If the user wants the sun accumulated again, revert = delete the restored bucket (v8 machinery below is intact).
- **PER-TICK NORMALIZATION of the motion signals (2026-08-07, the 16:23 session log proves both stages were dead)**: the 15:04 instrumentation build's log showed `camReactive=0.000` on 100/130 samples with `camReactive` at only 0.03–0.15 during clear motion bursts (frame 733: M=5–10 render px; 793: M=7–11; 973: M=10–18 — weights collapsed to the 0.18 floor) and **zero DIAG_FAST / FAST-JITTER lines all session** (both logs are also `config.debugMode > 0`-gated and debugMode=0). Root cause: a UNITS BUG — the camReactive floor `(dPosFrame−0.05)/0.55` and the fast stage's `dPosFrame > 0.12` gate were calibrated in PER-TICK space (walking 0.216 blocks/tick, sprint 0.28) but `dPosFrame` is the PER-RENDER-FRAME camera delta: at 60fps walking moves only 0.216/3 = **0.072 blocks/frame**, so walking yielded `(0.072−0.05)/0.55 = 0.04` instead of 0.30, and the fast stage's 0.12/frame = 0.36 blocks/tick threshold (faster than sprint) never fired at 60fps. Fix: measure the frame duration (`System.nanoTime()` in the FSR2/3 jitter block, new field `fsr23LastMotionNanos`, dt clamped to [0.001,0.25]s → fps clamped to [10,240]) and feed **`dPosTick = dPosFrame * fps/20`** into both stages, making them FPS-independent: walking 0.216/tick → floor 0.30 and fastFactor = (0.216−0.06)/0.30 = 0.52 (partial jitter kill — reviewer-corrected from /0.15: full kill at walking would regress edge reconstruction, since during walking history is NOT fully rejected (floor 0.30 < alpha-boost knee 0.3) so sub-pixel jitter still reconstructs edges; /0.30 keeps ~0.54 display px of spread at 45%); sprint 0.28/tick → 0.42/0.73; elytra 1.0/tick → 0.9 cap/1.0 full kill. Rotation terms stay PER-FRAME (dRotFrame — pans are depth-exact, the per-frame angular delta is the correct unit for screen motion). Also un-gated `[FAST-JITTER]` and `[DIAG_FAST]` from `config.debugMode` (they're already throttled to 120/60 frames) and added `dPosTick` to the DIAG_FAST line so the next session verifies engagement. The pair now works as designed together: floor shifts the blend toward fresh during motion, fast stage kills the jitter so the fresh share is stable = no shake; standing still both are 0 = full temporal reconstruction. The two entries above describing the old per-frame thresholds are superseded.
- **FSR2/3 scale-independent lock build (2026-08-06, "still wobbly + jagged edges" fix)**: log comparison across sessions pinned the mechanism — at 65% scale ACCUM_WEIGHT showed lock life R≈193-250 (raw, ≈22/30) and weight B≈2.0; at 25% scale (420×263 → 1680×1050) lock life was **0 at every sample** and weight dropped to 0.85. Root cause: ACCUMULATE's lock life is only ever REFRESHED by `newLock` — the LOCK_CS ridge (thin-geometry) detector — and otherwise decays. At low render scales thin geometry (grass/flowers/block edges) is sub-pixel, so LOCK_CS stops firing → lock life decays to 0 → `lockTrusted=false` → `histContribFactor=0` → `historyYCoCg = clampedHistory` (the history pulled into the CURRENT frame's 3×3 box) → the temporal filter degenerates to the current-frame neighborhood → the ±0.5 render px jitter shows through as wobble and no sub-pixel detail accumulates (jagged) — and it scales with 1/renderScale, matching the reports. Fix: in the lock lifecycle, stable accumulating frames now BUILD trust instead of only decaying — `else if (!isNewSample && isExisting && reactiveFactor < 0.4) { newLockLife = min(reproLockLife + 1.0, LOCK_LIFETIME); lockLuma = mix(lockLuma, upsampledYCoCg.x, 0.1); }` — a short warm-up then full original-history blend (vs the box-clamped fallback) at ANY render scale. Steady state is NOT 30: the stored R channel is scaled by `(1 - reactiveFactor*0.5)` before readback, so lock life saturates at `L = 2/reactive` (review 2026-08-06) — ≈10 at 25% (reactive 0.196), ≈24 at 65% (reactive 0.083, matching the observed R≈193-250 raw) — still sufficient since `lockContrib` saturates at `reproLockLife ≥ 3`; the real trust limiter is `lockLuma` convergence (~16 frames of the 0.1 mix to pass the `lumaDiff < 0.2` gate). The 0.4 threshold is the tunable knob: if lock life stays ≈0 at low scale (debug overlay mode 5), raise it toward 0.5 (lumaDiff is the real protection). The `lockLuma` mix (not hard-set) preserves the `lumaDiff < 0.2` trust gate's ability to catch content change/disocclusion; the `reactiveFactor < 0.4` gate prevents lock building during fast motion (ghosting guard). `velFactor` is NOT usable at the lock site (it is declared inside the isNewSample else-branch, out of scope at function-level lock block) — reactiveFactor only. Embedded + on-disk ACCUMULATE_CS both pipelines; FSR3 keeps its 0.04 decay, FSR2 its 1.0 decay. Expected: at 25-45% scale lock life recovers to ~30 and weight/edge smoothing behavior matches the 65% experience.
- **FSR2/3 sky de-jitter (2026-08-06, "still kind of shaking + jagged edges" fix)**: after the scale-invariance fix the accumulation recovered (ACCUM_WEIGHT 0.7 → 2.0, lock 200+) but residual shake + jagged edges persisted. Root cause: ACCUMULATE_CS forced `lrUv = hrUv` for sky/cloud/nearSky pixels — skipping the de-jitter (`lrUv = hrUv - uJitter/uRenderSize`) that every other pixel gets. Sky/clouds are `isNewSample` every frame (no accumulation), so the output WAS the raw jittered render: the sampled sky direction oscillated by the full per-frame Halton delta (±0.5 render px = ±0.77 display px at 65%) — the shake — and the fresh 65% render of sky/cloud edges stayed aliased — the jaggedness. The de-jitter sign/magnitude is proven correct because terrain (which uses it) is stable at weight 2.0. Fix: removed the override entirely (embedded + on-disk, both pipelines) — sky/clouds now sample through the same de-jittered lrUv; the nearSky horizon band must de-jitter too or it becomes a 1-px jittered seam between de-jittered regions. No accumulation change: sky stays fresh (rotation would ghost with constant-depth MVs), it is just sampled at the jitter-tracking position, so it renders as if unjittered. Expected: sky/horizon/clouds rock-stable; cloud edges still slightly aliased (inherent to a fresh 65% render) but no longer moving. Remaining jagged sources (inherent, not regressions): thin geometry (flowers/grass/leaves) at low render scale, and the jittered first-person hand (near depth, huge MVs → never accumulates → shows full jitter; the documented hand-jitter tradeoff).
- **Reversed-Z support**: `uFarDepth` uniform auto-detected from projection `m22` sign. All depth comparisons use `abs(d - uFarDepth)` instead of direct comparison.
- **Diagnostic overlay** (`debugMode` config 0-6): sky classification, depth heat map, motion vectors, isNewSample, lock status, accum weight. RCAS skipped when debug active.
- **32x1 work groups**: ACCUMULATE_CS and RCAS in FSR2/3 pipelines use 32x1. FSR1 RCAS uses 16x16 workgroups with shared memory tile for better GPU occupancy.
- **texelFetch for intermediates**: ACCUMULATE_CS uses `texelFetch()` (nearest) for all intermediate buffer reads. History texture retains `textureLod` for sub-pixel temporal blending.

## Gotchas
- **`mv * uDisplaySize` FIXED (2026-08-06, FSR2/FSR3 accumulate)**: `mvPx = length(mv * uDisplaySize)` measured the velocity in display pixels while `mv` is render-UV — at 75% scale that read 33% high, inflating `velFactor` → `confidence` → tighter ellipsoid clamp → LESS temporal smoothing → more shimmer. Now `length(mv * uRenderSize)` (render pixels, matching the depth-clip pass's `nucleusVel` and FSR2's reference calibration). Verified scale-invariant math: a render-UV delta and a display-UV delta are the SAME number under uniform upscale (render pixel p → display pixel p·D/R; UV delta p/R equals (p·D/R)/D), so reprojection itself needs no conversion.
- **`reprojUv = hrUv + mv` is CORRECT (2026-08-06 correction)**: the historical "mixes display UV + render UV" note was misleading for this MERGED display-res accumulate — `mv` is a render-UV *delta*, and normalized UV deltas are scale-invariant under uniform upscale (a 0.05 render-UV delta = 0.05 display-UV delta), so adding it to the display-UV `hrUv` reprojects correctly at any scale. Do NOT "fix" it by multiplying by `uDisplaySize/uRenderSize` — that over-scales the offset by the upscale ratio and misaligns history.
- **GUI/HUD rendering at native resolution**: `renderLevel(TAIL)` injection calls `onSceneRendered()` which runs the FSR upscale on the 3D scene only, copies the result to `mainRenderTarget`, and sets it as the active target. GUI then renders at full native resolution.
- **Screens render behind FSR (2026-08-04)**: `onRenderBegin` no longer early-returns when `client.screen != null` — the world renders behind chat/inventory/pause/options every frame, and every GUI pass (HUD, chat, screens) composites on top of the upscaled scene at native res. Skipping screens made the world render at native res while chatting (no render scaling → resolution pop + iGPU load). Only `client.level == null` skips (title/loading screens). Safety net: `worldRenderedThisFrame` flag (set at `onSceneRendered` entry) makes `onRenderEnd` skip the fallback upscale if the world didn't render (dimension-change transition). FG_DX12 double-present still skips the second present while a screen is open (intentional). In FG mode, `onSceneRendered()` additionally dispatches frame generation, copies FG output to `mainRenderTarget` BEFORE GUI rendering — so GUI renders on top of the FG warped frame. No early swap.
- `restoreDefaultTarget()` required on every early-return path in `onRenderBegin`/`onRenderEnd` to prevent stale render target state
- **FSR2 lock B stored `adjAccum / 15.0` (wrong)**: creates zero-trap
- **Jitter cancellation DOUBLE-COUNT — the standing-still wobble/smear root cause (2026-08-07, "smearing + shaking + jagged edges" fix)**: RECONSTRUCT already subtracts the jitter delta (`mv = rawMv·uMotionVectorScale − uMotionVectorJitterCancellation` → `uDilatedData.rg` = TRUE content motion — this is exactly why the DEPTH_CLIP reactive fix worked). ACCUMULATE then read that already-cancelled MV and ADDED the cancellation back (`reprojUv = hrUv + mv + uMotionVectorJitterCancellation`), a DOUBLE-CANCEL: the history read wobbled by `(J_prev−J_curr)/renderSize` every frame even standing still — up to ±3–4 display px at 4× upscale — so the box clamp rejected misaligned history at edges (jagged/shake) and loosely blended it on smooth surfaces (smear), surviving every weight/lock fix that came before. The 2026-08-06 "restore" misdiagnosed it: the real baseline bug was `mv / vec2(uRenderSize)` (dividing an already-render-UV MV by render size again), and the "fix" conflated fixing the units with needing the cancel term. Correct line: `reprojUv = hrUv + mv;` (history is written de-jittered via `lrUv = hrUv − uJitter/uRenderSize`, so true-motion reprojection is exact). All 4 copies (fsr2/fsr3 embedded + disk). — `adjAccum` = `accumWeight - decay` = `(prevAdjAccum / 15.0 * 15.0) - decay` = `prevAdjAccum - decay`. Since it starts at 0, it stays 0. Fix: store `newAccumWeight / 15.0` where `newAccumWeight = adjAccum + upsampledWeight`. FSR3 always did this correctly.
- **FSR1 EASU is the ONLY graphics pass in the mod** — must force clean GL state. `drawEasu()` runs a fullscreen `glDrawArrays` triangle, unlike FSR2/3/RCAS which are compute dispatches (immune to GL state). If GL_BLEND/GL_SCISSOR are left enabled by the world pass, the triangle blends/clips over the previous frame's destination content → "decaying"/smearing artifact. `drawEasu` saves blend/scissor/cull/depth-test/depth-mask/color-mask, disables them, draws, and restores in a `finally`. Color mask queried via `glGetInteger(GL_COLOR_WRITEMASK)` (packed bits — `glGetBoolean` would be a 4-value OOB write in LWJGL). `blitTex` (glBlitFramebuffer) and RCAS (compute) need no such fix.
- **`glClearTexImage` for per-frame clears (FSR2/3)**: `reconstructedPrevDepthTex` + `newLocksTex` are cleared with a single `glClearTexImage` (GL 4.4+) instead of the old bind-FBO → attach → `glClearBuffer` → unbind dance (~6 driver calls saved per clear, per frame, per pipeline).
- GL_RG8 (not GL_R8) for shading change detection in FSR3 (G channel = luminance ratio diff)
- Lock status texture is GL_RGBA8 (R=lock life, G=lock luma, B=accum weight, A=unused)
- **FSR1 upscale**: `glBlitFramebuffer` (GL_LINEAR) for bilinear upscale, or optional EASU compute shader (12-tap edge-adaptive Lanczos, ~12 tex fetches/pixel) enabled via `config.fsr1Easu` toggle. After upscale, RCAS sharpen runs as compute shader (32x1 workgroups).
- **RCAS output routing (2026-08-04)**: RCAS writes to our OWN RGBA8 texture (`rcasOutTex`), then a fixed-function 1:1 `glBlitFramebuffer(GL_NEAREST)` copies it to the caller's output texture. Writing the caller's output directly via `glBindImageTexture(..., GL_RGBA8)` was fragile — a format mismatch (RGBA8 vs RGBA16F) or a feedback-loop class conflict (output attached to a bound FBO) produced GL_INVALID_OPERATION (0x0502) at dispatch time on strict drivers (AMD 860M), permanently disabling EASU. The blit converts to any output format natively.
- **Jitter zeroed for the FSR1 spatial fallback (2026-08-04)**: when `spatialFallbackActive()` (FSR1 configured, DH active, FSR4 suspended by the GPU-saturation guard, or FSR4/FG selected but not initialized), `onRenderBegin` zeroes jitterX/jitterY. FSR1 has NO de-jitter pass — a jittered render would shift ±0.5px/frame on the output (the standing-still shake that appeared exactly when FSR1 took over after an FSR4 suspension). Shared helper so the zero-jitter site and the FSR1 dispatch site can't drift.
- **Hand projection jitter (2026-08-04)**: In 26.1.2 the first-person hand renders INSIDE `renderLevel` (after the world) but uploads its projection through a DIFFERENT path than the world — `hud3dProjectionMatrixBuffer.getBuffer(Projection)` (native-sized `hudProjection`) instead of `levelProjectionMatrixBuffer.getBuffer(Matrix4f)` (which `captureAndJitter` jitters). The hand was therefore rendered UNJITTERED while the world shifted ±0.5px/frame → FSR's temporal reconstruction couldn't align the hand region with history → SDK discarded it → raw spatial upscale showed through as jagged fist/held-item edges while terrain stayed smooth. Fix: `GameRendererMixin.jitterHandProjection` `@Redirect` on `ProjectionMatrixBuffer.getBuffer(Projection)` in `renderLevel` — builds the matrix via `Projection.getMatrix`, applies the same clip-space translation jitter (`FSRProcessor.jitterHandProjection`, which shares `clipSpaceJitter()` with `captureAndJitter` so the two can't drift), and uploads via the `Matrix4f` overload (bypasses the version cache; next `getBuffer(Projection)` call rebuilds clean, so GUI/ortho uploads are unaffected). Deliberately does NOT touch `jitterAppliedThisFrame` (the world's guard, already set by hand-upload time). Spatial fallback passes through unchanged (jitter zeroed → `clipSpaceJitter()` returns null). Known limitation (not a regression): hand bob/swing animation motion isn't in the MV field, so minor smear during swing is expected — inherent to FSR temporal integration.
- **FSR1 EASU is a GRAPHICS pass** (glDrawArrays fullscreen triangle) — the only one in the mod besides RCAS-via-blit. Must force clean GL state (blend/scissor/cull/depth/colorMask off) before drawing and restore in a finally, or the triangle blends over the previous frame ("decaying" artifact). Use `glGetInteger(GL_COLOR_WRITEMASK)` (packed bits) not `glGetBoolean` for the mask query — the 4-value boolean query would write past LWJGL's 1-element buffer (heap corruption).
- **FSR1 RCAS feedback-loop fix**: after blit/EASU draws into `intermediateTex`, it stays attached to the draw FBO — RCAS then samples it while it's attached to the CURRENT draw framebuffer → GL_INVALID_OPERATION (0x0502) every frame on strict drivers (AMD). Fix: `glBindFramebuffer(GL_FRAMEBUFFER, 0)` between the upscale pass and the RCAS dispatch. Bare gradle client passed by luck; modded clients (Sodium etc.) failed every frame.
- **FSR1 auto-fallback**: if the EASU graphics draw errors (external mod GL-state conflict), `easuErrorsDetected` permanently switches to bilinear blit + RCAS for the session. GL errors are drained per-stage with correct hex formatting (C#-style `{:X}` in SLF4J shifts args and hides the stage).
- **Swap approach (non-Iris)**: `getMainRenderTarget()` is swapped to a `ScaledRenderTarget` proxy that wraps our scaled textures but reports native dimensions (for Voxy's compute dispatch sizing). After scene renders, FSR upscales to native, restores mainRT, GUI renders at full resolution.
- **Monitor-move resize fix (2026-08-06, "UI cut off except bottom-left" bug)**: Minecraft's own resize path (`onResolutionChanged` → `mainRenderTarget.resize(w,h)`) can land on the `ScaledRenderTarget` proxy while it is swapped into the `mainRenderTarget` field (window dragged to another monitor — different DPI/resolution fires a framebuffer-size callback mid-session). The proxy's `resize()` was a NO-OP, so the REAL target kept its old size while the window was at the new size → GUI rendered at the new window size into a stale-size texture → clipped except bottom-left. Fix (two layers): (1) `ScaledRenderTarget` now holds a `delegate` reference to the real main render target (passed at construction from `originalMainRenderTarget`) and its `resize()` FORWARDS to it — so MC's resize through the proxy resizes the real target; (2) `FSRProcessor.onResize` explicitly resizes `originalMainRenderTarget` to the new window size when its dimensions are stale (belt-and-suspenders for any path where the proxy wasn't in the field; harmless no-op if MC already resized it). Proxy's own dimensions remain FSRProcessor-managed (unchanged). Also: `onResize` now early-returns on `width<=0 || height<=0` — GLFW fires 0×0 framebuffer callbacks on minimize/restore and the WindowMixin is `@At(TAIL)` so it fires regardless of MC's internal 0-dim guard; previously a 0×0 event zeroed `displayWidth/Height` (broken pipeline math) and would now also resize the real GL target to 0×0.
- **`glClearBufferuiv` needs 4 elements**: `glClearBufferuiv(GL_COLOR, 0, new int[]{0})` throws `IllegalArgumentException: must be at least 4` — LWJGL treats `uiv` as RGBA unsigned int, so pass `new int[]{0, 0, 0, 0}`. Same as `glClearBufferfv` which correctly used `float[]{0,0,0,0}`.
- **`glClearTexImage` MUST pass a NULL data pointer (2026-08-06, atio6axx.dll 0xC0000005 at world-join on the ultrawide)**: a NON-null `ByteBuffer` makes the driver treat the pointer as a `GL_PIXEL_UNPACK_BUFFER` offset. At world-load Minecraft binds a PBO for chunk texture uploads, so the heap address of a `ByteBuffer.wrap(...)` becomes a garbage offset → `EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION` (NULL+0x10) inside `atio6axx.dll` on the very first FSR4 frame's `ensureFsr4Staging` (crash stack: `GL44C.glClearTexImage → ensureFsr4Staging → copyDepthToShared → onSceneRendered`). The T&C `fsr4PrevColorTex` init clear was the first data-bearing call in the mod and crashed instantly; ALL `glClearTexImage` calls must pass `null` (clear-to-zero is fine when the texture is fully overwritten before first use — the T&C prev-color is populated by the per-frame copy before `uTCActive` ever gates it on).
- **CRITICAL: viewport must be explicitly set to scaled resolution** — `ScaledRenderTarget` reports `width = displayWidth`, so Minecraft's viewport setup uses full resolution. Without explicit `glViewport` override in `onRenderBegin`, the scene renders at native resolution (same pixel count as unmodded) and GPU clips to the smaller texture — zero performance gain from render scaling, only FSR compute overhead. Fix: `glViewport(0, 0, scaledW, scaledH)` in `onRenderBegin` (after proxy swap), restored to `displayW, displayH` in `onSceneRendered`.
- **Iris path (no swap)**: Iris creates its own G-buffers at window-reported FB size. No mainRT swap. If an external render-scale mod reduced resolution, FSR1 upscales via blit+RCAS. Otherwise just RCAS sharpen. Jitter disabled.
- **32x1 work group bounds**: RCAS dispatches as `(dstW+31)/32, dstH`. ACCUMULATE dispatches as `(renderW+31)/32, renderH`.
- **Embedded SPIR-V size fields are BYTES, not words (2026-08-06)**: `kFsr4VkMvGenSpvSize` was once generated as the word count (1197) instead of bytes (1197×4=4788). `vkCreateShaderModule` rejects a non-word-aligned code size with -3 (VK_ERROR_INVALID_SHADER_NV) — silent in production as "pipeline not ready — skipping" every frame (reads as a quality bug, not a crash). Any `.comp` → `.spv` → `.h` regeneration must write `words*4`. The real-depth MV-gen was disabled by exactly this for a whole round of "still jagged water" reports.
- **VKMod real-depth readback probe (2026-08-06)**: one-shot `vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer` of the center 16×16 of the MV-gen STAGING depth into a HOST_VISIBLE buffer; consumed next frame after the PREVIOUS frame's fence signals (mixin stores the fence AFTER recordVkModMvGen, so `g_vkModFrameFence` during the next call is fence_N — even if VulkanMod resets the fence mid-frame, frame N+1 completion implies frame N completion, so the wait is safe). Logs `DEPTH READBACK` 9 samples — proves the depth convention reaches the SDK. Terrain≈0.001/sky≈1.0 = non-reversed correct; all≈0.5 = dummy bound (real depth never set); terrain≈1.0/sky≈0 = REVERSED-Z, needs `ndcZ = 1.0 - depth*2.0` in `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`. All failure branches latch `g_vkModDepthReadbackDone=true` (no per-frame retry/log spam) and `vkBindBufferMemory` is checked (unbound allocation would map garbage as if real depth).
- **VKMod real-depth is SILENTLY DISABLED by the native no-op (2026-08-06)**: the first readback run showed ALL 9 samples = 0.500000 — the 1×1 dummy is bound, not the framebuffer depth. Root cause is NOT the shader (compiles + dispatches fine after the byte-count fix) — it's `setVkModDepthSource`: when Java passes `(0L, 0)` (depthImg null / getId()==0 / mixin catch fired), the native early-return `newImage(0) == boundImage(0)` returns with NO log, forever. No "undefined depth format", no view error — pure silence. One-shot diagnostics added to pin it: Java `[VKMod] depthImg id=... fmt=... | NULL | threw`, native `setVkModDepthSource: image=... fmt=... bound=... (first call)` + `rebinding to image=...`. Next run will tell whether the scaled framebuffer's `getDepthAttachment()` is null, the mixin catch fired, or `getId()` isn't a raw VkImage. See `VULKANMOD_FSR4_FINDINGS.md` "DUMMY IS BOUND".
- **Cloud detection in DEPTH_CLIP_CS**: far-plane + bright desaturated color → `cloudReactive` blended via `max()` into `reactiveMask`. Suppresses temporal blending on semi-transparent, independently-moving cloud pixels. Same heuristic in both FSR2Pipeline and FSR3Pipeline.

## Frame Generation DX12 (FSR3_FG_DX12 mode) — WIRED ✅
- **Recovered from git history** (`a0c2e78`): `fg4_context.h/.cpp` implements FSR3 FG via `amd_fidelityfx_framegeneration_dx12.dll` v4.0.1 (unified FFX API 2.x) — `ffxCreateContext` (frame gen → backend DX12 → version chain), `ffxConfigure` (no-swapchain mode, `FFX_FRAMEGENERATION_FLAG_NO_SWAPCHAIN_CONTEXT_NOTIFY`), then two-phase dispatch: `PrepareV2` (depth+MV+camera) + `FrameGeneration` (presentColor → outputs[0]). Dedicated command allocator/list/fence so the FG SDK never shares the D3D12Device round-robin allocator with FSR4 (SDK caches internal refs — sharing caused 0xc0000005). Crash recovery: consecutive-crash counter → reinitialize() or permanent disable (MAX=5).
- **Header**: `third_party/AMD-FSR-API/include/ffx_framegeneration.h` (new) — exact ABI of the official SDK header: effect id 0x00020000, CREATE=0x01, CONFIGURE=0x02, DISPATCH=0x03, PREPARE_V2=0x0c, VERSION=0x0e; `FfxApiRect2D` uses x/y/width/height (layout-identical to official left/top/width/height); version 4.0.1.
- **Pool**: D3D12SharedResources now has 6 textures — `FG_OUTPUT=5` (display-size RGBA8) added. FSR4 upscales scene → pool[4] OUTPUT; FG reads pool[4] as presentColor + pool[1] depth + pool[2] MV, writes the interpolated frame to pool[5].
- **JNI**: `Fg4Native` (5 methods) bound in jni_bridge.cpp. `nativeInit(renderW,renderH,dispW,dispH,poolPtr,colorIdx=4,depthIdx=1,mvIdx=2,outputIdx=5,dllDir)`; `nativePrepare(frameID, frameTimeDeltaNs→ms, jitterX/Y, near, far, fovYRad, viewSpaceToMeters, camPos/up/right/forward, reset)`; `nativeGenerate`, `nativeFinish`, `nativeShutdown`.
- **Wiring** (FSRProcessor): FG init after FSR4 init in both init paths (skipped under VulkanMod — its dispatch block is isFsr4()-only). Per frame in onSceneRendered: FSR4 dispatch → waitIdle → FG prepare+generate → `fgFlipState` toggle → generated frames copy pool[5] (`copyFgOutputToMainRT`), real frames copy pool[4]. Consecutive-failure counter (3) disables FG; resets on success.
- **FSR4 failure fallback**: FSR1 fallback condition now covers `isFrameGenDx12()` too (`(isFsr4() || isFrameGenDx12()) && !fsr4Initialized`).
- **Removed dead bypass**: the old `g_bypassFsr4` CopyResource flag was invalid (CopyResource needs identical dims; color is render-size, output display-size) and is gone. FSR4 runs its real temporal upscale; FG interpolates that.

## Diagnostics logging gating
- **Native `diagLog` gated behind a global flag** (`diag.h` `g_diagnosticsEnabled`): all three impls (jni_bridge.cpp, fsr4_context.cpp, fg4_context.cpp) are no-ops unless enabled. Java calls `Fsr4Native.setDiagnosticsEnabled(config.debugMode > 0)` at init + `syncNativeDiagnostics()` on config change. When `debugMode == 0`, **all** per-frame native + `[DIAG]`/`[PERF]`/`[DIAG_FG]` Java logs are silent — no per-frame file I/O (`fopen`/`fwrite`/`fflush` per call was 53k lines/session before this fix).
- **Per-frame copy logs throttled**: `recordFrameCopyOps` (Copy1/Copy2) + `waitForFrameCopy` log only every 128th frame or on slow waits (≥6ms).
- `shouldDebug()` readback block runs every 600 frames (~10s), and only when debug mode is on.
- **FSR2/3 `logAccumWeight` + OUTPROBE gated on debugMode (2026-08-08, "anything above FSR1 is just making me lose frames" fix)**: the `[ACCUM_WEIGHT]` readback ran EVERY 60 FRAMES UNCONDITIONALLY in both FSR2Pipeline and FSR3Pipeline (`if (diagCounter++ % 60 == 0) logAccumWeight(...)`) — each call does a full `glMemoryBarrier(GL_ALL_BARRIER_BITS)` + 27 `glReadPixels` across 3 FBO passes (lock status, history color, MV+reactive), every read a render-thread stall until the GPU drains. On the user's 3440x1440 ultrawide iGPU session that was a visible hitch every second on every FSR2/3 session, plus unconditional log spam — a real contributor to the frame-loss report. Fix: `if (debugMode > 0 && diagCounter++ % 60 == 0)` in both pipelines (the `debugMode` field is refreshed every frame via `setDebugMode(config.debugMode)` at the dispatch sites). Same class of fix applied to the `[OUTPROBE]` burst (FSRProcessor onRenderBegin): its arming `fsr2FrameCount == 300` is now `config.debugMode > 0 && fsr2FrameCount == 300` — the 45-sample burst (each sample a full GL_ALL_BARRIER_BITS + 4 readbacks) is pure diagnostics from the completed 2026-08-08 standing-still-shake investigation and must never run in production. The functional `probeFsr4Depth` (2 reads/session, gates `uUseDepth` for all three pipelines) is intentionally left ungated — it is runtime logic, not diagnostics. Jar redeployed 19:47 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260807-1947-perf`).

## VulkanMod sync fix (2026-08-05 v7) — no more per-frame GPU drain
- **`waitForFrameCopy()` is now a BOUNDED per-frame-fence wait, not `vkQueueWaitIdle`.** VulkanMod's `Renderer` exposes per-frame submission fences (`inFlightFences` + static `getCurrentFrame()`); the main-pass mixin grabs `inFlightFences[getCurrentFrame()]` via reflection and stores it via `VulkanInterop.storeFrameFence`. Native `waitForFrameCopyBounded` waits on that fence (`vkWaitForFences`, 8ms cap + one 33ms extended re-wait per timeout episode) — only THIS frame's submission (scene render + our Copy1/Copy2) needs to complete before the D3D12 dispatch reads the input. Timeout → returns JNI_FALSE → Java skips the dispatch that frame (display keeps the previous FSR4 output) instead of blocking the CPU on an unbounded queue drain. No fence stored (reflection failure) → falls back to the old `vkQueueWaitIdle`.
- **D3D12 wait moved from mid-frame to the copy mixin (GPU overlap).** `Fsr4Native.waitIdle()` was removed from the onRenderBegin dispatch block; the D3D12 dispatch is submitted async and runs on the GPU WHILE the scene renders. `VulkanInterop.waitForD3D12Output()` (called from `VulkanModMainPassMixin` right before it records Copy2 output→swapchain) waits on the highest fence value ever signaled on either round-robin allocator — by then the dispatch has overlapped the entire scene render, so the wait is ~0ms. This is the VKMod equivalent of the GL path's `fsr4Async` overlap.
- **VKMod FSR-disable freeze (2026-08-06)**: `renderScaleConfigured()` didn't check `config.enabled`, so FSR-off left the scaled framebuffer live while the pipeline stopped dispatching → Copy2 kept presenting the STALE last FSR4 output → frozen display. Fix: disabled path in `onRenderBegin` (VKMod-FSR4 only, gated on `isActive()`) does `shutdownFsr4()` + `fsr4VkModActive=false` and schedules the swapchain recreate back to native (gated on new `renderScaleSwapped()` = enabled-INDEPENDENT check); `renderScaleConfigured()` now includes `cfg.enabled`; the end() hook outer gate is `renderScaleSwapped()` with the inner `renderScaleActive()` gate removed (branches self-guard via src!=dst); the state tracker adds `enabledChanged` so RE-ENABLE schedules the recreate back to scaled.
- **VKMod REAL per-pixel depth (2026-08-06, "jagged water" fix)**: the constant-depth (`ndcZ=0` ≈ 1-block) MV assumption gave distant water ~100x wrong parallax during translation → SDK rejected history → aliased water. VulkanMod keeps a real depth buffer (Framebuffer.getDepthAttachment(), usage=39 incl. SAMPLED, render pass leaves it DEPTH_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL — DEPTH.defaultLayout=3 verified by bytecode), so the VK MV-gen now samples it (binding 3 combined image sampler): `ndcZ = depth*2-1` (non-reversed, matches the SDK's non-inverted expectation), real depth written to the pool R32F, `depth>=0.999` → sky (mv=0, reactive=1, GL parity). Native: 4th descriptor binding + nearest sampler + 1x1 R32F dummy (0.5) fallback + `setVkModDepthSource` JNI (DEPTH-aspect view on image change) + depth barriers around the dispatch. **Bound-image tracking** (`g_vkModDepthSrcBoundImage`): setVkModDepthSource no-ops only when the image is already BOUND; ensureVkModMvGenPipeline rebinds the real depth after every descriptor-set rebuild — otherwise a pool rebuild strands the binding on the dummy forever (constant depth silently returns after version-switch / disable→enable). GL path is unchanged (no depth available from GpuDevice).
- **VKMod MV-gen must use the REAL captured projection, not the rebuild (2026-08-06, "shake + pixelated sun" root cause)**: the FSR4 dispatch derives near/far from `lastProj` at `onRenderBegin` — `captureAndJitter` (the `@ModifyArg` DOES fire under VulkanMod) set it to the REAL render projection (`near=0.50 far=40920.04` in diag log). But the VKMod `onSceneRendered` block unconditionally called `updateProjectionFromCamera()` FIRST, overwriting `lastProj` with a rebuilt matrix (`near=0.05, far=renderDist*64≈768`) before the MV-gen read it. Constant-depth (`ndcZ=0`) MV-gen maps to view-space mid-depth z≈2·n·f/(n+f): ≈1.0 real vs ≈0.1 rebuilt — **10× parallax error** → translation MVs ~10× too large → reactive (`smoothstep(0.5,16,mvLen)`) saturated ≈1 everywhere → SDK discarded ALL history → spatial-only output (jittered input shows through = shake; no temporal AA = pixelated sun). Fix: `isSaneProjection()` (near∈(0,1000], far>near, no NaN; far deliberately unbounded — VulkanMod's infiniteZFar gives far→+Inf which barely affects ndcZ=0) gates the rebuild; MV-gen now uses the real capture, rebuild only for garbage (`far=-986895.06`). Dispatch + MV-gen read the SAME matrix. One-shot INFO logs verify: `VKMod MV-gen projection: ... (captured real render capture)` and `VKMod projection override: applied jitter=...` (proves the jitter override fires). Known limitation: sun/sky still gets constant-depth MVs during translation (infinite-depth ≈ 0 parallax vs mid-depth assumption) → reactive can still fire on the sky; candidate fix = color-based sky guard in the VK MV-gen (see VULKANMOD_FSR4_FINDINGS.md).
- Old `waitForFrameCopy` (queue-drain version) is left in jni_bridge.cpp as dead code / documented fallback logic.

## VulkanMod render-scale POC (2026-08-05 v8) — qualityScale now works under VulkanMod
- **Problem**: the VulkanMod path ignored `qualityScale` — `onRenderBegin` hard-coded `renderW = displayW`, so FSR4 inited 1:1 (`1920x1201 -> 1920x1201` at 25%) and the D3D12 pipeline-test CopyResource was a legal-but-invisible no-op. VulkanMod 0.6.8 has NO render scale of its own: `DefaultMainPass.createResources()` sets `mainFramebuffer = swapChain` and the world renders straight into the swapchain images.
- **Fix**: `config.vulkanModRenderScale` (default ON) + a `DefaultMainPass.createResources()` mixin that replaces `mainFramebuffer` with a **scaled-size Framebuffer** (`Framebuffer.builder(scaledW, scaledH, 1, true).setFormat(swapChain.getFormat()).build()`) right after the vanilla PUTFIELD and BEFORE `createRenderPasses()`/`createAttachmentTextures()` run — so the render pass, attachments, and viewport **auto-derive** from the scaled framebuffer. The else-branch of `createAttachmentTextures()` already exists for non-swapchain framebuffers (it builds 1 color + depth GpuTexture from the framebuffer's own images).
- **Blit**: new native `recordRenderScaleBlit` (jni_bridge.cpp) records into the SAME command buffer in the `end()` mixin (before `vkEndCommandBuffer`): src COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL→TRANSFER_SRC, dst UNDEFINED→TRANSFER_DST (full overwrite), `vkCmdBlitImage` with VK_FILTER_LINEAR, dst TRANSFER_DST→PRESENT_SRC_KHR, src TRANSFER_SRC→COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL (next frame's render pass). Layout constants verified against VulkanMod 0.6.8 bytecode: `RenderPass$AttachmentInfo$Type.COLOR.defaultLayout = 2` (COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL), and vanilla `end()` only does the PRESENT transition `if (mainFramebuffer == swapChain)` — skipped in render-scale mode, so no double-transition. The render pass leaves the scaled color in COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL, matching the blit's src oldLayout.
- **GUI renders at scaled res** (HUD draws into whatever's bound = the scaled main framebuffer) — the accepted POC tradeoff; the bilinear blit upscales it. Only the world gets the spatial upscale; no temporal FSR on this path.
- **FSR4 D3D12 skipped while render-scale active** (`!vkRenderScale && (isFsr4() || isFrameGenDx12())`): the blit IS the upscaler, and the pipeline-test CopyResource can't resize (input≠output). The fallback log line now distinguishes render-scale mode from plain pass-through. The FSR4 **dispatch block** in onSceneRendered is also gated on `!VulkanModCompat.renderScaleActive()` so a mid-session settings toggle (scale OFF→ON after FSR4 initialized) doesn't keep dispatching useless D3D12 work whose output is never presented.
- **No manual framebuffer cleanup in the mixin** — vanilla `createResources()` ALREADY calls `mainFramebuffer.cleanUp(true)` at the top when `mainFramebuffer != swapChain` (bytecode-verified), so our scaled fb is freed on every resize re-create. Adding our own `cleanUp()` would DOUBLE-FREE the same VkImage (UB/crash).
- **`vkRenderScale` declared at method scope** (not inside the isLoaded() block) because the isActive() block below also reads it — Java scoping.
- **Render-scale state set for ANY loaded VulkanMod** (`isLoaded()`, not `isActive()`): the scaled-framebuffer + blit path is a pure Vulkan operation needing no external-memory/D3D12 interop, so it must not be gated behind the FSR4 interop check. `isActive()` remains the gate for the FSR4 D3D12 path only.
- **CRITICAL TIMING FIX (2026-08-05, "identical to regular Minecraft" bug)**: the scaled-framebuffer swap silently never happened — the world rendered at native res and the end() hook did a 1:1 self-blit of the swapchain image (visually identical to vanilla). Root cause: `VulkanModCompat.renderScale` (the static read by the mixin's `renderScaleActive()`) was only set per-frame in `onRenderBegin`, but `DefaultMainPass.createResources()` runs during VulkanMod's `Renderer.init()` (VkGpuDevice ctor → VRenderSystem.initRenderer) — BEFORE the first frame callback — so the static was still the 1.0 default and the mixin bailed. `createResources` only re-runs on window resize, so the swap never got a second chance. Fix: `VulkanModCompat.getRenderScale()` now reads the LIVE config (`FSRMod.getInstance().getConfig()`, loaded at mod init before any rendering — order-independent) and only falls back to the static.
- **IN-GAME-ONLY GATE (2026-08-05, "EVERYTHING is upscaled including the title screen" bug)**: render scale must apply ONLY to the 3D world — menus, title screen, world-select GUI must render native. VulkanMod's DefaultMainPass renders EVERYTHING (GUI + world + title panorama), so a swap active at the title screen makes the whole UI render at 480x300. Fix: `renderScaleActive()` now = configured AND `Minecraft.getInstance().level != null` (in-game). `onRenderBegin` detects the in-game state flip (title ↔ world) and calls `VulkanModCompat.scheduleRenderScaleUpdate()` → `Renderer.scheduleSwapChainUpdate()` (public static) → `recreateSwapChain()` → `mainPass.onResize()` → `createResources()` → the mixin FIELD hook re-evaluates the scaled-framebuffer swap with the new state at the NEXT frame start (one native frame at world-join, then scaled). The mixin `end()` hook gates on `renderScaleConfigured()` (not `renderScaleActive()`): when configured but no world loaded, the framebuffer is the native swapchain and vanilla end() PRESENT handles it — must NOT fall through to the D3D12 FSR4 copy path. FSR4 init is gated on `!renderScaleConfigured()` (not `!vkRenderScale`, which is now in-game-gated and false at the title screen).
- **SELF-BLIT GUARD in the end() blit (review fix)**: on the world-join transitional frame the flip was just detected but the swapchain recreate lands at the NEXT frame's beginFrame — so mainFramebuffer is STILL the swapchain while renderScaleActive() is already true. Blitting src==dst==the same swapchain image is a self-blit with contradictory barriers (COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL→TRANSFER_SRC then UNDEFINED→TRANSFER_DST on the same image) that discards the fresh frame → 1-frame garbage at world join. The blit now only runs when src dimensions differ from dst (mirrors the createResources hook's scaledW==w && scaledH==h early-return).
- **Flip-recreate gated on renderScaleConfigured() (review fix)**: without it, FSR4-under-VulkanMod users (render scale off) would get a full recreateSwapChain + waitIdle GPU drain at EVERY world enter/exit for no benefit.
- **QUALITY SLIDER FIX (2026-08-05, "the quality slider did nothing" bug)**: moving the slider only updated `config.qualityScale` — nothing re-ran `createResources`, so the framebuffer stayed at the old scale while the end() hook switched from blit to the D3D12 FSR4 path (log smoking gun: `17:59:09 FSR4: VulkanMod init OK (1920x1201 -> 1920x1201)` — FSR4 initialized at native res against the stale 480x300 framebuffer). Fix: `onRenderBegin` now tracks `lastVkModScale` (the desired scale, 1.0=disabled) alongside `lastVkModInGame`; ANY change (slider drag, toggle flip, or world enter/exit) schedules `Renderer.scheduleSwapChainUpdate()` → recreate → mixin re-evaluates the swap at the new scale. Debounced (`VK_MOD_RECREATE_DEBOUNCE_MS` = 500ms): a slider drag changes qualityScale on EVERY drag event, and each would otherwise queue a recreateSwapChain (waitIdle GPU drain) per frame → stutter. World-flips always recreate (rare); scale changes throttle to one per window. **CRITICAL debounce detail (review fix)**: `lastVkModScale` must update ONLY when the recreate is actually scheduled — an unconditional update ABSORBED throttled changes (fast drag 0.25→0.8 within 500ms scheduled only the 0.25→0.3 step; once the drag settled, scaleChanged was already false and the final 0.8 was never applied). With the update inside the schedule branch, a pending change stays dirty (`scaleChanged` true) until the window elapses, then the recreate fires with the settled value. The recreate reads the LIVE config at recreate time (mixin `getRenderScale()`), so the actual scale always matches the settled slider value. During the debounce window the end() blit reads src dims from the ACTUAL framebuffer (not config), so the old-scale framebuffer still presents correctly until the recreate lands.
- **STALE-FRAMEBUFFER GUARD on the D3D12 path (review fix, 2026-08-05)**: when the user DISABLES render scale (slider to 100% or toggle off), `renderScaleConfigured()` flips false immediately but the swapchain recreate lands at the NEXT frame's beginFrame at the earliest (debounce can stretch that to 500ms = dozens of frames). During that window the end() hook would fall past the `renderScaleConfigured()` return into the D3D12 FSR4 copy path while (a) the scene rendered into the STILL-scaled framebuffer, not the swapchain image (Copy1 reads stale swapchain content), and (b) FSR4 may have NEVER been initialized (gated off while render scale was configured) → `recordFrameCopyOps` operates on null external VkImages → potential native crash. Fix: the D3D12 path now requires `mainFramebuffer == swapChain` (same condition vanilla's own end() uses for its PRESENT transition; mirrors the self-blit guard) — while the fb is still scaled, the D3D12 copies are skipped entirely (one native-scale frame via the vanilla path, then the recreate lands).
- **KNOWN LIMITATION (architecture, not fixable at this hook point)**: VulkanMod 0.6.8 renders the GUI (hotbar, crosshair, text) INTO the same main framebuffer as the world — there is NO separate GUI pass to keep native-res (verified: no gui package in VulkanMod's render tree; the GUI draws via the main pass). So render scale upscales the in-game UI too — at 0.25 that's visibly soft text ("the ui was upscaled along with the actual game and the quality was horrible"). 0.5–0.75 keeps the HUD legible; 0.25 is the worst case. The GL path does NOT have this problem (GUI composites at native res after the upscale). Fixing it under VulkanMod would require intercepting the GUI render pass and redirecting it to a separate native-res framebuffer + final composite — a much larger, mixin-fragile effort than this POC.
- **CRASH FIX (2026-08-05, latest.log 17:22)**: the debug-mode viewport query in `onSceneRendered` (`GL43C.glGetIntegerv(GL_VIEWPORT)` on `debugMode > 0 && frame%60==0`) crashed at world-join under VulkanMod with render-scale active — `ExceptionInInitializerError: No GLCapabilities instance has been set` (Vulkan replaces GL entirely, no GL context exists). The query runs BEFORE the `if (VulkanModCompat.isActive())` guard at the bottom of onSceneRendered, so it was unguarded. Fix: gate the debug block on `!VulkanModCompat.isLoaded()` (same pattern as `gpuRenderer`'s existing line-91 guard). Any future GL call added before the VKMod guard in onSceneRendered must carry the same gate.
- Settings: quality tab toggle "VulkanMod Render Scale" (only meaningful under VulkanMod; no-op on GL path).

## FSR2/3 shaders downloaded at game launch, not packaged (2026-08-05)
- **`ShaderSourceManager`** sources the FSR2/3 GLSL from on-disk files (`config/fsr-reloaded/shaders/fsr2|fsr3/NAME.glsl`) instead of only inline Java strings. Pipelines call `ShaderSourceManager.get("fsr2"|"fsr3", "NAME", embedded)` at compile time; disk files take precedence (users can edit shaders without rebuilding the jar).
- **Launch-time download**: when `config.fsr2ShaderUrl` is set, a daemon thread downloads the pack zip at launch (`fsr2/*.glsl` + `fsr3/*.glsl` entries, path-sanitized, atomic writes) and a `.pack-url` marker prevents re-fetching the same URL. This is the "FSR2 files downloaded when the game launches instead of being packaged into the mod" channel — the mod jar does not need to carry the shader sources as its primary store.
- **Bootstrap**: `config.fsr2ShaderBootstrap` (default true) writes the embedded copy for any missing file so the mod always works offline; set false for strict no-embed (FSR2/3 then require the download).
- **Why FSR2 cannot run via AMD's VK runtime under VulkanMod (verified 2026-08-05)**: the modern AMD FSR SDK (2.x) explicitly has NO Vulkan support ("Vulkan is currently not supported in SDK"); the last VK-capable release is FidelityFX SDK 1.1.4 whose only asset is a 470MB zip (impractical as a launch-time download); the open-source FSR2 VK backend is source-only and its precompiled SPIR-V permutation headers are NOT shipped (they are generated by a shader toolchain at build time — the vendored FSR2-OpenGL fork's VK sample has no such step). The mod's FSR2/3 are custom-adapted GLSL (32x1 workgroups, Minecraft uniforms, cloud detection) that cannot be replaced by AMD's vanilla files. FSR2/3 remain GL-path-only; the VulkanMod path stays FSR4 (now with the sync fix above).

## FSR2/3 Optimizations (2026-08-04)
- **`glClearTexImage` replaces FBO-clear dances** for `reconstructedPrevDepthTex` + `newLocksTex` (both pipelines): one GL 4.4 call instead of bind-FBO → attach → glClearBuffer → unbind. Identical output, ~6 fewer driver calls per clear.
- **Logging gated behind `debugMode`**: `[DIAG]`/`[DIAG_FG]`/`[PERF]`/`shouldDebug()`/`logSlow` only when `config.debugMode > 0`. Native `diagLog` (all 3 copies) gated via `diag.h` + JNI `setDiagnosticsEnabled`. Per-frame VulkanMod copy logs throttled to every 128th frame / slow-only.

- **GL sync fence caps are 8ms (not 50ms)** in both FSR4 and FG_DX12 paths. The old 50ms cap blocked the CPU for 3+ frames whenever a GPU-heavy mod (Voxy's compute meshing) queued work on the same GL context ahead of our copies — `glClientWaitSync` waits for ALL queued GL work, turning a deep Voxy queue into a per-frame multi-frame stall. 8ms caps the worst case at half a frame; the existing skip paths fall back gracefully (previous frame / single-present).

## Frame Generation (alternating real/FG output)
- `FSR3_FG` mode: FG dispatch runs in `onSceneRendered()` on **alternating frames** only (controlled by `fgFlipState`). Real frames: FSR output copied to mainRT, GUI renders on top. FG frames: FG dispatched (interpolates prev→curr), FG output copied to mainRT, GUI renders on top of FG.
- Alternation means **~50% of frames have no FG overhead** (0ms) — average FG cost is ~4ms per frame (vs 8ms every frame before fix). User sees: Real, FG, Real, FG, Real, FG sequence.
- **FG_DX12 uses DOUBLE-PRESENT, not alternation** (the only FG mode that actually doubles display FPS): `onSceneRendered` copies the generated FG frame (pool 5) to mainRT and sets `fgSecondPresentPending`; the vanilla loop presents FG+GUI; then `RenderSystemMixin` at `RenderSystem.flipFrame` TAIL calls `FSRProcessor.onAfterSwapBuffers()` which copies the REAL FSR4 frame (pool 4) to mainRT, `blitToScreen()`, and `presentFrame()` (glfwSwapBuffers) — a second image per render loop. Display FPS ≈ 2× render FPS (capped by monitor refresh/vsync). Alternation (real/FG single-present) gained ZERO smoothness because display FPS = render FPS.
- **Presentation order is FG-first, real-second** — the SDK interpolates between real N-1 and real N, so the generated frame must appear BEFORE real N. Presenting real-first would create a temporal inversion (backward jump) every frame.
- **HUDLessColor — GUI on BOTH double-presented frames**: `configDesc.HUDLessColor` (set at configure) = pool 4 (FSR4 output, real frame WITHOUT GUI); `presentColor` at generate = pool 6 (`FG_PRESENT` = mainRT captured AFTER the GUI painted). The SDK diffs the two to extract the HUD and re-composites it onto the generated frame.
