FG generate is DEFERRED from `onSceneRendered` to `presentDirect()` (blitToScreen HEAD hook — runs after the HUD painted): onSceneRendered stashes prepare params (`fgGeneratePending` + `fg*` fields) and leaves the real frame in mainRT; presentDirect copies mainRT→pool6, runs prepare+generate, copies pool5→mainRT. Second present (`onAfterSwapBuffers`) copies pool6 (real+GUI) back to mainRT — so BOTH frames show the HUD (no 50% HUD strobe).
- **Nested present is recursion-safe**: `onAfterSwapBuffers` clears `fgSecondPresentPending` BEFORE calling `presentFrame()`, so the re-entered flipFrame TAIL injector is a no-op (`fgInSecondPresent` is belt-and-suspenders).
- **`presentFrame()` is called directly, NOT `flipFrame()`** — flipFrame also runs Tesselator.clear, DynamicUniforms.reset, and LevelRenderer.endFrame, which must only run once per frame. `GpuDevice.presentFrame()` is just glfwSwapBuffers.
- **Vsync interaction**: with vsync ON, each present blocks for a vblank → render loop runs at ~half rate while display shows full rate (the standard FG model). With vsync OFF, uncapped loop presents 2 frames/loop. This is expected, not a bug.
- `savePrevFrame()` saves current upscaled scene AFTER copy to mainRT (always, regardless of flip state) for next frame's interpolation.
- **FG parameter fixes**:
  - `uTanHalfFovY` = `1.0f / proj.m11()` (derived from projection matrix, not hardcoded 1.0)
  - `uJitter` = actual render jitter from Halton sequence (not zero)
  - Set via `fsr3Pipeline.setFgJitter(jitterX, jitterY)` and `fsr3Pipeline.setFgTanHalfFovY(tanHalfFovY)` before each FG dispatch
- **FG source for interpolation**: `uCurrInterpolationSource` = current FSR3 upscaled output (already temporally blended) — known limitation: double-blending between temporal accumulation and FG interpolation causes slight blur. Future fix: expose raw pre-accumulation color as FG source.
- **MV field (bilinear splatting, no atomics)**: `FG_GAME_MV_FIELD_CS` reads dilated data at each pixel's render position and writes packed MV to gameMvField via simple `imageStore`. Each pixel splats to 4 bilinear texels for sub-pixel MV coverage (reduces ghosting at motion boundaries). No atomic operations — separate atomicMax on X/Y textures caused XY component decoupling (X from one pixel, Y from another = invalid MV, causing all-direction ghosting). Plain `imageStore` keeps XY pairs intact per-thread.
- No early swap: Minecraft's normal `window.swapBuffers` presents `mainRenderTarget` (FG + GUI). FPS equals render FPS.
- Blend weight: 65% current + 35% motion-warped previous (falls to 100% current on disoccluded pixels)

## Iris Shaders
- **`IrisCompat.java`**: Reflection-based `IrisApi.getInstance().isShaderPackInUse()` via `net.irisshaders.iris.api.v0.IrisApi`
- **`RenderScaleCompat.java`**: Reflection-based detection of `dev.zelo.renderscale.RenderScale` (Fabric mod `renderscale`)
- **No mainRT swap**: Iris manages its own render targets internally. Swapping `mainRenderTarget` causes shader pack rendering bugs (clouds-through-blocks in Complementary, missing terrain in Eclipse). This issue also affects RenderScale itself (see RenderScale issue #47 "Invisible with Iris shaders", #59 "Distant Horizons + Iris").
- **onSceneRendered (Iris)**: Detects actual `mainRT` size. If an external render-scale mod (RenderScale, etc.) reduced it, FSR1 upscales blit+RCAS. Otherwise just applies RCAS sharpen if `config.sharpness > 0`.
- **Jitter disabled** when Iris active (no per-pixel MV or depth access)
- **Why FSR1 not FSR2/3**: Iris doesn't expose depth or motion vectors per pixel; temporal accumulation requires neither.
- **For actual render scaling**: An external render-scale mod (RenderScale) is required when Iris is active. Our mod applies FSR1 upscale + RCAS on top of whatever the render-scale mod produces.
- **`config.irisQualityScale`** field (default 0.95) — used only when an external render-scale mod is not detected; purely informational without one.
- **`VK_EXTERNAL_MEMORY_HANDLE_TYPE_OPAQUE_WIN32_BIT`** = 0x00000002 (NOT 0x00000001 which is FD type)
- **`VK_EXTERNAL_SEMAPHORE_HANDLE_TYPE_OPAQUE_WIN32_BIT`** = 0x00000002
- **RenderScale detection**: Logged once on first Iris frame. When detected, `RenderScaleCompat.logStatus()` prints the configured scale factor. The Iris path checks `mainRT.width < displayWidth` at runtime to detect any render-scale mod.

### IRIS DEPTH-BUFFER VERSION CONTRACT (2026-08-29 — "switching FSR version makes the terrain disappear until a quality change", ROOT CAUSE)

- **Symptom**: with a shader pack active, switching the FSR version (FSR1/2/3/4, any direction) left a sky-only composite — no terrain — and it stayed broken across further version switches. Changing the quality/Iris slider brought the terrain back. Reproduced in the 2026-08-25 14:26 session (`logs/latest.log`): healthy probes until 14:27:15 (`terrain var` 34-340), then from 14:27:17 onward `terrain luma~114 var~1.3` (flat sky gradient, blue-dominant) through FSR1→FSR4→FSR3_UPS, healing only after the 0.6→0.55→0.6 slider change at 14:27:25-26.
- **Cause**: `IrisRenderingPipeline#beginLevelRendering` calls `RenderTargets#resizeIfNeeded(((Blaze3dRenderTargetExt) mainRT).iris$getDepthBufferVersion(), mainRT.getDepthTexture(), mainRT.width, mainRT.height, format, directives)` EVERY frame, but `resizeIfNeeded` adopts the freshly passed depth texture ONLY when `cachedDepthBufferVersion != depthBufferVersion` **or** the width/height/depth-format changed. Otherwise it keeps the previously cached `currentDepthTexture` and never re-attaches it to the owned framebuffers.
  - The vanilla bump lives in Iris's `MixinRenderTarget#iris$onDestroyBuffers` — an `@Inject` on `RenderTarget.destroyBuffers()`. Our `ScaledRenderTarget` **overrides `destroyBuffers()` as a no-op** (it does not own the textures), and a brand-new proxy instance is constructed every frame, so the inherited counter was permanently `0`. Iris cached `0` once and never re-adopted a depth texture again.
  - `markDirty()` destroyed + recreated the scaled scene `TextureTarget` on every version switch **at unchanged dimensions**, so Iris's only remaining trigger (size change) never fired: the pack kept depth-testing the world against a **closed** texture → terrain failed the depth test, sky (no depth test) still drew. A quality change altered the dimensions → `sizeChanged` → rebind → terrain back. That is the whole "until I play with the quality settings".
- **Fix (two parts, both required)**:
  1. `ScaledRenderTarget` now carries a `bufferVersion` (extra ctor arg) and overrides `iris$getDepthBufferVersion()` / `iris$getColorBufferVersion()` to return it. Same name + signature as the mixin-added parent methods = a plain Java override, so there is **no compile-time dependency on Iris**. `FSRProcessor.sceneTargetVersion` is bumped at the single `new TextureTarget("fsr-scene", ...)` site and passed to every proxy. Iris now re-adopts depth on ANY recreate, at any size.
  2. `markDirty()` no longer destroys the scene target when the switch does not resize it (`keepSceneTarget`). The scaled scene target is a plain color+depth pair at `scaledWidth x scaledHeight` — the FSR version has zero effect on it, so a version switch now touches only the FSR pipelines (also removes pointless GL texture churn on every toggle).
- **Secondary fix in the same change**: `markDirty()` sized the target with `config.qualityScale` while the real path (`onRenderBegin`) uses `config.getIrisScale()` under a pack — so it computed 1092x682 instead of 1008x630. Now uses the Iris-aware scale via the frame-locked `irisActiveThisFrame` (never a live `IrisCompat` call — 2026-08-19 pack-activation frame tear).
- **Do NOT** reintroduce an unconditional `renderTarget.destroyBuffers()` in `markDirty()`. If a same-size recreate is ever needed again, it is now safe *because* `sceneTargetVersion` bumps — but keeping the target is strictly better.
### DIAGNOSTIC READBACK vs THE SATURATION GUARDS (2026-08-29 — "every second it blinks")

- **Symptom**: after the fix above, one BLACK frame (HUD included) every ~1-2s, only with a shader
  pack, on FSR2/3/4 but not FSR1. Session proof in `logs/latest.log` 17:32:46–17:33:32.
- **Cause**: every synchronous pixel readback is a **pipeline flush**, and its cost lands INSIDE the
  window the GPU-saturation guards time (`t0BeginEnd` -> pre-check). The `[TERRAIN-PROBE]` block
  readback fired every 2s and cost ~26ms (worldMs 4.5ms -> 24-40ms against a 12ms threshold), so
  **every probe frame tripped the FSR4 saturation skip** — `[TERRAIN-PROBE]` and `[FSR4-SKIP]` fire
  on exactly the same timestamps, 2s apart. The skip presents the PREVIOUS frame's FSR4 output and
  clears `fsr4PrevColorFresh` (T&C gated off next frame) = the blink. The old comment claiming "one
  16x16 block pair every 2s — negligible cost" was simply wrong: with `glReadPixels` the pixel COUNT
  is irrelevant, the flush dominates.
- **Fix (three parts)**:
  1. `diagnosticReadbackThisFrame` — set by `readTexBlockRgba8()` (and the probe site), cleared at
     the top of `onSceneRendered`. Both saturation guards now ignore those frames for the **spike
     test AND the baseline EMA** (`fsr4SatSpike` in the FSR4 pre-check, `saturationSpike` in the
     shared FSR1/2/3 guard). A self-inflicted stall is not GPU saturation; folding it into the EMA
     would also blunt the guard against real saturation.
  2. **Probe cadence** — `armTerrainProbeBurst()` gives a dense 2s cadence for 10s after the only
     events that rebuild Iris's pipeline (world join, `markDirty()`, `onResize()`), then drops to a
     30s heartbeat. A permanent 2s cadence bought a visible hitch forever to guard a failure that
     can only occur right after a rebuild.
  3. `[BLIT-AUDIT]` false positive fixed — the zero-blit warning now requires
     `mainRenderTarget != null`, otherwise every menu/title/no-world frame reported a "black frame".
- **CRASH FOLLOW-UP (2026-08-29)**: the burst arming above made the probe fire on the FIRST swap
  frame after a world join — the most volatile frame there is (Iris pipeline destroy+recreate,
  Photonics reallocating, FSR1 initialising). The very next run crashed with `exitcode
  -1073741819` (0xC0000005) ~4s after world join, **no hs_err and no crash report** = the documented
  atio6axx.dll signature. The log shows exactly one `[TERRAIN-PROBE]` sample, immediately before the
  crash. The terrain probe is now **`config.debugMode > 0` gated** (precedent: the FSR2/3 OUTPROBE
  readback burst is gated the same way — "should never run in a production session"), and
  `armTerrainProbeBurst()` uses a **4s settle delay** instead of firing immediately.
- **Rule**: any NEW synchronous readback on the render path must set
  `diagnosticReadbackThisFrame`, or it will reintroduce the blink — and never schedule one during a
  pipeline-rebuild window.
- **Unconfirmed**: the probe is the prime suspect but not proven; a pre-existing flaky world-join
  crash is possible on this stack. If it recurs with the probe disabled, investigate the world-join
  path (Iris pipeline recreate + FSR4 init + Photonics realloc) rather than the probe.
- **Note**: the `irisSwapBrokenLatch` (TERRAIN-PROBE) only masked this by dropping to native pass-through, and only after ~6s of 3 throttled votes. It is intentionally NOT cleared on a version switch — clearing it would trade a degraded-but-working session for up to 6s of missing terrain if this ever regressed. It clears on world join.

### PACK-TOGGLE SCALE CHANGE (2026-08-29 — "disable + re-enable the pack: terrain disappears the first time")

- **Symptom**: toggling the shader pack off and on again left a broken image (terrain gone) on the
  FIRST cycle; a second cycle was fine.
- **Cause**: the effective scale flips between `config.getIrisScale()` (pack ON, 0.6 → 1008x630) and
  `config.getScale()` (pack OFF, 0.65 → 1092x683). A pack toggle therefore changes `scaledWidth`/
  `scaledHeight` WITHOUT hitting any of the three rebuild paths — `markDirty()` (config change),
  `applyAutoScale()` (quality slider) or `onResize()` (window resize). Nothing re-sized anything:
  - FSR4's D3D12 context stayed at 1092x683 and **refused to dispatch**:
    `[FSR4-PATH] context 1092x683->1680x1050 != scene 1008x630->1680x1050 — FSR4 dispatch skipped
    (FSR1 until recreate)`;
  - the FSR1 fallback pipeline stayed at 1092x683 and upscaled the 1008x630 scene with **stale UVs**
    → a magnified corner crop → what looked like "terrain disappeared" (the same failure class as the
    documented corner-zoom bugs).
- **Fix**: `lastAppliedScaleW/H` compared against the freshly computed size every frame in
  `onRenderBegin`; any change that no other path already handled calls `applyAutoScale()`
  (`[SCALE-SYNC]` log). `rebuiltThisFrame` prevents double work when markDirty/applyAutoScale already
  ran. The first frame only establishes the baseline (`lastAppliedScaleW > 0` guard).
- **Secondary fix in the same change**: `applyAutoScale()` hardcoded `config.qualityScale`, so ANY
  rebuild that ran while a pack was active sized the pipelines for the non-Iris scale — that is
  precisely how the FSR4 context got stuck at 1092x683. It now uses the same Iris-aware expression as
  `onRenderBegin` and `markDirty()`.
- **Note**: the FSR4 context mismatch is also why FSR4 "isn't at its full potential" right after a
  pack toggle — it silently degrades to FSR1 until something else forces a recreate.

## Distant Horizons
- **`DistantHorizonsCompat.java`**: Fabric API `isModLoaded("distanthorizons")` detection
- **DH depth separate**: DH v2.1.0+ uses its own framebuffers with separate depth textures (`dhDepthTex0`/`dhDepthTex1`) and different near/far planes
- **FSR2/3 auto-fallback**: When DH detected, `dhActive` runtime flag forces FSR1 (spatial) regardless of config version — DH depth not available to temporal reconstruction pipeline. `isFsr2()`/`isFsr3()`/`isTemporalUpscaler()` all return false. Config version is NOT modified (no save persistence).
- **Jitter disabled**: `isTemporalUpscaler()` returns false → no jitter applied
- **FSR1 works**: FSR1 dispatch triggers on `config.version == FSR1 || dhActive`
- **Iris + DH**: DH renderes through Iris `dh_terrain`/`dh_water` programs; our FSR1 blit+RCAS on mainRT works as post-process
- **Viewport**: DH reads current viewport during LOD rendering. Our scaled viewport means DH renders at scaled resolution, which FSR1 then upscales — consistent quality
- **Compositing timing**: DH composites LODs into mainRT during LevelRenderer. If this happens before `onSceneRendered`, DH content is captured in the upscale. If after, DH content at native resolution blends with upscaled vanilla (acceptable since DH LOD terrain is already simplified)

## FSR4 Phase 0 (GL-Vulkan Interop Test) — 100% COMPLETE ✅
- **`test/phase0/`**: Standalone Gradle project (LWJGL 3.3.3, Java 25). Tests GL_EXT_memory_object + VK_KHR_external_memory interop.
- **Run**: `cd test/phase0 && .\gradlew.bat run` (copies root's gradlew wrapper automatically)
- **AMD Radeon 860M (RDNA 3.5, Windows 11) final results**:
  - `GL_EXT_memory_object`: Supported
  - `GL_EXT_memory_object_win32`: Supported
  - `glTextureStorageMem2DEXT`: Works
  - `glGetMemoryObjectWin32HandleEXT`: MISSING (GL cannot EXPORT — forward GL→VK path DEAD)
  - `glImportMemoryWin32HandleEXT`: AVAILABLE (GL CAN IMPORT — reverse VK→GL path CONFIRMED)
  - `glImportSemaphoreWin32HandleEXT`: MISSING (no semaphore sync — `glFinish()` fallback required)
- **VK→GL reverse pipe test (2026-07-02)**: Full round-trip — 6/6 verifications passed:
  1. ✅ VK allocates image with `VkExternalMemoryImageCreateInfo` (handle type 0x00000002)
  2. ✅ VK allocates memory with `VkExportMemoryAllocateInfo` → `vkAllocateMemory`
  3. ✅ VK exports Win32 handle via `vkGetMemoryWin32HandleKHR` → `0x728`
  4. ✅ GL imports handle via `glImportMemoryWin32HandleEXT` → creates GL memory object
  5. ✅ GL binds texture via `glTextureStorageMem2DEXT`
  6. ✅ **VK clears BLUE → GL reads BLUE** (VK→GL shared memory works)
  7. ✅ **GL writes RED → VK reads RED** (GL→VK shared memory works)
  8. ✅ **VK clears GREEN → GL reads GREEN** (bidirectional verified, no aliasing)
- **Handle type fix**: Used `0x00000002` (correct Win32 type). Previous test used `0x00000001` (FD type, wrong for Windows — that would have failed silently).
- **No JNI in Phase 0**: LWJGL static bindings sufficed for the pure-Java proof.
- **Architectural risk eliminated**: The pipe is proven before writing any C++ code.

## FSR4 Phase 3 — SDK Integration (IN PROGRESS 🔄)

### Provider Discovery (2026-07-05)
- **amd_fidelityfx_loader_dx12.dll** v2.3.0: returns `rc=2` (unknown descriptor type) for upscale descriptors — doesn't know how to dispatch to provider via new unified API
- **Provider direct** (`amd_fsr4_api.dll` / `amd_fidelityfx_upscaler_dx12.dll`): returns `rc=1` for bare upscale desc, `rc=0` (OK) with full chain
- **Skip loader entirely**, use provider's `ffxCreateContext`/`ffxDispatch`/`ffxDestroyContext` directly via `GetModuleHandleW` + `GetProcAddress`

### Descriptor Chain (Must be upscale root)
```
upscaleDesc.pNext → backendDesc (ffxCreateBackendDX12Desc)
backendDesc.pNext → versionDesc (ffxCreateContextDescUpscaleVersion, version=FFX_UPSCALER_VERSION)
```
- `ID3D12Device8` preferred, `ID3D12Device5` fallback
- SEH (`__try/__except`) protection around `ffxCreateContext` (provider can crash on invalid descriptor chains)

### D3D12 Shared Resources (5-texture pool)
- Color (RGBA8), Depth (R32F), MotionVectors (RG16F), Reactive (R8), Output (RGBA8)
- Allocated as D3D12 committed resources with shared heap, shared via Win32 handles
- GL imports handles via `glImportMemoryWin32HandleEXT` + `glTextureStorageMem2DEXT` (VK interop path)
- VK interop textures used for GL→D3D12 texture copies via shared memory

### Dispatch Flow
1. Scene renders to scaled render target (OpenGL)
2. `glCopyImageSubData` → copy color to shared GL texture (VK-backed). Depth is constant 0.5 → filled **once per pool allocation** (`fsr4DepthFilledForPool` tracks the pool pointer) instead of every frame — saves a full-screen R32F copy + FBO state churn per frame.
2.5. MV gen + reactive mask compute shaders → shared MV (RG16F) + reactive (R8) textures (via MANAGED staging → `glCopyImageSubData` — direct imageStore into external-memory textures doesn't persist on this driver)
3. GL sync: `glFenceSync` + `glClientWaitSync` (16ms timeout) — ensures copies complete
4. D3D12 dispatch: `ffxDispatch` records compute into D3D12 command list
5. `ExecuteCommandLists` + fence signal
6. D3D12 fence wait (`waitForFenceValue`) before reusing allocator
7. `copyFsr4OutputToMainRT` — copy D3D12 output back to GL via shared memory
8. GUI renders at native resolution on top

- **DYNAMIC RESOLUTION — TRIED AND REVERTED (2026-08-08, "it was zooming into the bottom left corner of my screen")**: the pool-only recreate experiment (keep the FSR4 context alive across quality-scale changes, only swap the D3D12 pool, with `FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_DYNAMIC_RESOLUTION` + `maxRenderSize=display`) caused a magnified bottom-left CORNER CROP: the FSR4 SDK dispatch reads the input with the CONTEXT-CACHED render size (the JNI never passes renderW/H per dispatch), so the resized pool was sampled with stale UVs → the image zoomed into the corner, getting worse with each auto-quality step (each step swapped the pool under the surviving context). REVERTED to the proven full-teardown path: quality-scale changes go through `needsRecreate` → `shutdownFsr4` + `allocateFsr4Pool`(new size) + `initFsr4`(new size). `FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_DYNAMIC_RESOLUTION` and `maxRenderSize` removed from the create desc (flags = AUTO_EXPOSURE only). `fsr4DynamicRes` field and both pool-only branches (GL + VKMod) deleted. `applyAutoScale()` now tears down FSR4/FG exactly like `markDirty()` (context + pool) — leaving the old-size pool alive during the recreate debounce window would clip the new-size scene into the old pool = the same corner-crop transient; with the pool gone, the dispatch guard (`getColorAllocPtr()!=0`) blocks and the spatial FSR1 fallback renders until the re-init. The user-facing fix: quality slider + target-FPS auto-quality changes are safe again (full recreate, no crop); the 500ms debounce may briefly show FSR1 during a step — pre-experiment behavior. (2) **D3D12 timestamp queries** (kept — unrelated to the crop): `Fsr4Context` brackets `ffxDispatch` with `EndQuery(D3D12_QUERY_TYPE_TIMESTAMP)` begin/end and resolves into a persistently-mapped READBACK buffer (GPU-side, same command list — no barriers needed); `finish()` reads the two UINT64s after the frame fence completes and logs `[FSR4GPU] SDK dispatch GPU time: %.2f ms` every 60th dispatch (diagLog-gated → silent in production). Race-free under the dual round-robin allocators: the GPU executes lists FIFO, so dispatch N's resolve always runs before dispatch N+1's EndQuery overwrites the query slots. Guards (review 2026-08-08): `timestampResolved_` is set only when ResolveQueryData was recorded, so the SEH-crash path (fence signaled WITHOUT executing the list) and the rc!=OK path never log stale/zero timestamps; the read also checks `waited` from the fence wait + `t0==0||t1==0||t1<t0`. `GetTimestampFrequency` is on `ID3D12CommandQueue` (NOT the device) — and `<windows.h>`'s `max` macro collides with `std::max` (use ternaries). Gives the first REAL per-frame SDK GPU cost measurement — the rest of the diag pipeline is CPU-side estimates.

- **TARGET-FPS AUTO-QUALITY (2026-08-08, "make an option that automatically tweaks the settings to hit a certain fps")**: `config.targetFps` (0=off, 30-240) + a cycling Target-FPS button (Off/30/60/90/120/144/240) on the Quality tab, with a live status line (`Target 60 — measured 62 fps @ 55%`). `FSRProcessor` measures the render-loop frame time at the top of `onRenderBegin` (time since the previous call — the whole render+present cadence; runs before the Iris/VulkanMod early returns so it works under every renderer; `>1000ms` gaps like alt-tab are ignored so one hitch can't tank the EMA) into a 0.1-alpha EMA, and `updateAutoQuality()` (called from the same spot) compares `1000/emaMs` against the target: below `target*0.94` → quality down 0.05 (0.10 when the deficit is >30%), above `target*1.06` → quality up 0.05, on a 2000ms cooldown (hysteresis so it settles). Vsync-capped detection: if `fps >= refreshRate-1` the target is unreachable (vsync holds fps below it) — pour the headroom into quality instead of oscillating. **Plateau guard** (review fix): if 4 consecutive lowering steps never recover, an EXTERNAL cap (MC's own framerate limit in options — which the vsync branch can't see since fps < refresh-1) is assumed and the adjuster holds for 30s before retrying, instead of grinding quality to the 25% floor. Gates: `targetFps>0 && enabled && level!=null && screen==null` (menus/pause drop fps — don't thrash) `&& !Iris` (scale comes from irisQualityScale there) `&& !isFrameGen() && !isFrameGenDx12()` (FG modes already target fps by construction). **v2 (2026-08-08, "it is zooming in more and more by the second" fix)**: the v1 logic limit-cycled. Root cause (log-verified): the vsync-capped branch (`fps >= refresh-1` → raise quality) fired with target 90 on a 60Hz vsync display, climbing 0.45→0.65 in 0.05 steps every 2s; once the heavier render dropped fps below target the below-target branch lowered again → a permanent every-2s 0.05-step oscillation (each step a full FSR4 dynamic-res pool recreate + history reset = visible pops the user read as zooming). v2 changes: (1) **unreachable-target hold** — if fps is pinned at the refresh rate AND `targetFps >= refresh`, HOLD and do nothing (the target is unachievable under vsync; v1's "pour headroom into quality" was the cycle driver). Target < refresh with fps pinned at refresh still falls through to the normal raise. (2) **wide hysteresis + confirmation streak** — band widened to ±10% (0.90/1.10) and a change requires 2 CONSECUTIVE out-of-band readings in the same direction (~4s), so a single wobble or a transient recreate hiccup can't trigger; direction flips restart the streak. (3) **proportional lower step** — `0.05 * (1 + min(3, ceil(deficit/0.15)))`, so a big deficit converges in 1-2 steps instead of 10+. (4) **long plateau hold** — unreachable-target holds are now 5 minutes (was 30s), including an immediate hold if the 25% floor is hit while still below target; `resetAutoQuality()` (called from the Target-FPS button) clears the hold instantly on a target change. The plateau hold is SOFT (review fix): every interval, if fps has recovered into the band (`fps >= target*0.90`) the hold clears instantly so quality rises back — it guards against grinding, never locks quality low after a transient storm. Known limitation: the unreachable-target detection depends on `Window.getRefreshRate()` succeeding — if it returns 0 (unusual API paths), the vsync case is invisible and the target-90@60Hz case degrades once (4 proportional lowers) then parks in the 5-min hold instead of cycling. Expected: target 90 @ 60Hz → zero adjustments after the initial settle; target below refresh → raises to 100% once, then holds. Changes persist via `config.save()` and route through **`applyAutoScale()`** — a light-weight twin of `markDirty()` that recomputes the scaled render target + pipelines and resets temporal history AND tears down the FSR4/FG contexts + pool (the dynamic-res pool-only branch it used to rely on was REVERTED — see the corner-zoom bullet above; leaving the old-size pool alive during the debounce window clips the new-size scene = corner crop, so it must tear down like `markDirty()`). The next `onRenderBegin`'s `needsRecreate` re-inits at the new size; during the ≤500ms debounce the spatial FSR1 fallback renders (safe — the dispatch guard blocks on `getColorAllocPtr()!=0`). The quality slider also uses `applyAutoScale()`. `sharedTexMgr`'s FSR1 shared output is display-size only — a scale change doesn't invalidate it (destroyed only for version switches via `markDirty()`). One-frame-stale present on the transition frame (`fsr4AsyncPending` cleared) matches `markDirty()`'s existing behavior — benign. **Deferral interplay (review 2026-08-08, don't misdiagnose as overshoot)**: after an auto-quality step `fsr4Initialized` is false, so on a busy GPU the recreate block's FIRST-INIT DEFERRAL (`!isFsr4InitCalm()` + 6s grace, on top of the 500ms debounce) can hold FSR4 on FSR1 for up to ~6.5s — during that window measured fps is HIGHER (FSR1 is cheaper), so the next interval may raise quality once before FSR4 re-engages and fps drops again. Same as pre-experiment `markDirty()` behavior; the confirmation streak + hysteresis damp it. **v3 (2026-08-08, "it does not tweak the settings at all" + "voxy lagspikes are back" fix)**: the v2 confirmation streak was SELF-DEFEATING on exactly the machine it was built for. The unreachable-target branch (`fps >= refresh-1 && targetFps >= refresh`) ran on EVERY calm frame at the refresh ceiling — i.e. the frames BETWEEN Voxy/chunk-streaming spikes on a vsync'd or frame-capped display — and it RESET `autoOutOfBandStreak`/`autoLastDirection`/`autoLowerStreak` before returning. So spike→streak 1, calm→reset 0, spike→1, … — a below-target storm could never accumulate the 2 consecutive out-of-band readings required to lower qualityScale, the feature never acted (settings never tweaked), and the Voxy lag spikes it was meant to mitigate came back at full force. The reset was also dead logic: in that branch fps ≥ refresh−1 ≥ target−1 > target*0.90, so direction can never be −1 there (the in-band branch already resets state when appropriate). Fix: the branch now just returns — no state mutation. Also shortened `AUTO_Q_INTERVAL_MS` 2000→1500ms (2 confirmations ≈ 3s, steps ≥3s apart — still far below the v1 oscillation period, but a sustained storm's FIRST tweak lands ~1s sooner). `getAutoQualityStatus()` now explains inaction instead of silently showing a static line: appends `(holding — quality couldn't reach target, retrying in Ns)` during a plateau hold, or `(capped at NHZ vsync/frame-limit — target unreachable; disable vsync or lower the target)` when fps is pinned at refresh with target ≥ refresh — so "it does not tweak" is now diagnosable from the settings screen itself. Expected: on the vsync'd/capped 60Hz case with target ≥ 60, a Voxy storm now lowers quality (the streak survives the calm-in-between frames) and the status line explains the no-op when the target is genuinely unreachable.

### Init Sequence (FSRProcessor.onRenderBegin)
1. Check `Fsr4Native.isAvailable()` (DLLs loaded in static initializer)
2. Allocate D3D12 shared resources pool (5 textures, VK interop handles)
3. Call `Fsr4Native.initFsr4()` → creates `Fsr4Context` + `D3D12Device`
4. Debounced recreation (500ms min interval)
5. Frame time budgeting: skip if scene took >10ms, fallback after 10 consecutive skips
6. **GPU-saturation guard (fast movement)**: when the GL queue falls behind (chunk streaming + Voxy meshing on the same GL context), the GL sync fence times out every frame → every frame skipped → <10 FPS death spiral. Logic in `onSceneRendered` FSR4 block:
   - **ASYNC MODE (config.fsr4Async, default ON, non-FG) — NO 33ms extended wait, QUIET SKIP on timeout (2026-08-05)**: the fence wait keeps the 8ms cap (glClientWaitSync returns EARLY ~1-3ms on a short queue — it is NOT a fixed 8ms block; a 0ns poll would never see this frame's copies+MV-gen compute complete and would starve FSR4 entirely → permanent FSR1). On timeout: skip THIS frame's dispatch QUIETLY — the previous dispatch's output was already drained into mainRT at the top of the block, so the displayed image stays real FSR4 output (aging up to `FSR4_SKIP_SUSPEND_AFTER` frames during a sustained ramp) and the skip is counted. NO FSR1 inline — running FSR1 on every timeout was the "FSR4 acts like FSR1" regression (FSR1 quality at the first hiccup of every busy episode). Why the old 33ms re-wait was removed for async: during elytra + Voxy the queue stays >8ms deep for hundreds of frames and the extended wait almost always *succeeded*, resetting `fsr4ConsecutiveSkips` to 0 every frame — so Tier-2 suspension never fired and the render thread burned 8ms + 33ms (~41ms → <25 FPS) on EVERY frame. Now after `FSR4_SKIP_SUSPEND_AFTER` (6) consecutive timeouts the formal suspension takes over.
   - **SYNC MODE (fsr4Async off, or FG_DX12 which needs the same-frame output) — unchanged**: only the FIRST timeout of an episode attempts the one-time 33ms extended re-wait (recovers a one-off hiccup, preserves temporal quality); later consecutive timeouts skip cheaply.
   - After `FSR4_SKIP_SUSPEND_AFTER` (6) consecutive timeouts, `fsr4Suspended=true` → routes through the pure-GL FSR1 spatial path (no cross-API fence, cannot stall). On suspend, `hasPrevFrame=false` + `fsr2FrameCount=0` force a clean history rebuild on resume. Suspension resets on reinit/shutdown.
   - **DEFERRED RESUME-PROBE FENCE (2026-08-06, "FSR4 never goes back automatically" fix)**: the probe fence is now created at the END of each suspended frame's probe block and polled at the START of the NEXT frame's block (field `fsr4ProbeFence`) — it guards only work submitted through the PREVIOUS frame, so the 0ns poll CAN signal once the GPU drains past it. The previous design polled a fence created in the SAME block, which guarded THIS frame's just-submitted scene render — on a GPU-bound iGPU it could never signal, the 2-signal clean streak was unreachable, and FSR4 stayed parked on FSR1 until a manual version toggle forced a reinit (exact user report). Fence refreshed every suspended frame (flush + `glFenceSync` at block end, skipped the frame the streak resumes); consumed via `glDeleteSync` after polling. Stale-fence case (re-suspend long after a resume) is benign — it is guaranteed-signaled, so the first probe counts immediately and the 2-streak + pre-check (3-frame re-suspend) bound any oscillation.
   - **Resume probe must use a BOUNDED wait, not a 0ns poll (2026-08-05)**: the probe fence guards ALL pending GL work INCLUDING this frame's scene render (submitted moments ago, several ms of GPU time) — a pure `glClientWaitSync(probe, 0, 0L)` can NEVER signal, so `fsr4Suspended` never cleared and FSR4 stayed on FSR1 permanently after the first saturation episode (the "switches to FSR1 permanently" bug). Now: free 0ns poll first (catches the already-drained case at zero cost), then a throttled bounded wait — every 16th suspended frame (`(++fsr4ProbeCounter & 15) == 0`), up to 8ms — so a recovered queue is detected within ~0.25s while the stall averages <1ms/frame during heavy saturation. The bounded wait is skipped while the dispatch-failure 2s backoff is still active (queue draining is irrelevant until the cooldown elapses). When the probe clears suspension mid-frame, FSR4 dispatches THAT same frame with `reset=true` (clean history rebuild); jitter alignment stays consistent because onRenderBegin zeroed jitter while suspended (scene unjittered + SDK jitter (0,0) = correct), resuming normally next frame.
   - **Dispatch failures are recoverable suspensions, NOT permanent downgrades (2026-08-05)**: `Fsr4Native.dispatchFsr4` returning false previously set `config.version = FSR1` for the whole session (the other half of "FSR4 acts like FSR1"). Now it sets `fsr4Suspended=true` with a 2s backoff (`lastFsr4DispatchFail`/`FSR4_DISPATCH_FAIL_BACKOFF_MS`) before the probe may re-arm FSR4 — a genuinely broken D3D12 path can't retry a failing dispatch every frame, while a transient hiccup recovers automatically with a clean history rebuild.
   - **FG_DX12 fence timeouts are graceful, not runtime failures (2026-08-05)**: `doFrameGenDispatch()` returns 0=success / 1=transient GL fence timeout (single-present the real frame, NOT counted toward `fgRuntimeFailCount`) / 2=real native failure (counted toward `FG_MAX_RUNTIME_FAILURES`=3 permanent disable). Previously EVERY timeout counted toward the permanent disable, so a few busy frames at world-join silently turned FG_DX12 into "just FSR4" (the "FG DX12 acts like FSR4" regression). First timeout of an episode still attempts the 33ms extended wait (`fgTimeoutLogged` one-shot — set UNCONDITIONALLY, debug-gated only for the log, so the throttle works with logging off).
   - **GL error drain after the FSR4 fence (2026-08-05)**: a `glGetError()` loop right after `glClientWaitSync` consumes MV-gen compute errors that AMD reports asynchronously at the next fence — without it they were misattributed to FSR1's RCAS dispatch when the spatial fallback ran next (`GL_INVALID_OPERATION` 0x502 → EASU permanently disabled at world-join). Also unbinds GL_TEXTURE_2D on unit 0 after MV-gen (the shared depth texture was left bound there, creating a sampler+image feedback loop with RCAS's image-unit-1 binding — `glActiveTexture(GL_TEXTURE0)` must precede the bind, since glBindTexture targets the CURRENTLY active unit).
   - **Known tradeoff**: while suspended the scene renders UNJITTERED (clean FSR1). During the ≤6 skip frames before formal suspension the scene was already rendered jittered, so the skipped frames can shift ±0.5 render px — sub-pixel, masked by the fast motion that caused the saturation.
   - **All per-frame FSR4/FG timeout warnings are debug-mode-gated** (`shouldDebug()`) and once-per-episode (suspension warn at exactly skip==6; FG_DX12 warn via `fgTimeoutLogged` one-shot, reset on success). Log formatting itself added measurable overhead during saturation.
   - **Pure-GL saturation guard (FSR1/FSR2/FSR3_UPS, 2026-08-05)**: the non-FSR4 versions had NO saturation guard — during chunk-streaming storms (DH LOD→real chunk transitions, Voxy meshing on the same GL context) they kept running their full per-frame passes (RCAS/EASU compute; ~8 compute dispatches + 2 glClearTexImage + 2 copies for FSR2/3) on an overloaded queue, and with vsync each present blocked on the deep queue. `onSceneRendered` now EMA-tracks the world-render time (`t0BeginEnd`→entry — the render thread stalls inside the world pass when the queue is deep, so worldMs is a reliable saturation signal) and degrades to a SINGLE bilinear blit (`blitSceneToMain` = FSR1Pipeline with sharpness 0 — no compute/barriers/extra copies) while `worldMs > max(2×EMA, 20ms)`. **Only CALM frames feed the EMA** (the baseline must not adapt to the storm or the spike detector stops firing mid-storm and resumes full FSR work while the GPU is still saturated — found in review); cold start treats any frame above the 20ms floor as a spike (initial chunk gen is already a storm). Stays degraded until 10 consecutive clean frames, then resumes with `hasPrevFrame=false` + `fsr2FrameCount=0` (clean temporal rebuild — the storm changed the scene, so stale pre-storm history would ghost for a frame). Excludes FSR3_FG (GLSL frame-gen alternation state machine) and FSR4/FG_DX12 (their own fence-based guards above). Enter/exit logged debug-only.
   - **SAT-guard cold-start seed (2026-08-07, "all FSR2 edges jagged incl. terrain + sky + still shaking" — ROOT CAUSE of a full session on FSR1)**: the EMA above was seeded ONLY by the first CALM frame, so on an always-busy machine (iGPU + Sodium/Voxy/Create: world pass >20ms on essentially EVERY frame — a fresh-world Voxy meshing storm never settles) `firstSample` stayed true forever → every frame re-entered cold-start and counted as a spike → `saturationDegraded` stuck true → FSR2/FSR3 never dispatched. Session-log smoking gun: only `FSR1: Initializing pipeline (saturation blit)` logged, ZERO `FSR2: All 6 shaders compiled` lines in 1.5 min of in-world play (config `version=FSR2`). The user saw the FSR1 bilinear 4× blit (jagged edges on terrain AND sky) while `onRenderBegin` still applied the temporal Halton jitter (`isTemporalUpscaler()` is config-based) → the blit presents a jittered render with no de-jitter → the ±1.5 display-px oscillation = the "still shaking". Fix mirrors the FSR4 pre-check EMA cold-start fix (2026-08-06): seed `satEmaWorldMs` from the FIRST sample unconditionally (before the spike test), so the guard settles into a storm-appropriate baseline within a few frames and fires only on genuine ~2×-above-baseline spikes. Also: `onRenderBegin`'s jitter block now zeroes jitter when `saturationDegraded` too (alongside `spatialFallbackActive()`) — the degraded path is `blitSceneToMain`, another de-jitter-less spatial blit, so it must never present a jittered render. The zeroing is gated to the modes the guard actually routes to the blit (`isFsr2() || (isFsr3() && !isFrameGen())`): FSR3_FG explicitly ignores degradation (keeps its temporal pipeline) and FSR4/FG_DX12 never reach the guard (a stale `saturationDegraded` after an in-game FSR2→FSR4 switch must not permanently zero FSR4's jitter). Both changes are FSRProcessor-only; no shader edits needed.

### FSR4 elytra crash 0xC0000005 (2026-08-05) — SEH hardening
- **Symptom**: game hard-crashes (exit code `-1073741819` = `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION`, no `hs_err` written) when flying with elytra in FSR4 mode — i.e. exactly when the GPU-saturation guard is churning (GL fence timeouts → suspend → resume-probe → re-dispatch with `reset=true` mid-episode).
- **Root-cause class**: the crash is native. `Fsr4Native.dispatchFsr4` previously caught only `std::exception` — which does **NOT** catch access violations. The pool-pointer dereference (`resolve` lambda reading `pool->resources[idx]`), the allocator `Reset()`, and `ExecuteCommandLists` were all exposed; a fault anywhere in the SDK / d3d12.dll / AMD driver during the deep-queue churn killed the process with no recovery path.
- **Fix**: `dispatchFsr4` is now SEH-wrapped end-to-end (helper-function pattern, same as `dispatchPrepareSeh`/`dispatchGenerateSeh` in fg4_context.cpp) — an access violation anywhere in the native dispatch returns `JNI_FALSE`, which the existing Java dispatch-failure path turns into a **recoverable suspension** (2s backoff + resume probe) instead of a process death. `waitIdle` is SEH-wrapped the same way. Java side: the FSR4 dispatch block now also requires `sharedTexMgr.getColorAllocPtr() != 0` (parity with the VulkanMod path) so a half-failed reinit can never hand the native layer a freed pool.
- **Nested-fault hardening**: `Fsr4Context::dispatch`'s own `__except` handler no longer calls `Close()`/`ExecuteCommandLists` on the command list — a fault inside an `__except` handler is NOT re-dispatched to the outer SEH (it terminates the process), and the list may be corrupt after a crash. It now only signals + advances the fence, letting the next dispatch's allocator `Reset()` fail gracefully.
- **Persistent-failure escalation**: consecutive `dispatchFsr4` failures (including SEH-caught crashes) now count toward `fsr4HardDisabled` (MAX=5, reset on any success — a genuinely good DISPATCH, or a deliberate settings change via markDirty/applyAutoScale; **NOT** on reinit success — the 2026-08-30 crash-loop fix, since a crash→rebuild→crash loop would otherwise wipe the counter every rebuild and never latch) — a genuinely broken D3D12 path permanently routes to FSR1 for the session instead of the 2s-backoff probe re-arming it every 2s forever. Mirrors FG's `FG_MAX_RUNTIME_FAILURES` pattern.
- **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED via Windows WER (2026-08-05)**: the faulting module is **`atio6axx.dll`** (AMD OpenGL ICD) with exception `c0000005`, ~2 minutes after the hardened build shipped — i.e. a **GL-driver crash**, NOT fsrvk.dll and NOT the FSR SDK. SEH in our JNI frames cannot catch it (the fault is inside the LWJGL→driver call stack on GL calls, below our `__try`). The crash trigger is the FSR4-specific cross-API GL work during elytra saturation: `glCopyImageSubData` into/out of the D3D12-imported external-memory textures + the MV-gen compute, all running on a deeply saturated iGPU queue. FSR1/2/3 never touch the external-memory textures, which is why the crash is FSR4-only.
  - **Driver-crash cooldown**: `fsr4FenceTimedOutPrevFrame` — on the frame AFTER a GL fence timeout, the FSR4 block skips ALL cross-API work (no copies, no MV gen, no dispatch) and presents the previous FSR4 output, halving GL-driver exposure per storm episode. Set on every timeout path, cleared on any successful fence.
  - **Pre-dispatch saturation pre-check (worldMs spike → skip BEFORE any risky GL op runs)**: a fence timeout alone is not enough — the crash-prone copies + MV-gen execute BEFORE the fence wait, so the first storm frame would still run them (the driver faults fast — 3 crashes in 3 minutes on Aug 5). The FSR4 block now gates on `fsr4WorldMs` (t0BeginEnd → block entry) against an FSR4-PRIVATE calm-baseline EMA (`fsr4EmaWorldMs` — the shared SAT EMA never updates on FSR4 frames because this block returns before that guard runs), cold-start = any frame > 20ms is a spike. On a spike or prior-fence-timeout: skip ALL cross-API work (incl. the drain's output copy) and present the previous FSR4 output; after 3 consecutive such frames, suspend to FSR1 so a sustained storm shows fresh (FSR1) images instead of a frozen last-FSR4 frame. Applies to async AND sync/FG modes.
  - **Suspension + resume tuned for always-busy iGPUs (2026-08-05 v3/v4)**: the WER timeline proved this crash is NEW (Jul 27-29 crashes were a different bug — `BEX64` in `ucrtbase.dll`; Aug 5's 7 crashes are all `APPCRASH` `c0000005` in `atio6axx.dll`). Trigger: the 2026-08-05 resume-probe fix re-arms FSR4 mid-storm; earlier builds' 0ns-poll probe could never signal, so FSR4 silently stayed on FSR1 during storms (which never touches the D3D12-imported textures) — that accidental protection was what made the crash never happen before. Resume probe (v3): 0ns-poll failures between the throttled bounded waits are NEUTRAL (not resets) — on an always-busy iGPU the 0ns poll never signals (it guards the just-submitted scene render), so resetting on every failure made the 2-signal streak unreachable and FSR4 stayed suspended PERMANENTLY. Only a FAILED bounded wait (queue couldn't drain in 8ms) resets the streak; 2 signals (0ns poll or bounded wait) resume FSR4, so recovery is ~0.5s after a storm on normal-busy GPUs.   - **ASYNC FENCE: single 32ms cap, no 33ms extended wait (2026-08-06, review-corrected)**: async mode's fence wait is now ONE `glClientWaitSync` capped at 32ms (was the two-stage 8ms base + 33ms extended re-wait). Key insight: `glClientWaitSync` returns WHEN SIGNALED, not at the timeout — so the old extended wait consumed only the actual drain time, and for a steady render in the 8-33ms band it re-armed every frame (`fsr4ConsecutiveSkips` reset on success) with the 8ms base stage always timing out first (that stage was pure waste). One 32ms cap: healthy renders return in 1-3ms, a 10-30ms render returns at its own drain time, and only a genuinely >32ms queue (render <~30 FPS) times out → async skips THAT frame quietly (1-frame latency, previous FSR4 output already in mainRT) → suspension after 6 (GPU can't keep up anyway). Sync/FG keep 8ms + 33ms first-timeout recovery (same-frame output matters). A 16ms cap was tried first and REJECTED in review — it would have timed out on steady 16-33ms renders where the old extended wait completed, regressing FSR4 to FSR1 via skip-suspension.
   - **PARITY BYPASS at >=90% render scale (2026-08-06)**: at near-parity scales FSR4's upscale is at most 1.11x, but its fixed display-res round trip (D3D12 dispatch + external-memory output copy + cross-API sync, ~8-12ms on an iGPU) exceeds the render savings (19% fewer pixels at 90%) — net FPS LOSS. `fsr4ParityBypassActive()` routes through the cheap FSR1 blit+RCAS instead (no D3D12/external-memory/fence). It is part of `spatialFallbackActive()` so the two consumers stay consistent (onRenderBegin jitter zeroing + the FSR1 routing in onSceneRendered); the FSR4 dispatch block gate additionally excludes it (`!fsr4ParityNow`). FSR4 stays initialized — dropping the scale below 0.9 re-engages the temporal pipeline instantly, with a forced clean history rebuild on the transition (hasPrevFrame/prevViewProj froze while bypassed; reprojecting stale history would ghost a frame). GL-path only (VulkanMod has its own render-scale machinery).
   - **Fence-timeout policy UNIFIED across async + sync (2026-08-05 v4, "FSR4 only works with async off" fix)**: async mode originally had NO extended recovery wait — cheap-skip on every timeout + suspend after 2 consecutive timeouts. On this GPU-bound machine (90% util; the scene render alone routinely exceeds the 8ms GL fence cap) ordinary chunk-streaming timeouts cheap-skipped and hit the 2-strike suspension within a few frames, parking FSR4 on FSR1 most of the time; the ~0.5s sticky resume then re-suspended on the next spike (the user's exact report). Sync mode's policy — ONE 33ms extended re-wait on the first timeout of an episode, cheap-skips after, suspend only after `FSR4_SKIP_SUSPEND_AFTER` (6) consecutive timeouts — recovers those same ordinary timeouts and is proven crash-free on this driver (the 12:43 build's pre-check + cooldown gate is what actually stopped the atio6axx.dll crashes; sync mode's 33ms policy never crashed even before the pre-check existed). So async now uses sync's policy VERBATIM: the `fsr4AsyncMode` branch and its 2-strike suspension are deleted, and the pre-dispatch worldMs pre-check remains the PRIMARY storm defense for both modes. The 41ms-per-frame stacking regression cannot return for storms the pre-check can SEE (CPU-side worldMs spikes skip before the fence is created); a GPU-side-only saturation (worldMs calm) retains exactly sync mode's profile — extended-wait-success-every-frame resets the counter and stacks ~41ms/frame — which is the same policy proven crash-free and user-approved on this machine.
  - **Sticky resume**: the resume probe now requires **2 consecutive** signaled probes (`fsr4ProbeCleanStreak`) before re-arming FSR4 after a suspension — a single signaled probe can be a mid-storm lull, and re-entering the cross-API path then is exactly when the driver faults.
  - **First-init deferral — "FSR4 crashes when LOADING into the game" (2026-08-05 v5)**: the user's decisive A/B test — load with FSR4 on → crash; load with FSR1 and switch to FSR4 later → no crash — isolates the fault to the INIT path, not dispatch. The FSR4 init (importing the 7 D3D12 external-memory handles into GL + creating the D3D12 device) runs inside `onRenderBegin` WHILE the world-load storm saturates the GPU queue; the cross-API GL ops fault `atio6axx.dll` exactly then (the WER crash clusters at 11:29/11:32 and 12:22/12:24 were repeated launch attempts, not elytra flights). The dispatch-time guards (pre-check, cooldown, suspension) never see init. Fix: the GL-path first init is DEFERRED until a frame-duration EMA (`fsr4InitEmaMs`, updated at the END of `onRenderBegin` so the init block reads the previous frame's duration) is calm (≤ `FSR4_INIT_CALM_MS` = 26ms) — OR a 6s grace (`FSR4_INIT_GRACE_MS`) elapses, so FSR4 always engages (never leave the user on FSR1 indefinitely). While deferred, `spatialFallbackActive()` (`(isFsr4()||isFrameGenDx12()) && !fsr4Initialized`) routes to the FSR1 spatial fallback — the exact proven crash-free path — with jitter zeroed. `GL43C.glFinish()` drains the queue right before the pool import (the driver faults when imports run against queued storm work; one-time init cost). `lastFsr4ContextChange` is NOT touched while deferring, so the 500ms debounce never blocks the per-frame retry. A mid-game config switch to FSR4 inits immediately (EMA already calm) — preserving the user's working manual workaround. VulkanMod path untouched (D3D12-only, no GL driver involved).
  - **User action still required**: update the AMD driver (Adrenalin) — a GL-driver access violation is a driver bug; the mod-side mitigation only shrinks the exposure window. Re-check WER (`/c/ProgramData/Microsoft/Windows/WER/ReportArchive/AppCrash_javaw*/Report.wer`, `Sig[3].Value` = faulting module) after each repro.

### Known Issues
	- GL sync timeout during heavy world loading (16ms budget insufficient when many chunks compile)
	- FSR4 is D3D12-only — requires AMD FSR4-compatible GPU
	- 60+ MB DLLs in JAR (`amd_fsr4_api.dll` ~28MB, `amd_fidelityfx_loader_dx12.dll` ~28MB)

### VulkanMod heap corruption on settings-open — FIXED (2026-08-05 v6)
- **Report**: VulkanMod + FSR4 via gradle crashed when opening the settings screen. WER: `c0000374` (STATUS_HEAP_CORRUPTION) in ntdll (`PCH_78_FROM_ntdll`) — a DIFFERENT class from the GL path's `atio6axx.dll` `c0000005`. Heap corruption is detected by the heap manager at the NEXT big allocation — opening settings (widget/texture allocation storm) is the detector, not the cause.
- **Root cause 1 — broken pipeline-test allocator protocol (`dispatchFsr4VkMod` bypass path)**: the `g_debugBypassFsr4` CopyResource path recorded into `D3D12Device`'s round-robin command list WITHOUT the protocol `Fsr4Context::dispatch` uses. It never waited for the other allocator's fence, never `Reset()` the current allocator, never re-bound the list after `advanceAllocator()` flipped allocators. So every frame it called `Close()` on an already-closed list and `ExecuteCommandLists` re-submitted the PREVIOUS frame's command buffer — with pool resource pointers that were freed on recreate — while the allocator memory was still in flight → D3D12 use-after-free → heap corruption. Fix: replicate `Fsr4Context::dispatch`'s protocol (wait other allocator fence (2s) → `commandAllocator()->Reset()` → `commandList()->Reset(...)` → record → close → execute → signal → advance).
- **Root cause 2 — pool destroy without D3D12 drain (`releaseFsr4Pool`)**: `D3D12SharedResources::destroy()` calls `Release()` on all 7 resources with no fence wait. Opening settings triggers a pool recreate (config change → `needsRecreate`); if the previous frame's dispatch (bypass `signalFence` is on the DEVICE fence, which `Fsr4Context::finish()` never waits on) is still executing, releasing those resources = use-after-free. Fix: `releaseFsr4Pool` now drains the D3D12 queue to `max(currentAllocatorFenceValue, otherAllocatorFenceValue)` (5s cap) before `pool->destroy()`.
- **Root cause 3 — VkImage destroy without VK queue drain (`destroyVkModImages`)**: destroyed the external VkImages / released the D3D12 keep-alive refs without waiting for VulkanMod's graphics queue — the previous frame's `recordFrameCopyOps` (Copy1/Copy2) may still reference them (use-after-free on a driver already proven fragile with external memory). Fix: `vkQueueWaitIdle(g_vkModGraphicsQueue)` at the top of `destroyVkModImages()` (rare — recreate/teardown only).
- **Refcount leak fixed**: `importOutputFromD3D12Pool` dropped `g_vkModD3D12Output` without the matching `Release()` for its keep-alive `AddRef()` — one D3D12 resource leaked per recreate. Now releases.
- **RENDER SCALE + FSR4 COMBINED (2026-08-05, "FSR4 looks like FSR1" — the SECOND root cause)**: the user's config has BOTH `version=FSR4` AND `vulkanModRenderScale=true`, and the diag log showed the session ran ENTIRELY in render-scale mode (only `recordRenderScaleBlit ... (LINEAR)` lines; zero FSR4 init/dispatch) — so the "FSR1 look" was a plain bilinear LINEAR blit, worse than FSR1. Render-scale mode was previously MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE with FSR4 (init gated on `!renderScaleConfigured()`, dispatch gated on `!renderScaleActive()`, and the mixin's render-scale branch returned before the D3D12 path). Now they combine — this is exactly what FSR4 is FOR: render at 25% (480×300) for the perf win, FSR4 temporally upscales to native (1920×1201):
  - **Split image dims (native)**: `g_vkModImageWidth/Height` = RENDER (input) dims, new `g_vkModOutputWidth/Height` = DISPLAY (output) dims. `initExternalImages(renderW, renderH, dispW, dispH)` sets both. Input/depth/MV/reactive imports + MV-gen dispatch use render dims; the OUTPUT VkImage import uses display dims (importing a display-size D3D12 allocation with render-size extents would alias out of bounds on Copy2).
  - **`recordVkModFsr4ScaledCopy(cmdBuf, srcImage, srcW, srcH, dstImage, dstW, dstH)`**: the render-scale + FSR4 copy — Copy1 scaled-framebuffer color → pool[0] input at RENDER size (srcW×srcH), Copy2 pool[4] output at DISPLAY size (dstW×dstH) → swapchain, with the full barrier set (src COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL→TRANSFER_SRC→back; input→GENERAL; output full MEMORY barrier for the D3D12 write; dst→PRESENT_SRC). Extents are CLAMPED per-axis to the actual image dims (during the scale-change recreate window the framebuffer can land at the new scale before the pool recreates — they run on separate debounced schedules — so an unclamped Copy1 could write out of bounds).
  - **FSR4 init no longer gated on `!renderScaleConfigured()`**: when render scale is configured, `renderW/renderH = Math.max(1, Math.round(display * getRenderScale()))` (same formula as the mixin's createResources) and `dispW/dispH = display`. The D3D12 pool already supported render≠display (the GL path's pool was 1092x683 → 1680x1050).
  - **Dispatch `!renderScaleActive()` gate removed**: FSR4 dispatches in BOTH modes; with render scale the SDK upscales render→display.
  - **Mixin end() render-scale branch** (gates on `fsr4VkModActive` ONLY — active = all three pool-texture imports succeeded):
    - **Bootstrap frame (`!fsr4InputPopulated` — pool just (re)created)**: the dispatch gate in onRenderBegin was blocked for one frame, so pool[4] has NEVER been written — Copy2 would present undefined content. Record `recordVkModFsr4ScaledCopy(..., includeCopy2=false)` (Copy1 scene→pool[0] + MV-gen only, output untouched) + `recordVkModMvGen` so the NEXT frame's dispatch has fresh inputs, present THIS frame via `recordRenderScaleBlit`, then set `fsr4InputPopulated=true`. Valid same-command-buffer ordering: barrier 7 restores src to COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL before the blit's own src transition.
    - **Steady state (`fsr4InputPopulated`)**: `waitForD3D12Output()` then `recordVkModFsr4ScaledCopy(..., includeCopy2=true)` + `recordVkModMvGen` (real FSR4 upscale, 1-frame latency consistent with the async model).
    - **TIMEOUT**: `recordRenderScaleBlit` fallback ONLY — DO NOT record Copy1/MV-gen: this frame's D3D12 dispatch is still IN FLIGHT reading pool[0..3], overwriting them mid-read would corrupt its inputs (data race, not just aging). fsr4InputPopulated stays true so inputs age gracefully.
    - **Why `fsr4InputPopulated` does NOT gate the branch (2026-08-05, the "quality did not change one bit" bug)**: it's set to true ONLY inside the FSR4 branch — gating the branch on it is a BOOTSTRAP DEADLOCK (first frame: flag false → blit branch → flag never set → bilinear blit forever). The flag correctly gates the DISPATCH in onRenderBegin (don't feed the SDK an unpopulated pool), never the copy recording. On D3D12 wait TIMEOUT it must NOT early-return (in render-scale mode the scene is in the SCALED fb, not the swapchain — an early return presents stale swapchain content).
  - **Shared `storeFrameFence(Renderer)` helper**: extracted so the render-scale FSR4 branch stores the frame fence too (that branch returns before the D3D12 path's fence store; without it the next frame's `waitForFrameCopy()` fell back to a full `vkQueueWaitIdle` GPU drain). Stored OUTSIDE the self-blit guard (the world-join transitional frame still renders the scene into the swapchain — a skipped copy must not skip the fence store or the next frame drains the queue).
  - **Copy extent clamps (native)**: `recordVkModFsr4ScaledCopy` clamps Copy1/Copy2 extents per-axis to the actual image dims (`std::min` with g_vkModImageWidth/Height and g_vkModOutputWidth/Height) — during the scale-change recreate window the framebuffer can land at the new scale BEFORE the pool recreates (separate debounced schedules), so an unclamped Copy1 could write out of bounds. NO 0-extent early return: a zero-extent `vkCmdCopyImage` is a valid no-op, and returning after the barriers would leave input stuck in TRANSFER_DST + src in TRANSFER_SRC (broken layout tracking).

- **VulkanMod FSR4 now runs the REAL FSR4 SDK (2026-08-05, "output looks like FSR1" bug — root cause 1)**: the pipeline-test bypass (`g_debugBypassFsr4` CopyResource color→output) was the ROOT CAUSE of "FSR4 looks like FSR1" under VulkanMod — it was a 1:1 passthrough, the SDK never ran. It was auto-enabled in `importOutputFromD3D12Pool` + `setDebugPipelineTest(true)` because the pool's depth/MV/reactive inputs were NEVER filled: the GL MV-gen compute shaders can't run without a GL context. Fix — a real VK compute path in `fsrvk.dll`:
  - **`fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`** (compiled to embedded SPIR-V in `fsr4_vk_mvgen_spv.h` via glslangValidator at build time): one 8x8 dispatch writes ALL THREE FSR4 inputs into the imported pool textures — depth=constant 0.5 (R32_SFLOAT), motion vectors (RG16F, computed from invCurrVP/prevVP push constants, constant ndcZ=0 — same conventions as the GL MV_GEN_CS), reactive (R8, MV-magnitude smoothstep(0.5,16) + motion floor, no shading histogram — needs ping-pong color textures, marginal under VKMod). Push constants = 2 mat4 = **exactly 128 bytes** (a render-size ivec2 would exceed the guaranteed minimum → shader reads `imageSize()` instead).
  - **`importPoolTextureAsVkImage(poolPtr, poolIndex, slot, vkFormat)`**: generic D3D12→VK import for pool[1] DEPTH (R32_SFLOAT=100), [2] MV (R16G16_SFLOAT=88), [3] REACTIVE (R8_UNORM=9) as STORAGE images aliasing the shared memory (mirrors `importInputFromD3D12Pool`, usage STORAGE|TRANSFER_DST).
  - **`recordVkModMvGen(cmdBuf)`** (called by `VulkanModMainPassMixin.end()` right AFTER `recordFrameCopyOps`, BEFORE the inFlightFences store — so the frame fence covers the compute and the next frame's `waitForFrameCopy()` + D3D12 dispatch see valid inputs): lazily builds a compute pipeline (shader module, 3× STORAGE_IMAGE descriptor set, push-constant layout), records UNDEFINED→GENERAL barriers (full per-frame overwrite makes discard legal), binds, dispatches, then GENERAL→GENERAL MEMORY_WRITE→MEMORY_READ barrier (same cross-API pattern as recordFrameCopyOps barrier 6/7) to make the writes visible to the D3D12 shared-memory read.
  - **STAGING-IMAGE FIX (2026-08-05, the real "no edge smoothing" root cause)**: the VK MV-gen originally wrote depth=0.5/MV/reactive via raw `imageStore` DIRECTLY into the D3D12-imported pool images — but on this AMD driver compute `imageStore` into external-memory images does NOT persist (the exact GL-path bug AGENTS.md documents; the GL path routes through MANAGED staging → `glCopyImageSubData`). The FSR4 SDK therefore read depth≈0 → classified every pixel as sky/disoccluded → discarded ALL history every frame → the output was a pure spatial upscale: with jitter=0,0 it looked like FSR1/bilinear; with Halton jitter fed it shook (the jitter showed through an un-accumulated frame) and edges stayed jagged ("shaking but no edge smoothing"). Fix mirrors the GL workaround: MV-gen now writes into **VK-OWNED staging images** (depth/MV/reactive, device-local, STORAGE|TRANSFER_SRC — reliable imageStore), then `vkCmdCopyImage` staging→imported (transfer copies DO persist — Copy1's vkCmdCopyImage into the imported color image is how the scene reaches the SDK). Barrier chain: staging UNDEFINED→GENERAL → dispatch → staging GENERAL→TRANSFER_SRC_OPTIMAL → imported UNDEFINED→TRANSFER_DST_OPTIMAL → 3× copy → imported TRANSFER_DST_OPTIMAL→GENERAL (TRANSFER_WRITE→MEMORY_READ, visible to D3D12). Staging images created in `ensureVkModMvGenPipeline`, freed in `destroyVkModMvGenPipeline` (views→images→memory); forward declaration lives at GLOBAL scope before the unnamed namespace (the namespace closes before the MV-gen section, so a declaration inside it would name a different function — MSVC C2129).
  - **`setVkModMvGenMatrices(invCurrVP, prevVP)`** (called from FSRProcessor.onSceneRendered VKMod block): the shader needs clean unjittered matrices; under VulkanMod the projection hook doesn't capture the world projection (`far=-986895.06` garbage in the diag log), so the VKMod block rebuilds `lastProj` from the camera via `updateProjectionFromCamera()` and computes inv(currViewProj) + prev view-proj (identity on the first frame — harmless because frame 1 dispatches with reset=true).
  - **VKMod FSR4 jitter (2026-08-05, "no quality change / no edge smoothing" — root cause 3)**: the diag log showed the SDK dispatching every frame with `jitter=(0.0000,0.0000)` — the VKMod dispatch hardcoded `0.0f, 0.0f` and onRenderBegin zeroed jitterX/Y. FSR4's temporal edge smoothing IS the accumulation of jittered sub-pixel samples — with zero jitter the SDK has no sub-pixel sample diversity and degrades to a spatial reconstruction (looked like FSR1/bilinear; OpenGL mode feeds Halton jitter so it smooths). Fix: the VKMod block now advances Halton jitter (same `jitterManager.next` as GL) when `isFsr4() && fsr4Initialized && !fsr4Suspended` (NOT `isTemporalUpscaler()` — FSR2/3 under VKMod fall back to the bilinear blit which has no de-jitter pass; advancing jitter there would jitter the world and the blit would present a ±0.5px shake). The dispatch passes `prevJitterX/Y` (captured BEFORE this frame's advance) because the D3D12 pool holds the PREVIOUS frame's scene, which was rendered with the previous jitter via the vanilla projection hook (the `captureAndJitter` @ModifyArg in renderLevel — VulkanMod consumes the vanilla projection buffer, so the jittered matrix reaches its shaders). One-frame alignment: frame 1 advances jitter to J₁ (no dispatch — pool unpopulated), frame 2 captures prevJitter=J₁ and dispatches with it, matching the pooled frame-1 scene; first dispatch is reset=true so history is discarded anyway. `scaledWidth/Height` are set to the ACTUAL VKMod render dims (scaled framebuffer when render-scale on, else display) so `clipSpaceJitter` converts against the true render size — when render scale is off, the config-derived scaledWidth would be `round(display*qualityScale)` while the scene renders at display size (wrong jitter magnitude).
  - **VRenderSystem.projection override (2026-08-05, the "shaking when moving, still pixelated" bug — root cause 4)**: CORRECTS the parent bullet's claim — under VulkanMod the jittered matrix does NOT reach the world render. Decompiled-verified: our `captureAndJitter` @ModifyArg returns a jittered COPY; the vanilla GPU buffer slice gets jittered, but the LOCAL VARIABLE in renderLevel keeps the original matrix. VulkanMod's own `render.GameRendererMixin` is an `@Inject` at the `RenderSystem.setProjectionMatrix` INVOKE that captures a `@Local Matrix4f` (the ORIGINAL unjittered local) into the static `VRenderSystem.projection`, and its chunk renderer (`chunk.LevelRendererMixin` → `WorldRenderer.renderSectionLayer` → `VRenderSystem.applyMVP(modelView, projection)`) renders the terrain with that field. So the world rendered UNJITTERED while the SDK was told jitter=(prevJitter) → phantom jitter: the SDK de-jitters a frame that was never jittered → shake (amplified by motion) + history reprojection misaligns every frame → SDK rejects history → spatial-only (pixelated, no temporal edge smoothing). Fix: `GameRendererMixin` gets a NEW `@Inject` at the same `RenderSystem.setProjectionMatrix(GpuBufferSlice, ProjectionType)` INVOKE (`ordinal = 0` = the LEVEL upload; the hand upload is ordinal 1 and deliberately untouched) that calls `FSRProcessor.applyVulkanModProjectionOverride()`. Mixin priority is lowered to `100` (< VulkanMod's default 1000) so our handler applies AFTER theirs at the same injection point. The override gates on `VulkanModCompat.isLoaded()` (NOT `isActive()` — VulkanMod's renderer reads the field whenever it is loaded, independent of our D3D12 interop) + `jitterAppliedThisFrame`, then reflectively reads `net.vulkanmod.vulkan.VRenderSystem.projection` and jitters VulkanMod's OWN matrix in place via the shared `clipSpaceJitter()` translation (`translation(jit).mul(cur)`) — we do NOT substitute our vanilla `lastProjJittered` because VulkanMod builds its projection with an infinite far plane (`depth.CameraMixin.infiniteZFar`), and preserving its exact matrix avoids far-plane/fog divergence. The reflection Field is cached in a static (no per-frame class scan); a rename degrades to a no-op logged ONCE (`vkModProjectionOverrideFailedLogged`) so a silent regression is detectable. KNOWN LIMITATIONS (not regressions): (a) only the TERRAIN path uses `VRenderSystem.projection` — entity/HUD passes go through `calculateMVP()` reading the `projectionMatrix` MappedBuffer, which the override doesn't touch, so entities render unjittered (partially-jittered frame — strictly better than fully-unjittered; entity-path fix is future work if artifacts show); (b) priority=100 applies to ALL handlers in GameRendererMixin (low risk — no other mod targets those injection points). Full decompile-verified trace in `VULKANMOD_FSR4_FINDINGS.md`.
  - **Camera guard fixed**: the VKMod dispatch near/far extraction now validates BOTH bounds (near>1000 || far<=0 || far>1e7 || far<=near → fallback 0.1/1000) — the old near-only guard let `near=0.50 far=-986895.06` through, breaking the SDK's history accumulation.
  - **Frame-1 gate (`fsr4InputPopulated`)**: the VKMod dispatch is gated on a flag set by the end() hook after the first Copy1+MV-gen recording (cleared on init/recreate) — the first frame's pool[0] is undefined memory (Copy1 hasn't run yet), so dispatching would feed the SDK garbage and present the garbage via Copy2 (a world-join flash).
  - **Review fixes (2026-08-05)**: push constants use ONE `float[32]` global, not two `float[16]`s — C++ doesn't guarantee global adjacency, and vkCmdPushConstants reads 128 CONTIGUOUS bytes (the two-array version could silently push garbage prevVP → ghosting). `ensureVkModMvGenPipeline` destroys stale pipeline objects before rebuild (idempotent — a re-import leak otherwise leaks a shader module + pipeline layout + pipeline + descriptor pool per resize). `waitForD3D12Output()` now returns boolean and the end() hook SKIPS all copies on fence timeout — Copy2 against a half-written D3D12 output is a data race; the swapchain image holds the raw scene and presents natively (the VKMod equivalent of the GL path's graceful single-present).

### Performance optimizations (2026-08-04, no visual change)
- **Rate-limited per-frame native logging**: `waitIdle: FSR4 finish OK` (jni_bridge.cpp) and `[Fg4Context] generate: ... OK` (fg4_context.cpp) only log when the wait/dispatch is slow (≥6ms) or every 128th frame. Previously each logged via `fopen/fclose` + `fprintf(stderr)` + `fflush` on EVERY frame (53k waitIdle + 4k generate lines in one session) — measurable CPU waste.
- **Constant depth filled once per pool**: `copyDepthToShared` (0.5 mid-depth) now runs only when `fsr4DepthFilledForPool != getColorAllocPtr()` (i.e., after pool (re)allocation), not every frame. Removes a full-screen R32F copy + FBO clear/bind/unbind per frame; the shared depth content never changes between frames.
- **Removed the pre-copy `glMemoryBarrier`** before the color `glCopyImageSubData` (GL orders copies after the render pass; the `GL_ALL_BARRIER_BITS` before the fence is the one that matters).
- **`shouldDebug()` throttled to every 600 frames** (~10s) instead of 60: the debug block creates 3 GL textures + multiple `glGetTextureSubImage` readbacks + heavy logging — a visible ~1-per-second hitch at 60fps. Cheap `[DIAG]`/`[DEBUG_FSR4]` logs still use their own `%60` cadence.
- **Async FSR4 overlap (`config.fsr4Async`, default ON, FSR4 mode only)**: the FSR4 dispatch is submitted WITHOUT `waitIdle()`; the fence wait + output copy are deferred to the START of the next frame's FSR4 block (`fsr4AsyncPending` drain). By then the 3–6ms of D3D12 GPU work has already run concurrently with the present + the next scene render, so the wait is ~0ms instead of stalling the CPU mid-frame. Tradeoff: the presented frame is the PREVIOUS frame's upscaled output (~1 frame input latency — pixels identical, just one frame behind). First frame + FG mode stay fully synchronous (FG needs the same-frame output for HUDLessColor/presentColor). No native changes: `dispatch()` already submits async and `destroy()` drains via `finish()`. The drain MUST run before the shared-input color copy (the in-flight dispatch reads it). `fsr4AsyncPending` cleared at every teardown/recreate site so a stale flag never copies garbage from a fresh pool.

### FSR4 depth/MV write path (Radeon 860M, 2026-08-04)
- **readTexPixels depth-readback bug FIXED (2026-08-06, "probe readback failed" on 26.1 instance)**: the FBO fallback attached the texture to GL_DEPTH_ATTACHMENT while it was STILL attached to GL_COLOR_ATTACHMENT0 — a depth texture on a color attachment keeps the FBO permanently incomplete, so the depth retry could never succeed (the probe logged "readback failed" and real depth silently never engaged). Fix: detach COLOR_ATTACHMENT0 (attach texture 0) BEFORE the DEPTH_ATTACHMENT attach, and detach any leftover DEPTH_ATTACHMENT at the start of every color read. The GL45 path got the same treatment: glGetTextureSubImage(GL_RED) on a depth texture is GL_INVALID_OPERATION — retry with GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT.
- **FSR4 pre-check EMA cold-start FIX (2026-08-06, "went to fsr1 at world-join")**: the EMA seeded only on a CALM frame; on a GPU-bound machine (iGPU + Voxy/C2ME/Create at 2580x1080) no frame was calm, so the EMA never seeded, every frame re-entered cold-start and counted as a spike, and FSR4 suspended to FSR1 after FSR4_PRE_SUSPEND_AFTER (3) frames — permanently. Now the FIRST sample seeds the baseline unconditionally (spikes can't fire until a baseline exists) — a stormy machine settles into a storm-appropriate baseline within a few frames and FSR4 stays active.
- **FSR4-fallback in-game notification (2026-08-06)**: edge-triggered chat message (`\u00a7e[FSR] ...` via `client.player.sendSystemMessage`) when FSR4 suspends to FSR1 (GPU saturation / fence timeouts / dispatch failure) or hard-disables, and a "FSR4 restored" message when it resumes. Detection lives in `onRenderBegin` right after `insideFrame=true` (state settled by last frame's onSceneRendered), gated on `isFsr4()||isFrameGenDx12() && config.enabled`, edge-detected via `lastNotifiedFsr4Fallback` with a 10s cooldown (`FSR4_FALLBACK_NOTIFY_COOLDOWN_MS`) so storm flip-flopping can't spam chat. Deliberately does NOT notify the `!fsr4Initialized` init-deferral window (normal world-join transient, resolves within 6s). Best-effort (try/catch) — never disturbs the render path.
- **STALE prev-color T&C guard (2026-08-06, "edge smoothing stopped even when standing still")**: the T&C prev-color snapshot is NOT taken on skip paths (pre-check spike, fence timeout, dispatch failure — all return before the copy). On resume, MV-gen compared the current frame against a snapshot that was potentially many frames old / from a different camera position → reactive fired everywhere → the SDK discarded ALL history → spatial output. Fix: `fsr4PrevColorFresh` flag cleared on every skip/suspend path, set true right after each snapshot, and `uTCActive` now gates on `hasPrevFrame && fsr4PrevColorFresh` — one frame of T&C delay after an episode instead of a global reactive storm.

- **FSR4 sky/sun/clouds NOT upscaling = MV-gen force-reactive bucket (2026-08-08, "the terrain is upscaling but not the sun and clouds" fix)**: both FSR4 MV-gens (GL `MV_GEN_CS` in FSRProcessor.java AND the VK `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`) had a `depth >= 0.999` early-return that wrote `mv = 0` + **fully reactive = 1.0** for sky/sun/clouds — telling the SDK to render them SPATIALLY every frame. Terrain accumulated jittered sub-pixel samples (smooth) while the sun/clouds were re-rendered fresh at render scale → blocky/aliased edges (at 0.7 scale the sun/cloud silhouettes are the most visible fresh-render pixels). This was the FSR2/3 problem solved 2026-08-06 (v3 sky change) that FSR4 never received. Fix (mirrors FSR2/3 v3): removed the force-reactive early-return in BOTH shaders — with REAL depth the far plane IS resolvable (depth≈1.0 → ndcZ≈0.998 = the sky's true depth), so sky reprojection is EXACT (rotation MVs are depth-independent; translation parallax at the far plane ≈ 0) and the SDK accumulates the sun/clouds like terrain. Ghosting stays bounded by the mvLen reactive ramp (fast turns → fresh), the T&C comparison (drifting clouds that outrun the 5x5 window → fresh), and the SDK's internal confidence. ALSO fixed the GL constant-depth fallback: `ndcZ = 0.0` maps to z ≈ 2·near·far/(near+far) ≈ **1 block** (the same near-plane assumption that caused "jagged water" on the VK path pre-real-depth) → sky/far MVs ~100x too large during motion → SDK rejected history → blocky sky on GL sessions too. Raised to `ndcZ = 0.999` (z ≈ 100 blocks), matching FSR2/3's MV-gen. VK SPIR-V regenerated (`fsr4_vk_mvgen_spv.h` + `.spv`, 9580 bytes, glslangValidator clean); GL shader is inline Java. Known remaining limitation: the cutout-edge detector (`colorEdge` at cloud/sun boundaries) fires on `mvLen > 0.5` during camera ROTATION on the VK path (no CPU cam-motion gate there — push constants are exactly 128 bytes), so cloud/sun edges stay aliased while looking around fast; still-camera and translation are smooth. Follow-up candidate: shader-computed camera-translation proxy (reproject the screen center; pure rotation leaves it fixed) to gate the VK cutout. **v2 (2026-08-08, "the edges are slanted instead of straight" fix)**: the first fix made the sun/clouds accumulate — but the user then saw SLANTED edges (the horizon/gradient bands). Root cause: the sky region mixes camera-LOCKED content (the gradient/horizon — ZERO true screen motion under any camera movement) with world-ANCHORED content (sun/moon/stars/clouds). A single far-plane MV cannot serve both: under a yaw even the screen center gets ~f·Δyaw px of world MV — correct for the sun, WRONG for the camera-locked gradient. The SDK accumulates the misaligned gradient history and its clamp does NOT reject it the way FSR2/3's box clamp does → the horizon/gradient smears slanted while panning. Fix (both MV-gens, mirrors FSR2/3's `isNewSample` sky gate): a SKY MOTION POLICY — sky accumulates ONLY while ~static (smooth sun/cloud edges, the v1 win) and goes FULLY reactive the moment the camera genuinely pans → the SDK renders it fresh → straight edges. Fresh-sky-while-moving is the long-accepted pre-fix behavior (aliased but straight); smooth-when-still is retained. Sky classifier: VK uses `depth >= 0.999`; the GL constant-depth path (no depth) uses the relaxed color heuristic (luma>0.58 && chroma<0.12 white / luma>0.55 && b>1.15·max blue, screen-Y > 0.5). **v3 (2026-08-08, bob-safe gates — the v2 threshold re-blockied the sun/clouds while STANDING STILL)**: the v2 `smoothstep(0.2, 1.0, mvLen)` threshold was below the idle bob's far-plane MV contribution. The bob is a real ~0.002-0.005 rad/frame rotation; at f≈600 that produces **1.2-3.0 render px of far-plane MV** — crossing v2's 1-px full-reactive point → the sky flushed fresh every bob cycle → blocky sun/clouds returned exactly when standing still. Two calibrated gates, per-path constraints: (1) **GL path** — replaced the MV gate with a CPU bob-excluded ROTATION flag `uCamSkyMotion` (set from the existing per-frame `camRotDelta` quaternion delta in the FSR4 dispatch block; fires only when `camRotDelta > 0.006` rad/frame ≈ FSR2/3's `uCamStillExact` rotation threshold, ~2x the bob's 0.002-0.005; NaN on the first frame → accumulate). ROTATION-ONLY by design: pure translation (walking with a steady mouse) has ~0 far-plane parallax, so it must NOT flush the sky fresh or the sun/clouds re-blocky while walking. When the flag fires, the whole sky region goes fully reactive (`reactive = max(reactive, uCamSkyMotion)`); when 0, sky accumulates. New uniform + location + dispatch-site setter (`mv_uCamSkyMotion`). (2) **VK path** — no CPU signal available (push constants are exactly 128 bytes = 2 mat4; a float would exceed the guaranteed minimum), so the gate is a shader-side equivalent: **angular far-plane MV** — `focalPx = min(renderSize.x/(2·uInvCurrVP[0][0]), renderSize.y/(2·uInvCurrVP[1][1]))` (proj[0][0] = 1/tan(fovX/2), so focalPx = the focal length in render px, derived from the existing push-constant matrix — no new uniform), then `reactive = max(reactive, step(0.006, mvLen / focalPx))`. Hard threshold **0.006 rad/frame ≈ 20°/s pan, bit-matching the GL flag's 0.006 rad**. Expressed in ANGULAR units because a fixed-px threshold is focal-dependent: the first attempt (`smoothstep(3.5, 6.0, mvLen)`) was calibrated at f≈600 (the 25%-scale focal, 1680-wide display → 420-wide render), but the user runs 0.7 scale on a 3440×1440 ultrawide where f_h ≈ 1720 — the same bob (0.002-0.005 rad/frame) produces **3.4-8.6 px of far-plane MV, straight through a 3.5-6.0 px gate** → re-blocky sun/clouds standing still on exactly the machine being fixed (reviewer catch 2026-08-08). `min(x,y)` focal is conservative (overestimates the angle when axis focals differ — trips sooner). Both validated with glslangValidator; VK SPIR-V regenerated (10260 bytes); GL shader extracted + validated; `compileClientJava` + `build` green, jar redeployed. **v4 (2026-08-08, "the smoothing for the sky was happening before" fix — v3 over-corrected)**: the user's next report was that the sky smoothing they had gained was GONE. v3's pan-reactive policy was right that the gradient must not accumulate world-anchored history, but wrong in mechanism: gating the WHOLE sky region on a 0.006 rad/frame rotation threshold flushed the sun/clouds fresh during ANY normal looking around (20°/s at 60fps is a gentle pan), so the sun/clouds were blocky nearly all the time — the exact complaint from v1.
