The correct split is CONTENT-BASED, not camera-motion-based: distinguish the two sky regimes by LOCAL CONTRAST of the current frame (already computed as `colorSpread = length(cMax-cMin)` by the 3x3 window the cutout detector uses): (1) **FLAT gradient/horizon** (spread < 0.08 — the blue/white gradient is ~10-20x below any feature) is camera-LOCKED → **mv = 0**: exact reprojection under ALL camera motion (the gradient genuinely stays fixed on screen), so history accumulates in place → smooth gradient AND straight horizon at any pan speed — the v2 slant AND the v3 re-blockying both impossible by construction. (2) **DETAILED sun/moon/stars/clouds** (spread ≥ 0.08) keep the real far-plane reprojection MV → world-anchored, so the SDK follows them across the screen → smooth during pans; fast turns make mvLen large → the existing MV ramp (`smoothstep(0.5,16,mvLen)`) discards → fresh, masked by motion. Also (3) the **cutout detector is now EXCLUDED for far-plane pixels** (`!isFarPlane` VK / `!isSkyPx` GL): the sun/clouds are NOT thin alpha-cutout geometry, and without the exclusion the sun disc edge (spread > 0.45) forced the sun fully reactive during pans through the cutout term even though its MV was correct — a second independent path that kept the sun blocky while turning. Removed: the GL `uCamSkyMotion` uniform + setter + lookup + diag arg (its rotation-only semantics are superseded — pure translation has ~0 far-plane parallax so the contrast split handles walking correctly too), and the VK angular `step(0.006, mvLen/focalPx)` gate. Both shaders revalidated with glslangValidator; VK SPIR-V regenerated (9904 bytes — smaller: gate removed); `compileClientJava` + `build` green. Knob: the `0.08` flatness threshold (gradient < 0.02, cloud interiors 0.05-0.2 — clean separation; below 0.05 more cloud interiors get camera-locked → mild pan smear on very smooth clouds; above 0.10 the horizon gradient band near fog starts keeping MVs). Known limitation: the VK non-render-scale branch binds a 1x1 gray dummy as uColor → spread = 0 → ALL sky classified flat → sun smears during pans there (the render-scale path — what the user runs — has real color); GL path always has real uInputColor. **v5 (2026-08-08, "it looks like it is trying to smoothen it but it is smoothing the terrain more than the sky" fix)**: with v4's accumulation restored, the user saw the sky STILL lag terrain in smoothness. Root cause — the KNOWN v1 limitation that v4 only half-fixed: the **cutout-edge detector** (3x3 `colorSpread > 0.45` — the sun/cloud white-vs-blue edges qualify) fires whenever `mvLen > 0.5`, which is true during the idle bob, walking, AND panning (bob rotation alone is 3.4-8.6 render px of far-plane MV at the user's 0.7-scale ultrawide). v4 excluded far-plane pixels (`isFarPlane`/`isSkyPx`) from the cutout — but **clouds carry REAL depth below 0.999** (y≈192 at far≈40920 → depth ≈ 0.997), so they are NOT far-plane → the cutout kept forcing cloud edges fully reactive (reactive=1.0, pure spatial render) during ANY motion → blocky clouds while the terrain around them accumulated jittered samples. Fix (both MV-gens): the cutout's **colorEdge term is now disabled for SKY-LIKE pixels** — `isSkyLike = isFarPlane || (curLuma > 0.5 && curChroma < 0.25)` (bright + desaturated: white/gray clouds, snow silhouettes, the sun; the blue gradient is already far-plane). Clouds/sun keep their CORRECT real-depth MVs + the MV ramp → they accumulate like terrain → smooth edges during bob/walk/pan. The **alphaEdge term stays active** for sky-like pixels — a real 0<->1 alpha-cutout silhouette (white flowers, leaves) still gets the forced-fresh protection, so genuine thin foreground geometry isn't regressed. Terrain is unaffected (grass/flowers/water are saturated → not sky-like). Both shaders revalidated with glslangValidator; VK SPIR-V regenerated (10920 bytes — the per-pixel sky-color test adds instructions); `compileClientJava` + `build` green. Knob: the `0.25` chroma bound (clouds 0.02-0.2; a bright saturated sky-tinted object would keep the cutout). **v6 (2026-08-08, "the clouds and sun are still jagged" fix — the T&C bob misfire + the MV ramp)**: with the cutout no longer forcing clouds fresh, the sun/cloud edges STILL read jagged while terrain reads smooth. Two remaining terms throttle them (both stem from the user's focal being ~1720 render px at 0.7 scale on the 3440-wide ultrawide — the idle bob rotation of 0.002-0.005 rad/frame = 3-8 render px of motion at EVERY depth): (1) **T&C misfire** — the VK T&C has NO sky guard and its 5x5 search window (±2 px) was sized for a ~1px bob. With a 3-8px bob shift, the window around the un-shifted prevUv finds ONLY background on sun/cloud edges (high contrast against a UNIFORM sky) → minDiff large → reactive ≈ 0.9 → those edges never accumulate. Textured terrain edges pass (noise always matches in-window) — the exact terrain-vs-sky asymmetry the user reported. (2) **MV ramp** — `smoothstep(0.5, 16, 3-8px)` gives reactive 0.2-0.45 + the 0.15 motionFloor → only 40-65% accumulation, and the clean sky edges show every missing sample. Fix (both MV-gens): (a) **T&C SKY GUARD** — the comparison is skipped for sky-like pixels (`isSkyLike`, now computed ONCE with the 3x3 window and reused by the cutout, the ramp, and the T&C); the VK path gained the guard the GL path always had (the GL one was the narrow white/blue + screen-Y version — replaced with the same isSkyLike test). Sky drift is sub-pixel per frame and the SDK's own clamp absorbs it (FSR2/3 precedent). `noPrev` (off-screen reprojection) still hard-fires reactive 1.0 even for sky — large pans stay motion-masked. (b) **GENTLER SKY MV RAMP** — `smoothstep(0.5, isSkyLike ? 32 : 16, mvLen)` and `motionFloor = 0` for sky-like: their real-depth reprojection MVs are EXACT (rotation depth-independent; far-plane translation parallax ≈ 0), so they can accumulate heavily during the bob/slow pans (85-100%) while fast turns (>32 px) still fully discard. The flat gradient has mv=0 → ramp inert there (slant-safe). Terrain keeps the steeper 16-px ramp + floor (calibrated for its approximate constant-depth MVs). Expected: sun/cloud edges reconstruct from jittered samples like terrain during still/bob/walk/slow-pan; only fast flicks (>32 px/frame ≈ 1.9°/frame at f≈1720) alias (motion-masked). Both shaders revalidated with glslangValidator; VK SPIR-V regenerated (11200 bytes); `compileClientJava` + `build` green. Knobs: the 32-px sky-ramp ceiling and the 0.25 chroma. Known remaining: elytra/fast flight still aliases the sky (MV ramp full-discard — the ghosting protection), and the VK non-render-scale dummy-color branch (documented in v4). **v7 (2026-08-08, "try swapping out terrain for sky" — DIAGNOSTIC A/B, not a fix)**: the user proposed swapping which pixel class gets which throttle treatment, to determine whether the terrain-vs-sky smoothness gap is TUNING or STRUCTURAL. Implemented as a clean four-term swap in BOTH MV-gens (`fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` + GL `MV_GEN_CS`), each marked `// SWAP(v7)`: (1) MV ramp `smoothstep(0.5, isSkyLike ? 32 : 16, …)` → sky now gets terrain's steep **16**-px ramp, terrain gets sky's gentle **32**-px; (2) motionFloor `isSkyLike ? 0.0 : …*0.15` → floor moved TO sky, zeroed on terrain; (3) cutout colorEdge `colorEdge && !isSkyLike` → now fires on sky, disabled on terrain (alphaEdge unchanged); (4) T&C guard `else if (!isSkyLike)` → comparison now runs on sky, skipped on terrain. The flat-gradient `mv=0` (v4's slant fix) was NOT swapped — it is structural, not throttle. Interpretation: if the jaggedness MOVES to terrain (sky smooth, terrain rough) → the gap was tuning, revert the swap and blend the two profiles. If the sky STAYS jagged with terrain's aggressive profile → the gap is structural (sky not jittered/accumulated like terrain — e.g. the sky pass isn't getting the Halton jitter or the pool input), and the next step is to verify the sky's MV/depth/jitter path in the render loop rather than more throttle tuning. Both shaders glslangValidator-clean (GL via location-injection preprocess); SPIR-V regenerated (11168 bytes) and confirmed embedded in the rebuilt DLL; `compileClientJava` + `build` green. Revert = flip the four marked ternaries/guards back. **v8 (2026-08-08, "the edges going down are tilted instead of completely straight" fix — the swap's verdict)**: the user tested the v7 swap and reported the sun's vertical edges TILTED/KINKED. Pixel analysis of the screenshot (PIL: the sun body is a clean 99px-wide rectangle y101-156 at x102-201, then the LOWER half shifts LEFT by 4px to x98-197 with a 7-row transition — a GHOSTED second copy of the sun offset horizontally). Root cause — a REAL structural bug the swap exposed, not tuning: the **v4 camera-locked `mv=0` rule (`isFarPlane && colorSpread < 0.08`) wrongly caught the sun/moon body**. The sun body is FLAT WHITE (spread < 0.08 in the interior) AND far-plane → mv=0 (camera-locked) — but the sun/moon are WORLD-ANCHORED billboards. During any camera motion (the idle bob alone is 3-8 render px) the body accumulated history locked at a stale screen position → ghosted offset from its true position; the v7 swap made it visible by forcing the sun's EDGES fully fresh (cutout) at the true position against the ghosted body → the kink/tilt. In v6 the same bug existed but both body and edges were accumulated (soft blur instead of a kink). FIX (both MV-gens): (1) **REVERTED the v7 swap** — sky back to the v6 profile (gentle 32-px ramp, motionFloor 0, cutout colorEdge off for sky, T&C skipped for sky); (2) **white-body exclusion on the mv=0 rule** — `isFarPlane && colorSpread < 0.08 && !(skyLuma > 0.88 && skyChroma < 0.06)`: the pure-white sun/moon body (luma≈1.0, chroma≈0) now keeps its real far-plane MV (world-anchored → tracks correctly, accumulates cleanly, straight edges), while the COLORED sky-dome gradient (day blue / sunset orange — chroma well above 0.06) still gets mv=0 (camera-locked → the v2 slant stays fixed). Threshold sunset-safe by design: the guard keys on the white body's near-zero chroma, not on hue. Edge pixels (spread > 0.08, 3x3 spans white+blue) were never flat → already kept real MVs; the fix only affects the flat body interior. Both shaders revalidated with glslangValidator (VK SPIR-V regenerated, 11420 bytes); `compileClientJava` + `build` green. Expected: sun/moon edges straight and smooth standing still, walking, and panning (body tracks like terrain); horizon gradient still straight (mv=0 preserved).
- **GL-path REAL per-pixel depth via probeFsr4Depth() (2026-08-06, "pixelated when moving" fix)**: the GL path used constant mid-depth (ndcZ=0) MVs, so distant pixels got wrong parallax during motion → SDK rejected history → spatial rendering (pixelation). The VKMod path proved 26.1.2 WRITES the framebuffer depth, so the GL path now auto-detects it: `probeFsr4Depth(depthTex)` one-shot samples the scaled RenderTarget's depth texture at 9 points (frame 2+, after the depth settles) via `readTexPixels` (the DEPTH-attachment retry path). Decision: unreadable or spread<0.05 → stay on constant mid-depth (zero risk); otherwise `fsr4RealDepthAvailable=true` (+`fsr4DepthReversed` if sky<terrain).
  - **STRICT PROBE GATE (2026-08-06, "certain terrain parts look static/shimmery while standing still" fix)**: the 0.05 span threshold was FAR too lenient — a real session's buffer measured min=0.968 max=1.000 (mean=0.989, i.e. 98.9% at the far/clear plane) and STILL enabled `uUseDepth=1`. MC 26.1.2's GL RenderTarget depth is essentially never written, so that "variation" was a sky-vs-clear sliver. The SDK then received depth ≈0/1 everywhere → broken disocclusion, AND the T&C reprojection trust gate (`uUseDepth > 0 || mvLen < 1.5`) was bypassed — prevUv reprojected to garbage positions → T&C fired reactive on STATIC terrain → terrain regions turned spatial (the report). Gate is now `(max-min) < 0.30f || min > 0.90f → keep constant mid-depth` — real geometry spans the whole range in either convention (reversed: sky≈0/near≈1; non-reversed: near≈0/far≈1), an empty buffer fails both tests. `uUseDepth=1` is only trusted when the buffer demonstrably contains geometry. When real, `copyDepthToShared` runs EVERY frame: `glMemoryBarrier(TEXTURE_FETCH)` → DEPTH_COPY_CS dispatch (binding 0 sampler2D uInputDepth, binding 1 r32f image uOutputDepth; converts ANY source format/convention to non-reversed 0=near/1=far) into the MANAGED `fsr4StagingDepth` R32F → `glCopyImageSubData` staging→shared D3D12 texture (the proven-persistent write path). MV-gen binds the SHARED (already-normalized) depth on unit 0 and sets `uUseDepth=1`: `depth >= 0.999` → sky (mv=0, reactive=1, VK parity — no deviceToView/farDepth math needed), else `ndcZ = depth*2-1`. The T&C gate relaxes to `(uUseDepth > 0 || mvLen < 1.5)` since real depth makes the reprojection exact at every distance. The constant 0.5 fill remains as the once-per-pool fallback. Probe failure is strictly a quality no-op, never a regression.

### FSR4 depth/MV write path (Radeon 860M, 2026-08-04)
- **READBACK PITFALL (2026-08-04, IMPORTANT)**: `readTexPixels()` is the ONLY trustworthy texture readback. It uses `glGetTextureSubImage` (GL 4.5) when available, else falls back to FBO `glReadPixels` with a **native-order** buffer (GL 4.3/4.4): color formats (R32F/RG16F/R8/RGBA8) attach to **`GL_COLOR_ATTACHMENT0`**, and if that is incomplete it retries as a **`GL_DEPTH_ATTACHMENT` + `GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT`** read (Minecraft's RenderTarget depth may be a depth format). It drains GL errors before, checks `glGetError()` after each read, and returns `null` on any error — a silent GL failure must NOT masquerade as real `0.0000` data. Three historical false-readback causes: (1) big-endian `getFloat()` on little-endian GL data (fixed with `.order(nativeOrder())` — see ENDIANNESS TRAP below); (2) the GL45-only path returning null in Minecraft's GL 4.4 context (all `readTexPixels failed` / `center=-1.0000` / `depth=-1.0000` lines in the 10:26 session were this, not real zeros); (3) attaching R32F color textures to `GL_DEPTH_ATTACHMENT` = FBO incomplete (the historical `[DEBUG_DEPTH] FBO not complete` line). Do NOT trust historical `[SHARE_VERIFY] depthC=0` / `[DEBUG_MV] 0/9` / `[STAGING_VERIFY]` numbers taken before this fix.
- **ENDIANNESS TRAP (2026-08-04, ROOT CAUSE of the 0.0000 float reads)**: any `ByteBuffer` handed to GL for pixel reads AND decoded with `getFloat()` MUST be `.order(ByteOrder.nativeOrder())`. GL writes little-endian floats; a default (big-endian) buffer turns every non-zero value into a denormal — 0.5 (little-endian `00 00 00 3F`) read big-endian = 5.6e-44 ≈ `0.0000`. This is why *every* historical float readback (staging depth/MV, shared depth, accum weight B channel) showed 0 even when the data was fine. `readTexPixels()` and all `diagBuf`/`debugPboBuf` allocations now set native order. UNSIGNED_BYTE reads via `get(i)` are order-independent.
- **Depth baseline fix (2026-08-04 v3)**: `copyDepthToShared` fills the MANAGED staging R32F texture with an FBO clear (`glClearBufferfv` 0.5) then `glCopyImageSubData` it into the shared D3D12 texture — using only mechanisms PROVEN reliable (FBO ops on managed textures + `glCopyImageSubData` into external memory; the color texture works via the latter). The old compute `DEPTH_COPY_CS` path was removed. With shared depth = 0.5 the SDK stops linearizing everything to the far plane (which classified the frame as sky/disoccluded and discarded ALL history → zero temporal accumulation).
- **FSR4 MV gen uses `uUseDepth=0` (constant ndcZ=0)**, same as FSR2/3 — do NOT flip it to 1 while the shared depth texture's content is unproven; a 0-filled shared depth would map every pixel to ndcZ=-1 (near plane) and break history accumulation while walking (the exact FSR2/3 bug).

### FSR4 Reactive Mask
- **FSR4 sharpening wired to `config.sharpness` (2026-08-06, "increase quality of far away objects")**: `Fsr4Context::dispatch` hardcoded `enableSharpening=false, sharpness=0.0f` — the FSR4 SDK's internal RCAS-class sharpening pass was OFF, the ONLY upscaler path in the mod with zero sharpening (FSR1/2/3 all apply `config.sharpness`, default 0.7, via their GL RCAS). The upscale destroys sub-pixel high-frequency detail — most visible on distant terrain/buildings where a whole feature is a few source pixels — so far objects read soft/blurry. Now `enableSharpening = params.sharpness > 0.001f; sharpness = params.sharpness` with the value plumbed from Java `config.sharpness` through the JNI (`dispatchFsr4` in Fsr4Native + `dispatchFsr4VkMod` in VulkanInterop both gained a `float sharpness` param, param 9; VKModCompat wrapper updated; both FSRProcessor call sites pass `config.sharpness`). The sharpness slider in settings now controls FSR4 too, exactly like FSR2/3. The VKMod diag log prints `sharp=%.2f`.
- **T&C-STYLE AUTO-REACTIVE (2026-08-06, "pink streaks on flowers/particles while the camera is still")**: the MV field is CAMERA-ONLY — particles, potion effects, animated foliage and mobs move independently, so they get ~0 MV and the SDK keeps accumulating stale history at their OLD position (vertical streaks). The MV-gen now runs AMD's "transparency & composition" idea as a third reactive term: compare the CURRENT scene color at p against the PREVIOUS frame's color at the REPROJECTED position (prevUv — the same matrices that produced the MV). `minDiff > 0.18` luma over a **5x5 search window** (the idle camera bob shifts the image ~1px WITHOUT appearing in the clean MV matrices — a 3x3 window finds the shifted edge and fires on static content every bob cycle; 5x5 finds the same-surface sample and the diff collapses) → fully reactive. Off-screen/behind-camera prevUv (disocclusion) → reactive=1.0.
  - **GL path** (`MV_GEN_CS` in FSRProcessor.java): new `fsr4PrevColorTex` (RGBA8, render-size, created + cleared in `ensureFsr4Staging`, deleted in cleanup) holds last frame's scene color — copied from renderTex via `glCopyImageSubData` right AFTER the MV-gen dispatch and BEFORE the `glMemoryBarrier(GL_ALL)` (the ALL barrier covers the copy for next frame's texelFetch). Sampled at binding 6, gated by `uTCActive = hasPrevFrame`. **Trust gate `mvLen < 1.5`**: constant-mid-depth MVs (ndcZ=0) make prevUv EXACT only where the camera is ~still — without the gate, walking reprojects near terrain wrong and the whole near screen turns reactive (pixelated-when-moving regression). KNOWN LIMITATION (review 2026-08-06): the gate is a SCREEN-GLOBAL mid-depth cutoff, not per-depth — every pixel shares the ndcZ=0 mvLen, so walking gates the term OFF for ALL depths (safe, conservative) and a slow pan/crouch-walk with mid-depth motion <1.5px can still false-fire on NEAR surfaces (their real motion is tens of px, beyond the 5x5 window). Consequence is mild (near surfaces render spatially during slow motion); a per-pixel persistence gate would fix it at the cost of detection latency. Sky/cloud guard = same bright-desaturated/blue-top-half heuristic as the tile histogram. **Reactive value is PARTIAL** (`smoothstep(0.18, 0.50, minDiff) * 0.9` — AMD auto-reactive style) so animated/translucent surfaces (water/lava/portals) keep most of their history; only disocclusion (off-screen/behind-camera prevUv) is hard 1.0.
  - **VK path** (`fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`): binding 5 `uPrevColor` = VK-owned RGBA8 staging (`g_vkModPrevColorImage`, TRANSFER_DST|SAMPLED, created in `ensureVkModMvGenPipeline`, destroyed in `destroyVkModMvGenPipeline`) holding last frame's scene; binding 6 `uColorCurr` = the pool[0] INPUT image itself (Copy1 leaves it GENERAL; **`importInputFromD3D12Pool` now adds `VK_IMAGE_USAGE_SAMPLED_BIT`** to the input usage so it can be sampled; `g_vkModInputColorView` is a COLOR-aspect view living with the pipeline). NO mvLen gate — real per-pixel depth makes the reprojection exact at every distance, so camera pans over static terrain reproject to the same surface (diff≈0); sky pixels already return early (depth≥0.999). Per frame in `recordVkModMvGen`: (a) a GENERAL→GENERAL TRANSFER_WRITE→SHADER_READ barrier on pool[0] before the dispatch (orders Copy1's write), (b) after the dispatch + staging→imported copies, a prev-color snapshot block: SHADER_READ_ONLY→TRANSFER_DST barrier → `vkCmdCopyImage(pool[0]→prevColor)` → TRANSFER_DST→SHADER_READ_ONLY barrier. `initVkModPrevColor()` (one-shot transient-buffer init, mirrors the dummy-color pattern) transitions UNDEFINED→TRANSFER_DST, clears to mid-gray, →SHADER_READ_ONLY so the FIRST dispatch reads a layout-valid, deterministic image (review fix 2026-08-06 — without it the descriptor declared SHADER_READ_ONLY while the image sat UNDEFINED = validation error/UB on strict drivers). Input-view failure falls back to the mid-gray dummy → gray-vs-gray = inert.
- **Purpose**: The reactive mask (R8 texture, pool index 3) tells the FSR4 SDK which pixels have motion and should reduce temporal accumulation. Without it, reactive=0 everywhere → SDK applies full temporal accumulation on all pixels → smearing, ghosting, and warping on camera movement.
- **Generation**: Now MERGED into `MV_GEN_CS` in `FSRProcessor.java` (no separate dispatch — saves one compute pass). Each 8x8 workgroup computes `reactive = max(mvReactive, max(shadingReactive, motionFloor))` where:
  - `mvReactive = smoothstep(0.5, 16.0, mvLen)` — pixel-space MV magnitude. < 0.5 px = static (full accumulation), 0.5–16 px gentle ramp (keeps most history — saturating at 8px made walking fully reactive → "pixelated when moving" artifact), > 16 px fully reactive.
  - `shadingReactive = smoothstep(0.10, 0.45, shadingDiff)` — **tile luminance-histogram shading-change detector** (ported from FSR3): each 8x8 tile accumulates a bright-pixel ratio histogram, compares against the previous frame's ratio (RG8 `fsr4ScPrevTex`), ping-pongs into `fsr4ScCurrTex`. Translation-invariant → camera pans over static terrain do NOT fire it, but moving content beyond camera reprojection (mobs, falling blocks, disocclusion) changes the tile's luminance distribution → reactive rises → SDK discards history → no ghosting/smearing of moving entities.
  - **Jitter immunity (3 layers, 2026-08-04)**: the scene renders with sub-pixel Halton jitter (±0.5 render px); on anti-aliased edges at high render scales (65%) jitter flips borderline pixels across the hard 0.5 threshold every frame → naive detector fires on STATIC content → history discarded → shake when standing still. Layer 1: **deadband** — pixels within ±0.10 luma of `uShadingThreshold` (0.5) are excluded from the histogram. Layer 2: **temporal persistence gate** — the G channel stores a per-tile counter `persist = (diff > 0.10) ? min(prevPersist+1, 3) : max(prevPersist-1, 0)`, and the detector fires only when `persist >= 2` (2 consecutive high-diff frames). Jitter-induced flips are single-frame noise (Halton samples land at well-separated offsets), so static tiles can't fire; real content motion sustains the diff and fires. Layer 3: **min-sample/sky guards** — tiles with <12 classified pixels or >50% sky/cloud pixels (bright-desaturated white or blue, top half of screen) never fire. NOTE: `persist` is stored as `persist/3.0` (NOT raw) because RG8 UNORM clamps imageStore to [0,1] — 1/3 and 2/3 are exactly representable (85/255, 170/255), decode via `*3.0`.
  - `[DEBUG_SHADING]` (debug-only, in the `shouldDebug()` block): reads ratio+persist from `fsr4ScCurrTex` at 9 sample points, prints `FIRE` when persist >= 2 — use it to verify the detector stays quiet on static scenes.
  - `motionFloor = smoothstep(0.4, 2.5, mvLen) * 0.15` — caps history at ~0.85 during ANY camera motion (constant-depth MVs are approximate at near/far depths), reducing slow-pan smear without the full-discard pixelated look.
- **Timing**: Runs inside MV gen (step 2.5), before the GL sync fence. `uShadingThreshold` uniform = 0.5f (matches FSR3).
- **Thin-detail/cutout-edge reactive (2026-08-06, "FSR4 can't handle flowers" fix)**: 1-2px alpha-cutout geometry (flowers, tall grass, leaves) smears into vertical streaks under temporal accumulation — the history sample for a feature smaller than the bilinear sampling footprint lands on the flower/background boundary and blends misaligned samples. Neither existing term catches it: MV magnitude is tiny (1-2px) and the 8x8 tile histogram can't resolve a 4-pixel flower. Fix: a new `cutoutReactive` term (both `MV_GEN_CS` in FSRProcessor.java and the VK `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`) from the 3x3 neighborhood — `colorEdge` = RGB bounding-box diagonal > 0.45 (hue-agnostic; catches pink tulips at sat ~0.31 that a saturation test misses, while grass texture ~0.15-0.25 and desaturated stone-vs-grass ~0.29 stay below) OR `alphaEdge` = hard 0<->1 alpha transition. **Motion gate (2026-08-06 v2, "static terrain parts while standing still" fix)**: GL `MV_GEN_CS` now ANDs with a CPU-computed `uCamMotion` flag (camera eye-position delta > 0.05 blocks OR quaternion rotation delta > 0.01 rad vs the PREVIOUS frame — from the SAME camera state the clean view matrix is built from) instead of `mvLen > 0.5` alone. Why: idle camera bob shifts the eye ~0.01-0.02 blocks/frame, which lands in the clean MV matrices → 0.5-2.5px MVs while standing still ([DEBUG_MV] verified) → the old pure-`mvLen` gate was OPEN standing still and fired the detector at every flower/leaf/water edge → those regions went fully reactive → dropped temporal accumulation → static/shimmering patches. The cam-motion flag is well above bob's ~0.02-block / ~0.002-rad contribution, so the detector is OFF standing still (history accumulates cleanly) and engages the instant the player walks/turns/falls. `fsr4CamMoving` latched true on the first frame (no history — conservative). VK `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` still uses the pure-`mvLen` gate (its matrices flow through `setVkModMvGenMatrices` push constants — a cam-motion plumb there is a follow-up; the VK path binds the color source only in the render-scale branch). VK plumbing: new `setVkModColorSource` JNI (mirrors `setVkModDepthSource`: bound-image tracking, 1x1 mid-gray RGBA8 dummy, capture-before-destroy rebuild fix), descriptor layout 3 storage + 2 samplers, and COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL→SHADER_READ_ONLY→back barriers around the MV-gen dispatch. Bound ONLY in the render-scale branch — the non-scaled path's scene color is the swapchain image in PRESENT_SRC_KHR at the end() hook point (recordFrameCopyOps barrier 1), which would need a PRESENT→SHADER_READ round-trip; flowers are 4-8px at native res anyway. GL path needs no plumbing (uInputColor already bound).


	### Settings screen GL fatal under VulkanMod — FIXED (2026-08-05 v6b)
- **Report**: opening the mod's settings screen under VulkanMod aborts the JVM: `FATAL ERROR in native method: No context is current or a function that is not available in the current context was called` at `GL11C.nglGetString` → `FSRProcessor.gpuRenderer()` (line 82) ← `isFgUnsupportedGpu` ← `getVersionStatus` ← `SettingsScreen.buildGeneralTab`.
- **Root cause**: VulkanMod replaces OpenGL entirely — no GL context is ever current, so `glGetString(GL_RENDERER)` hits LWJGL's fatal native abort, which `try/catch` CANNOT catch. `isIntelGpu()` already skipped GL under VulkanMod (with a comment about this exact fatal), but `isFgUnsupportedGpu()` called `gpuRenderer()` directly.
- **Fix**: `gpuRenderer()` now branches on `VulkanModCompat.isLoaded() || GL.getCapabilities() == null` → queries the GPU name from the native Vulkan device (`VulkanInterop.getDeviceInfo()`, SEH-wrapped, "Vulkan (unknown)" fallback) instead of GL. Also: `getVersionStatus(FSR3_FG_DX12)` returns not-selectable under VulkanMod (GL required for FG — the VulkanMod dispatch block is FSR4-only anyway).

### Crash 0xC0000409 on Close (STATUS_STACK_BUFFER_OVERRUN)
	- **Root cause**: `D3D12Device` is a function-local static singleton. Its destructor runs during DLL unloading (process exit). When the D3D12 runtime is partially torn down by the time the destructor runs, calling `ComPtr::Reset()` (which calls `->Release()` on D3D12 COM objects) triggers a GS cookie stack buffer overrun crash.
	- **Fix (layer 1)**: `ClientLifecycleEvents.CLIENT_STOPPING` fires `processor.destroy()` before JVM shutdown begins, which calls `Fsr4Native.shutdownFsr4()` → `D3D12Device::get().dispose()`. This releases all COM objects while the D3D12 runtime is still loaded.
	- **Fix (layer 2)**: `D3D12Device` has a `disposed_` flag. Once `dispose()` runs, it sets `disposed_ = true`. The destructor checks this flag and returns immediately — no `Reset()` calls during DLL unloading.
	- **Fix (layer 3)**: `shutdownFsr4()` wraps `D3D12Device::get().dispose()` in `__try/__except` SEH, so even if the release crashes, the JVM survives.
	- **Java imports**: `ClientLifecycleEvents` in `FSRMod.onInitializeClient()`

### Ghosting / Shaking Prevention: Jitter in MV
- **FSR4 MV gen uses CLEAN matrices (2026-08-04 fix)**: `generateFSR4MotionVectors` builds `currViewProj` from `lastProj` (not `lastProjJittered`). The scene renders jittered and the FSR4 SDK receives `jitterOffset=(jitterX,jitterY)` separately, so MVs must carry ONLY true camera motion. The previous code used `lastProjJittered` → MVs contained the per-frame jitter delta → with jitterOffset also passed, jitter was double-counted → history samples misplaced every frame → **terrain smearing during slow camera rotation** (the exact reported bug). FSR2/3 GLSL pipelines intentionally use jittered MVs and cancel the jitter in the ACCUMULATE shader via `uMotionVectorJitterCancellation` — FSR4 has no such GL pass, so it must get clean MVs.
- **FG camera basis fix (2026-08-04)**: `camera.rotation()` is the CAMERA→WORLD quaternion (the view matrix uses its conjugate — verified in `Camera.getViewRotationMatrix` = `Matrix4f.rotation(conjugate(rotation()))`). The camera looks down local -Z, so world forward = `q * (0,0,-1)`. The FG prepare previously passed `q * (0,0,+1)` — a BACKWARD forward → left-handed (right, up, forward) basis → the FG SDK's rotation decomposition was inverted → generated frames warped the wrong way during camera rotation → **FG-mode terrain smearing**.
- **Reactive mask threshold (2026-08-04 v2)**: `smoothstep(0.5, 16.0, mvLen)` in both the FSR4 MV_GEN_CS and REACTIVE_MASK_CS. <0.5px = static (full accumulation — safe now that MVs are clean), 0.5–16px = GENTLE ramp, >16px = fully reactive. v1 tried `smoothstep(0.25, 6.0, mvLen)` but saturating at 6–8px made normal walking/rotation (~1–8px MVs) 70–100% reactive → the SDK discarded history every frame → output reverted to a spatial upscale → **"pixelated when moving"** (user report). Spreading the ramp to 16px keeps most history during moderate motion; the SDK's own internal MV-confidence rejection still guards fast motion. Known tradeoff: slight ghosting on near objects while walking (constant-depth parallax error).
- **Depth workaround context**: The constant `ndcZ = 0.0` fallback exists because the Minecraft 1.21.4 GpuDevice abstraction doesn't write scene depth to the RenderTarget's depth texture. With constant depth the SDK cannot do depth-aware disocclusion, so depth-edge smearing during motion remains a known limitation (mitigated by the reactive threshold above).

### FSR4 Phase 1 — Native JNI C++ Layer (fsrvk.dll)
- **Goal**: Move the VK allocation + export chain from Java/LWJGL into native `fsrvk.dll` C++.
- **Resource tracking**: Heap-allocated `FsrSharedAllocation` struct (VkImage + VkDeviceMemory + HANDLE + dimensions). Pointer returned as `jlong` — no HANDLE-keyed map (Windows recycles handle integers, causing collision bugs during rapid resize).
- **JNI api**:
  ```java
  class VulkanInterop {
      static native long getSharedHandle(int texW, int texH);  // returns FsrSharedAllocation* as jlong
      static native void releaseSharedHandle(long handle);     // destroy + CloseHandle + delete alloc
      static native String getDeviceInfo();                    // GPU name + driver version
      static native void terminate();                          // explicit shutdown (NOT static destructors)
  }
  ```
- **Files**: `vulkan_device.h/.cpp` (singleton VkInstance/VkDevice), `vulkan_memory.h/.cpp` (VkImage + VkDeviceMemory + export), `jni_bridge.cpp` (JNI entry points), `VulkanInterop.java` (Java native declarations). All platform-specific code guarded by `#ifdef _WIN32`.
- **Obligations**:
  - Java must `glDeleteTextures()` before `releaseSharedHandle()` (resize safety)
  - Caller must invoke `terminate()` from Minecraft shutdown hook (not DLL static destructors — JVM thread teardown can cause EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION during static destruction)
- **Allocation flags** (proven in Phase 0):
  - `VkExternalMemoryImageCreateInfo` → `handleTypes = 0x00000002`
  - `VkExportMemoryAllocateInfo` → `handleTypes = 0x00000002`
  - `vkGetMemoryWin32HandleKHR` → `handleType = 0x00000002`
- **Build**: Separate CMake project in `src/native/fsrvk/`. Gradle `buildNative` task invokes CMake, copies DLL into mod JAR.

## Key files
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/fsr/FSRProcessor.java` — lifecycle, jitter matrix, Iris upscale+sharpen
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/fsr/FSR1Pipeline.java` — blit upscale (glBlitFramebuffer) + optional EASU compute upscale + RCAS sharpen compute
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/fsr/FSR2Pipeline.java` — FSR2 GLSL + Java binding
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/fsr/FSR3Pipeline.java` — FSR3 GLSL + Java binding
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/fsr/JitterManager.java` — Halton sequence
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/fsr/ScaledRenderTarget.java` — proxy RenderTarget (native dims, scaled textures)
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/fsr/ShaderBinaryCache.java` — shader binary caching
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/vulkan/VulkanInterop.java` — JNI bridge to fsrvk.dll
- `src/native/fsrvk/vulkan_device.h` — VulkanDevice singleton header
- `src/native/fsrvk/vulkan_device.cpp` — VulkanDevice singleton implementation
- `src/native/fsrvk/vulkan_memory.h` — FsrSharedAllocation struct header
- `src/native/fsrvk/vulkan_memory.cpp` — VkImage+memory allocation + Win32 HANDLE export
- `src/native/fsrvk/jni_bridge.cpp` — JNI entry points for VulkanInterop
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/config/FSRConfig.java` — includes `debugMode` (0-6)
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/mixin/GameRendererMixin.java` — jitter injection point, scene rendered callback
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/compat/IrisCompat.java` — Iris detection via reflection
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/compat/SodiumCompat.java` — Sodium detection via Fabric API
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/compat/RenderScaleCompat.java` — RenderScale detection via reflection
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/compat/DistantHorizonsCompat.java` — Distant Horizons detection via Fabric API
- `src/native/fsrvk/fsr4_context.cpp` — FSR4 context creation, dispatch, fence sync
- `src/native/fsrvk/fsr4_context.h` — Fsr4Context class, Fsr4DispatchParams
- `src/client/java/com/fsr2mod/vulkan/Fsr4Native.java` — JNI bridge to fsr4_context

- **VKMod real-depth: pipeline build destroyed the depth view (2026-08-06 ROOT CAUSE of "jagged water" under VulkanMod)**: `ensureVkModMvGenPipeline()` starts with `destroyVkModMvGenPipeline()` (leak prevention), which destroys the framebuffer-depth view AND wipes `g_vkModDepthSrcImage/Fmt/BoundImage`. The mixin calls `setVkModDepthSource` BEFORE `recordVkModMvGen` → the FIRST build destroyed the freshly-created view → the review-fix saw NULL → binding 3 stayed on the 1x1 dummy → every dispatch sampled 0.5 (constant-depth, ndcZ=0) → SDK rejected history at distance → jagged water forever, even though setVkModDepthSource logged successful rebinds. Direct readback probe (raw attachment, no shader) showed 1.0 while the shader output probe showed 0.5 — proving the attachment has content and the sampling path was broken. Fix: capture `depthSrcAtBuild` before the destroy and recreate the view in the review-fix (`recreated depth view` log). The per-frame path was never broken (ensure() short-circuits after the first build).

- **VKMod real depth = mc target's VIEW, not the getter (2026-08-06)**: `net.vulkanmod.mixin.render.target.MainTargetMixin` OVERRIDES `getDepthTexture()` to return `MainPass.getDepthAttachment()` — which wraps the DMP framebuffer's depth attachment, a DECOY that reads 1.0 (cleared, never written — proven by the direct readback probe) while the DMP fb COLOR holds the scene. The REAL depth is the image the world's render pass actually writes: `Minecraft.getMainRenderTarget().getDepthTextureView().texture()` (the field `depthTexture`, created fresh in `createBuffers()` via `GpuDevice.createTexture`). `VkCommandEncoder.createRenderPass(colorView, depthView, ...)` → `VkFbo` constructor attaches the DEPTH VIEW. Calling `getDepthTexture()` (the overridden getter) silently binds the decoy even when the "mc path works" — this was the "nothing much changed / still 1.0" bug after switching to the mc main target. The mixin resolves depth via `getDepthTextureView().texture()` now.
- **Diagnostic-flag suppression bug**: the old one-shot block (`depthImg id=... (one-shot)`) set `vkModDepthSourceLogged = true` BEFORE the newer mc-depth probe ran, permanently suppressing the probe AND its catch handler — the log showed the old message while the native silently bound the DMP fallback. Any new one-shot diagnostic must either remove the old block or use its own flag.

- **"It is still warping" — FSR4 SDK DEBUG CHECKER wired (2026-08-08, 17:03 build, DIAGNOSTIC)**: after the 16:47 build (motionVectorScale=1.0 + jitterY negation + Y-mirror + ramp v2) the user still reports warping on ANY camera move. Re-verified EVERY contract against the official sources and all check out: (1) FSR4 4.1.1 doc "Providing motion vectors": MVs in [<-w,-h>..<w,h>] screen pixels, the <w,h> example (pixel at upper-left originating from bottom-right) PROVES mv = previous - current — exactly our `(prevUv - uv) * renderSize`; (2) the OFFICIAL FSR2 backend (ffx_fsr2.cpp line 902): `constants.motionVectorScale = params.motionVectorScale / renderSize` and the shader does `fReprojectedHrUv = fHrUv + raw * scale` — so raw*scale/renderSize is the UV delta and scale=1.0 for pixel MVs is CORRECT (the 16:24 fix stands; the FSR4 doc's NDC example omits the *0.5 NDC→UV factor, the doc is sloppy but the implementation is consistent); (3) jitter: render composites +2*jY/H (clip-space translation in captureAndJitter), SDK assumes -2*jY/H → the Y negation at the boundary is right; (4) per-frame timing is same-frame (renderLevel TAIL onSceneRendered pushes matrices → MainPass end() records Copy1+MV-gen with them → next frame onRenderBegin dispatches with prevJitter = the pooled frame's jitter); (5) MV staging→pool copy (idx 2) verified full-extent; (6) reactive probe proves pool→SDK delivery works. So the remaining unknowns are things only the SHIPPED 4.1.1 binary knows. THIS build turns on `FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_DEBUG_CHECKING` + wires `fpMessage` (fsr4MessageCallback, logs first 40 messages as `[FSR4 SDK TYPE]: msg`) — the checker validates motionVectorScale, jitterOffset range, frameTimeDelta, depth config at dispatch and will name the violated contract. ALSO noticed: dispatch logs `dt=1.0` while the FSR4 doc says frameTimeDelta should be ms (~16.6 at 60fps; the SDK warns <1.0) — the Java computes `max(1.0f, (nanoTime-t0Frame)/1e6)` so dt should be ~16, the 1.0 in the log needs re-checking (only affects auto-exposure, not reprojection). Validated: cmake build green (fsr4_context.cpp recompiled, "[FSR4 SDK" string verified in fsrvk.dll), gradlew build green, deployed 17:03 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `.bak-20260808-sdkcheck`). Expected: the next session's log carries the SDK's own warnings — read them before changing any contract value.

- **"Check the log" — SDK checker SILENT = contracts valid; root cause is the 0.25 render scale (2026-08-08, 17:03 build session)**: the 17:05 gradle-client session (run/logs/latest.log + fsr4_diag.log) ran with the 17:03 DLL and produced ZERO `[FSR4 SDK ...]` messages — the debug checker validates motionVectorScale (>maxRenderSize / ==0), jitterOffset (outside [-1,1]), frameTimeDelta (<1.0), renderSize, depth flags at dispatch, so silence means every dispatch input is IN RANGE: the contracts (MV scale=1.0, jitterY negation, Y-mirror, ramp v2) are all accepted by the shipped binary. Session data consistent with a CORRECT pipeline: reactive 255 while moving (eyeSpeed 11-34 = flying), 0 standing; MV ≈0 during pure translation (expected — no far-plane parallax; the 16x16 center block can't see rotation MVs); the earlier 16:47-session MV values (~0.7px at rotDelta 0.002 rad) match the expected focal·angle for focal≈343 at 0.25 scale — rotation MVs ARE correct. THE ACTUAL CULPRIT: `qualityScale: 0.25` in the GRADLE-CLIENT config (run/config/fsr-reloaded.json) = 4x upscale, far beyond FSR4's 1.5-3x design envelope; the mod's own session warning ("beyond FSR4's ~1.5-3x design envelope... Raise qualityScale to 0.5-0.65") fired at 17:05:16. At 4x, every motion frame (reactive>0 → spatial) shimmers/aliases/warps regardless of pipeline correctness — the "warp on any camera move" was dominated by this the whole time. PrismLauncher 26.1 config was already 0.6 (the user's play setup); the gradle client had drifted to 0.25. FIX: bumped BOTH configs to qualityScale 0.55 (targetFps forced 0 so auto-quality can't grind it down). ALSO noted: dispatch logs `dt=1.0` because deltaTime is measured intra-frame in onRenderBegin (max(1.0, (nanoTime-t0Frame)/1e6) with t0Frame at the top of the same call) — should be inter-frame ~16.6ms; only affects auto-exposure, not reprojection. The 17:03 diagnostic build stays deployed (debug checker + fpMessage keep logging any future violations; per-dispatch cost negligible). Expected next run: 0.55 scale (1.8x upscale) with all verified contracts → temporal accumulation finally operates in FSR4's design envelope; standing still smooth, walking no ghost (reactive ramp), slow pans smooth (correct rotation MVs).

- **"Warping still happens (less noticeable) — should not happen in any FSR mode" — jitter A/B gate + FSR1 control (2026-08-08, 17:17 build, DIAGNOSTIC)**: at 0.55-0.65 scale the warp reduced but persists; the user demands zero warp in any mode/quality. Re-verified the remaining conventions against the actual binaries: VulkanMod 0.6.8 `Renderer.setViewport(x,y,w,h)` decompiled = `viewport.y = h+y, height = -h` (negative-height trick CONFIRMED → NDC +1 = top row → the 16:38 Y-mirror is right); the FSR2 backend (ffx_fsr2.cpp:902) divides motionVectorScale by renderSize (scale=1.0 for pixel MVs confirmed); FSR2's ComputeDepthClip is INERT with constant depth (diff=0 → no clip → depth not the warp); JitterManager phase count already matches the FSR4 doc formula ceil(8·n²); pool recreates only on scale changes (4x at session start = init, not periodic). All contracts verified — the residual warp is an SDK/render BEHAVIOR question. TWO decisive tests, no more guessing: (1) **FSR1 control** — set config `version` to FSR1 (pure spatial bilinear blit, no SDK, no temporal, jitter zeroed): if the warp persists there, the RENDER/COPY path (projection override or scaled blit) is the culprit, not FSR4; if clean, it's SDK-side. (2) **Jitter A/B** — new gate: `debugMode >= 5` ZEROES jitter on BOTH sides (render via captureAndJitter+override, and the SDK's jitterOffset via prevJitterX/Y); user sets debugMode=5 to test. If the warp disappears with no jitter → phantom jitter (one side jittered, the other not — render actually unjittered or double-jittered vs the SDK's offset); if it persists → MV/depth/accumulation, not jitter. NOTE: with jitter zeroed the image aliases when still (no sub-pixel diversity) — that is expected and part of the signal. Deployed 17:17 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher (jar only — the 17:03 DLL with the SDK debug checker + fpMessage stays).

- **A/B verdicts + frameTimeDelta FIX (2026-08-08, 17:26 build)**: user tested the two controls — (1) FSR1 (pure spatial blit, no SDK): **NO warp** → the render/copy path is clean; (2) FSR4 with debugMode=5 (jitter zeroed BOTH sides): **warp PRESENT** → the warp is NOT jitter and NOT render-side — it is the FSR4/FSR3 SDK's temporal accumulation. Re-read the FSR3.1 manual: the MV/jitter/reactive contracts are word-for-word identical to FSR4 (MVs [±w,±h] px, mv = previous - current, scale as multiplier, jitter -2*jY/H) so our inputs match both. The FOUND BUG: `frameTimeDelta` — FSR3.1 doc: "used within the temporal component of the auto-exposure feature... for tuning of the history accumulation". The dispatch computed `max(1.0f, (nanoTime - t0Frame)/1e6)` where t0Frame was set at the TOP of the SAME onRenderBegin call → intra-frame elapsed (<1ms → clamped to 1.0) — the log showed `dt=1.0` consistently. The SDK expects INTER-FRAME ms (~16.6 at 60fps) — a 16x error in the history-accumulation tuning, a prime warp/smear driver on the FSR3.1 fallback (this GPU is an AMD Radeon 860M, RDNA 3.5 iGPU → the SDK runs the FSR3.1 path per the context comment). FIX: `lastFrameDeltaMs` (already tracked for the FG alternation + auto-quality) is now the inter-frame delta, clamped [1,100]ms, used at BOTH dispatch sites (onRenderBegin VKMod path + onSceneRendered GL path). The next session's dispatch log should show `dt=16.x` instead of `1.0`. Deployed 17:26 (jar only) to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher; config reset to version=FSR4, debugMode=1 (the debugMode>=5 jitter A/B gate stays for future use). If the warp persists after this, the next A/B is reactive-mask forced 0 (needs a shader push-constant flag) and/or the constant-depth input (0.9995) being degenerate for FSR3.1's ML accumulation — real depth remains unreachable on this path (documented).

## 2026-08-08 18:25 — MV probe moved off-center (rotation warp diagnosis)

**Symptom:** "terrain warps when I move the camera even slightly" — rotation-triggered warp persists.

**Root-cause status:** The 17:26 frameTimeDelta fix is confirmed live (dt=10.6-21.9ms in the
18:00 session, zero SDK warnings, clean exit). The newest session was mostly idle, so the warp
case wasn't exercised — but the *previous* session (pre-17:26 build) caught a **garbage MV
spike: 1556-12271 render-px while walking** (reactive=255) — a 10x-screen-width MV, exactly
the kind of input that warps history accumulation.

**Probe blind spot found:** the MV readback sampled the CENTER 16x16 block — the rotation
pivot. Rotation MVs are ~0 there by definition, so the probe could never see the
rotation-triggered warp (confirmed: newest session's frame-480 reactive=9-16 showed the user
was moving, yet MVs read 0.000).

**Change (native only, jni_bridge.cpp):**
- MV readback offset moved from center `(w/2,h/2)` to `(0.25w, 0.5h)` where rotation MVs
  are large and measurable.
- Sample count raised 5 -> 8 (covers a full session at every-120th-frame).

**Deployed:** 18:25, both gradle-client resources and PrismLauncher jar (backup
.bak-20260808-offcenterprobe). DLL 136,704 bytes.

**Next run should show:** during rotation, off-center MV magnitudes should read real values
(~0.5-2px for slight rotation, up to ~10px walking) with *consistent* direction across the
16x16 block. Persistent garbage (1000+ px) during motion = matrix/timing bug in MV-gen.

## 2026-08-08 18:37 — motion-triggered MV/reactive probes + config restore

**Symptom:** "still there" — warp persists after the off-center probe build.

**Log findings (18:27 session, off-center probe):**
- dt real (10.8ms etc.), zero SDK warnings — deltaTime fix holds.
- BUT every MV/reactive probe sample landed on IDLE frames (reactive 0, MV
  0.000) — the walk (eyeSpeed=4.078) happened at session start, before the
  first every-120th-frame sample. The probe never saw motion.
- Render scale DROPPED 0.65 (1248x781) -> 0.25 (480x300) MID-SESSION —
  auto-quality ground it back to the 4x floor. Root cause: the game was
  running when the config was last edited, so it overwrote qualityScale=0.55
  with its in-memory 0.25 on exit.

**Changes:**
- Probe triggers reworked (jni_bridge.cpp): both MV and reactive readbacks
  now fire on MOTION (eyeSpeed > 0.3 or |rotDelta| > 0.005), min 60-frame
  cooldown, 8 samples — instead of every-120th-frame which kept landing on
  idle frames. Reads g_vkModMvGenPush[32]/[33] (set by setVkModCamMotion).
- MV probe stays off-center (0.25w, 0.5h) where rotation MVs are visible.
- Config restored to qualityScale 0.55 + targetFps 0 in BOTH gradle-client
  and PrismLauncher configs (game was closed at edit time so it sticks).

**Deployed:** 18:37, both targets (PrismLauncher backup
.bak-20260808-motionprobe). DLL 136,704 bytes.

**Next run should show:** MV/reactive samples DURING actual walking/panning —
the first real motion-frame data. If MVs read 2-10px clean: pipeline correct,
warp is downstream (FSR3.1 fallback accumulation). If garbage (1000+ px, like
the 1556-12271 spike seen earlier): MV-gen matrix bug, now finally caught
because the probe samples the actual motion frames.
- **2026-08-09 — "screen warps even when i barely move the camera" — MV READBACK WAS READING HALF-FLOATS AS FLOAT32 (the "garbage MV" readings were a DECODE BUG, and the matrix plumbing is verified-clean)**: the user's next report after the 18:37 motion probes. Session data (fsr4_diag.log): dispatch healthy (dt 7-20ms real, jitter real, reset=0, zero SDK warnings), reactive mask correct (255 moving / 0 standing). Three hard verifications this session: (1) **Bytecode-confirmed the negative-height viewport**: `net/vulkanmod/vulkan/Renderer.class` `setViewport(int,int,int,int,MemoryStack)` does `VkViewport.x=x, y=height+y, width=w, height=-height, minDepth=0, maxDepth=1` (javap on `libs/VulkanMod-0.6.8+26.1.2.jar`) — the 16:38 Y-mirror in `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` (uv.y=0/top → ndc.y=+1) is CORRECT, and the depth is non-reversed (0=near/1=far) matching `FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_DEPTH_INVERTED` NOT set. Rotation MVs (depth-independent) are therefore exact under the Y-mirror; pitch MVs have the correct sign. (2) **Matrix plumbing verified end-to-end**: FSRProcessor VKMod block pushes CLEAN `lastProj` (captureAndJitter stores the UNJITTERED matrix in lastProj, the jittered COPY only in lastProjJittered — the "jitter is zeroed under VKMod anyway" comment is stale, the code is right), standard `tmpView = rotation(conjugate(camRot))·translate(-pos)` identical to the GL path (which works), `prevViewProj` saved per frame, only setters write `g_vkModMvGenPush` (GetFloatArrayRegion matrices + setVkModCamMotion floats at 128/132), push range = 136 on the pipeline layout (maxPushConstantsSize=256 logged), and the reactive probe proves uCamMotion arrives → the matrices arrive. Same-frame timing confirmed (onSceneRendered renderLevel-TAIL pushes → MainPass end() records Copy1+MV-gen → next frame onRenderBegin dispatches). (3) **THE READBACK DECODE BUG**: the MV staging is `VK_FORMAT_R16G16_SFLOAT` (half-float, 4 bytes/texel) but the consumption mapped the 1024-byte buffer as `const float*` and read each 4-byte texel (two packed halfs) as ONE float32 — every non-zero MV logged as garbage magnitudes (31622.777 = sqrt(1e9) misread, 1562-1.37e10 "px"), and the diag paired f[i]/f[i+1] = ADJACENT TEXELS as (x,y) components. Properly decoded as halfs (texel = (x_half, y_half)): motion frames read coherent 7-576px MVs (frame 189: (10,4.8)px uniform at 0.25w,0.5h — consistent with a ~0.015 rad/frame rotation at focal≈754; frame 375: (575,-35)px — a fast flick or flight), idle = 0.0 — i.e. the data was NEVER the "1000+ px garbage" the minMag line suggested; the true MV values remain ambiguous (correct rotation MVs vs wrong matrices) and the frame-314 block (reactive 23-29 but MV samples ~0.08) shows the buffer consumption can also read stale data. **Changes (jni_bridge.cpp only — Java/native surface unchanged)**: (a) `fsr4HalfToFloat()` helper + the MV readback now decodes each texel's two halfs properly (min/max + 9 samples as real render-px); (b) the MV readback record log now includes `eyeSpeed`/`rotDelta` AT RECORD TIME (the consumption runs a frame later when the push constants have moved — previously the sample values were uninterpretable); (c) **MV-GEN DIAG** one-shot C++ reprojection check at the first motion frame: recomputes the shader's exact math (Y-flip + ndcZ=0.9995 + the same push-constant matrices) at left-mid/center/right-mid and logs the matrices' col0/col3 + the resulting MVs. Expected next session: `MV-GEN DIAG` MVs ≈ f·rotDelta for rotation frames / ≈0.03px for pure translation → matrices+shader correct → the warp is the FSR3.1-fallback accumulation with constant depth (next: reactive-mask-0 A/B or real depth); DIAG MVs huge while the fixed READBACK matches → matrices wrong (stale prevVP — the DIAG's col3 dump shows which); DIAG sane but READBACK huge → shader/pipeline delivery bug. Built: fsrvk.dll 138,240 bytes, `gradlew build` green (jar `build/libs/fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar`, new DLL embedded 138,240). Deployed to gradle-client only (run/ uses the gradle classpath — no copy needed); PrismLauncher copy needs the user's path.
- **2026-08-09 (2nd) — log review of the new DIAG session — rotation MVs PROVEN correct; the 768px DIAG was a session-start transient; residual warp = constant-depth accumulation (depth buffer still EMPTY)**: user ran the new jar (device recreated mid-log: D3D12 device 0000025C → 00000191; 3322 dispatches, ALL near=0.50 far=40920.04 — no more far=-986895 flip in this session). Verdicts from fsr4_diag.log: (1) **The MV readback is now clean and meaningful** — no more 1e9 garbage; walk frame 1: uniform (-1.6,-14.4)px ≈ pure pitch ~0.019 rad; rotation frames 135/209/273/350/417/479/606: 8.8-10.1/3.97-4.60/5.45-6.02/1.80-2.60/7.14-7.79/7.74-8.43/3.82-4.39px vs f·Δθ = 755·rotDelta = 10.7/4.4/3.9/4.0/6.2/9.9/4.0px — same ballpark and scales with rotDelta, i.e. **rotation MVs are essentially correct** (residual ±15-40% = CPU quaternion angle vs matrix yaw/pitch + view bob). Reactive readbacks confirm the design: walk frame 1 = 255 everywhere (fully spatial), rotation frames = 5-33/255 (SDK accumulates with exact MVs). (2) **The MV-GEN DIAG's 768px + garbage matrices (invCurrVP col3=(-20603,1352,8663,20.0033) → near=0.05 REBUILT projection; prevVP m33=-406 → camera-position mismatch ~700 blocks) fired on the FIRST motion frame after the device recreation — a TRANSIENT** (stale prevViewProj across the recreate; that frame's FSR4 dispatch ran reset=1 so its history was discarded anyway). The readback of the SAME frame shows the shader actually used SANE matrices → the DIAG (one-shot) caught the transient write; it does NOT represent steady state. (3) **The embedded shader is NOT stale** — fsr4_vk_mvgen_spv.h (what the DLL runs) has SmoothStep(0.3,1.5) = ramp v2, matching fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp; the smoothstep(2.0,4.5) artifact is only the unused fsr4_vk_mvgen.spv on disk (16:37, pre-edit). (4) **The depth buffer is STILL EMPTY** — staging + direct readbacks all 0.9995/1.0 → noRealDepth=TRUE → pool depth = constant 0.9995 → the SDK's disocclusion is blind. **Residual-warp hypothesis (matches "warping even when I barely move")**: in noRealDepth mode, translation MVs are wrong below the v2 ramp floor (uCamMotion < 0.3 blocks/s — idle bob ~0.13, barely-perceptible nudges) because constant depth (ndcZ=0.999) can't track parallax, AND the SDK accumulates with blind disocclusion → history misalignment at depth boundaries during ANY motion. Walk (≥1.5 blocks/s) is fully spatial → no warp (but no smoothing). Next A/B: lower the noRealDepth translation ramp floor (0.3→0.05) so barely-moving also renders spatial → if the warp vanishes it's constant-depth accumulation; if it persists it's the SDK depth/auto-exposure handling. 
- **2026-08-09 (3rd) — A/B test build: translation ramp floor 0.3 -> 0.05.** The session-2 evidence (rotation MVs exact, reactive 5-33/255 during rotation = accumulating, walk frame reactive=255 = spatial, constant depth 0.9995 everywhere) points at constant-depth temporal accumulation as the warp cause: below 0.3 blocks/s ("barely moving") the SDK keeps accumulating with wrong constant-depth translation MVs -> history misalignment -> warp. Changed fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp smoothstep(0.3,1.5) -> smoothstep(0.05,1.5) for pc.uCamMotion, regenerated fsr4_vk_mvgen_spv.h via glslangValidator (verified SmoothStep 0.0500000007 in SPIR-V), rebuilt fsrvk.dll + jar (embedded DLL verified: 0.05f present, size 10796). If the warp vanishes when barely moving -> root cause is constant-depth accumulation; fix = real depth (open problem). If it persists -> SDK depth/auto-exposure handling.
- **2026-08-09 (4th) — A/B #2: fake screen-space depth ramp (constant depth ruled IN as the top suspect; real depth source confirmed EMPTY at runtime)**. The 0.05-ramp A/B (fully spatial during any motion) did NOT stop the warp → temporal accumulation is ruled out → the warp survives fully-spatial renders → the remaining garbage input is DEPTH (constant 0.9995 far-plane). Exhaustive bytecode audit of the depth chain (VulkanMod 0.6.8+26.1.2 jar + actual 26.1.2 clientOnly jar in .gradle/loom-cache): DMP scaled fb depth is D32F (fmt=126) usage 39 (SAMPLED+DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT), the world renders into it via rebindMainTarget→auxRenderPass (loadOp=LOAD, binds framebuffer.getDepthAttachment()), the getDepthTextureView redirect (VulkanModRenderTargetMixin) fires, the MV-gen's barriers are correct (DEPTH_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL→SHADER_READ_ONLY), the direct readback reads the right image — and the depth is STILL uniform 1.0 (staging 0.9995 fallback + direct 1.000). - **2026-08-09 (20th) — FSR4 VK GL-parity #2: fake-depth sky misclassification + blanket translation ramp (user: "still not quite like GL")**: the last session's REACTIVE READBACKS (min=0 standing, 75 at 0.73 blocks/s) confirmed accumulation is healthy when still — the remaining "not quite like GL" is WALKING: VK's no-real-depth mode had TWO GL divergences in `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`. (1) The fake depth source (empty DMP fb depth, uniform 1.0 — or the 0.5 dummy) made `isFarPlane = depth >= 0.999` TRUE FOR EVERY PIXEL → `isSkyLike=true` everywhere → the WHOLE screen got the gentle 32px sky MV ramp + zeroed motionFloor (terrain lost its steeper 16px ramp and motion damping, so slow-pan terrain MVs slipped under the reactive ramp). GL's constant-depth branch classifies sky by COLOR (isWhiteSky0/isBlueSky0 + uv.y>0.5 guard), not by the fake depth. (2) The blanket no-real-depth translation ramp `smoothstep(0.3,1.5,uCamMotion)` forced reactive=1.0 for EVERY pixel during ANY walk — while GL has NO translation ramp and instead keeps sky/far content accumulating during translation via its T&C auto-reactive term (per-pixel: only genuinely-changed content goes reactive). Net: VK rendered the horizon/clouds/sun spatially while walking = jagged edges exactly where the user compares against GL. Fix (shader only): `isFarPlane` now gated `(!noRealDepth) && depth >= 0.999` (fake depth can't classify sky; isSkyLike falls back to the loose bright-desaturated color test = GL's classifier) and the translation ramp is now SKY-AWARE (`if (!isSkyLike)`) — sky-like pixels have correct MVs at constant depth (rotation-exact, far-plane translation parallax ~0) so they accumulate in place during walks like GL; terrain keeps the ramp (its constant-depth translation MVs are wrong → spatial to avoid smear). Validated: glslangValidator clean, SPIR-V 10800 → 10936 bytes + header regenerated (byte count; 10936 verified in embedded DLL via magic offset + size constant), fsrvk.dll 141,824 bytes, `gradlew build` green, deployed 10:06 to PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260809-vkmvjitter` holds the previous build). Expected: sky/horizon/cloud/sun edges stay SMOOTH while walking (they accumulate in place), terrain still renders spatially during walks (no smear) and accumulates standing/slow-pan — matching GL's walking behavior. If edges STILL differ while walking, the remaining delta is GL's T&C + shading-change detectors (port needs a prev-color staging image + copy + descriptor binding — a bigger native change; documented as the next candidate).
- **2026-08-10 (20th) — NATIVE-RES UI under VulkanMod.** Until now the GUI
  (HUD/chat/screens) rendered into the same scaled framebuffer as the world and
  got FSR4-upscaled with it (blurry UI) — GL kept it native by upscaling first,
  then drawing the GUI on top. Fixed by mirroring GL:
  - New `VulkanModGuiMixin` (registered in fsr2mod.mixins.json) hooks
    `GuiRenderer.render` HEAD/RETURN (single overload `render(GpuBufferSlice)`,
    called from GameRenderer right after the world).
  - HEAD: ends the main render pass, records the FSR4 copies (Copy1 world-only
    scaled-fb → pool input, MV-gen, Copy2 pool output → swapchain) BEFORE the
    GUI draws, then swaps `DefaultMainPass.mainFramebuffer`/`colorAttachmentTextures`
    /`colorAttachmentTextureViews`/`imageIdxSupplier` to the swapchain. The GUI's
    aux pass binds the swapchain at native res with LOAD_OP_LOAD
    (`OptionalInt.empty()` in executeDrawRange; all target accessors
    getColorTexture/View + getDepthTexture resolve dynamically through
    MainPass, so the swap propagates). Viewport comes from the bound
    framebuffer dims → native.
  - RETURN: ends the GUI pass, restores the scaled fb, transitions swapchain
    COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL → PRESENT_SRC_KHR (MainPass.end only transitions
    when mainFramebuffer IS the swapchain, so this is required and not doubled).
  - MainPass.end hook (`beforeVkEndCommandBuffer`) skips re-recording when
    `VulkanModUiHelper.copiesRecordedThisFrame` is true.
  - Fail-safe: any exception → restore scaled fb, clear flags → old upscaled-UI
    behavior, never a blank frame.
  - Compile notes: LWJGL `VkCommandBuffer` is NOT on the client compile
    classpath — get the command buffer via reflection (getCommandBuffer().address()
    through getMethod) and invoke `transitionImageLayout` via method reflection
    with the runtime cmd-buffer class; `GpuDevice.backend` is private → read via
    getDeclaredField+setAccessible. Verified all VulkanMod APIs against the
    26.1.2 jar (Renderer.endRenderPass, SwapChain extends Framebuffer w/
    getWidth/Height, VkGpuDevice.gpuTextureFromVulkanImage/createTextureView).
  - Deployed to gradle client + PrismLauncher 26.1; configs debugMode=1.
  - Verify: HUD/chat/UI text should be pixel-crisp at native res while the
    world is upscaled. If the GUI doesn't show (blank/black), check the log for
    "[VKMod] native-res UI ... failed" and the fail-safe restored old behavior.
- **2026-08-10 (21st) — BLACK SCREEN fix: helper must NOT live in the mixin
  package.** The native-res UI build rendered a black screen; the log showed
  `(fsr-reloaded) VulkanMod copy: Mixin transformation of
  com.fsr2mod.mixin.vulkanmod.VulkanModUiHelper failed` repeating every frame.
  That message is KnotClassDelegate.getPostMixinClassByteArray wrapping ANY
  Throwable from IMixinTransformer.transformClassBytes. The trigger: the plain
  (non-@Mixin) helper class `VulkanModUiHelper` lived in
  `com.fsr2mod.mixin.vulkanmod` — inside our mixin config's package
  (`package: com.fsr2mod.mixin`) — so the transformer processed it as a mixin
  class, validation failed, and EVERY load threw → NoClassDefFoundError at the
  call site → the frame-recording path died → black. Root-cause rule: plain
  helper classes referenced by mixins must live OUTSIDE the mixin package
  (this mod's convention: `com.fsr2mod.compat` / `com.fsr2mod.vulkan` /
  `com.fsr2mod.fsr`). FIX: moved `VulkanModUiHelper` to
  `com.fsr2mod.vulkan` (package line changed, file moved), added the import to
  `VulkanModGuiMixin` + `VulkanModMainPassMixin` (the latter previously relied
  on same-package access). Verified in the deployed jar: helper under
  com/fsr2mod/vulkan/, mixin bytecode calls
  com/fsr2mod/vulkan/VulkanModUiHelper.*. Redeployed to gradle client +
  PrismLauncher 26.1.
- **SINGLE-SOURCE SHADERS (2026-08-11, "optimise the overall architecture" A)**: the FSR2/FSR3 GLSL shaders are no longer embedded as Java text-block constants. The CANONICAL copy of each shader is a classpath resource under `src/main/resources/shaders/{fsr2,fsr3}/{NAME}.glsl` (17 files: 6 fsr2 + 11 fsr3 incl. FG concats) — the single source of truth, loaded via `ShaderSourceManager.get(group, name)`. The pipelines (`FSR2Pipeline` 1912→803 lines, `FSR3Pipeline` 2811→1213 lines) compile from the resource; the giant `String X = """..."""` constants are deleted. **Fix workflow changed forever**: edit the canonical .glsl → rebuild → every copy (jar, bootstrap, download pack) updates. No more "all 4/5 copies" patch scripts. Scripts: `scripts/single_source_shaders.py` (extracts embedded constants into resources, JLS 3.10.6-exact text-block semantics — verified byte-equal against the old on-disk bootstrap copies) + `scripts/strip_embedded_shaders.py` (removes the constants, rewrites get() calls). FG shaders are stored as the FULL concatenated source (FG_SHARED_H + FG_PACKING_H + body with newline separators, byte-matching the validated instance copies). **Bootstrap-sync markers**: `ShaderSourceManager.get` now writes a `.glsl.canon` sidecar (SHA-256 of canonical) beside every bootstrap copy; an UNCHANGED bootstrap copy whose canonical moved on is auto-refreshed on next load (jar update → instance updates itself), while downloaded/user-edited copies (content ≠ marker) are never touched. This also FIXED a latent divergence found during the refactor: the PrismLauncher 26.1 instance's on-disk copies were STALE (missing the hand-zone identity reprojection + depth-trust reword + RCAS port — they predate the marker system), and since disk takes precedence, real-play was running older shaders than the gradle-tested build. Refreshed all 17 instance copies to canonical + wrote markers during deploy. FSR1's RCAS stays a single embedded copy (structurally different — 16×16 tile shared-mem vs 32×1 image2D — not a duplication). FSRProcessor's FSR4 GL MV-gen stays embedded (single copy; unification with the VK MV-gen is the A+B follow-up).

- **Dead `isLoaded()` gate in `applyVulkanModProjectionOverride` (2026-08-12, "fsr 4 in gl does not do edge smoothening properly anymore" — the scary log line was a red herring)**: the pure-GL PrismLauncher session logged `FSR4: VulkanMod projection override failed (ClassNotFoundException: net.vulkanmod.vulkan.VRenderSystem) — world renders unjittered, temporal quality degraded` on EVERY launch despite that instance having NO VulkanMod installed. Root cause: a line-fuse bug in the working tree — `if (!VulkanModCompat.isLoaded()) return;` had been merged onto the TAIL of the preceding `//` comment line, so javac treated it as comment text and the gate never compiled (verified: `javap` on both the 17:00 jar and the recompiled class showed the method starting directly with the `jitterAppliedThisFrame` check; after the fix it starts with `invokestatic isLoaded → ifne → return`). Consequence: in any no-VulkanMod instance the override ran `Class.forName("net.vulkanmod.vulkan.VRenderSystem")` every frame, threw, and logged the misleading warning — which reads like a temporal-quality regression but is NOT: in pure GL the world still renders jittered via the vanilla `captureAndJitter` @ModifyArg path, and the VulkanMod projection override only exists because VulkanMod's chunk renderer reads `VRenderSystem.projection` instead of the vanilla buffer. The actual GL FSR4 edge-smoothing investigation concluded the dispatch is healthy (reset=false hasPrev=true, Halton jitter cycling, dt=15.9ms, mvScale display is 1/renderSize but the native SDK hardcodes 1.0 = pixel MVs, which both GL and VK MV-gens emit — consistent); the user's "edges not smooth anymore" was the FG_DX12 config (presented image is the interpolated FG frame, softer by design) and/or 0.25 render scale (4x beyond FSR4's envelope). Fix: split the fused line back onto its own line (only fused `//...if` line in the file — verified by grep). Deployed 17:30 to gradle run/mods + PrismLauncher 26.1 (md5 3ce81daa). Expected next launch: no more `VulkanMod projection override failed` warning in pure-GL instances.
- **Native heap corruption crash (0xC0000374) + no-VulkanMod depth redirect (2026-08-12, "Process crashed with exitcode -1073740940" fix)**: Windows Event Log pinned the crash to 17:53:20 in the PrismLauncher 26.1 instance — javaw.exe, 0xC0000374 STATUS_HEAP_CORRUPTION, faulting module ntdll.dll (the heap manager that DETECTED the corruption, not the culprit), no hs_err written (native heap corruption outside a JVM exception path). Crash session evidence: (1) that instance has NO VulkanMod (every VulkanMod mixin logged ClassNotFoundException); (2) ZERO FSR4/FG log lines — the pipeline never initialized, the whole session ran the FSR1 spatial fallback (config was FSR3_FG_DX12 without VulkanMod → spatialFallbackActive via !fsr4Initialized); (3) `fsr4_diag.log` (native side) ends at 17:08 — NOTHING written during the crash session, proving fsrvk.dll's FSR4/FG code never executed; (4) crash hit right after frame #2269's FSR1 EASU dispatch amid a world-load storm (Voxy init: RocksDB/LMDB/ZSTD natives, 4GB sparse geometry buffer, 10+10 worker threads; c2me natives-math; AMD driver 26.7.1). Conclusion: the mod's native FSR4/FG path was provably NOT running — the heap corruption is attributable to the surrounding native stack (Voxy/c2me/AMD driver), not fsrvk.dll. **One real mod bug exposed and fixed**: `VulkanModRenderTargetMixin` targets VANILLA `RenderTarget` (applies even without VulkanMod) and its gate `renderScaleSwapped()` is ENABLED-INDEPENDENT by design (main-pass presentation hook needs it) — so in the no-VulkanMod instance with `vulkanModRenderScale: true` in config, the depth-view redirect FIRED at 17:52:26 (`[VKMod] getDepthTextureView redirected to MainPass depth` in the log), creating a needless GL texture view of the vanilla depth on the pure-GL path. Fix: added `if (!VulkanModCompat.isLoaded()) return;` FIRST in `fsrAlignDepthViewToMainPass` (bytecode-verified: isLoaded → ifne → renderScaleSwapped → ifne), so the redirect only engages when VulkanMod is actually the renderer. Compiled + jar redeployed 18:35 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup: previous jar was the 17:30 isLoaded-gate build).

- **FG prepare `flags` used the wrong enum constant (2026-08-12, "the edges have sharp spikes" cleanup)**: `fg4_context.cpp` set `prepareDesc.flags = FFX_FRAMEGENERATION_ENABLE_DEPTH_INVERTED`. That constant is a **create-context** flag (`FfxApiCreateContextFramegenerationFlags`, bit 3 = 8, "depth is inverted [1..0]"), but the `ffxDispatchDescFrameGenerationPrepareV2.flags` field takes **dispatch** flags (`FfxApiDispatchFramegenerationFlags`) — where 8 = `FFX_FRAMEGENERATION_FLAG_NO_SWAPCHAIN_CONTEXT_NOTIFY`. So the line was silently setting the wrong flag (harmless in no-swapchain mode, but misleading and a latent trap). Depth-inversion is already correctly NOT set at context creation (`fgDesc.flags = 0` — Minecraft GL depth is near=0/far=1, matching the fsr4_context.cpp note that removed `FFX_UPSCALE_ENABLE_DEPTH_INVERTED`). Fixed to `prepareDesc.flags = 0` (no prepare debug flags used). Verified against the official FSR 4.0.1 `ffx_framegeneration.h`. Rebuilt fsrvk.dll (141,824 bytes), jar redeployed 19:17 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1. NOTE: this is a cleanup, not the spike fix — FG edge spikes on the GL path are the no-real-depth disocclusion limitation (constant far depth → the SDK can't see object boundaries → interpolates across them).
- **FG edge spikes root-caused + one-shot log warning (2026-08-13, "the spikes show up even when the downscaled edges are perfectly smooth")**: the user's repeated "sharp spikes at edges" reports on the GL path trace to FSR3_FG_DX12 double-present, NOT the FSR4 upscaler. Verified against the FSR 4.0.1 `ffx_framegeneration.h`: `ffxDispatchDescFrameGenerationPrepareV2` has **no reactive/disocclusion input** — FG's disocclusion detection runs entirely on depth discontinuities. The GL path's depth probe latches `no real geometry (min=1.000 max=1.000)` (uniform far plane), so FG cannot see object boundaries and interpolates ACROSS them → edge spikes during motion, while the FSR4 base frames (presented in between in double-present mode) stay smooth — matching the user's observation exactly ("downscaled edges perfectly smooth, spikes in output"). Not fixable by feeding reactive (the API has no such field); plain FSR4 has no such artifact (the user confirmed GL FSR4 smooth at 30% scale). Added a one-shot `FG_DX12: no real depth ... spikes are the FG interpolated frames, not the FSR4 upscale` warning in `probeFsr4Depth`'s latch path (new field `fgNoDepthWarned`) so the symptom is explainable in-log. Deployed 09:58 to gradle run/mods + PrismLauncher 26.1. Recommendation to user: run plain FSR4 (both configs now FSR4 at 0.55/0.65) for clean edges; FG on GL fundamentally needs real depth.
- **FSR4 GL MV-gen Y-MIRROR vs the SDK convention — "GL FSR4 jagged edges at 65% while smooth at 30%" (2026-08-13, FIX)**: the white-block-vs-sky screenshot was a pure FSR4 GL session (26.1, no VulkanMod, Sodium, no FG). Every SDK input was healthy (reactive=0 center, hasPrev=true, jitter ±0.5 render px cycling, clean MVs from lastProj, ndcZ=0.999 constant reprojection, waitIdle 0-10ms, output copy to mainRT confirmed) — yet the block's silhouette was jagged while interiors stayed smooth, and the severity scaled with render scale (0.30 smooth / 0.65 jagged). Root cause: **the GL MV_GEN_CS emits vertically MIRRORED motion vectors**. The FSR4/FSR3.1 SDK interprets MVs in y-DOWN screen space (positive mv.y = content moved UP on screen — the AGENTS.md-documented convention the VK MV-gen was flipped to match at 2026-08-08 16:38, per the FSR4 docs example). The GL shader computes prevUv in GL texture space (row 0 = BOTTOM of the render), so its emitted mv.y is the vertical mirror of the SDK's expectation. The SDK fetches history at the mirrored vertical position → at high-contrast silhouettes (white block vs uniform sky — no texture noise to mask the mismatch, unlike terrain) the luma-lock rejects the misaligned history → the jittered spatial sample shows through → jagged edges, while interiors (uniform, low-contrast) accumulate fine. Severity scales with the vertical MV magnitude: idle-bob rotation MVs ∝ focal length (render height), so 0.65 (focal ~429px) produces 2-3px bob MVs → 2-6px mirrored misalignment → lock rejection; 0.30 (focal ~165px) stays sub-threshold → smooth. FSR2/3 GL was never affected because its SDK consumes MVs as convention-agnostic offsets in the app's own uv space (self-consistent regardless of orientation); the D3D12 FSR4 SDK is convention-bound — the same reason the VK path needed its 16:38 Y-flip (terrain warp = the same mirrored-MV misalignment with larger MVs during camera motion). Fix (one line, in the embedded FSR4 GL MV_GEN_CS in FSRProcessor.java): `mv.y = -mv.y;` immediately before `imageStore(uOutputMV, ...)`. Negate at the store only — the reprojection chain and the T&C prevColor sampling stay in GL uv space (self-consistent), only the SDK-facing sign is fixed; mvLen-based reactive terms (mvReactive/motionFloor/cutout/T&C gates) are sign-invariant and unchanged. Validated: `gradlew build` green, `mv.y = -mv.y` verified in the class constant pool, jar redeployed 10:40 to gradle-client run/mods + PrismLauncher 26.1. Expected next run: [STAGING_VERIFY] mv.y signs flipped vs before (cosmetic), and the white block / sun / cloud silhouettes accumulate with smooth edges at 0.65; if the Y convention was instead already correct (i.e. the SDK is convention-agnostic like FSR2), the flip is harmless (self-consistent offset field) — the risk is bounded to motion-direction accuracy, which the VK path's validated 16:38 parity strongly contraindicates.
- **FSR4 GL/SDK contract REVERTED to the pre-Vulkan-fixing values — "the smoothening is still not working properly. it was working fine before all of the vulkan fixing" (2026-08-13, FIX)**: after the 10:40 Y-flip deploy still read as jagged (10:42 session), the user's anchor ("working fine before all of the vulkan fixing") was taken literally. `git diff f7a8682` enumerated exactly what the Vulkan-fixing changed on the SHARED GL path: (1) `fsr4_context.cpp` jitterOffset.y `+jitterY → -jitterY`, (2) motionVectorScale `1/renderSize → 1.0`, (3) FSR4 GL MV_GEN_CS constant fallback `ndcZ 0.0 → 0.999`, plus (4) the 10:40 mv.y flip. Root-cause synthesis from the FSR4 4.0.2 integration docs (gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk2/techniques/super-resolution-ml): docs confirm pixel-space MVs + scale 1.0, jitter composite (2*jX/W,-2*jY/H), and "positive mv.y = content moved up" — the current values are docs-CORRECT — but the docs-correct REAL-MV reprojection at constant depth (ndcZ=0.999 ≈ 100-500 blocks) carries a bob-parallax error ∝ focal length for content nearer than ~100 blocks, which crossed the SDK's luma-lock tolerance at 0.65 render scale (jagged silhouettes) while staying sub-threshold at 0.30 (smooth) — the observed scale-dependence. The PRE-fix GL was smooth because scale=1/renderSize crushed the MVs to ~0.001: the SDK accumulated IN PLACE with jitter-only de-jitter = temporal supersampling, immune to the constant-depth error. The empirically-validated state is the pre-fix contract, so ALL FOUR deltas were reverted (raw +jitterY, scale 1/renderSize, ndcZ=0.0, y-up MVs) — restoring the exact pre-Vulkan-fixing behavior the user validated. Tradeoff (accepted — it IS the pre-fix behavior): no MV-based motion tracking; during camera motion the SDK keeps history in place, the luma-lock rejects, and frames go spatial (the "pixelated when moving" tradeoff documented at 16:24 — this is what the user's "working fine" baseline was). Validated: `build_native.bat` recompiled fsr4_context.cpp (fsrvk.dll 141,824 bytes, 10:54), `gradlew build` green, jar embedded the fresh DLL (unzip -l 141,824), mv.y flip absent + ndcZ=0.0 present in the class constant pool, deployed 10:55 to gradle-client run/mods + PrismLauncher 26.1. Expected: GL FSR4 edges smooth at 0.65 like before the Vulkan fixing. If the user wants motion tracking back, the next experiment is per-path real MVs (scale 1.0) WITH the shared depth raised to 0.9995 so the SDK's depth and the MV-gen's ndcZ=0.999 agree (the 16:24 direction, but with the depth made consistent — the current config's depth 0.5 vs MV depth 0.999 mismatch is the untested variable).
- **FSR4 motion-tracking experiment + SDK sharpening wired to the slider (2026-08-13, user-approved "do num 1 and hook num2 to the sharpness slider")**: two changes. **(1) Motion tracking** — the GL FSR4 path gets REAL pixel motion vectors again (motionVectorScale back to 1.0 in `fsr4_context.cpp` dispatch; this was reverted to 1/renderSize at 10:55 because docs-correct MVs made edges jagged, but that regression is now attributed to the depth/MV MISMATCH, not the MVs). The matching change sets BOTH depth sides to far depth so they AGREE: the shared depth constant the SDK receives is now device 0.9995 (was 0.5) in `copyDepthToShared`, and the MV-gen constant fallback is now ndcZ 0.999 (was 0.0) — device 0.9995 == ndcZ 0.999 == z≈500 blocks, the SAME world depth (0.9995 = 0.999·0.5+0.5). Rotation MVs are depth-independent (exact at any constant); translation parallax ~0 at z≈500. The 10:55 state had ndcZ=0.0 + device 0.5 (both z≈1) with crushed MVs = in-place supersampling (validated smooth). The 08-08 desync state (ndcZ=0.999 + SDK 0.5) was the scale-dependent jaggies. This is the first state where BOTH are far and MVs are real — if edges regress, revert BOTH the scale and the 0.9995/0.999 depths together.
