Jitter offset stays RAW (validated smooth GL baseline; VK uses the same +2·jY/H render so parity holds). **(2) FSR4 SDK internal sharpening** — new `fsr::g_fsr4Sharpness` global (diag.h) + `Fsr4Native.setSharpness(float)` JNI (jni_bridge.cpp) + `syncNativeDiagnostics()` calls it with `config.sharpness` every frame; `Fsr4Context::dispatch` sets `enableSharpening = g_fsr4Sharpness > 0.001` and `sharpness = g_fsr4Sharpness` (SDK range [0,1], direct slider mapping, 0 disables). Same slider drives FSR1/2/3's GLSL RCAS. Both GL and VKMod dispatch route through the same Fsr4Context::dispatch, so both honor it. Validated: cmake build green (fsrvk.dll 141,824 bytes), `gradlew build` green, bytecode verified (setSharpness in Fsr4Native.class, 0.9995f depth constant, ndcZ=0.999 in shader string), DLL symbol verified, deployed 11:51 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (all three jars sha256-identical).
- **FSR4 still-camera MV gate — "the upscaler thinks I'm going right when I'm not moving at all" (2026-08-13 fix)**: the 11:51 motion-tracking build (real pixel MVs at motionVectorScale=1.0 + far depth 0.9995/0.999) made the FSR4 SDK follow REAL per-pixel MVs for the first time. The idle camera bob is real micro-motion — [DEBUG_MV] readbacks showed ~0.4-0.8px MVs (oscillating ±, nonZero=8/9) while the player was still, and `camStill=false` on every [DIAG] sample — whose NEAR-content parallax the constant-depth (z≈500) reprojection underestimates (it reprojects everything as if 500 blocks away). Standing still, the SDK reprojected history by those wrong bob MVs → misaligned history → the accumulated image drifted/shook sideways ("going right"). The validated-smooth 10:55 baseline never had this because its 1/renderSize scale crushed MVs to ~0.001 — the SDK accumulated in place. Fix: the GL FSR4 MV-gen already had the CPU-computed bob-EXCLUDED `uCamMotion` flag (position delta > 0.05 blocks OR rotation delta > 0.01 rad vs previous frame — same signal the cutout detector gates on); added `if (uCamMotion <= 0.0) mv = vec2(0.0);` right before `imageStore(uOutputMV)`. When the camera is genuinely still the SDK accumulates history IN PLACE (the validated-smooth behavior — also collapses the reactive mask to 0 via mvLen=0 → full accumulation); when the player walks/turns (uCamMotion=1) real MVs flow and the SDK tracks motion. This is the FSR2/3 `uCamStillExact` idea applied to the FSR4 GL MV-gen. Validated: `gradlew build` green, deployed 12:10 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (all three jars sha256-identical d3dd76cd). Expected: standing still → no drift/shake, smooth edges; walking/panning → motion tracking (the experiment's goal).
- **FSR4 SDK depth restored to mid 0.5 — "edge smoothing present but weaker than the validated 10:55 baseline" (2026-08-13 v2 fix)**: the 12:10 build (still-camera MV gate) killed the phantom rightward drift (MVs verified 0.0 standing still, [DEBUG_MV] nonZero=0/9) but the user's next report was that edge smoothing was present yet weaker than the validated baseline. The remaining experiment delta was the FAR SDK depth constant (device 0.9995, set at 11:51 to match the MV shader's far reprojection). Restored the SDK's constant depth to device 0.5 (the validated pre-experiment value) — a constant FAR depth makes the FSR4 SDK classify the whole frame as sky/far-plane (its internal sky/disocclusion logic) and discard history → weaker temporal accumulation → softer/jagged edges. The MV shader's constant reprojection STAYS far (ndcZ 0.999): standing still the still-camera MV gate zeroes MVs so the reprojection depth is never used, and during motion far reprojection keeps rotation MVs exact + translation parallax ~0; the SDK depth and MV reprojection only need to agree when the SDK would use the depth (disocclusion), and constant depth disables that regardless of value. Net config vs the validated baseline: motionVectorScale 1.0 (real MVs) + still-gate (mv=0 standing still) + SDK depth 0.5 + MV ndcZ 0.999 + sharpness slider hooked. Validated: `gradlew build` green (jar d48ec1f3), bytecode verified (clearDepth array at offset 214 now 0.5f, was 0.9995f), deployed 12:30 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (all three jars sha256-identical). Expected: standing still → the validated baseline's full in-place accumulation → smooth edges at 65%; walking/turning → real MVs track (no smear).
- **FSR4 GL camera-motion reactive floor (2026-08-13, "walking smear" fix — FSR2/3 uCamReactive parity)**: after the motion-tracking experiment (real pixel MVs, scale 1.0) + still-camera MV gate (mv=0 when uCamMotion=0), the remaining FSR4 GL artifact was smear/edge-wobble while WALKING: the constant-depth MVs (z≈500) UNDERESTIMATE near terrain's true screen motion, so the SDK blended misaligned history at ~90% weight. FSR2/3 already solved this with a CPU camera-motion reactive FLOOR (2026-08-07 "fix the shaking" entry). Ported to the FSR4 GL MV-gen: new `uniform float uCamReactive` (0..0.9), applied AFTER the MV/shading/motion terms and BEFORE the sky/cutout overrides: `reactive = max(reactive, uCamReactive)`. Java side computes it in generateFSR4MotionVectors from the SAME per-frame deltas that drive uCamMotion (camPosDelta/camRotDelta): translation normalized to PER-TICK via measured frame time (FSR2/3's proven units: walk 0.216/tick → 0.30, sprint 0.28 → 0.42, elytra → 0.9 cap — raw per-frame deltas shrink at high FPS, 0.072/frame @60fps vs 0.030 @144fps, which would silently derate the floor), rotation stays per-frame (fires only on extreme turns ≥0.04 rad/frame; pans are depth-exact + covered by the MV ramp). Bob ≈0.01-0.02 blocks/frame → dPosTick ~0.03-0.06 < 0.05 threshold → floor 0 standing still = bit-identical to the pre-floor path (the still-camera mv=0 gate + uCamReactive=0 keep standing-still accumulation untouched). First-frame guard: camRotDelta is NaN on frame 1 → floor 0. New fields `fsr4CamReactive` + `fsr4PrevMotionNanos`; uniform lookup `mv_uCamReactive` (added to the MV_GEN_CS uniform diag line). Shader declaration + application in the embedded MV_GEN_CS only (no disk copy — FSR4 GL MV-gen lives inline in FSRProcessor.java). Validated: gradlew build green, javap confirms mv_uCamReactive/fsr4CamReactive fields + uCamReactive shader string, deployed 12:xx to run/mods + PrismLauncher 26.1 (sha256-identical).
- **FSR4 GL shading-change reactive softening (2026-08-13, "voxy lods don't fully render properly" fix)**: Voxy swaps LOD tiles ASYNCHRONOUSLY while the camera moves; each swap changes the 8x8 tile luminance histogram, the 2-frame persistence gate confirms it, and the shading-change term (`shadingReactive = smoothstep(0.10, 0.45, shadingDiff)`) hit the FULL 1.0 → the FSR4 SDK discarded ALL history on those tiles → the low-res scaled LOD content rendered SPATIALLY → upscaled low-detail geometry = white stretched polygon artifacts (log-verified: `STAGING_VERIFY reactive=1.00` with tiny `mv=(-0.9,-0.4)` at the exact AsyncNodeManager "Resizing scratch data buffer" moment — MVs that small can't fire the 0.5-16px MV ramp, so reactive=1.0 was the shading-change term). Fix: `shadingReactive = smoothstep(0.10, 0.45, shadingDiff) * 0.5` — caps the term at 0.5 so LOD-streamed tiles keep HALF their history weight (blend current+previous instead of pure spatial); genuine disocclusion still reaches full discard via the MV ramp (moving content has real MVs). Only the embedded FSR4 GL MV_GEN_CS (no disk copy). Validated: gradlew build green, bytecode confirmed (`* 0.5` present), deployed 12:5x to run/mods + PrismLauncher 26.1 (sha256-identical).
- **FSR4 fast-motion floor softened — "when I move fast then no upscaling happens" (2026-08-13, v2 of the uCamReactive floor)**: the v1 floor capped at 0.9 with a 0.04 rad/frame rotation threshold, and the user's next report was "when I move fast then no upscaling happens". The session log was decisive: `[STAGING_VERIFY] reactive=0.90 mv=(3.9,0.2)` during fast motion — the MV ramp alone gives ~0.22 for a 3.9px MV, so the 0.90 came ENTIRELY from the CPU floor, and at 0.9 reactive the FSR4 SDK keeps only ~10% history → pure spatial upscale → jagged/no temporal reconstruction ("no upscaling"). Fix in `generateFSR4MotionVectors`: (1) cap 0.9 → 0.7 (elytra now keeps 30% history; the SDK's internal box clamp still rejects misaligned history, so the smear-margin cost is small; walk 0.30 / sprint 0.42 floors unchanged); (2) rotation threshold 0.04 → 0.10 rad/frame (5.7°/frame = 343°/s at 60fps) — rotation MVs are depth-independent/EXACT at constant depth, so a normal fast pan/flick was flooring the WHOLE screen to 0.9 even though the SDK could track it perfectly; only a genuine whip fires now, matching the FSR2/3 comment "pans are depth-exact, MV ramp covers them". Standing still stays bit-identical (both terms 0 when still). Validated: build green, `0.7d` cap + `0.1f` rotation threshold confirmed in the deployed bytecode (`fsr4CamReactive` computation, translation slope 0.05/0.55 unchanged), all three deployed jars sha256-identical (15:26). Expected next run: `STAGING_VERIFY reactive` should now read ≤0.7 during fast motion (was 0.90), and fast flicks/pans should keep visible temporal smoothing instead of going pure spatial. NOTE: the skip cluster (#2619-2639) in the same session was a separate GPU-saturation event (fast motion + Voxy meshing + chunk streaming → fence timeout → FSR4 presents the previous frame); unrelated to the floor.
- **LINUX FSR4 COMPATIBILITY — FSR4 selection runs the mod's FSR3 temporal engine when the AMD SDK is unavailable (2026-08-14, "linux is not compatible with fsr 4")**: the AMD FSR4 SDK (`amd_fsr4_api.dll` + DX12 backend) ships Windows-only — no Linux build exists, and vkd3d-proton's FSR4 (3.0, DXIL→SPIR-V cooperative-matrix translation) is a game-launcher layer that cannot help a native Java mod's D3D12/Win32 interop, plus its fast path needs RDNA4 (our 860M is RDNA 3.5 — the SDK itself falls back to FSR 3.1 compute there anyway). So the reliable+licensed Linux path is routing a FSR4 config to the mod's OWN GLSL FSR3 temporal engine. Implementation: new `isLinux()` + `fsr4SdkSupported()` (= `Fsr4Native.isAvailable()`) in FSRProcessor; `isFsr4()` now ALSO requires `fsr4SdkSupported()` (false on Linux → every SDK block — D3D12 pool init, dispatch, saturation guards, FG, notifications — stays inert), and `isFsr3()` now returns true for `config.version == FSR4 && !fsr4SdkSupported()` (the FSR3 temporal pipeline handles jitter, MV gen, reactive mask and dispatch end-to-end — same contract, GLSL compute, no D3D12). Settings: `getVersionStatus(FSR4)` returns a NEW selectable-with-notice `VersionStatus.okWithNotice("AMD FSR4 SDK is Windows-only — on Linux, FSR 4 runs the FSR3 temporal engine")` on Linux (still blocked under VulkanMod — GL compute unavailable there), VersionStatus gained a `notice` field shown in the pill tooltip + under the version grid when the current version carries one; one-shot session log records the routing. Windows behavior unchanged (SDK present → real FSR4). NOTE: this also covers a Windows box with missing SDK DLLs (graceful FSR3 instead of silent FSR1). CHANGELOG "Linux compatibility" section added.
- **FSR3_FG_DX12 on the VulkanMod path — step 1 (single-present) WIRED (2026-08-15, "what about in windows but on the vulkanmod side")**: FG was previously disabled under VulkanMod by design (`&& !VulkanModCompat.isActive()` on both init sites, version-status blocker "VulkanMod active — GL required for FG", and the VKMod dispatch block only drove `isFsr4()`). Step 1 makes FSR3_FG_DX12 selectable under VulkanMod and runs the FSR4-uplevel + FG pipeline end-to-end in SINGLE-PRESENT mode (the real frame presents as today; the generated frame stays in pool 5 — nothing reads it yet). Changes: (1) VKMod dispatch gate `isFsr4()` → `(isFsr4() || isFrameGenDx12())` (init + jitter gates too); (2) VKMod FG init un-gated (the block at the VKMod init site now gates on `VulkanModCompat.isActive()`, presentColor = pool 4 — the FSR4 output, no GUI; pool 6 is only populated on the GL path, so step-1 generated frames carry NO HUD); (3) new `stashVkModFgParams()` in the VKMod dispatch block (stashes fgFrameID/fgDeltaNs/prevJitter/near/far/fovY from lastProj + camera pos/up/right/fwd from the PREVIOUS frame's camera, matching the one-frame-delayed pooled scene) + `onVkModFsr4OutputReady()` which runs nativePrepare/nativeGenerate/nativeFinish; (4) both mixin/UIHelper sites call `onVkModFsr4OutputReady()` immediately after `waitForD3D12Output()` succeeds — the point where pool 4 (FSR4 output) is guaranteed fully written by the D3D12 dispatch; (5) version-status FSR3_FG_DX12 under VulkanMod now returns okWithNotice instead of blocking. Verified: compileClientJava + build green, all three deployed jars sha256-identical (run/mods + 26.1 instance + backup `.bak-20260815-vkmod-fg-step1`). Proof of step 1 = `[Fg4Context] generate: frameID=... OK took=...ms` in fsr4_diag.log while FSR3_FG_DX12 is selected under VulkanMod (no crashes, single-present continues). Step 2 (planned): swapchain→pool 6 GUI capture + actually present pool 5 (alternating or double-present through VulkanMod's swapchain, with GUI double-paint avoided).
- **FG_DX12 STEP 2 on VulkanMod — the double-present + FSR4 motion-adaptive jitter (2026-08-15, "implement step 2. also in fsr4, it was shaking a bit when i moved and barely noticeable but still there when standing still")**: step 1 (2026-08-15) proved FG init+generate on the VKMod path in single-present mode. Step 2 wires the generated frames onto the display, and the FSR4 shake gets the FSR2/3-style motion-adaptive jitter treatment. **Present architecture (real-first — VulkanMod owns the real-frame present)**: (1) `VulkanModUiHelper.endNativeResGui` records `recordVkModSwapchainToFgPresentCopy` — swapchain A (real frame + GUI, still COLOR_ATTACHMENT_OPTIMAL after the GUI aux pass, BEFORE `transitionToPresent`) → pool 6 (FG_PRESENT, display-size RGBA8) — this is the SDK's presentColor; HUDLessColor stays pool 4, so the SDK extracts the HUD from the diff and composites it onto generated frames (GL parity). Non-scaled path skips the capture (presentColor=pool 4 — the GUI is baked into the FSR4 output at 1:1). (2) New `VulkanModRendererMixin` (Renderer.endFrame TAIL) → `FSRProcessor.presentVkModFgFrame()`: waitForFrameCopy (the main submission with the capture + FSR4 copies just completed → pool 6/pool 4 valid) → FG prepare+generate (stashed params) → `acquireVkModFgImage(swapchainId)` (bounded 16ms; starved swapchain → skip = single-present) → `recordVkModFgOutputCopyOneShot(img, w, h)` — the NEWLY-WIRED one-shot submit (the dedicated `g_vkModCopyCmdBuf`/`g_vkModCopyFence`, created by `initExternalImages` but previously UNUSED — its comment said "deliberately unused"; now it records pool5 → acquired image B (UNDEFINED → TRANSFER_DST → PRESENT_SRC), submits on the graphics queue, waits bounded) → `presentVkModFgImage(presentQueue, swapchain, idx)` (queue via `DeviceManager.getPresentQueue().vkQueue().address()`, consumed via the acquire semaphore). Display: real N first (VulkanMod's own present), generated N second. Every failure degrades to single-present. **Key ordering constraint that forced this shape**: the generate needs pool 6 = real N + GUI, but the capture executes with the main submission at endFrame — so the generate CANNOT run before the submit (step 1 ran it pre-submit at `waitForD3D12Output`; that is why the pre-GUI call sites in the mixin + UIHelper were REMOVED and the run moved to the endFrame TAIL). The capture-vs-generate race (VK copy in the main submission writes pool 6, the D3D12 FG SDK reads it) is closed by waitForFrameCopy. `vkModFgNeedsCapture` guard: frames where GuiRenderer.render never ran have no capture → FG skipped that frame (stale pool 6 would corrupt the generated frame). **New native surface**: `importFgPoolTextureAsVkImage(poolPtr, idx, slot, fmt)` (display-size import — slot 0 = pool 5 FG_OUTPUT, slot 1 = pool 6 FG_PRESENT; the generic slot importer uses RENDER-size extents so it can't alias these), `recordVkModSwapchainToFgPresentCopy`, `recordVkModFgOutputCopyOneShot`, `acquireVkModFgImage` (lazy `g_vkModFgAcquireSemaphore`), `presentVkModFgImage`, teardown in `destroyVkModImages` (images + semaphore + the D3D12 keep-alive refs). FG init on the VKMod path now picks presentColor by path: `vkFgScaledPath = renderScaleSwapped()` → 6 (scaled) / 4 (non-scaled), HUDLessColor=4. **FSR4 VKMod motion-adaptive jitter** (the "shaking when I moved" fix): the VKMod jitter block previously always passed full ±0.5 render-px Halton; when the camera moves, FSR4 renders spatially (constant-depth MVs can't track translation) and the full jitter oscillates on fresh frames = shake. Now per-frame camera deltas (dedicated `vkModPrevCam*` fields — the FSR2/3 `fsr23*` block never runs on the VKMod path) drive `ampScale = 1.0 - 0.6*clamp(max((dPos-0.02)/0.08, (dRot-0.006)/0.014), 0, 1)` (walk ~0.07 blocks/frame → 0.625 factor → 40% amplitude; idle bob 0.01-0.02/0.002-0.005 → full amplitude standing still). 40% floor keeps sub-pixel diversity so edges still reconstruct while moving. Validated: build_native.bat green (fsrvk.dll 147,968 bytes — up from 141,312 step-1), gradlew build green, jar deployed to gradle-client run/mods + PrismLauncher 26.1 (sha256 c1190104... identical, backups `.bak-20260815-vkmod-fg-step1`). **Next run must show in fsr4_diag.log**: `importFgPoolTextureAsVkImage: SUCCESS — FG_OUTPUT/FG_PRESENT`, `FG_DX12: VulkanMod double-present ACTIVE`, and generate throttle lines still firing (`[Fg4Context] generate: frameID=N OK`) — if the double-present line never appears, check `acquireVkModFgImage` returning -1 (starved) or the capture guard (needs the GUI path); the real frame presents normally either way (single-present).
  - **BUGFIX (2026-08-15, same session)**: the first step-2 deployment ran 1448 frames with ZERO `[Fg4Context] generate` lines after the colorIdx=6 init — the FG run was bailing silently every frame. Root cause: `presentVkModFgFrame()` reset `vkModFgCapturedThisFrame = false` at its TOP, then the `vkModFgNeedsCapture && !vkModFgCapturedThisFrame` guard read the just-reset value → always false → scaled path returned before the generate (no log — silent). Fix: the reset moved to the VKMod dispatch block next to `stashVkModFgParams()` (frame-start reset — the capture, recorded during the GUI phase later in the same frame, sets it true; endFrame consumes). The game log's only FG_DX12 line was `Fg4Native init OK` with zero failures — confirming the silent-bail path. Rebuilt (sha 5b3eac51) + redeployed to both targets; bytecode verified `presentVkModFgFrame` no longer clobbers the flag.
  - **FG TRANSLATION GATE (2026-08-15, "i dont see any form of edge smoothening" with FG on)**: after the double-present went live, the user reported the image lost all edge smoothing. The FSR4 side was healthy (jitter flowing, projection override firing every frame, reactive/MV readbacks explainable by center-block content + camera state — NOT an accumulation failure), so the regression was the FG-generated frames themselves: at constant depth (the VKMod reality — the DMP fb depth is cleared 1.0, never written), the MV-gen's TRANSLATION MVs are ~0 (far-plane assumption), so the FG SDK — whose disocclusion detection runs entirely on depth discontinuities (constant 0.5 → NONE) and whose warp trusts the MVs — cannot track a walk: the generated frame is a static blend of two offset frames → edge smear, shown at 50% duty cycle (real-first/FG-second is structurally forced — VulkanMod owns the real-frame present, and the pool-6 capture executes with the main submission, so the generate can only run post-submit; GL's FG-first order is unreachable). Fix: `presentVkModFgFrame()` still runs prepare+generate EVERY frame (preserving the FG SDK's consecutive-frameID pairing — skipping the generate would make the next FG frame interpolate a 2-frame gap), but skips the acquire/copy/present when `vkModFgEyeSpeed > 0.5` blocks/sec (the eyeSpeed already computed at the setVkModCamMotion call site, stored in a field; bob ~0.13 stays, crouch-walk 1.08+ gates). Single-present during motion (only the smooth real frames), 2x resumes when still/rotating (rotation MVs are depth-independent → FG interpolates correctly). One-shot log `FG_DX12: VulkanMod translation gate ENGAGED`. If the user STILL sees no smoothing while standing still after this, the isolation test is: toggle version to FSR4 (no FG) — smooth there ⇒ FG frames were it; still jagged ⇒ FSR4 accumulation itself regressed (next suspects: the motion-adaptive jitter scaling or the reactive mask). Rebuilt + deployed (sha 5ff07b41).
- **VKMod FSR4 jagged-while-walking = the blanket translation ramp saturated reactive to 255 (2026-08-17, "it is still jagged" fix)**: after the FG translation gate fixed the FG-frame smear, the user still saw jagged edges. The log settled it: `REACTIVE READBACK min=255 max=255` at frame 65 while walking (eyeSpeed=4.01) — the VKMod MV-gen's no-real-depth translation ramp `smoothstep(0.3, 1.5, uCamMotion)` hit 1.0 on ANY deliberate movement, so the FSR4 SDK kept ZERO history during walks → pure spatial upscale → jagged terrain edges, exactly where the user compares against GL. The GL FSR4 path is smooth because it uses a CAPPED camera-motion FLOOR (`fsr4CamReactive` in `generateFSR4MotionVectors`: `clamp(max((dPosTick-0.05)/0.55, (camRotDelta-0.10)/0.05), 0, 0.7)` — walk 0.216/tick → 0.30, sprint 0.28 → 0.42, cap 0.7) so the SDK keeps ~70% history during motion and the internal box clamp rejects the misaligned constant-depth history (tiny smear margin at extreme speed = the accepted GL-validated tradeoff). Fix (shader only — `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`): replace the full-reactive ramp with an EXACT port of the GL floor, sky-skip preserved: `float dPosTick = pc.uCamMotion / 20.0; float camFloor = clamp(max((dPosTick - 0.05) / 0.55, (pc.uCamRotDelta - 0.10) / 0.05), 0.0, 0.7); reactive = max(reactive, camFloor);`. uCamMotion is eye SPEED in blocks/sec, so dividing by 20 (ticks/sec) reproduces the GL per-tick normalization FPS-independently (walk 4.3 → 0.215/tick → 0.30 — matches the GL value exactly). Idle bob (0.13 blocks/s → 0.0065/tick) stays under the 0.05 threshold → floor 0 → standing-still accumulation bit-identical to before. Rotation term fires only on whips ≥ 0.10 rad/frame (rotation MVs are depth-exact; the MV ramp covers ordinary pans — GL parity). Validated: glslangValidator clean, SPIR-V 10,780 → 11,292 bytes + header regenerated (BYTE-count comment), fsrvk.dll 136,192 → 147,968 bytes, `gradlew build` green, deployed 21:40 to gradle-client resources + PrismLauncher 26.1 (backup `fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar.bak-20260817-fgfloor`; all three jars sha `d6fcf8c2`). Expected next run: REACTIVE READBACK while walking should read ~77 (0.30·255) instead of 255 — the SDK keeps ~70% history → edges reconstruct from jitter samples during motion instead of rendering spatially. If the walk now reads smooth but smears slightly at sprint, the cap 0.7 is the knob (0.9 = more history/more smear margin); if it still reads 255, check that `setVkModCamMotion` eyeSpeed values are actually reaching the shader (the cam-motion arrival probe).

- **VK FSR4 "thinks the player is moving when it's not" — still-camera mv=0 gate (2026-08-18, "it is still jaged and the upscaler thinks the player is moving when its not" fix)**: the GL MV_GEN_CS (the validated-smooth reference) has a `if (uCamMotion <= 0.0) mv = vec2(0.0)` gate that forces MVs to zero when the CPU still-flag is clear — because the idle bob + per-frame jitter delta still produce 0.5-2.5px MVs while the player stands perfectly still. `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` was MISSING this gate (its only mv=0 was the sky camera-locked rule), so standing still the SDK reprojected history by wrong micro-motion (misaligned accumulation → "thinks moving") and mvLen>0 raised mvReactive/motionFloor (~0.13-0.15) → reduced temporal accumulation → jagged edges exactly where GL reads smooth. Fix (shader only): ported the gate as `if (pc.uCamMotion <= 0.5 && pc.uCamRotDelta <= 0.01) mv = vec2(0.0)` placed after the sky camera-locked rule — the VK path carries eye SPEED (blocks/sec) instead of GL's boolean, so 0.5 separates idle bob (~0.13) from walk (~4.3); rotation reuses GL's 0.01 rad/frame threshold (standing bob ~0.001, slow pan ~0.0105+). mv=0 collapses mvLen→0 → mvReactive/motionFloor 0, camFloor is already 0 for a still camera, and the cutout gate is camMoving=false → reactive 0 → full in-place accumulation, bit-identical to GL standing-still. SPIR-V 11292 → 11548 bytes, DLL 147,968 → 148,480, deployed 15:44 (backup `.bak-20260818-stillgate`). Tradeoff: while the camera is still a moving mob/particle gets mv=0 and accumulates in place (mild ghost) — the GL path covers that with its T&C auto-reactive term, which the VK path does not have (known gap; the user's report is static-terrain jaggies, the dominant case).

- **VK FSR4 jitter double-count — MV-gen must use CLEAN matrices, not jittered (2026-08-18, "no edge smoothening + shakes when starting to move" fix)**: the VK path built its MV-gen currViewProj from `lastProjJittered` with a comment claiming "GL-parity". That claim was FALSE — the GL FSR4 MV_GEN_CS uses `lastProj` (CLEAN) with an explicit comment: the scene renders jittered and the SDK receives jitterOffset separately, so MVs must carry ONLY true camera motion; using the jittered matrix bakes the per-frame jitter delta into the MV, and since the SDK ALSO gets jitterOffset the jitter is double-counted → history samples misplaced every frame → smearing during motion + "the upscaler thinks the player is moving when it's not" + jagged standing-still edges. (FSR2/3 GLSL legitimately use jittered MVs because their ACCUMULATE shader cancels the jitter via uMotionVectorJitterCancellation; FSR4 has no such GL pass.) Fix (Java only): the VK block now uses `lastProj` (clean) for currViewProj and saves that clean matrix into prevViewProj, exactly like the GL path; the shader push-constant comment corrected to "CLEAN — GL-parity" (comment-only, SPIR-V unchanged). Combined with the same-day still-camera mv=0 gate, the VK standing-still MVs are now truly ~0 and the SDK accumulates in place. Deployed 15:50 (backup `.bak-20260818-cleanmv`).

- **VK FSR4 slow-pan terrain smear — still-gate rotation threshold 0.01 → 0.003 (2026-08-18, "when I slowly move my camera I see smearing of the terrain" fix)**: the still-camera mv=0 gate (added the same day) used GL's 0.01 rad/frame rotation threshold, but a DELIBERATE slow pan lands at 0.005-0.010 rad/frame — log-verified (frame 146: rotDelta=0.00590 → MV=0.000, reactive=0): the gate fired and forced mv=0 while the camera was genuinely rotating, so the SDK accumulated history IN PLACE against content moving on screen → terrain smear. GL tolerates this because its T&C auto-reactive term catches the per-pixel color change; the VK path has no T&C. Rotation MVs are DEPTH-INDEPENDENT (exact at constant depth), so a slow pan should flow real MVs and let the SDK track it — only the idle bob (standing ~0.001 rad/frame, pitch swing ~0.002 per the GL comment) must be zeroed. Fix: `pc.uCamRotDelta <= 0.003` (sits between the bob and the slowest deliberate pan). SPIR-V 11548 → 11564 bytes, DLL 148,480, deployed 15:55 (backup `.bak-20260818-slowpan`).

- **FG jitterY sign was INVERTED vs FSR4 — generated frames vertically misaligned (2026-08-18, "the anti-aliasing is not working properly" fix)**: fg4_context.cpp still passed `prepareDesc.jitterOffset.y = -params.jitterY` (the 2026-08-08 negation) while fsr4_context.cpp had REVERTED to raw `+jitterY` on 2026-08-13 (empirically: pre-fix GL raw was validated smooth, negated correlated with edge jaggies on both paths). FG was never reverted with it → every FG-generated frame de-jittered vertically inverted → the two input FSR4 frames misaligned by 2*(J_N.y − J_N-1.y) ≈ up to 2px → vertical blur/ghost at edges in HALF the displayed frames (double-present) → "AA not working properly" despite healthy FSR4 accumulation (log: reactive 2-38 + real MVs on slow pans). Fix (native only): `prepareDesc.jitterOffset.y = params.jitterY` (raw), matching fsr4_context.cpp; comment cites the 2026-08-13 revert. DLL 148,480, deployed 16:02 (backup `.bak-20260818-fgjitter`). Known remaining FG limitation (not this bug): constant depth (0.5) on the VKMod path means the FG SDK has no depth discontinuities for disocclusion — expect minor edge ghosting on generated frames during rotation regardless; gating the FG present on rotation (like the translation gate) is the follow-up if it still reads soft.
- **"Jagged sun edges with VulkanMod (both FSR4 and FSR3-FG-DX12)" — missing sky-pixel classifier in the VK MV-gen (2026-08-18, FIX)**: the user reported jagged sun edges when looking at the sun at a diagonal, on the VKMod path, in BOTH FSR4 and FSR3-FG modes, while GL stayed smooth. Root cause: `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`'s `isSkyLike` was ONLY the loose bright-desaturated test `(skyLuma > 0.5 && skyChroma < 0.25)` (plus the depth far-plane, which is FALSE in noRealDepth mode) — and BLUE SKY FAILS it (chroma ~0.5 > 0.25 at luma ~0.64). The GL MV_GEN_CS classifies sky with the isWhiteSky0/isBlueSky0 tests + screen-Y guard (`luma0 > 0.58 && chroma0 < 0.12`, `luma0 > 0.55 && b > max(r,g)*1.15`, `uv.y > 0.5` GL-bottom-up). Consequence: the blue sky ringing the sun disc was NOT sky-like → the cutout detector's `colorEdge` term (3x3 spread > 0.45 — the sun's white-vs-blue rim is a huge spread) fired on the sun's EDGE pixels during ANY motion (camMoving gate) → cutoutReactive=1.0 → the SDK rendered the sun disc SPATIALLY → jagged edges exactly where GL accumulates and reads smooth. Fix (shader-only): ported the GL classifier into the VK shader — `isSkyPx = isFarPlane` (real-depth mode, GL uUseDepth parity) or, in noRealDepth mode, `(isWhiteSkyPx || isBlueSkyPx) && uv.y < 0.5` — **Y GUARD FLIPPED vs GL**: the VK render uses the negative-viewport-height trick (NDC +1 = TOP row, uv.y=0 = top), so the sky half of the screen is `uv.y < 0.5`, not `> 0.5`. `isSkyLike` now includes isSkyPx. Also the CAMERA-LOCKED rule gate changed from `!noRealDepth && isFarPlane` (dead in noRealDepth mode — the flat gradient was never locked on the VK path) to `isSkyPx` (GL parity; safe because in noRealDepth mode isSkyPx comes from the strict color classifier + top-half guard, NOT the old "every pixel looks far-plane" smear). Effects: the sun rim's blue-sky pixels are sky-like → excluded from cutout colorEdge → they accumulate (gentle 32px sky MV ramp, zeroed motionFloor) → sun disc edges smooth like GL; flat sky gradient camera-locked under motion (no slant). Validated: glslangValidator clean, SPIR-V 11,548 → 12,372 bytes + header regenerated (BYTE count, exact format), fsrvk.dll 148,480 → 148,992, `gradlew build` green, deployed 17:55 to gradle-client resources + 26.1 instance (sha `4bf6f6e6`, backup `.bak-20260818-sunclassifier`). Note: FG-mode generated frames still interpolate across the sun edge without depth discontinuities (inherent FG limitation, the mod's own warning) — but the REAL frames (half the display) are now smooth, which is the GL parity the user asked for.
- **VK sun rim STILL jagged after the sky classifier — cutout gate was missing the `mvLen > 0.5` term (2026-08-18, "it is still not smooth" fix)**: the isSkyPx classifier port fixed the blue-sky colorEdge exclusion, but the sun rim stayed jagged. Root cause: the VK `fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp` cutout gate was `camMoving && (alphaEdge || ...)` while the GL MV_GEN_CS gate is `uCamMotion > 0.0 && mvLen > 0.5 && (alphaEdge || ...)` — the VK port DROPPED the `mvLen > 0.5` requirement. Without it, ANY camera motion (camMoving = speed > 2.5 blocks/sec OR rotDelta > 0.01) fired alphaEdge on the sun's hard alpha-cutout rim EVEN WHEN THE SUN BARELY MOVED ON SCREEN. Looking at the sun puts it near screen CENTER — a camera rotation moves it only ~0.5px there (it sits near the rotation pivot), so GL's mvLen gate CLOSES the cutout on the sun rim → the sun accumulates → smooth. The VK gate fired on camera speed alone → the sun rim went fully reactive every frame → spatial render → jagged sun edges exactly where GL reads smooth. Also covers walking-with-sun-on-screen: the sun is at infinity, so its per-pixel MV ≈ 0 under translation (no parallax) — GL keeps it accumulating, VK was forcing it spatial. Fix: added `mvLen > 0.5` to the VK cutout gate (mvLen is the FINAL MV after the camera-locked + still-camera gates, matching GL's ordering where mvLen is computed after its still gate). Validated: glslangValidator clean, SPIR-V 12,372 → 12,428 bytes + header regenerated (BYTE count), fsrvk.dll 148,992 bytes (12,428 embedded, 12,372 gone — byte-count gotcha), gradlew build green, deployed sha `fb65edc2` to gradle-client resources + 26.1 instance (backup `.bak-20260818-mvlen`).
- **FSR-OFF STATE CLEANUP + FSR4 FLAP FIX (2026-08-23, "black corner + voxel edges when FSR off" + "still blinks after ~10s" fix)**: two compounding bugs after the WindowMixin zoom fix. (1) The `!config.enabled` early-return in `onRenderBegin` ran BEFORE the block clearing `photonicsProxyActive`/`irisSwapActiveStatic`/`windowScaleActive` (~2731), so toggling FSR off with the Iris+Photonics swap live left the Photonics mixin redirect STALE: it kept returning proxy dims (1092x683) while Iris rendered the pack native (1680x1050) → Photonics sized its GI buffers for the proxy, the pack sampled a wrong-size GI texture = black corner + blocky voxel edges in the BASE game. The disabled path now clears ALL swap state every disabled frame (`irisSwapActive`, `irisSwapActiveStatic`, `photonicsProxyActive`, `photonicsBuffersAligned`, `windowScaleActive`). (2) The blink ~10s after start: the FSR4 saturation EMA froze while suspended (only calm ACTIVE frames updated it), so Photonics-era FSR4 frames always tripped the pack-tightened 1.5x/12ms pre-check; and the resume gate used the relaxed 2.0x/20ms while the active path suspended at 1.5x/12ms under iris — a 1.6x frame resumed then instantly re-suspended (log: `restored 15:43:06` → `suspendedFor=7ms` repeated every 1-3s). Fix: (1) the suspended-frame calm block now folds live worldMs into `fsr4EmaWorldMs` at 0.25 alpha (a 1-3s storm barely moves it, a sustained new baseline settles in seconds) — same live-baseline principle as the FSR2/3 cam-motion floor; (2) resume threshold = `max(0.8 * spikeMult * ema, spikeMin)` using the SAME `1.5x/12ms` (iris) / `2.0x/20ms` values as the pre-check — the 0.8 hysteresis margin makes a resume durable; (3) `suspendAfter` is 3 on BOTH paths now (was 2 under iris — Photonics' routine 1-2 frame GI bursts at ~1.5x are normal frame-rate, only a sustained storm should suspend). **v2/v3 (16:08, "blinking less often but still there")**: the 15:57 log proved the resume requirement was frame-counted — 10 frames at 60fps = 0.17s — so a momentary queue drain resumed straight into the next GI burst (log: `restored 16:00:50` → `suspendedFor=178ms`, clock resets 3181→2732→2482 every ~3s). Fixes: (1) NEW `FSR4_WORLD_CALM_MS = 2500L`: BOTH resume gates (fence-drain AND world-calm) require the calm streak to persist 2.5s REAL time (`fsr4WorldCalmStartMs` field) — a frame count alone resumes into the next burst, duration is the gate; (2) suspended-frame EMA tracking is now UPWARD-ONLY (`else if (calmWorldMs > fsr4EmaWorldMs)`) — the old 0.25-alpha bidirectional fold dragged the baseline down to the FSR1-cheap suspended cost, making every resumed FSR4-active frame read as a 1.5x spike; only heavier frames raise the baseline while suspended, downward tracking happens on ACTIVE calm frames; (3) POST-RESUME BASELINE ABSORB: during the 1.5s grace the pre-check folds the measured ACTIVE worldMs into the EMA (`fsr4EmaWorldMs = max(ema, worldMs*0.85)`) so the spike line reflects the true resumed cost (threshold ≈1.3x of observed active) and only real deviations can re-suspend.
- **IRIS JITTER-BLIND PERIODIC RE-PROBE REMOVED (2026-08-23, "blinking after a few minutes, only fixed by toggling off/on" — the 1.21.11 port-specific root cause)**: the Iris jitter probe classifies the shader pack as "jitter-blind" (the composite ignores vanilla projection jitter) via a one-shot multi-frame evaluation at startup. The old code ("IRIS JITTER-BLIND SELF-HEALING") periodically re-probed every 60 seconds while blind: cleared `irisJitterBlind`, re-armed `irisJitterProbeDone`, and let the probe re-evaluate. On a pack whose composite movement oscillated around the detection threshold, the re-probe verdict flipped (blind→not-blind→blind...) — each flip toggled jitter between zeroed and active, producing a visible quality blink at the 60-second re-probe interval. The periodic re-probe is removed: the one-shot probe (fires on fresh install or reinit via `irisJitterProbeDone=false` at dimension-change and `markDirty`) is sufficient; the blind verdict stays sticky for the session. `IRIS_JITTER_BLIND_REPROBE_MS` and `irisJitterBlindAtMs` removed; the blind log now says "sticky for session". Confirmed in the 1.21.11 instance: `debugMode=0` session (07:44) showed FSR4 init OK → fell back to FSR1 1s later (saturation), no TIMING/DIAG data; the Iris re-probe was the only 1.21.11-specific code path capable of producing periodic blinks.
- **FSR4 stuck on FSR1 after saturation — WORLD-CALM RESUME + post-resume grace (2026-08-23, "blinking after a few minutes, only fixed by toggling off/on" fix)**: the 26.1 session log (19:35:54 "FSR4 fell back to FSR1 (GPU saturated)" then nothing until quit 19:37:37) pinned the exact user symptom: after a few minutes of play the image degrades (FSR1 bilinear quality = the reported "blinking") and only a manual off/on (forced reinit) restores it. The 2026-08-18 sustained-calm gate fixed the FLAP (rapid resume→re-suspend) by requiring DENSE probe signals, but on an always-busy iGPU the probe fence may NEVER signal on consecutive frames — that is the always-saturated case where FSR4 stays parked on FSR1 for the WHOLE session instead of flapping. Fix (Java only, FSRProcessor, resume-probe block): while `fsr4Suspended`, ALSO count CPU-side calm world frames (`calmWorldMs < max(SAT_SPIKE_MULT * fsr4EmaWorldMs, SAT_SPIKE_MIN_MS)` — the same spike the pre-check uses for the reverse direction, measured from `t0BeginEnd`), and resume after `FSR4_WORLD_CALM_FRAMES=10` consecutive calm frames (~2s), gated by the existing min-parked (`FSR4_SUSPEND_MIN_MS=1500`) and dimension-hold (`FSR4_DIM_CHANGE_HOLD_MS=10000`) windows — the calm path therefore cannot re-arm into a real storm or the End-entry rebuild. FSR1 fallback frames are cheap, so this adds no stall; a genuinely stormy queue produces worldMs spikes that reset the streak. The dense-signal path is unchanged and still primary; the calm path is an addition for machines whose fence never signals. Also added `FSR4_RESUME_GRACE_MS=1500`: after ANY resume (fence or calm), the pre-check still SKIPS cross-API work on a spike frame (the atio6axx crash protection holds) but does NOT count the frame toward suspension (`inResumeGrace` wraps the `++fsr4PreCheckSkips`), so a single post-resume spike cannot instantly re-suspend — the flap that would make the calm-resume useless. `fsr4WorldCalmStreak` reset at all 3 suspension sites + the dimension-change hold + both resume paths; `fsr4ResumeAtMs` cleared on dimension change (each suspension gets a fresh grace). Validated: `compileClientJava` + `gradlew build` green (jar `build/libs/fsr-reloaded-2.0.1.jar` 09:06, NOT deployed yet). **Expected next run**: after a saturation suspension, FSR4 auto-restores within ~2s of the world render calming even if the probe fence never signals (log `FSR4: world render calm for 10 frames while suspended — resuming FSR4`), and a post-restore spike does NOT re-suspend within the 1.5s grace.
- **FSR4 FLAPPING UNDER A HEAVY SHADER PACK — pack-activation hold + baseline reseed (2026-08-23, "the blinking happens every second [with Photonics]" fix)**: the 12:30 session (Photonics enabled at 12:32:03) shows the suspend→resume flap in raw cadence: fallback 12:32:04 → restore 12:32:17 → (hidden fallback) → restore 12:32:29 → restore 12:32:52 — ~12s cycles, each with a temporal-history reset, read as "blinking every second". Root cause: the FSR4 saturation EMA was seeded during pack-OFF frames (few ms); activating a ~2x-cost pack makes EVERY pack-loading frame exceed the iris-tightened guard (1.5x/12ms, suspend after 2), so FSR4 can never hold — it suspends, the queue's calm-in-between reseeds nothing (the EMA only updates on calm FSR4-dispatch frames — which never run while suspended!), resumes, instantly re-suspends. ALSO: the pack's own pipeline rebuilds ("Destroying pipeline" 12:32:00 + 12:32:25, each with Full pipeline recreate) stomp the queue. Fix in FSRProcessor.onRenderBegin (after the frame-lock captures `irisActiveThisFrame`): on the pack flip to `active` (and FSR4/FG selected), force `fsr4Suspended=true` + `fsr4DimChangeAtMs=now` (REUSES the dimension-change hold — the resume probe's `dimHoldElapsed` gate now keeps FSR1 up for 10s through the pack's pipeline rebuild; the init deferral block's dim-hold branch also defers the re-init the same way, so the 840x525→1428x893 recreate lands after the storm, not into it) and RESEED `fsr4EmaWorldMs=0` (the next calm dispatch frame seeds from the PACK's real frame time — the guard's threshold becomes the pack's own load profile, so sustained game time stays within it and the flap stops after the first settle). `fsr4PackActivateHoldLogged` one-shot debug log. Also added a 3s-throttled `[FSR4-PATH] still on FSR1 fallback: suspended=.. hardDisabled=.. suspendedFor=..ms pack=.. render=..x..` INFO log inside the suspended probe block so the true flap cadence is measurable WITHOUT debug mode (chat notifications are cooldown-gated at 10s and hide it). Note: the same EMA-starvation-while-suspended issue applies to the regular saturation flap (non-pack) — the world-calm resume + grace handles the resume side; the reseed is what prevents the re-suspend.
- **FSR4 FALSE-SATURATION FLAP — adaptive GL-fence cap (2026-08-23, "I don't think it's GPU saturation, framerate is stable, no Voxy" — the user was RIGHT)**: the async FSR4 fence used a FIXED 32ms cap (`fenceTimeoutNs`). The fence measures THIS frame's GL copies + MV-gen, which complete at the render's own cadence: on this iGPU with the Iris pack at 840x525 a smooth ~30fps = ~33ms/frame, so EVERY frame's fence exceeded 32ms → `fsr4ConsecutiveSkips` hit 6 within ~0.2s → suspend → the probe re-armed immediately (queue drains milliseconds later) → suspend again… the 12:38 session's log shows it directly: FSR4 restored 12:39:06, then `[FSR4-PATH] still on FSR1 fallback: suspendedFor=11ms` / `=864ms` / `=7ms` / `=1019ms` every 3s — fresh suspensions every 1-3s at a perfectly stable framerate, no Voxy, no load storm. The fixed cap could not distinguish "slow but healthy" from "crashed". Fix: the cap follows the measured inter-frame EMA (`autoEmaFrameMs`, already updated every frame in onRenderBegin): async `max(32ms, 1.5×ema)`, sync `max(8ms, 1.5×ema)`. A smooth 33ms render gets a ~50ms cap → the fence signals before timeout → no skip, no suspend; only a genuine spike (frame time jumping above the EMA — chunk-gen storms) still exceeds the cap → quiet skip → 6-skip suspension → the world-calm/adaptive resume, which is the intended storm behavior. NOTE the fence is a CPU WAIT that returns when signaled — raising the cap costs nothing on healthy frames (they signal in 1-33ms anyway); it only reclassifies a steady slow frame as healthy. The 12:36 pack-activation hold (10s FSR1 at pack enable) and the rapid-storm backoff from the same session are kept; the flap cadence log (`[FSR4-PATH] still on FSR1 fallback` every 3s) stays as the diagnostic.
- **FSR4 corner-crop fix (2026-08-23, "it is zoomed into a corner" — the world fills the whole screen showing only a magnified corner region, UI unaffected)**: two parts. (1) **FAST RE-INIT AFTER DELIBERATE SCALE CHANGE**: `applyAutoScale()/markDirty()` set `fsr4Initialized=false`, which previously routed the re-init through the FIRST-INIT load-storm deferral (frame-EMA calm gate + 25s grace). A scale change therefore parked FSR4 on FSR1 for up to 25s while the scene rendered at the NEW size — the 1.21.11 session log shows the iris-quality slider change at 12:10:07 with the re-init landing only at 12:10:31 (user quit ~1s later). The deferral now applies ONLY to a genuine first init (`fsr4ContextRenderW == 0`); mid-session size changes re-init after the 500ms debounce (`FSR4_DEBOUNCE_MS`) instead of the 25s grace. (2) **HARD SIZE-ALIGNMENT GUARD on the GL FSR4 dispatch**: never run the dispatch while `fsr4ContextRenderW/H/DisplayW/H` differ from `scaledWidth/Height/displayWidth/Height` — `glCopyImageSubData` clips to the smaller of the two sizes and the SDK upsamples with the context-cached render size (the stale-UV corner zoom, same class as the 2026-08-08 dynamic-res corner-crop that was reverted). On a mismatch frame: set `fsr4Suspended=true` (the FSR1 spatial fallback renders fresh — no crop possible), log `[FSR4-PATH] context ... != scene ... — FSR4 dispatch skipped` once per episode (`fsr4SizeMismatchLogged`), and let the `needsRecreate` path re-align within the debounce. The dispatch condition ALSO refuses mismatched sizes inline (belt-and-braces — even a same-frame probe resume cannot dispatch a stale pool). New field `fsr4SizeMismatchLogged` (one-shot). Validated: `compileClientJava` + `gradlew build` green (jar `build/libs/fsr-reloaded-2.0.1.jar` 12:28, NOT deployed yet). **Expected next run (1.21.11 + Iris)**: moving the iris-quality slider no longer produces a 20-25s FSR1-only window (re-init ≤ 500ms debounce), and even a transient reset window can never present a corner-cropped image (FSR1 renders during any mismatch). If the zoom still appears after this deploy, the next suspects are the Iris-scaled proxy's size vs the pack's final-pass viewport (a pack-side 1092x683 viewport on a 1428x883 buffer renders the world in the top-left corner region) — the `[FSR4-PATH] ... != scene` log line will say which side drifted.
- **GL-path <1 FPS = FSR4 suspension/recovery FLAPPING — sustained-calm resume gate (2026-08-18, "it is getting less then 1fps in gl" fix)**: the 26.1 GL session's game log showed rock-solid **~2000ms stalls every other frame** (frame totals 1975/2004/1999/2109/2001/2091/2065/2068ms alternating with 8-12ms frames) for the whole ~70s new-world/Voxy storm — the exact signature of `fsr4_context.cpp:308 waitForFenceValue(..., 2000)` (the D3D12 allocator fence timeout) being hit by the async drain's `Fsr4Native.waitIdle()`. Root cause: the suspended-state resume probe re-armed FSR4 after just **2 clean probe signals**, which a storm's 8ms lull frames satisfy — FSR4 re-dispatched into the saturated shared-iGPU queue, its D3D12 work couldn't run (GL queue full), the next frame's waitIdle ate the full 2000ms timeout, suspended, and the 2-signal resume re-armed again at the next lull → the flap cycle. Even a saturation-skip frame showed worldMs=4103 (renderLevel 4.1s) — driver command-submission throttling spilling back into the GL queue from the still-pending D3D12 work of the prior frame. Fix (Java only, FSRProcessor): the resume gate now requires (a) a minimum parked time (`FSR4_SUSPEND_MIN_MS=1500`, `fsr4SuspendedAtMs` set at ALL 3 suspension sites: pre-check FSR4_PRE_SUSPEND_AFTER, FSR4_SKIP_SUSPEND_AFTER fence timeouts, dispatch-failure) and (b) **dense signals** — the clean streak RESTARTS on any signal whose gap from `fsr4FirstCleanProbeMs` exceeds `FSR4_CALM_SPAN_MS=1000`, so the streak can only reach 2 when the queue signals on consecutive frames. Storm lulls (separated by multi-second stalls) restart the streak every signal → FSR4 stays parked on FSR1 (fresh, smooth frames) through the whole storm; a genuinely drained queue signals every frame → resume ~2 frames after the storm ends (~0.5s for a borderline-busy machine whose probes come from the 16th-frame bounded wait). The 0ns-poll-neutral semantics (2026-08-05 v3) are PRESERVED — failures still never reset the streak directly (the deferred-fence reasoning still holds); only the dense-signal restart + min-parked time gates were added, so the "FSR4 never resumes on an always-busy iGPU" regression cannot return. Validated: `gradlew build` green, deployed to gradle-client resources + 26.1 instance (sha e99e66ce, backup .bak-20260818-sustainedcalm). **Next run must show**: during a new-world/Voxy storm, FSR4 suspends once (chat "fell back to FSR1") and STAYS parked (no repeated "restored"/"fell back" flapping, no ~2s stalls — frames stay ~8-30ms on FSR1), then "restored" fires exactly once when the storm genuinely ends.
- **World-join "repeats the last 2 frames" = FSR4 first-init force-running MID-STORM — grace extended 6s → 25s (2026-08-18, "it cant even get into the actual game anymore. it just repeats the last 2 fames" fix)**: with the sustained-calm resume gate (previous entry) eliminating the mid-session 2s flapping, the two follow-up sessions ran FSR4 at 8-36ms in-world — but the user still hit the world-join window: ~15-20s of ~1s frames (new world + Voxy meshing + C2ME on the iGPU) where the loading screen visibly freezes, "repeating the last 2 frames". The mod's contribution to that window was the FIRST-INIT DEFERRAL's 6s grace: the init force-ran mid-storm, and the init path's `GL43C.glFinish()` (the documented pre-import queue drain for the crash-prone external-memory import) + `allocateFsr4Pool` + `initFsr4` blocked the render thread ~1s (session log: `onRenderBegin (total) took 961ms` at frame #480 = the init frame, on top of a 256ms world render + 206ms present → 1424ms frames). Fix (Java only): `FSR4_INIT_GRACE_MS` 6000 → 25000 — the init now waits for the load storm to genuinely drain (the frame EMA drops below FSR4_INIT_CALM_MS within ~20 frames of the storm ending), covering this machine's 15-20s storms while always-busy machines still engage within the bound; ALSO reduces the documented "crashes only when LOADING with FSR4 on" driver exposure (init runs on a calm queue). Validated: `gradlew build` green, deployed to gradle-client resources + 26.1 instance (sha e4bab343, backup .bak-20260818-initgrace). **Expected next run**: world join stays on FSR1 bilinear through the storm (loading screen advances instead of freezing), FSR4 "init OK" fires ~1-2s after the storm ends, then smooth FSR4 as before. If the user reports FSR4 "never turns on" on a machine that never calms, the 25s bound is the knob.
- **Frozen/flickering display with Voxy = cross-API shared copies silently failing — BLACK-INPUT SANITY CHECK fallback (2026-08-18, "it just showed the loading terrain flickering. the game was probably running fine in the background" fix)**: the user's display stayed stuck on the loading-terrain screen (flickering between stale swapchain buffers, "repeats the last 2 frames") while the game loop ran fine behind it — and only WITH Voxy (no-Voxy session: SHARE_VERIFY colorC=(37,58,39) real; with-Voxy: (0,0,0) black; Aug 13 with Voxy: (23,37,26) correct). Root cause: Voxy's AsyncNodeManager issues GL work from its OWN thread on the same context; when it collides with the mod's `glCopyImageSubData` into the D3D12-imported shared textures, the copies silently fail → FSR4's shared input stays black/empty (SHARE_VERIFY depthC=0.0 + colorC=0,0,0; STAGING_VERIFY depth=0.0) → the SDK's output never updates → the presented frame freezes on the last pre-FSR4 content (the loading screen). The race is TIMING-dependent, not a code regression — the Aug 15 backup's FSR4 GL block is bytecode-identical in copy structure (22 glCopyImageSubData both) and the no-Voxy same-build session was clean. Fix (Java only, FSRProcessor): a throttled black-input sanity check — every 90 frames, AFTER the GL fence confirms the scene-color copy executed, read the shared color's center pixel via the managed verify-copy mechanism (direct glReadPixels of external-memory textures lies on this driver) and compare against the scene's center; 3 consecutive black-while-nonzero (sceneLuma>0.03, sharedLuma<0.004) reads hard-disable FSR4 for the session (`fsr4HardDisabled`, FSR1 spatial, chat "FSR4 disabled for this session (shared input black — likely a Voxy GL conflict)") — the display stays LIVE instead of frozen. The detection frame keeps the last presented output (stale but not black) rather than dispatching into black input. Validated: `gradlew build` green, deployed to gradle-client resources + 26.1 instance (sha d743d6da, backup .bak-20260818-blackinput). **Expected next run (with Voxy)**: either FSR4 works (the race didn't hit — display live, edges smooth), or within ~4.5s of a black input FSR4 falls to FSR1 with the chat notice and the world displays normally (bilinear) instead of flickering. The deeper fix (serializing the mod's copies against Voxy's async GL thread) is a follow-up; the fallback guarantees usability either way.
- **In-world FSR toggle crash — TWO distinct instance-specific bugs, both fixed 2026-08-18 ("crashes when I open a world with FSR reloaded off and then turn it on again")**: the user's report covered both instances. **(1) VULKANMOD instance (`Vulkan Optimized`) — `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` from `markDirty()` leaving `fsr4VkModActive=true`**: crash report 19:43:15 was `VK_ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` at `Renderer.submitFrame`; session log pinned it: FSR4 init OK at world open → toggle OFF (19:42:50) → toggle ON (19:42:54, re-init OK) → **version switched to FSR1** (19:43:02) → device lost 12s later. `markDirty()` (F10 key + settings version pills) tears down the FSR4 SDK context + frees the D3D12 pool (the memory the imported VkImages ALIAS) but — uniquely among every teardown path (disable path, needsRecreate, `applyAutoScale()`, world-unload, `destroy()` all clear it) — did NOT set `VulkanModCompat.fsr4VkModActive = false`. With the flag stale-true, the `VulkanModMainPassMixin` end() hook kept recording Copy1/MV-gen/Copy2 into imported VkImages aliasing FREED D3D12 memory → GPU writes to freed memory → device lost (deferred, hence the ~12s gap). Fix: `markDirty()` now clears the flag in its `if (fsr4Initialized)` teardown; the end() hook falls back to the safe bilinear blit until re-init re-arms it. **(2) GL instance (26.1) — silent GPU TDR from the FSR4 cross-API init running mid-storm**: session 2026-08-18-6 (the exact user repro: world open with FSR off → F10 cycle → FSR enabled at 20:02:35) died SILENTLY ~11s later (no crash report, no hs_err, no `Stopping!` — last log line is the server's `Can't keep up! 5949ms`; the render thread's [TIMING] lines stop mid-stream = TDR-class render hang). The `onRenderBegin` frame at 20:02:36 was 1426ms with the FSR1-fallback dispatch right after and NO `FSR4: init OK` — the cross-API first-init ran (the Vulkan-allocation→GL external-memory import `sharedTexMgr.create()` = the 1064ms onRenderBegin) while Voxy's AsyncNodeManager + C2ME were saturating the shared iGPU from other threads. The SDK-init block below it defers the first init until the frame EMA is calm (`isFsr4InitCalm()` ≤26ms + 25s grace), but the `sharedTexMgr`/Vulkan-interop block ran UNCONDITIONALLY on enable — no calm gate, no queue drain — so a mid-storm toggle imported external-memory handles into GL on a saturated queue (the documented AMD 860M cross-API fault class). Fix (Java only): the interop block now shares the SDK init's first-init deferral (same `isFsr4InitCalm()` gate + shared `fsr4InitDeferStartMs` grace; both creations wait together / proceed together; the SDK block owns the timer reset) and adds `GL43C.glFinish()` before the import (same documented drain the SDK init already has). Resize recreates (FSR4 already running) keep the immediate behavior. Verified the `markDirty()` GL relevance is nil (fsr4VkModActive only gates the VulkanMod end() hook — the GL crash needed this separate fix). NOTE: the 26.1 instance's OTHER recurring crash — `Exception stopping the server: RejectedExecutionException ... SavedDataStorage.scheduleSave` on quit — is a PRE-EXISTING machine/modpack shutdown race (logs show it since 2026-08-02, before today's changes; correlates with 5900-chunk C2ME/Voxy saves, not with the FSR toggle — sessions -2/-5 quit clean, 4 of 5 FSR4 sessions crashed at save, but -1/-3/-4/-7 all ran FSR4 fine for 10s-7min first). Not addressed here; out of FSR's control. Validated: `compileClientJava` + `gradlew build` green (jar `build/libs/fsr-reloaded-1.9.0.jar` 20:16, not yet deployed). Expected next run (GL): toggling FSR4 on in-world during a Voxy/C2ME storm stays on the FSR1 fallback until the queue calms (or 25s grace), then inits — no silent death; the toggle on a calm machine inits on the next frame as before.
- **IRIS GENERIC TEMPORAL MODE — FSR2/3 under ANY shader pack, zero per-pack config, with a jitter AUTO-PROBE (2026-08-18, "see how the super resolution mod works with shaders while also having fsr 2+ ... implement everything else but make sure it is not a 1:1 of superresolution")**: Super Resolution ships two tiers — per-pack JSON compat configs (SHADER_COMPAT: named composite-pass triggers, colortex texture bindings, JitterSourceConfig) and a pack-agnostic HACK tier (window-scale the whole pipeline). FSR Reloaded now gets the generic tier with TWO differentiators: **(a) zero-config by construction** — Iris sizes its internal render targets (gbuffers, colortex, composite) from `Minecraft.getMainRenderTarget().width/height` (`IrisRenderingPipeline.resizeIfNeeded`, decompiled 2026-08-18) and draws the final composite into mainRT's color texture (`FinalPassRenderer.renderFinalPass` binds `mainRT.getColorTexture()`), so the mod's EXISTING scaled mainRT swap (`ScaledRenderTarget` proxy + "fsr-scene" TextureTarget) scales the pack's ENTIRE pipeline and lands the composite in `renderTarget` — the FSR2/3 GLSL pipelines upscale it at the vanilla boundary (`onSceneRendered`, renderLevel TAIL) with zero pack cooperation; **(b) the jitter auto-probe** — Super Resolution trusts per-pack JSON that the pack's jitter matches FSR2's `jitterOffset`; we MEASURE it: after Iris-temporal engagement, when `fsr23CamStillExact` (camera provably still), the probe reads a 32×32 RGBA8 block of the composite (highest-variance of 5 candidate locations), re-reads it on a later still frame whose Halton jitter delta ≥ 0.25 render px, and searches the best integer shift in ±2 by MAD. Shift ≥ 1px with MAD < 0.8×zero-shift ⇒ pack consumes the vanilla projection jitter (full temporal contract); 6 clean static pairs with no shift ⇒ JITTER-BLIND — `onRenderBegin` zeroes the jitter for spatial accumulation (no ghosting from a jitter that never happened). Cap 60 attempts; inconclusive ⇒ assume works (captureAndJitter provably fires — Iris does not cancel vanilla renderLevel's `ProjectionMatrixBuffer.getBuffer(Matrix4f)`). **Wiring**: `onRenderBegin` — the legacy Iris early-return (no swap, FSR1/RCAS only) is now gated `irisActive && !irisTemporal` where `irisTemporal = pack in use && config.enabled && !DistantHorizonsCompat.isLoaded() && (isFsr2() || (isFsr3() && !isFrameGen())) && irisQualityScale < 1.0 && in-game`; FSR1/FSR4/FSR3_FG keep the legacy path. When temporal, the code falls through to the shared non-Iris block (scaled renderTarget + proxy swap + viewport + the full motion-adaptive jitter/camStill/camReactive machinery — unchanged). `onSceneRendered` — the legacy Iris FSR1/RCAS block runs only when `!irisSwapActive`; with the swap, the shared GL section dispatches FSR2/3 exactly like the non-Iris path (constant-depth MVs; `probeFsr4Depth` gates `uUseDepth` — the pack's own depth buffers don't land in the proxy depth, but if they did the probe would enable real per-pixel depth for free). `onRenderEnd` — the Iris cleanup early-return is gated `!irisSwapActive` so the sceneUpscaled cleanup path runs. Depth texture: `renderTarget` has one (TextureTarget depth=true); if Iris's depth copy-back writes it, `probeFsr4Depth` will detect real values and FSR2/3 get per-pixel depth MVs — the probe decides, no config. **Next run must show**: with a pack + FSR2/3 selected, `[IRIS-TEMPORAL] shader pack + FSR2 selected — scaled mainRT swap active` once, then `[IRIS-JITTER] probe: pack CONSUMES vanilla jitter` (or JITTER-BLIND → jitter zeroed) within ~0.5s, then the standard `[DIAG_FSR2]`/`[ACCUM_WEIGHT]` lines at scaled render res (pack gbuffers + composite at irisQualityScale). Pack gbuffers are scaled (perf win), composite upscaled by FSR2/3, GUI native. Known limits: FSR3_FG excluded (alternate-frame machinery not wired for the swap); DH excluded (own framebuffers bypass the proxy); pack-internal TAA/upscale passes (packs that hard-code internal resolution) can look wrong — the per-pack JSON compat tier remains a follow-up, but generic mode works with every pack that renders through the vanilla boundary (Iris's final pass is mandatory). **PHOTONICS - ColorFramebuffer redirect via Mixin (2026-08-23, "zoomed into a corner + blinking only when Photonics is enabled" fix, v2)**: Photonics (mod id `photonics`, Iris raytracing extension) sizes its voxel-raytrace `ColorFramebuffer`s from the WINDOW - the `resolutionSupplier` is `Window.getWidth/Height` (decompiled `ColorFramebuffer.lambda$new$0`), so at `irisQualityScale` 0.5 the raytracing buffers are 1680x1050 while the proxy is 840x525 - the 2x mismatch is the "zoomed into a corner" crop. Instead of excluding Photonics from the swap entirely, a new `PhotonicsColorFramebufferMixin` (`@Mixin(targets = "at.redi2go.photonics.client.rendering.opengl.rendering.ColorFramebuffer")`, registered in `fsr2mod.mixins.json`) `@Redirect`s the `Supplier.get()` call inside `updatePerFrame()` - when `FSRProcessor.photonicsProxyActive` is true (set alongside `irisSwapActive`), the supplier returns proxy dimensions (840x525) instead of window size. Photonics then renders its GI at proxy resolution, Iris composites at the same size, and FSR4 upscales - no crop, no mismatch, no blinking. The GI quality is at proxy resolution (the same tradeoff every scaled-pack path makes). `irisTemporal` gate no longer excludes Photonics (only DH remains excluded). `PhotonicsCompat.logStatus()` fires once per session. Tradeoff: Photonics' GI is at `irisQualityScale` resolution (0.5 = 840x525 at 50% scale); the user can raise `irisQualityScale` to recover GI detail. Deployed 13:07 (jar 47,439,342 B, backup `.bak-20260823-1307`). **v2 (13:31 / Shadow): `@Shadow` field write on the private final `resolutionSupplier` → `IllegalAccessError` at runtime (Java 21 enforces final: "Update to non-static final field … attempted from a different method than <init>"). v3 (13:43 / Redirect): `@Redirect` on `Supplier.get()` with the correct descriptor format `"Ljava/util/function/Supplier;get()Ljava/lang/Object;"` (NO colon after the method name — the v1 colon was the parse error) — handler receives the Supplier as a parameter and either returns proxy dimensions or calls `supplier.get()` for native size. No field writes, no descriptor errors. Deployed 13:43 (jar 47,439,821 B, backup `.bak-20260823-1343`). **v4 (13:55 / probe + self-heal)**: the 13:40 session ran v3 cleanly (no transform error, clean logout) but the zoom persisted — including on the Experimental-FSR1-under-Iris path (FSR1 dispatch under the swap was never validated; it is size-aware with full state hygiene, so the remaining suspect is the composite itself). Added: (1) `updatePerFrame` TAIL probe logging `[PHOTONICS-SIZE] proxyActive=... want=... real=... buf=... aligned=...` (first 3 frames then 5s throttle) — PROVES whether Photonics actually allocates at the proxy size; (2) self-healing verdict `FSRProcessor.photonicsBuffersAligned` (default true) set by the probe each frame — when proxy-active but Photonics allocated at window size, the onRenderBegin `irisTemporal` gate adds `!photonicsBlock` (`PhotonicsCompat.isLoaded() && !photonicsBuffersAligned`) and drops to native pass-through (static image beats the corner crop); (3) `PhotonicsCompat.logStatus()` one-shot gating (was spamming every frame — 13:40 log lines 197-245+). Deployed 13:55 (jar 47,440,567 B, backup `.bak-20260823-1355`). Deployed 13:30 (jar 47,439,679 B, backup `.bak-20260823-1331`). **v5 (14:05 / IRIS-COMPOSITE probe + self-heal)**: the 13:52 session (with the probe) was DECISIVE — `[PHOTONICS-SIZE] proxyActive=true want=1092x683 real=1092x683 buf=764x478 aligned=true`: Photonics' buffers are now CORRECTLY aligned at the proxy (764x478 is Photonics' OWN internal 0.7x GI scale applied after the supplier — harmless, its inner denoiser res), so the redirect WORKS and the zoom is NOT Photonics' buffer size. Yet the crop persists both with Photonics and with Experimental-FSR1-under-Iris — and the pre-Photonics sessions (08-22-6, 08-23-1..4 logs) show the SAME pipeline-then-swap ordering with NO zoom. So the wrong-size renderer is the pack COMPOSITE chain itself (Iris built its pipeline at native 1680x1050 at world-join BEFORE the swap engaged at 13:52:01; a native-size final-pass viewport on a 1092x683 target = top-left corner crop — the AGENTS.md 2026-08-23 "pack-side 1092x683 viewport on a 1428x883 buffer" suspect). v5 adds (FSRProcessor.onSceneRendered, before the display-viewport override): (1) **[IRIS-COMPOSITE] probe** — GL_VIEWPORT + the composite texture's real size (`glGetTexLevelParameteri` on the bound target, GL 3.3-safe — the DSA `glGetTextureLevelParameteriv` is GL45-only and does not compile on GL43C) vs the proxy; (2) **self-heal** — persistent mismatch (2s) triggers `forceIrisPipelineRebuild()` (reflection into `Iris.getPipelineManager().destroyPipeline()`, the reload-keybind path — Iris rebuilds at mainRT dims = proxy dims next frame, so gbuffers/colortex/composite re-allocate scaled; guarded no-op if absent), max 2 rebuilds; (3) **latch** — if the mismatch outlives 10s, `irisSwapForceDisabled` latches and the swap gates (`irisTemporal` + `irisFsr1Experimental`) drop to native pass-through permanently for the session (probe's else-branch clears the streak; one-shot OK log `[IRIS-COMPOSITE] OK: viewport=...x... tex=...x... match proxy=...x...` when aligned). `glGetTextureLevelParameteriv` does not exist on LWJGL GL43C (DSA form is GL45C-only) — the probe uses the classic `glGetTexLevelParameteri` after binding; `GL43C.glGetInteger` used for the previous binding restore. - **ComplementaryReimagined r5.8.1 SR-schema compat JSON authored in-repo (2026-08-18, "you make the json")**: the user wanted the per-pack JSON ("necessary for optimal fps") for Complementary rather than SR's download. It lives at `docs/shadercompat/ComplementaryReimagined_r5.8.1.superresolution.json` (SR V3 schema, `schema_version: 3`, `profiles."*"` — every field key-named from the decompiled `SRCompatConfigV3Parser`/`IrisShaderCompatEventHandler`, NOT guessed: `type: "before"|"after"` lowercase + case-sensitive, `source: "CONST"|"VARIABLE"|"UNIFORM"` and `type: "FLOAT"|"INT"|"VECTOR2F"` via `equalsIgnoreCase` against enum names, input keys `color`/`depth`/`exposure`, output key `upscaled_color` with `target: [buffer]`, region sentinels `-1`=src size/`-2`=dst size from `TextureRegion.resolveValue`, default profile key `"*"`). Pipeline facts verified from the pack's own shaders: `colortex0` = R11F_G11F_B10F gbuffer main color, `composite5` reads colortex0 → writes `colortex3` (RGBA8, "final color" + bloom), `composite6` = the TAA pass (reads colortex3 + `depthtex1`, writes back colortex3 + temp to colortex2), `composite7` = FXAA → colortex3, `final` reads colortex3 and TONEMAPS into mainRT. So the trigger is `before final`, input `color=colortex3` + `depth=depthtex1`, output `upscaled_color → colortex3`, `internal_format: rgba16f` (final tonemaps the upscaled full-res image = the quality win), `hdr: true` + `pre_exposure` CONST 1.0 + `auto_exposure: false` (Complementary's exposure is manual TM_EXPOSURE, no exposure buffer). **Jitter is the trap**: the pack's TAA jitter is an 8-frame Chocapic pattern (`lib/antialiasing/jitter.glsl`, applied in EVERY gbuffer vertex shader via `gl_Position.xy = TAAJitter(gl_Position.xy, w)`, scaled ×0.125 at default TAA_JITTER=1 ≈ ±0.11 render px — tiny) and there is NO uniform exposing it, so `UNIFORM` is impossible and `CONST` can't express a sequence → `jitter.source: "MOD"` (the upscaler's own jitter), with the instruction to set the pack's **TAA to OFF** so the pack doesn't double-jitter (with TAA off, Iris gbuffer projection follows the vanilla projection jitter the mod applies — the same contract the generic Iris mode's auto-probe verifies). Deployed as `ComplementaryReimagined_r5.8.1_FSR.zip` (original + superresolution.json at zip root) in the 26.1 instance's shaderpacks. **Scope honesty**: this JSON is consumed by the Super Resolution mod's parser, not by FSR Reloaded's current Iris mode (which is the zero-config generic boundary tier — it ignores pack JSONs by design). Engaging the JSON's `before final` tier inside FSR Reloaded requires the Iris composite-pass hook (`CompositeRenderer.renderAll` iterates private `passes`, each `CompositeRenderer$Pass` has a package-private `name` + inline GL draw — no clean per-pass method to inject; SR achieves the hook by swapping Iris buffer bindings via its own origin/scaled render-target renaming, `origin_render_target`/`scaled_render_target` in ShaderCompatHandler) — a follow-up candidate, NOT done here.
- **GL-INSTANCE CRASH 0xC0000005 in atio6axx.dll under Iris (2026-08-19, "it just crashes with this error code -1073741819" fix)**: first-ever FSR1+Iris session on the 26.1 instance (Iris 1.11.3, ComplementaryReimagined_r5.8.1_FSR pack toggled on mid-world at 21:44:06-07 + Voxy re-created its Iris render pipeline at 21:44:24-26) died ~3s after `FSR1: Initializing pipeline (Iris path)` dispatched EASU on the composite — silent, no hs_err/crash report, exit 0xC0000005. WER Application Error (21:44:29) pinned it: **faulting module atio6axx.dll (AMD OpenGL driver, 32.0.31035.1003), exception c0000005** — a driver-level access violation, not a JVM crash (no hs_err) and not a TDR (no Event 4101). The new FSR2/3 IRIS GENERIC TEMPORAL MODE was NOT involved (config was `version=FSR1` → legacy no-swap path, unchanged by the 08-18 work); the FSR1 legacy branch had fired the UPSCALE sub-branch (`actualW < displayWidth` was true) — the tell that mainRT was mid-recreation/tear from the pack reload. Fixes: (1) **stale-target adoption** — the legacy Iris branch now compares `client.getMainRenderTarget()` against the `mainRenderTarget` cached in onRenderBegin and ADOPTS the current target (with a one-shot `[IRIS] main render target was recreated mid-frame` warn) when a pack reload replaced it; previously FSR1 sampled/copied the cached instance's FREED GL textures → driver fault. (2) **FSR1Pipeline GL-state hygiene** — drawEasu now saves/restores the viewport AND the TEXTURE0 binding; blitTex restores the viewport; dispatchRcas restores the active program + TEXTURE0 binding (all three previously left the caller's state modified — state soup mid-Iris-frame on a driver that faults on it). Deployed 08:39 (jar 47,370,552 B; backup `.bak-20260818-2123`); instance config switched to `version=FSR2, irisQualityScale=0.7` (backup `fsr-reloaded.json.bak-20260818`) so the next launch exercises the FSR2/3 Iris temporal mode — if it crashes again the fault is in the temporal pipelines' own dispatch, not the legacy FSR1 path. Complementary TAA should be OFF (its 8-frame Chocapic jitter double-jitters against the upscaler's Halton).
- **ROOT CAUSE FOUND for the atio6axx.dll crash — PACK-ACTIVATION FRAME TEAR (2026-08-19, "same crash" second report)**: the 08:40 session (FSR2 + irisQualityScale 0.7 + Complementary FSR pack active from startup) crashed IDENTICALLY, and the new `[IRIS] main render target was recreated mid-frame (1680x1050 -> 420x263)` warning caught the mechanism red-handed: **420x263 = qualityScale 0.25** — the NON-Iris scaled proxy. Sequence: onRenderBegin(frame 1409) evaluated `IrisCompat.isShaderPackInUse()` = FALSE (the pack's first frame hadn't applied yet) → took the NON-Iris path → swapped in the ScaledRenderTarget proxy at 420x263 → world rendered into it → **Iris activated MID-FRAME** (Voxy re-created its Iris render pipeline at 08:41:00-01, right between onRenderBegin and onSceneRendered) → onSceneRendered RE-EVALUATED the pack state live = TRUE → legacy Iris branch on the torn state → FSR1 initialized + dispatched EASU on the 420x263 proxy while Iris's activation GL work was in flight → 0xC0000005 in atio6axx.dll ~6s later. The SAME torn state explains the 21:44 session (FSR1 config, pack toggled mid-world). **The bug: `isShaderPackInUse()` was evaluated THREE times per frame (onRenderBegin for the swap decision, onSceneRendered + onRenderEnd for the dispatch/restore decision) and a mid-frame pack activation made them disagree — the swap and dispatch ran on different state machines.** **v6 (15:08 / WindowMixin viewport override — the REAL root cause)**: the 14:30 session's probe was DECISIVE — `IRIS-COMPOSITE MISMATCH viewport=1680x1050 tex=420x263` — Iris's composite pass drew at the WINDOW size (1680x1050) into the proxy FBO (420x263), and `destroyPipeline()` reflection succeeded (`Destroying pipeline minecraft:overworld` 14:31:40) but Iris recreated at the SAME native size (`Creating pipeline` 14:31:50) — proving Iris sizes its pipeline from `Window.getWidth()/getHeight()`, not from `mainRT.width/height`. Our WindowMixin had NO getWidth/getHeight overrides (AGENTS.md was outdated), so Iris always saw the real window (1680x1050) regardless of the proxy swap. Fix: new `@Inject(HEAD, cancellable)` overrides on `Window.getWidth()`/`getHeight()` in `WindowMixin` — when `windowScaleActive && irisSwapActiveStatic`, return `scaledWidthForWindow/scaledHeightForWindow` (proxy dims). Iris now builds its pipeline and composite at proxy size. Static flags set in onRenderBegin, cleared in onSceneRendered (after FSR, before GUI) and onRenderEnd (belt-and-suspenders). Removed: `forceIrisPipelineRebuild()` (unreliable reflection), `isSwapForciblyDisabled()` latch, and associated fields. One-frame pipeline rebuild at swap-engage is the cost. Validated: build green (jar 47,441,401 B), deployed 15:08 (backup `.bak-20260823-1508`). Fix = FRAME LOCK: capture `irisActiveThisFrame` once in onRenderBegin (with `irisActivePrevFrame` for transition detection) and reuse it in onSceneRendered/onRenderEnd — never re-evaluate live. Plus two pass-through guards: (a) on the TRANSITION frame (`irisActiveThisFrame != irisActivePrevFrame` — pack setup in flight) onSceneRendered calls `restoreDefaultTarget()` and returns (one native frame, no FSR dispatch); (b) in the legacy Iris branch, a mid-frame mainRT replacement (`actualRT != mainRenderTarget`) now adopts the live target for BOOKKEEPING but SKIPS the FSR1 dispatch that frame (the previous round's "adopt AND dispatch" was still dispatching into the settling reload). With the lock, the mid-frame-flip frame (1409) runs consistently on the non-Iris path (proxy 420x263 → FSR2 upscale via the shared GL section — the standard flow), and the frame AFTER the flip (1410) passes through. Validated: build green, deployed 08:44 (jar 47,370,822 B, backup `.bak-20260819-0839`); config unchanged (`FSR2`, `irisQualityScale 0.7`). Expected log next launch: `[IRIS] shader-pack state transition (active=true) — skipping FSR this frame` once, then `[IRIS-TEMPORAL]` + `[IRIS-JITTER]` on the following frames. If the driver still faults AFTER those lines, the crash is in the temporal pipelines' own dispatch (a different layer) — but this fix removes the frame-tear class that produced BOTH reported crashes.
- **THE atio6axx.dll crash had a THIRD, exact cause — the IRIS-JITTER probe's FBO readback (2026-08-19, hs_err_pid22160)**: after the frame-tear fix, the 09:08 session PROVED the FSR2 Iris temporal mode works — `[IRIS-TEMPORAL] shader pack + FSR2 selected — scaled mainRT swap active (1176x735 -> 1680x1050)`, the `[IRIS] shader-pack state transition ... skipping FSR this frame` pass-through, `[CAMSTILL]` and a saturation-blit degrade all fired — then crashed with a REAL hs_err (the JVM caught this one) whose Java frames nailed it: `GL11C.glReadPixels <- readTexBlockRgba8 <- irisJitterProbe <- onSceneRendered`. The jitter auto-probe's GL-4.3/4.4 fallback (this AMD driver reports < 4.5, so `GL.getCapabilities().OpenGL45` is false) attached the scaled proxy's color texture to a scratch READ framebuffer and called glReadPixels at renderLevel(TAIL) — with Iris's framebuffer state live, that FBO-attach + readback combination faulted the driver (write at 0x...c9e0, atio6axx.dll+0x243604c). **Fix**: `readTexBlockRgba8` NEVER touches a framebuffer anymore — the non-4.5 fallback is now `glGetTexImage` (whole-texture read + block copy; no FBO attachment, immune to the caller's FBO state; vanilla TextureTarget color is RGBA8 so the read is a clean memcpy) with `glGetTexLevelParameteri` for dims. Plus FAIL-FAST hardening: `irisProbeReadFailed()` aborts the whole probe after 3 failed readbacks with the safe "assumes pack consumes vanilla jitter" verdict (unjittered accumulation is softer, never broken) instead of retrying into a driver that already faulted. Deployed 09:10 (jar 47,371,161 B, backup `.bak-20260819-0844`). Remaining probe reads: ~7 whole-texture readbacks total (~3.4MB each at 1176x735) over the first ~0.5s of Iris mode, then it concludes forever. If the driver STILL faults, the next hs_err Java frames will name glGetTexImage — the fallback is then to gate the probe behind debugMode (the verdict is not load-bearing for Complementary: TAA off ⇒ gbufferProjection follows the vanilla jitter captureAndJitter applies).

(2026-08-19, the "loaded in but upscaler dead" fix)**: the 09:19 session loaded WITHOUT crashing (probe aborted gracefully after 3 failed readbacks — the glGetTexImage fix + fail-fast held) but the log showed `[ERROR] ShaderSourceManager: no canonical source for fsr2/ACCUMULATE_CS (resource missing?)` + `FSR2: shader compilation failed`. The jar therefore shipped with NO classpath `/shaders/` resources, and ShaderSourceManager.get() loads the CANONICAL first and returns "" (fallback) when it's missing — the on-disk copies in config/fsr-reloaded/shaders/ were never consulted. Every FSR2/3 dispatch compiled nothing → the whole temporal upscaler was dead (the visible image was the raw 1176x735 composite at whatever fallback). **Recovery**: the instance's `config/fsr-reloaded/shaders/{fsr2,fsr3}` copies ARE the known-good canonical set (Aug 11-12 bootstraps/patch-lockstep copies — every AGENTS.md shader fix is Aug 6-12: real-FSR1 RCAS port verified via hitMin/hitMax in both RCAS_CS, uCamReactive ×2 in both ACCUMULATE_CS, camStill/camReactive/jitter-cancel all present).
