If the FSR2 dispatch faults too, the next WER/hs_err offset will say so.
- **"quality slider for FSR4 is not working / is it even working" (2026-08-19)**: the 09:27 session was the FIRST full FSR2-under-Iris success — `[IRIS-TEMPORAL]` swap (1176x735 -> 1680x1050), `[IRIS-JITTER] probe aborted after 3 failed readbacks` (fail-fast, no crash), `FSR2: All 6 shaders compiled (MV gen + 5 passes)` (the restored /shaders/ resources), live `[ACCUM_WEIGHT]` readbacks, `[DIAG_FSR2] frameIdx=20 jitter=(0.000,-0.167) prev=(0.031,0.426) hasPrev=true camStillExact=1`, 15-25ms frames, clean ~80s session + normal quit. The user's follow-up was about FSR4: **FSR4 under a shader pack is NOT working — by design (pass-through since the 09:26 change), and the "Quality" slider appeared to do nothing for it.** Two UI truths the screen now reflects: (1) the MAIN Quality slider (qualityScale) is hidden under Iris (inert — onRenderBegin uses irisQualityScale there); (2) the IRIS Quality slider (irisQualityScale) was shown for EVERY version under a pack, including FSR1/FSR4 (pass-through — irisQualityScale is only consumed by the irisSwapActive FSR2/FSR3-upscaler path), so for FSR4 it was an inert "control does nothing" — the exact bug class the screen's own comments warn about. **Fix (SettingsScreen.java)**: the Iris Quality slider is now gated to `FSR2`/`FSR3_UPS` (the versions that take the swap and use irisQualityScale); for FSR1/FSR3_FG/FSR3_FG_DX12/FSR4 under a pack it is replaced by a notice: `Iris Quality: N/A — <version> under a shader pack is pass-through; select FSR 2 / FSR 3 (upscaler) for pack scaling` (version pills rebuild the screen, so it swaps live). Honest answer to the user: FSR2/3 is the upscaler that works through Iris (proven 09:27); FSR4 needs VulkanMod (the 26.1.2/Vulkan Optimized instance) — in this GL-only instance FSR4 under a pack is pass-through, and without a pack it would take the crash-prone D3D12-interop path. Deployed 09:31 (jar 47,421,724 B, backup `.bak-20260819-0926`). NOTE: the user's config had irisQualityScale moved to 0.25 during testing — 0.5-0.65 is the supported quality range.
- **FSR4 VK hand-zone identity fix — "the terrain is getting smoothened but not my hand" (2026-08-20, FIX)**: with terrain/sky finally accumulating under VulkanMod, the FIRST-PERSON HAND stayed jagged. The hand-zone identity fix (isHand + pose gate, 2026-08-08) was never re-ported after the Aug-6 revert wiped it, and even the original depth-gated version (`depth < 0.55`) was inert on the VK path — real depth is unreachable (noRealDepth), so the gate never fired. Root cause: the hand renders in VIEW space (camera-attached) — its true screen position is FIXED under all camera motion — but the constant-depth reprojection gives it the world-anchored MV of whatever it covers, so the SDK reads the hand's history at the wrong place every frame and can never accumulate it → jagged fist/held-item edges while terrain reconstructs. Fix (`fsr4_vk_mvgen.comp`): `isHand = uv.y > 0.65 && abs(uv.x-0.5) < 0.45 && (noRealDepth || depth < 0.55)` → force `mv = 0` (identity reprojection — exact for camera-attached content), placed after the still-camera gate so it also applies during motion. With mv=0 the MV ramp + cutout are inert (mvLen==0) and the hand inherits the same noRealDepth camFloor as terrain (walk 0.30 / sprint 0.42 / cap 0.7), so it smooths exactly like terrain during motion and fully accumulates standing still — no reactive override, no new smear beyond the FSR2/3-accepted swing tradeoff. Known tradeoffs (no pose gate — needs a prev-frame color staging this shader doesn't have): (a) hand swing/sway animation motion is absent from the MV field → fast swings can smear (same accepted limitation as the FSR2/3 path); (b) looking straight down while walking can catch near ground in the zone. **ALSO: the .comp source was reconstructed from the recovered 12,428-byte SPIR-V** — the Aug-18 recovery restored only the binary (header = ground truth) and the on-disk .comp was the stale Aug-6 baseline (marked "do NOT recompile"). This session decompiled the header's SPIR-V with spirv-cross, verified every constant against the binary (noRealDepth 0.02/0.3, ndcZ 0.999, Y-flip, sky classifier 0.58/0.12/0.55/1.15, flat-gradient 0.08 + white-body 0.88/0.06, still gate 0.5/0.003, ramp 32/16, camFloor 0.05/0.55/0.1/0.05/0.7, cutout 2.5/0.01/0.5/0.45), and rewrote the .comp to match PLUS the hand fix — the .comp is the source of truth again and recompile-safe. Validated: glslangValidator clean (SPIR-V 12,428 → 12,956 bytes), spirv-val clean, header regenerated (BYTE count; 12,956 verified embedded in the DLL, old 12,428 gone), fsrvk.dll 137,216 → 137,728 (only jni_bridge.cpp recompiled — the header include), `gradlew build` green (jar 47,422,602 B), deployed 16:50 to gradle-client run/mods + PrismLauncher 26.1 (backups `.bak-20260820-handfix`). GL-path FSR4 MV-gen (FSRProcessor MV_GEN_CS) left unpatched (dead under VulkanMod — documented scoping).
- **ECLIPSE SHADER PACK COMPILE FIX — "terrain_translucent: RgbToHsv no matching overloaded function found" (2026-08-25, the pack-load failure that makes shaders DISABLE)**: the 11:04 session on 1.21.11 (pack: Eclipse-Shader-Unstable.zip) failed at world-join: `Shader compilation log for terrain_translucent: ERROR: 0:1355: 'RgbToHsv' : no matching overloaded function found ... Failed to create shader rendering pipeline, disabling shaders!` — Iris DISABLED ALL SHADERS (pack off, base game through the FSR fallback). Root cause (pack bug, not the mod — the pack was unpatched/unchanged): `shaders/dimensions/all_translucent.fsh` (and `composite1.fsh`) include `lib/hsv.glsl` ONLY inside `#ifdef IS_LPV_ENABLED` (resp. the LPV/Photonics guard), but both include `lib/specular.glsl` UNCONDITIONALLY, and specular.glsl calls `RgbToHsv()` (defined only in hsv.glsl) at its lines 295/1928. With `LPV_ENABLED=false` in the pack options (Eclipse-Shader-Unstable.zip.txt — POTATO/VL/LOW/MEDIUM profiles), `IS_LPV_ENABLED` is undefined → RgbToHsv undeclared → compile error → the whole pack fails. Why only `terrain_translucent`: Sodium's chunk TRANSLUCENT pass maps to ProgramId.Water → the pack's `gbuffers_water.fsh` → includes `/dimensions/all_translucent.fsh` (all worlds share the single /dimensions file). Solid compiled because `all_solid.fsh` includes hsv.glsl UNCONDITIONALLY (line 7) — the translucent variant's guard is the inconsistency. **Fix (pack zip in the instance)**: moved `#include "/lib/hsv.glsl"` out of the LPV guard to an unconditional position (right after the SSBOs include) in BOTH `all_translucent.fsh` and `composite1.fsh` — mirroring all_solid.fsh; lpv_common/lpv_render stay guarded. No double-include with LPV on (the guarded copy was removed, so exactly one unconditional include either way). Audited every other RgbToHsv/HsvToRgb consumer: all_solid ✓ unconditional, voxels.fsh ✓ unconditional, all_particles.fsh ✓ (guard commented out), composite4.fsh ✓ (both include and call inside `#if RAINBOW > 0`), lpv_render ✓ LPV-only, voxy_solid ✓ self-guarded. Script `scripts/patch_eclipse_hsv.py` (idempotent; backs up to `.bak-20260825-hsvfix`). **v2 (11:19) — the FIRST deploy at 11:13 SILENTLY DID NOTHING, and the 11:16 session still failed (same error, same line 1355)**: `patch_zip` extracted the pack into a temp dir but looked for the shader files at `<tmp>/shaders/...` while `extractall` nests them under `<tmp>/<TopLevelDir>/shaders/...` (the pack's top-level folder) — so the zip was re-zipped UNPATCHED; worse, the 11:13 "verification" was a FALSE POSITIVE (it matched the pre-existing GUARDED include at line 187 instead of the unconditional one at line 11, and the identical 1355 line number in the 11:16 error — no +4 shift from the added lines — was the tell). Fixed: resolve the pack root under the extraction (`find_pack_root`), and verify_file now counts only real `#include` lines and asserts a single unconditional hsv include (< line 50) before/after writing, plus a re-read of the written zip asserting the patch comment. Re-deployed 11:19 (zip 47,097,055 B, line-11 include + guarded-copy-gone verified via fresh extraction). **v3 (11:2x) — next bug in the same class, exposed by the hsv fix: `terrain_translucent: 'GetLpvPosition' : no matching overloaded function found` (lines 1386-1407, varying per preset)**. The user's report made the pattern explicit: ALL presets below HIGH fail (POTATO/VL/LOW/MEDIUM/ALTERNATE_MEDIUM), HIGH+ work — i.e. everything with `LPV_ENABLED=false` fails, exactly the guarded-include class. Second instance: `lib/lpv_common.glsl` (defines `GetLpvPosition` + `LpvSize`/`LpvBlockSkyRange`) is included only under `#ifdef IS_LPV_ENABLED`, but `specular.glsl` (unconditional in both patched files) calls `GetLpvPosition` from unguarded functions (`planeIntersect`, `photonicsReflectionShading`), and all_translucent/composite1 call it directly at their lines 1061/1589 unguarded. lpv_common.glsl is include-safe without LPV (LpvSize has a `#else uint(5)` fallback; GetLpvPosition's LPV-off branch uses `relativeEyePosition`, which IS an Iris exclusive uniform — `IrisExclusiveUniforms.class` — available in all programs; all_solid already includes lpv_common unconditionally). All other LPV identifiers in the chain are guarded (specular's `SampleLpvLinear` sits under `#if defined IS_LPV_ENABLED && defined MC_GL_ARB_shader_image_load_store`; composite3/all_particles' GetLpvPosition calls are guarded). Fix (same two files): strip hsv + lpv_common from the guarded blocks and include both unconditionally after the SSBOs anchor (mirroring all_solid.fsh); keep lpv_render (needs LPV-only textures) guarded; ALSO moved composite1's manual `uniform vec3 relativeEyePosition;` out of its LPV/Photonics guard (redundant with the Iris uniform but all_solid/all_translucent declare it unconditionally — belt-and-braces for LOW). Re-deployed 11:2x to the instance (backups `.bak-20260825-hsvfix` = original, `.bak-20260825-hsvonly` = intermediate; script `scripts/patch_eclipse_hsv.py` v3 idempotent — skips if the patch marker is present). Next preset test should compile terrain_translucent on every preset; any further LOW-only failures would be in programs the aborted pipeline never reached (composite1 itself is the next candidate — same two fixes apply to it already). **Tie-in to the "terrain disappears when switching FSR versions" campaign**: a pack compile failure of this class is exactly the "terrain vanishes" mechanism — an FSR version switch triggers markDirty → mainRT resize → Iris pipeline rebuild → compile fails → shaders disabled → pack terrain replaced by base-game rendering; the 08-24 terrain probe/latch work treated the symptom, this removes one of the causes. Next launch: pack compiles, `Using shaderpack: Eclipse-Shader-Unstable.zip` followed by a clean pipeline create (no terrain_translucent error, no "disabling shaders"). NOTE: the pack must be reloaded (restart or F3+T) for the patched zip to take effect; if the user updates/re-downloads the pack, re-run the script (or report the include-order bug upstream — the pack's own `all_solid.fsh` shows the intended pattern).
- **PACK-SWITCH FSR4 CRASH — 0xC0000005 when the pack switch (or FSR re-enable) collides with the cross-API D3D12 init (2026-08-25, "Process crashed with exitcode -1073741819" — crash dump javaw.exe.26384.dmp, 11:54:44)**: the Eclipse compile fix WORKED (11:53 session: `[IRIS-COMPOSITE] OK ... match proxy`, `FSR4-PATH real D3D12 dispatch engaged`), then the user re-toggled FSR4 (off→on at 11:54:18: `bypass: resources destroyed` = shutdownFsr4, `init OK (924x578 -> 1680x1050)`, D3D12 pool `CreateCommittedResource`×10 + `init OK ... cached`) WHILE switching the pack Eclipse→BSL (11:54:22: `Destroying pipeline` → recreate + Photonics `aligned=true` re-alloc). Crash 11:54:44 with fsrvk.dll + amd_fsr4_api.dll + amdxc64.dll loaded; EXCEPTIONCODE=0xc0000005 (access violation) from a native thread. The minidump has no saved thread context (ctxsz=0) so exact stack is unrecoverable, but the crash class is unambiguous: the FSR4 D3D12 SDK path dispatching/initializing while the Iris composite pipeline is mid-rebuild (the documented AMD-driver atio6axx/amdxc64 fault — the same family as the 08-19 pack-transition crashes). **Two gaps exposed**: (1) the pack-activation hold/skip fires only on the activation FLIP (`irisActiveThisFrame && !irisActivePrevFrame`) — a pack SWITCH (Eclipse→BSL) keeps irisActive true and evades it, yet still tears down + rebuilds the pipeline; (2) the FSR4 re-enable re-init runs IMMEDIATELY because `deferFsr4Init` requires `fsr4ContextRenderW==0` (the sentinel is never cleared on disable), so the cross-API D3D12 init bypasses the first-init calm gate. **Fix (both in FSRProcessor)**: (1) new **pack-switch hold** — track `fsr4PackNamePrev` (from `IrisCompat.getCurrentShaderPackName()` — public API `/getIrisConfig().getPackName()` fallback) each frame while a pack is active; a name change while `(isFsr4()||isFrameGenDx12())&&enabled` sets `fsr4DimChangeAtMs`, force-suspends FSR4, and clears the calm-streak/EMA — reusing the ENTIRE 10s FSR4_DIM_CHANGE_HOLD machinery (resume requires `dimHoldElapsed`; re-init requires the same `dimChangeAtMs < hold` defer; no dispatch into the rebuild). Logged once per switch (`FSR4: shader-pack switch {} -> {} — holding FSR1 for {}ms`). The name isn't read when a pack is off (`fsr4PackNamePrev=null`), so a fresh activation isn't a false "switch". (2) **FSR re-enable re-init deferral** — the `FSR4 not selected` teardown branch now also resets `fsr4ContextRenderW/DisplayW = 0`, so a later off→on routes through the FIRST-INIT calm-gate + grace instead of running the cross-API pool/SDK init unconditionally (the documented "crashes when I open a world with FSR off and turn it on" enabler). Deliberate in-session SCALE changes keep their fast-reinit path (their teardown sites don't clear the sentinel). Validated: compile + build green (jar 47,446,000 B, md5 62098594d192045c), bytecode verified (`getCurrentShaderPackName` call + `fsr4PackNamePrev` field), deployed 11:59 to the 1.21.11 instance (backup `.bak-20260825-packswitch` = the crashing 11:16 build). Expected next run: switching packs (or toggling FSR4 off/on) logs `shader-pack switch ... holding FSR1 for 10000ms` and FSR4 stays on FSR1 through the rebuild — no 0xC0000005. **v4 (11:5x) — `composite1.fsh: 'cameraPosition' : undeclared identifier` (the "now eclipse doesnt work at all" report)**: the v3 placement put the unconditional hsv/lpv_common includes at the TOP of both files (right after the SSBOs include), but glslang rejects FORWARD REFERENCES — `lpv_common.glsl`'s `GetLpvPosition` LPV-off branch uses `cameraPosition`, which is only declared by `lib/projections.glsl` (included at composite1 line 210 / all_translucent 169) — so composite1 failed at the merged-source line ~88 while all_translucent happened to pass (its projections include lands earlier relative to the failure point; empirically both are the same latent bug, only the line moved). The pack's ORIGINAL guarded includes sat at lines 224/187 (AFTER the projections include) — the author's placement was already correct; v3 moved them before it. Fix (v4, script rewritten): strip ALL hsv/lpv_common includes + patch comments, then insert the unconditional pair IMMEDIATELY BEFORE the `#include "/lib/specular.glsl"` line (its only unguarded caller) — by that point cameraPosition (projections) and relativeEyePosition (line 212/227) are both declared; no forward refs anywhere. Idempotent across the v3 layout. Validated: glslangValidator on the JCPP-merged sources (`#version 430 compatibility` + MC_HAND_DEPTH 0.125 injected, numeric-#line include expansion) — BOTH all_translucent.fsh and composite1.fsh now compile CLEAN (exit 0) at LOW config; also sweep-compiled every non-gbuffers dimension .fsh: only false-positive harness artifacts (Iris-provided `MC_RENDER_QUALITY`/`colortex4`/`RayJob`, and a pre-existing `diagonal3` macro-redefinition in composite2/3/4 that the real GL driver tolerates — HIGH works with the same code). Depth-tracker audit of every unguarded `RgbToHsv`/`GetLpvPosition` call across all dimension files: only all_solid (hsv unconditional ✓), voxels (hsv unconditional ✓) and composite3 (IS_LPV_ENABLED-guarded ✓) — the bug class is fully covered by the two patched files. Deployed 11:5x (script `scripts/patch_eclipse_hsv.py` v4; backups `.bak-20260825-hsvfix` = original). Next launch: every preset compiles — the whole LPV-off program set (terrain/water/composite1-12/deferred/final) is now verified at LOW.
- **BSL TERRAIN FLICKER NOTE (2026-08-25, "the terrain dissapearing glitch is also happening with bsl shaders exept a timy part of the terrain is flickering")**: the 11:29-11:31 session (BSL_v10.1.3 + Photonics + 1.21.11) shows BSL itself is healthy — it compiled with only Iris compat-patch warnings, `[TERRAIN-PROBE]` read REAL values throughout (e.g. `PROXY 1008x630 skyTop=(49,51,57) terrain=(35,39,47) var=1.2 brokenVote=0/3` — the 08-25 probe-readback fix works, no READ_FAIL, no latch), and the only `(0,0,0)` probes were during the Eclipse-failed period (shaders disabled = black frame). The flicker the user saw is the version-switch mechanism, not the pack: the session opened with FSR4 selected at scene 1008x630 (irisScale 0.6) while the FSR4 context was still 1092x683 (0.65) → `[FSR4-PATH] context 1092x683->1680x1050 != scene 1008x630->1680x1050 — FSR4 dispatch skipped (FSR1 until recreate)` → size-mismatch guard suspended FSR4, then GPU saturation (Photonics GI) also suspended it → FSR1 spatial fallback upscaled 1008x630→1680x1050 for the rest of the session (the `[SWITCH-DIAG] FSR1 fallback dispatching — spatialFallbackReason=FSR4_SUSPENDED` spam) until the user switched config to FSR1. The recreate path (debounced needsRecreate in onRenderBegin, FSR4_DEBOUNCE_MS) exists but never fired because the user changed version before the debounce elapsed. Takeaway: the 08-24 terrain-disappears campaign's instrumentation now WORKS (probes real, votes clean); a recurring flicker on BSL will show a brokenVote latch or a READ_FAIL — until then the mechanism is the documented switch/saturation fallback, and the recommendation stands: run FSR2/FSR3 through Iris (the supported upscaler under packs) or keep FSR4 on the VulkanMod instance.


- **TERRAIN-DISAPPEARS CAMPAIGN — probe + latch + fast-init + post-switch protection (2026-08-24, "terrain disappears when switching FSR versions" fix)**: the user's report (1.21.11 + Iris + Photonics): switching FSR versions in the settings screen made terrain vanish until another quality change. The 08-24 session (4 deploys: flapgrace 11:15, initfix 16:47, terrainprobe 19:18, current 19:51) added, all in FSRProcessor: (1) **VERSION-SWITCH DIAGNOSTIC** — the first 60 frames after markDirty log `[SWITCH-DIAG] frame N post-dirty: <reason>` with the exact gate blocking dispatch (not-FSR4 / fsr4Initialized=false / suspended / size-mismatch / DISPATCHING), closing the gap between "FSR4: init OK" and a real dispatch; (2) **POST-SWITCH TERRAIN PROTECTION** — during the first 8 frames after a switch the Iris pipeline is recompiling (gbuffers not linked yet, composite = sky+fog only); the FSR1 fallback SKIPS its blit those frames and lets the PREVIOUS mainRT content display (a valid complete scene), preventing the sky-only flash; (3) **TERRAIN-CONTENT PROBE** — reads 16x16 RGBA8 blocks (sky-top / mid-horizon / terrain-bottom, GL rows bottom-up so y=0 is terrain) from the proxy AND the displayed MAIN target every 2s up to 40 probes, logs `[TERRAIN-PROBE] ... meanRGB=... luma=... var=...` so "pack genuinely rendered sky-only" vs "FSR1 blit failed" is decidable; (4) **BROKEN-COMPOSITE DETECTOR + LATCH** — the proxy vote (bright flat-ish sky up top AND dark flat void below = the screenshot signature) counts broken votes; 3 consecutive latch `irisSwapBrokenLatch` → native pass-through for the rest of the world session (native always renders correctly — the proven pre-port behavior); the latch clears on a null→non-null level flip (`prevLevelNull` / world join) so the scaled path retries in the next world; (5) **DELIBERATE-SWITCH FAST INIT** — the 25s first-init load-storm grace applies only to a GENUINE first init (`fsr4ContextRenderW==0`); a settings click in an established world (>20s old, `worldJoinedAtMs`) re-inits after the 500ms debounce instead of parking FSR4 on FSR1 for 25s; (6) **DRAIN STALE ERRORS FIRST** in the readback — glGetError() is global; a leftover error from an earlier pass made every probe read "READ_FAIL" (the 19:26 session); drain before each read so only a FRESH error fails it.
- **PROBE READBACK FIX — glGetTexImage was reading the WRONG GL object (2026-08-25, the 19:57 session's all-READ_FAIL)**: the terrain probe deployed at 19:51 read `READ_FAIL` on EVERY sample (PROXY and MAIN alike) in the 19:55-19:58 1.21.11 session — the broken-composite vote could never fire, so the terrain-disappears self-heal was dead on arrival. Root cause: `readTexBlockRgba8`'s GL 4.3/4.4 fallback (this AMD driver reports <4.5) called `glGetTexImage(tex, ...)` / `glGetTexLevelParameteri(tex, ...)` passing the texture NAME as the `target` enum and never binding — glGetTexImage reads the texture BOUND to the target, so name-as-target is GL_INVALID_ENUM on every call (draining stale errors cannot help; each read generates a FRESH error). This also retroactively explains the Iris jitter probe's "aborted after 3 failed readbacks" at every Iris session since the 08-19 glGetTexImage switch — the probe has silently run its fail-fast "assumes pack consumes vanilla jitter" verdict (safe for Complementary with TAA off, but the verdict was never actually measured). Fix: bind `tex` to GL_TEXTURE_2D on the active unit (save `GL_TEXTURE_BINDING_2D`, restore in a finally), pass `GL_TEXTURE_2D` as the target to both calls. No FBO involved — the 08-19 atio6axx driver-fault class (FBO attach + glReadPixels at renderLevel TAIL) stays untouched. Validated: compileClientJava + build green (jar 47,445,607 B), deployed 10:58 to the 1.21.11 instance + gradle run/mods (backup `.bak-20260825-probefix`). Expected next run: `[TERRAIN-PROBE]` reads real meanRGB/luma/var values instead of READ_FAIL, and the Iris jitter probe's first readbacks succeed (it will MEASURE the pack instead of assuming).
- **FSR4 POST-RESUME FLAP — the dispatch-failure suspension was the one ungated site (2026-08-25, log "restored 19:58:17 → suspendedFor=7ms")**: the 19:55-19:58 session still flapped on a ~3-6s cycle (restored 19:58:17 → suspendedFor=7ms; restored 19:58:33 → 6ms) with Photonics GI bursts every few seconds. The 08-23/08-24 resume grace (FSR4_RESUME_GRACE_MS=1500) covers the pre-check skip path and the fence-timeout path — but the DISPATCH-FAILURE suspension (`Fsr4Native.dispatchFsr4` returned false) was NOT gated: the world-calm resume can re-arm FSR4 while the GPU queue is still busy (CPU-side calm world frames ≠ drained queue — the probe fence measures GPU drain, the calm streak only measures worldMs), and the very first resumed dispatch fails fast → `fsr4Suspended=true` within milliseconds → the whole flap. Fix: `dispInResumeGrace` — within the 1.5s grace a dispatch failure does NOT suspend (the frame keeps the previous FSR4 output, stale but valid); `lastFsr4DispatchFail` backoff still applies and the 5-failure hard-disable escalation still fires on a genuinely broken D3D12 path. All suspension sites are now grace-gated: pre-check, fence-timeout, dispatch-failure, and (2026-08-23) the pack-activation hold. Expected next run: the restored → 7ms re-suspend cycle is gone — a resume survives the first Photonics burst, and only a sustained storm (5+ failures or 3+ spikes AFTER the grace) can re-suspend, which then needs more calm frames (rapid-episode escalation).
